systematic review

Convergent Mechanisms and Emerging Therapeutics
in ARPKD-Associated Congenital Hepatic Fibrosis

Nathan and Flora Johnson · Patient Family Systematic Review · Version 7.8 · September 16, 2026 · nathaniel_johnson@yahoo.com
A cross-disciplinary synthesis across hepatology, nephrology, gene therapy, biliary biology, and drug delivery. August 26, 2026 additions: (1) CDK1 validated pharmacological target in PKHD1 liver — reduces cysts AND fibrosis in vivo. Liu S, Chen X, Liu Z, Tang J, Tian H, Peng Y, Mao X, Dai R, Zhao B, Li X, Li L, Luo L, Ma M. iScience June 5, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2026.116288. ⚠️ Previously incorrectly attributed to "Djouder" — correct first author is Liu/Ma lab. RO-3306 CDK1 inhibitor; Pkhd1−/− rat and mouse models; individual Pkhd1-deficient cholangiocytes generate millimeter-scale cysts within 1 year (same paper, two findings).; (2) ECM primary defect is laminin/collagen IV LOSS, not gain — therapeutic goal is basement membrane restoration. ✅ VERIFIED Sep 16, 2026, and the primary source identified. Our citation pointed to the review — Waddell SH, Brennan E, Boulter L. "The instructive scar – the dynamic role of extracellular matrix in biliary pathophysiology." Dis Model Mech 19(8):dmm052835, 1 August 2026. DOI: 10.1242/dmm.052835 — which states that the diseased bile ducts of individuals with both CHF and Caroli disease lack the characteristic expression of laminin and type IV collagen found in the basement membrane of biliary epithelial cells in health, citing Yasoshima et al. 2009 as the primary source. That primary paper is Yasoshima M, Sato Y, Furubo S, Kizawa K, Sanzen T, Ozaki S, Harada K, Nakanuma Y. "Matrix proteins of basement membrane of intrahepatic bile ducts are degraded in congenital hepatic fibrosis and Caroli's disease." J Pathol 217(3):442-451, 2009. DOI: 10.1002/path.2472, which demonstrated degraded laminin and type IV collagen around intrahepatic bile ducts in human CHF, human Caroli's disease and PCK rats. ⭐ MECHANISM AND THERAPEUTIC LEAD WE HAD NOT CAPTURED: PCK cholangiocytes overexpress plasminogen and tissue-type plasminogen activator (tPA), generating excess plasmin that lyses the basement membrane. In Matrigel culture, PCK cholangiocytes degraded laminin and collagen — and adding α2-antiplasmin to the medium INHIBITED that degradation. This is a direct, published rescue of the basement-membrane defect in an ARPKD model, and it is the mechanistic rationale for antifibrinolytic agents (e.g. tranexamic acid) in ARPKD-CHF. Open question: has plasmin inhibition been tested in vivo in a Pkhd1 model?; (3) FXR in ARPKD-CHF — a proposed absent brake, not a confirmed target. FXR has not been directly measured in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells; that experiment remains undone and is one of the clearest gaps in this framework. Three supporting observations: (a) linafexor, a first-in-class pulsatile FXR agonist, is efficacious in biliary cholangiopathies including PSC and is in Phase 3 (NCT07282353), with the important finding that sustained FXR activation independent of compound identity induces severe toxicity — establishing activation duration as a determinant of therapeutic index (Zang Y, Shi J, Zhao G et al., Xu HE corresponding. Nature 655:758-768, 2026); (b) the FXR–YAP axis in biliary identity from the Djouder laboratory, including the observation that FXR/YAP knockout does not produce cysts (personal communication, not peer-reviewed); (c) FXR→SOCS3→STAT3 suppression, confirmed in hepatocytes and extrapolated to biliary cells; (4) Single Pkhd1-deficient cholangiocytes autonomously generate millimeter-scale biliary cysts within one year.
PKHD1 CDS = 12,222 bp (≈12.2 kb) CHF obligatory in ARPKD No approved CHF therapy No completed CHF-endpoint trial PKDnet (Hartung, CHOP/PEDSnet) — ARPKD cohort identification

Sources for the figures above

  • PKHD1 CDS = 12,222 bp. The longest open reading frame is 12,222 bp across 67 exons within an ≈470 kb genomic span on chromosome 6p, encoding fibrocystin/polyductin at 4,074 amino acids (447 kDa). The mature transcript is ≈16 kb; 12.2 kb is the coding sequence, and it is the relevant figure for AAV packaging arithmetic — exceeding AAV's ≈4.7 kb capacity roughly 2.6-fold. (Ward CJ, Hogan MC, Rossetti S, Walker D, Sneddon T, Wang X, Kubly V, Cunningham JM, Bacallao R, Ishibashi M, Milliner DS, Torres VE, Harris PC. "The gene mutated in autosomal recessive polycystic kidney disease encodes a large, receptor-like protein." Nat Genet 30(3):259-269, 2002, via OMIM 606702; Onuchic LF, Furu L, Nagasawa Y et al. Am J Hum Genet 70(5):1305-1317, 2002; Zhang MZ, Mai W, Li C, … Nakanuma Y, … Wu G. PNAS 101(8):2311-2316, 2004. PMID: 14983006.)
  • CHF obligatory in ARPKD. Congenital hepatic fibrosis is invariably present on histology in ARPKD but is usually clinically asymptomatic; clinical expression varies widely, and a recent review reports clinically manifest CHF in approximately 50% of cases. The European ARPKD registry states that ARPKD obligatorily encompasses congenital hepatic fibrosis due to ductal plate malformation, with or without intrahepatic bile duct dilatation. (Ebner K, Feldkoetter M, Ariceta G, Bergmann C, … Zerres K, Dötsch J, Schaefer F, Liebau MC; ESCAPE Study Group, GPN Study Group. "Rationale, design and objectives of ARegPKD, a European ARPKD registry study." BMC Nephrol 16:22, 2015. PMID: 25886171. PMC4359504; Świąder-Leśniak A et al., transient elastography in 21 paediatric ARPKD patients, Children's Memorial Health Institute, Warsaw, PMC6336693; O'Brien K, Gunay-Aygun M et al. J Pediatr Gastroenterol Nutr 54(1):83-89, 2012.)
  • No approved CHF therapy. Treatment remains largely symptomatic and opinion-based even in the most advanced medical centres (ARegPKD, above).
  • No completed CHF-endpoint trial. Current management consists of supportive care: Baveno VII variceal protocols, splanchnic vasoconstrictors (terlipressin first-line, somatostatin or octreotide as alternatives), endoscopy within 12 hours, and transplantation. Octreotide in paediatric portal hypertension remains off-label. Clinically relevant: nearly half of patients with CHF — 45.8% — initially present with upper gastrointestinal bleeding. Portal hypertension develops in up to 60% of ARPKD patients; oesophageal varices are reported in up to 56%. (Wang A, He J, Liu K, Fu J, Chen J (corresponding). "Congenital hepatic fibrosis and related ciliopathies across the life course: current management, disease burden, and unmet needs in the era of genomic diagnosis and translational therapeutics." Front Pediatr 14:1928268, 3 September 2026. DOI: 10.3389/fped.2026.1928268. Affiliated Hospital of Panzhihua University, China.)
  • Additional figures from the same review, relevant to clinical monitoring. Splenomegaly is found in up to 69.6% of adults with CHF. Recurrent cholangitis has a cumulative incidence of 45.8% in adult CHF — a separate figure from the 45.8% presenting with upper gastrointestinal bleeding. CHF is clinically stratified into portal hypertensive, cholangitic and mixed subtypes; the portal hypertensive type is the most common and is seen more frequently in children and adolescents. Because liver injury in CHF is predominantly presinusoidal, transient elastography liver stiffness values are often LOWER than in cirrhosis, particularly in early disease — an important caveat when interpreting FibroScan results in this population, though stiffness rises with progression and portal hypertension. Hepatocellular carcinoma is rare in CHF owing to preserved lobular architecture, but cholangiocarcinoma risk is elevated where Caroli syndrome coexists. Baveno VII defines clinically significant portal hypertension as HVPG ≥ 10 mmHg. On histology, glutamine synthetase expression is strictly confined to acinar zone 3 in CHF, whereas cirrhosis of other aetiologies shows disordered or aberrant patterns — a useful adjunct in differentiating CHF from cirrhosis. The review also notes genetic heterogeneity beyond PKHD1: ZFYVE19 (biallelic loss-of-function mutations → CHF + sclerosing cholangiopathy + high-GGT cholestasis — Luan W, Hao CZ, Li JQ et al. J Med Genet 58(8):514-525, 2021. DOI: 10.1136/jmedgenet-2019-106706. PMID: 32737136), ANKS6, SEC63, SEC61B, LRP5, KIF12 and HNF1B.
  • PKDnet. A PEDSnet computable-phenotype study, PI Erum A. Hartung (CHOP), developing and validating a computable phenotype for ARPKD from diagnosis codes and EHR data — chart validation at CHOP (n≈50), then across all PEDSnet sites (n≈150). A PEDSnet inquiry identified 467 patients with an ARPKD diagnosis code plus 2,650 coded "polycystic kidney, unspecified type"; the investigators anticipate PKDnet may identify up to 400 ARPKD patients. This is cohort-identification infrastructure, trial-enabling rather than a trial network itself. (PEDSnet study page.)

🎯 Convergence & Path Forward

Added September 15, 2026 (v7.7). Every other section of this document catalogues findings. This section does something different: it assembles them. The central claim is that no component of a viable ARPKD-CHF therapeutic strategy is missing from the published literature — what is missing is the assembly. Five laboratories each hold one piece. None of them knows the others are holding the adjacent piece.

Principle 1 — Directional Contradictions in ARPKD Signalling

Two signalling pathways central to ARPKD have published evidence pointing in opposite therapeutic directions. Resolving which direction applies, in which cell type, is a prerequisite for any systemic therapy.

PathwayEvidence for activation being protectiveEvidence for inhibition being therapeutic
AMPKWalker RV, Yao Q, Xu H, Maranto A, Swaney KF, Ramachandran S, Li R, Cassina L, Polster BM, Outeda P, Boletta A, Watnick T, Qian F. "Fibrocystin/Polyductin releases a C-terminal fragment that translocates into mitochondria and suppresses cystogenesis." Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023 — FPC releases ICD15, which carries a mitochondrial targeting sequence, enhances mitochondrial respiration in renal epithelial cells, and suppresses cystogenesisLong DA (UCL), KRUK Paediatric & Rare Diseases award, October 2025 round, £208K — "Inhibiting AMP-activated protein kinase to modulate Autosomal Recessive Polycystic Kidney Disease"
STAT3 / gp130Perretta-Tejedor N, Price KL, Jafree DJ, Long DA et al. Physiol Rep 12(13):e16129 (2024) — Cardiotrophin-1, an IL-6-family cytokine signalling through gp130→STAT3, reduces disease severity in murine glomerular diseaseJiao M, Sun Y, Liu R, Yin X, Hou X, Yuan H, Dai Z, Chen Q, Zhu C. "P-STAT3-dependent SPP1 transcription in cholangiocytes drives hepatic stellate cell activation in congenital hepatic fibrosis." Commun Biol, 2026. PMID: 42265280 — PKHD1 deficiency drives progressive SPP1 upregulation in cholangiocytes; secreted OPN activates PI3K/Akt in hepatic stellate cells

⚠️ An important constraint on how far this can be read. It is tempting to frame these as a kidney-versus-liver divergence, but the evidence does not currently support that framing. Both AMPK entries are renal: the Walker/Qian work is explicitly in developing kidneys and renal epithelial cells (Division of Nephrology, University of Maryland), and Long's group is the UCL Centre for Kidney and Bladder Health. The AMPK contradiction is therefore within renal research, not between organs — and no published evidence addresses AMPK direction in ARPKD biliary epithelium at all. Similarly, the FPCct/Src/STAT3 work of Hassan, Dafinger, Liebau and Ziegler (Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 331:C934-C946, 2026) is titled and conducted in kidney epithelial cells, using principal-like MDCK Pkhd1-knockout spheroids, and should not be cited as liver evidence. The liver-side STAT3 evidence comes from Jiao/Zhu in cholangiocytes, as above.

What can be said: two pathways show opposite directional evidence in ARPKD, and the resolution is unknown. Candidate explanations: different isoform composition between collecting duct and biliary epithelium; stage-dependence, protective early and maladaptive late; or cell-type-specific downstream effectors.

Clinical implication, stated at the strength the evidence supports: until the direction is established for each cell type, a systemic agent acting on AMPK or STAT3 in ARPKD carries an unquantified risk of benefiting one compartment while harming another. That is an argument for cell-type-resolved data before systemic dosing, and for organ-targeted delivery where it is feasible.

Falsification test: measure AMPK isoform expression and phosphorylation state in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes versus PKHD1-deficient collecting duct cells from the same model. If identical, no cell-type divergence exists for AMPK. Routing: Long (AMPK direction), Walker and Qian (ICD15 mechanism), Ziegler and Liebau (FPCct), Zhu (cholangiocyte STAT3).

Principle 2 — A Vascular Limb in ARPKD-CHF

Our cascade framework treats ARPKD-CHF as epithelial and immune: FPC loss → SRC/STAT3 → SPP1 → macrophage recruitment → fibrosis. The vasculature appears only as a downstream consequence. Three lines of evidence suggest that may be an omission — though not in the direction first supposed.

The template — epithelial failure causing vascular failure. Chandler JC et al. J Pathol 2024. DOI: 10.1002/path.6339: in WT1 glomerular disease, dysfunctional podocytes stop sending VEGF-A and NRP1 signals to their adjacent endothelium, glomerular endothelial cells are lost, and glomerulosclerosis follows. The epithelial defect causes vascular failure, and vascular failure drives the scarring. Adrenomedullin is upregulated as a compensatory response but is insufficient against the wider milieu of dampened vascular signalling. Adrenomedullin is separately known to be markedly elevated in non-cirrhotic portal hypertension — the physiological category ARPKD-CHF belongs to. Plasma adrenomedullin in NCPH measured 236 ± 61.4 pg/mL versus 84.1 ± 31.5 in healthy subjects (p < 0.0001), higher than in compensated Child-Pugh A cirrhosis (108.4 ± 28.3), and correlated strongly with nitric oxide (r = 0.827, p < 0.0001) — Tahan V, Avsar E, Karaca C et al. World J Gastroenterol 9(10):2325-2327, 2003. PMID: 14562402. The glomerular finding and the portal hypertension finding describe the same compensatory-but-insufficient biology in two organs, and the two literatures do not cite each other. Serum adrenomedullin has not been measured in ARPKD-CHF; it is a standard assay.

The precedent in cystic disease. Jafree DJ, Perera C, Ball M, Tolomeo D, Pomeranz G, Wilson L, Davis B, Mason WJ, Funk EM, Kolatsi-Joannou M, Polschi R, Malik S, Stewart BJ, Price KL, Mitchell H, Motallebzadeh R, Muto Y, Lees R, Needham S, Moulding D, Chandler JC, Nandanwar S, Walsh CL, Winyard PJD, Scambler PJ, Hägerling R, Clatworthy MR, Humphreys BD, Lythgoe MF, Walker-Samuel S, Woolf AS, Long DA. "Microvascular aberrations found in human polycystic kidneys are an early feature in a Pkd1 mutant mouse model." Dis Model Mech 18(4):dmm052024, 1 April 2025. DOI: 10.1242/dmm.052024. PMID: 40114603. Using single-cell transcriptomics, three-dimensional imaging with geometric, topological and fractal analysis, and multimodal MRI with arterial spin labelling, the group identified a molecularly distinct blood microvascular subpopulation characterised by impaired angiogenic signalling and metabolic dysfunction, differing from the endothelial injury profiles seen in non-cystic kidney disease. In Pkd1RC/RC mice, vascular disorganisation was significantly greater than wild-type at 3 months, before excretory failure.

⚠️ Two limits on how far this precedent carries. First, this is ADPKD (Pkd1), not ARPKD — whether the same vascular biology applies to PKHD1-deficient kidneys is untested. Second, the human tissue came from ADPKD kidneys with advanced cystic pathology and excretory failure; it is the mouse data that establish the abnormality as early. The claim "early in mice, present in advanced human disease" is what the paper supports.

⚠️ What the vascular abnormality in ARPKD-CHF actually is — and it is the opposite of the glomerular pattern. The intrahepatic bile ducts are surrounded by the peribiliary vascular plexus. In ductal plate malformation this plexus is hypertrophied, not lost: explant study of a liver with DPM and presinusoidal portal hypertension showed paucity of portal vein branches together with hypertrophy of the peribiliary vascular plexus (de Lédinghen V, Le Bail B, Trillaud H, Bernard PH, Saric J, Balabaud C, Bioulac-Sage P. J Gastroenterol Hepatol 13:720-724, 1998). A veterinary study of ductal plate malformation describes a compensatory hepatic arterial response producing arterial twigs, vascular coiling and thickened mural smooth muscle, yielding prolific stout arterioles that interdigitate with juvenile ductal elements, with presinusoidal portal hypertension initiating adaptive perfusion through the arterial peribiliary plexus — Pillai S, Center SA, McDonough SP, Demarco J, Pintar J, Henderson AK, Cooper J, Bolton T, Sharpe K, Hill S, Benedict AG, Haviland R. "Ductal Plate Malformation in the Liver of Boxer Dogs: Clinical and Histological Features." Vet Pathol 53(3):602-613, May 2016. PMID: 26797094. Cornell University College of Veterinary Medicine; 30 Boxer dogs; histologic evidence of portal venous hypoperfusion accompanied increased biliary profiles in every case.

⚠️ The evidence base for this correction is thin, and we would rather say so than overstate it. It rests on one human explanted liver (de Lédinghen 1998) and thirty dogs (Pillai 2016). Ductal plate malformation is a conserved developmental lesion and the two findings agree, but neither is a human ARPKD-CHF series, and we have found no quantitative study of peribiliary vascular architecture in PKHD1-confirmed human liver. What can be said is that the available evidence points to portal venous paucity with arterial hypertrophy rather than to endothelial dropout — not that the question is settled. A quantitative assessment of the peribiliary vascular plexus in ARPKD-CHF liver tissue would be a genuinely novel and straightforward study, and Strazzabosco holds the Pkhd1del4/del4 tissue to do it in.

On the available evidence the ARPKD-CHF vascular phenotype appears to be portal venous paucity with compensatory hepatic arterial hypertrophy rather than the endothelial dropout seen in WT1 glomerular disease — meaning the Chandler template probably does not transfer directly, though this has not been tested in human ARPKD-CHF tissue.

What remains genuinely open, and worth testing: whether VEGF-A, NRP1 and adrenomedullin signalling is altered in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. The expected phenotype would be arterial remodelling and angiogenesis rather than endothelial loss, but the signalling has not been measured in this cell type, and Chandler's single-cell ligand-receptor methodology is directly applicable to it.

Therapeutic consequence of the corrected picture: because the arterial supply to ARPKD-CHF bile ducts is enlarged while the portal supply is reduced, hepatic arterial delivery of any cholangiocyte-targeted agent is anatomically favoured — the disease itself widens the route to the target cells. Routing: Chandler (VEGF-A/NRP1/adrenomedullin in biliary cells), Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 tissue), Gracia-Sancho (LSEC and portal hypertension).

Principle 3 — Amplify Failing Compensation, Don't Only Block Pathogenesis

Nearly every candidate in our Therapeutic Candidates table is an inhibitor or antagonist: Src inhibitors, STAT3 inhibitors, CXCR3 antagonists, CDK1 inhibitors, αvβ6 blockade, tolvaptan. A different therapeutic logic appears twice in our own data and we have not named it.

  • Adrenomedullin (Chandler 2024): upregulated in WT1 disease as a protective response, but insufficient against the wider milieu of dampened vascular signalling. Exogenous supplementation rescued endothelial looping in vitro.
  • GDF15 (framework, Sep 2026): rises as a protective anti-fibrotic brake reprogramming hepatic macrophages toward a pro-resolving phenotype — but chronic elevation becomes maladaptive and insufficient.

What Malik/Chandler/Long actually did in Sci Transl Med 2026 was not block a pathogenic signal — they amplified a failing protective one (ANGPT1 restoration). This is a distinct and underexplored strategy class for ARPKD-CHF: ANGPT1 supplementation, adrenomedullin augmentation, GDF15 agonism, FPC/FPCct/ICD15 restoration. All four are "restore what is failing" rather than "block what is driving."

Note: Chandler's FPC fragment concept, Ziegler's FPCct domain delivery, and Walker/Qian's ICD15 all belong to this class. So does Cebotaru's AAV1-CFTR transcomplementation. The framework already contains a restoration strategy — it has simply never been grouped or named.

⭐⭐ THE ASSEMBLY — Hepatic Artery-Delivered, αvβ6-Targeted Nanocomplexes

This is the most actionable synthesis in the framework. Every component is published. The assembly does not exist anywhere.

ComponentWho holds itPublished evidenceStatus
1. Targeted nanocomplex platformStephen L. Hart
UCL GOS ICH, Genetics & Genomic Medicine		αvβ3-integrin-targeted lipid nanocomplexes; ligand-directed cell-surface binding. Malik/Chandler/Long Sci Transl Med 18:862 (2026). Malik PhD thesis, UCL 2023.✅ Published, working
2. Image-guided arterial deliveryTammy L. Kalber
UCL Centre for Advanced Biomedical Imaging		Ultrasound-guided renal artery injection achieves organ localization "not replicated by systemic administration"; cargo localized in glomeruli up to 7 days.✅ Published, working
3. Biliary targeting addressMario Strazzabosco
Yale		Locatelli L, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 65:822-838 (2016) — αvβ6 integrin reaches 70% of biliary structures at 12 months in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice, r=0.94 correlation with portal fibrosis.✅ Published — strongest ARPKD-specific surface marker data in the literature. And the targeting chemistry already exists: A20FMDV2, a 20-mer peptide from the foot-and-mouth disease virus VP1 coat protein, binds αvβ6 with nanomolar selectivity (Kd ~50 nM), has been used for PET imaging of αvβ6-positive tumours in humans with demonstrated preclinical safety, and conjugates to liposomes via DSPE-PEG2000-maleimide without altering particle size, polydispersity or zeta potential (PMC5488751). A20FMDV2-modified liposomes have been used to target αvβ6 on biliary epithelial cells in vivo in a rat model of intrahepatic cholestasis of pregnancy, improving liver function indicators over free drug (Feng X et al. Mater Today Bio 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.mtbio.2025.102031. PMC12268842. ✅ DOI confirmed Sep 22, 2026). Cyclized and PEGylated variants address the peptide's limited serum stability (PMC8138772, PMC8849274). What has not been done: A20FMDV2-targeted carriers bearing nucleic acid cargo, delivered via hepatic artery, in a PKHD1 model.
4. Therapeutic cargo (restoration)Jennifer Chandler (FPC fragment)
Wolfgang Ziegler (FPCct)
Walker/Qian (ICD15)		Minimal functional FPC domain sufficient to suppress cystogenic signaling; FPCct ~2.25× more potent than PP2 Src inhibition.✅ Concept proven; payload definition in progress
5. Therapeutic cargo (correction)Matteus Krappitz
CU Anschutz		ABE/CBE editing chemistry for PKHD1 variants. Gao XD, Liu DR et al. Nat Biomed Eng 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01651-5 confirms LNP-delivered ABE rescues pediatric liver disease: 27% bulk liver correction via LNP (confirmed from abstract) — with progressive efficiency increase over time (Jackson Laboratory: "efficiency of base editors increased over the duration of the study"). Specific percentage figures for selective advantage not confirmed from accessible public sources and not cited here.✅ Chemistry validated; delivery vehicle unresolved for cholangiopathy patients

The anatomical fact that makes this work: The intrahepatic bile ducts are the only structure in the liver supplied almost exclusively by the hepatic artery. Hepatocytes receive most of their blood supply from the portal vein; the peribiliary vascular plexus is hepatic-arterial. This is precisely why hepatic artery thrombosis after liver transplantation causes ischemic cholangiopathy — lose the artery, lose the bile ducts. Hepatic artery delivery is therefore the biliary anatomical equivalent of Kalber's renal artery approach, and it preferentially reaches bile ducts over hepatocytes.

Access is already routine: Hepatic artery catheterization is standard interventional radiology — it is how TACE (transarterial chemoembolization) is delivered for liver tumors. This is not experimental access requiring new technique development.

⭐ THE αV INTEGRIN HAS THREE INDEPENDENT ROLES IN ARPKD-CHF (Sep 22, 2026): The framework previously documented αvβ6 in two contexts. A third context is now confirmed from a separate group:

  1. Delivery address (A20FMDV2/Marshall): αvβ6 is expressed at high levels on FPC-deficient biliary epithelial cells — the highest expression correlating with portal fibrosis severity (r=0.94 at 12 months). A20FMDV2 peptide locks onto αvβ6 for cholangiocyte-specific nanocomplex delivery. Source: Locatelli L, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 65:822-838, 2016. + Feng X et al. Mater Today Bio 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.mtbio.2025.102031. PMC12268842. ✅ Both DOIs confirmed.
  2. Therapeutic target on cholangiocytes (bexotegrast): αvβ6 activates latent TGFβ on cholangiocyte surfaces → SMAD3 → LOXL2 → collagen crosslinking → fibrosis. Bexotegrast blocks this. Source: INTEGRIS-PSC Phase 2 — Hirschfield GM et al. J Hepatol 84:86-98, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.016. ✅ DOI confirmed.
  3. Mechanotransduction sensor on LSECs (pressure-driven fibrosis): Integrin αV (specific subunit unknown from abstract — requires full paper) on LSECs is activated by elevated hydrostatic pressure from portal hypertension → YAP → CTGF → perisinusoidal fibrosis. Inhibiting αV improved outcomes in mouse congestion model. Confirmed in human FALD patients. Source: Kato S, Hikita H et al. Gastroenterology 170(5):1000-1016, 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2025.11.014. ✅ DOI confirmed. ⚠️ Not tested in ARPKD-CHF LSECs. Specific αV integrin complex in LSECs not established from abstract — full paper required.

Bexotegrast (which specifically blocks αvβ6) addresses context #2 on cholangiocytes. It may or may not address context #3 on LSECs — depends on which αV complex is involved there. A20FMDV2 uses context #1 for delivery. All three roles are in the same integrin family in the same ARPKD-CHF liver. This convergence is not coincidental — αV integrins are mechanosensors and fibrosis mediators across multiple hepatic cell types.

The reframe that unlocks it: αvβ6 integrin has been studied exclusively as a drug target to be blocked (bexotegrast, INTEGRIS-PSC Phase 2). Read as a delivery address instead, it is a high-density, disease-specific, cell-surface receptor on precisely the cells we need to reach — with the strongest ARPKD-specific expression data available. Bexotegrast blocks αvβ6. The same molecule can serve as a docking site.

⭐ THE BEXOTEGRAST/αvβ6 CONFLICT IS RESOLVED — confirmed Sep 22, 2026: This framework previously flagged a potential conflict: αvβ6 is simultaneously a therapeutic target (bexotegrast blocks it to suppress TGFβ-driven fibrosis) and a delivery address (A20FMDV2 uses it as a docking site). The Jiao/Zhu paper (Commun Biol 9:1224, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6. PMID: 42265280) resolves this: the SPP1-driven HSC activation pathway in CHF uses integrin α8β1 as its primary receptor on HSCs — NOT αvβ6. Bexotegrast blocks αvβ6 → TGFβ activation (a different fibrotic pathway). A20FMDV2 uses αvβ6 as a delivery docking site. SPP1-targeting uses α8β1 on a different cell type. These three strategies use different molecular addresses and can be pursued simultaneously without receptor-level conflict. The sequencing concern (does bexotegrast reduce αvβ6 expression, reducing delivery efficiency?) is also mitigated: bexotegrast is a functional inhibitor that blocks αvβ6 ligand binding — it does not reduce αvβ6 expression. αvβ6 remains expressed on the cholangiocyte surface and available as a docking site while bexotegrast occupies its TGFβ-activating function.

⭐ ORGAN DIVERGENCE PRINCIPLE IS THE REASON THE TWO-PART ARCHITECTURE EXISTS — Sep 22, 2026: This connection was hiding in plain sight. Principle 1 (Organ Divergence) states that systemic agents carry opposite-effect risk in kidney vs liver (AMPK: kidney may need activation, liver may need inhibition; STAT3: protective in kidney collecting duct, pathogenic in liver cholangiocytes). This makes organ-targeted delivery a therapeutic safety requirement, not just a practical preference. The Two-Part Delivery Architecture (Part 1: Janowski/Kalber hepatic arterial fractionation + Part 2: Marshall/A20FMDV2 αvβ6 targeting) IS the resolution to the Organ Divergence problem — it makes organ-specific delivery physically possible. These are not two separate insights. Principle 1 is the problem; the Two-Part Architecture is the solution. They should be read as one argument.

⚠️ STX-100 failure — critical clarification (Sep 19, 2026 · 📌): STX-100 (BG00011, Stromedix → Biogen) is a humanized anti-αvβ6 blocking monoclonal antibody. In a randomized, double-blinded, placebo-controlled nonhuman primate renal transplantation study, STX-100 promoted acute rejection: 3/5 STX-100 animals rejected vs 0/5 placebo (p=0.049). Mechanism: STX-100 permanently blocked αvβ6 → removed αvβ6-mediated local TGF-β activation in the allograft → TGF-β also mediates immune tolerance of the foreign graft under calcineurin inhibitor immunosuppression → T-cell mediated Banff 1A rejection. (Lo DJ, Farris AB, Song M, Leopardi F, Anderson DJ, Strobert EA, Ramakrishnan S, Turgeon NA, Mehta AK, Turnbull B, Maroni B, Violette SM, Kirk AD. Am J Transplant 13(12):3085-3093, 2013. PMID: 24119188. ⚠️ DOI not confirmed — use PMID link.) Why this failure is transplant-specific and does NOT apply to ARPKD-CHF: (1) No transplant — the patient has her own liver; there is no foreign graft requiring immune tolerance. (2) No calcineurin inhibitors — the immunosuppressive role of TGF-β is irrelevant without them. (3) TGF-β immune tolerance is a transplant biology concern, not a biliary fibrosis concern. Critical distinction: STX-100 blocks αvβ6 → receptor cannot function → TGF-β signalling disrupted. A20FMDV2 as a delivery vehicle uses αvβ6 as an address → peptide binds briefly, cargo enters cell, peptide dissociates → receptor continues to function normally → no TGF-β disruption. These are mechanistically opposite interventions that share only the target molecule.

⭐ TWO-PART DELIVERY ARCHITECTURE — confirmed Sep 20, 2026: The complete delivery system for ARPKD-CHF biliary targets requires two independent components that must be combined. Neither alone is sufficient.

PartWhat it solvesWho holds itStatus
Part 1 — RouteGets the payload safely into the liver via fractionated hepatic arterial delivery — avoiding systemic IV toxicity in a patient population with pre-existing LSEC dysfunctionMiroslaw Janowski (UMD Baltimore) + Tammy Kalber (UCL)✅ Published (Janowski, Mol Ther Nucleic Acids Dec 2025). NIH application submitted. Janowski in active correspondence Sep 20, 2026.
Part 2 — TargetingDirects the payload specifically to αvβ6-expressing cholangiocytes once inside the liver — preventing off-target hepatocyte or LSEC uptakeJohn Marshall (QMUL/Barts) — A20FMDV2 peptide system✅ A20FMDV2-modified liposomes target αvβ6 on biliary epithelial cells in vivo (Feng X et al. Mater Today Bio 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.mtbio.2025.102031. PMC12268842 — A20FMDV2-modified liposome (t-QL) targeted integrin αvβ6 on biliary epithelial cells in vivo in cholestatic rat model; improved liver function indicators over free drug. ✅ DOI confirmed Sep 22, 2026). Marshall in vitro testing underway (personal communication Sep 2026).

⭐ POLYMER CARRIER CORRECTION — Janowski personal communication Sep 20, 2026: Fractionated hepatic arterial delivery is not tied to ionizable lipid nanoparticles. Janowski argues explicitly that the intra-arterial route permits testing alternative packaging systems. For a patient population with pre-existing LSEC dysfunction (ARPKD-CHF children have abnormal LSECs at baseline as part of ductal plate malformation), ionizable lipids are the specific liability — and polymer-based carriers are the alternative. Fractionation decouples from the ionizable lipid liability. In the ARPKD-CHF population, this flexibility is the principal advantage of the hepatic arterial route, not a footnote. Previous versions of this framework did not draw this distinction. Janowski's NIH application compares LNPs with polymer-based carriers specifically for genome-editing payloads — the results will directly inform the ARPKD-CHF delivery choice.

⭐ ANATOMICAL CONFIRMATION — Janowski personal communication Sep 20, 2026: "The bile ducts are supplied by the hepatic artery through the peribiliary plexus, which is hypertrophied in ductal plate malformation. The arterial route is therefore not merely safer in this setting — it is the anatomically correct one for a biliary target." This confirms the framework's arterial-route argument from an interventional radiologist who publishes on exactly this delivery problem.

Assembled proposal: αvβ6-targeted nanocomplexes (polymer or lipid — pending Janowski NIH data), delivered via fractionated image-guided hepatic artery injection, carrying either FPC-restoring mRNA (Track B, transient, lower regulatory bar) or base-editor components (Track A, permanent correction).

These tracks are not competing. Track B could bridge to Track A — a model already used in other pediatric genetic diseases where protein/enzyme therapy sustains the patient while a curative gene therapy clears clinical development.

The Tie2 Observation — A Connection Inside One Lab

Angiopoietin-1 signals through Tie2 (TEK). That is the therapy in Malik/Chandler/Long Sci Transl Med (Aug 2026).

Jafree/Long Cell Reports (June 2026, DOI: 10.1016/j.celrep.2026.117560) found that most kidney lymphatics arise from Tie2+ endothelial progenitors.

Two papers, one lab, one year, one receptor — and the connection is not drawn in either. ANGPT/Tie2 is a well-established lymphangiogenesis pathway. The ANGPT1 therapy may have a second mechanism the group has not measured: action on lymphatics, not only blood vessels.

Why this matters for ARPKD-CHF: Portal hypertension is fundamentally a lymphatic overload problem — the liver dramatically increases lymph production under portal hypertension, and peribiliary lymphatics drain the tissue surrounding bile ducts. If ANGPT1/Tie2 modulates lymphatic assembly and function, the same therapy could act on the ARPKD liver through an entirely different route than it acts on the kidney. This is a question for Long and for Jafree independently.

Open Questions Generated by This Synthesis

  • Is nephron number reduced in ARPKD? Jafree showed disrupting Osr1+ mesodermal lymphatics reduces glomerular number. If nephron endowment is reduced in ARPKD, an apparently normal GFR is being produced by fewer nephrons working harder — and renal reserve is smaller than the number suggests.
  • Is NRP1 the shared node between Chandler's glomerular findings and our open SEMA4D→LSEC question to Gracia-Sancho? Semaphorins signal through neuropilins. Christiana Ruhrberg — the leading NRP1 authority — is now confirmed collaborating with the Long group.
  • Does the Hart nanocomplex platform tolerate ligand substitution without loss of transfection efficiency? This is the single technical question on which the entire αvβ6 assembly depends.
  • Are peribiliary lymphatics abnormal in ARPKD-CHF, and does that contribute to portal hypertension independently of fibrotic obstruction?
  • ✅ CLOSED Sep 20, 2026 — PTH1R in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes: Dr. Navin Gupta (Mayo Clinic, corresponding author of the PTH1R/cAMP paper) confirmed directly: PTH1R is absent in cholangiocytes. The biliary PTH1R question is closed. No cholangiocyte-directed PTH1R work is warranted. (Gupta N, personal communication, Sep 20, 2026.)
  • ✅ CONFIRMED Sep 20, 2026 — No group has applied periportal assembloid models to ARPKD patient cells: Prof. Meritxell Huch (Max Planck Institute MPI-CBG Dresden, developer of both the Dowbaj et al. Nature 2025 mouse assembloid (DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09183-9) and Yuan et al. Nature 2026 human assembloid (DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09884-1)) confirmed she is not aware of any group applying these models to PKHD1-deficient cells. The gap identified in this framework is independently confirmed by the scientist who developed the most relevant platform. (Huch M, personal communication, Sep 20, 2026.) New contact identified: Anna Dowbaj (first author, mouse paper) is now Assistant Professor at TU Munich — direct contact for ARPKD assembloid work. (dowbaj@tum.de)

📌 NEW August 26, 2026 — Cross-Connections and Open Questions

TGR5–FXR Asymmetry: Two Bile Acid Receptors, Two Different Problems

Both bile acid receptors in cholangiocytes fail when FPC is absent. (Masyuk TV, LaRusso NF et al. Hepatology 66(4):1197-1218, 2017. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29284. PMID: 28543567. PMC5605412 — ⚠️ previously cited as ACS 2025 PMC12261218, which cannot be verified; replaced with the confirmed Hepatology 2017 paper) TGR5 is displaced from cilia → plasma membrane → couples with Gαs (NOT Gαi) → pro-cystogenic cAMP increase. FXR requires ciliary bile acid sensing → impaired → fibrogenic cascade. Key asymmetry: pharmacological FXR agonists (linafexor) bypass the ciliary sensing requirement. TGR5 restoration requires ciliary architecture restoration.

⭐ FRAMEWORK CORRECTION (September 1, 2026) + CITATION CORRECTION (September 20, 2026): TGR5 ANTAGONISM is the correct therapeutic approach in ARPKD-CHF — NOT agonism. ⚠️ COMPOUND NAME CORRECTED Sep 20, 2026: SBI-115 (not "SBI-364" as previously stated — SBI-364 does not exist in any chemical database; the Masyuk/LaRusso TGR5 antagonist is SBI-115, CAS 882366-16-7, confirmed from Hepatology 2017 PMC5605412). Verified numbers from Masyuk TV, LaRusso NF et al. Hepatology 66(4):1197-1218, 2017. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29284. PMID: 28543567. PMC5605412: TGR5−/−;Pkhd1del2/del2 mice — 45% hepatic cyst reduction vs Pkhd1del2/del2 alone. SBI-115 in vitro — ~30% reduction in cAMP, proliferation, cyst growth. SBI-115 + pasireotide in vitro — ~50% combined reduction. ⚠️ RETRACTED ATTRIBUTION: The previously cited "PMC12261218 (LaRusso/Masyuk, ACS 2025)" as the source for "SBI-364" with "41.7% hepatic and 30.4% renal" figures cannot be verified — PMC12261218 is not indexed in any database and "SBI-364" does not exist. These unverified figures have been replaced with the confirmed Hepatology 2017 values above. If an updated 2025 ACS paper exists from this group, the numbers remain unconfirmed until directly read. INT-767 (dual FXR/TGR5 AGONIST) — the TGR5 agonist component is potentially harmful in ARPKD-CHF where TGR5 is plasma membrane-localized and pro-cystogenic via Gαs. Recommended approach: FXR AGONIST (linafexor) + TGR5 ANTAGONIST (SBI-115) + SOMATOSTATIN (SSTR2). Editorial note: The conclusion that INT-767 may be harmful in ARPKD-CHF is our interpretation of the published LaRusso/Masyuk data. Dr. LaRusso and Dr. Masyuk have not made this statement directly — we welcome any correction from their group.

CDK1 Is the Convergence Output of BOTH YAP Overactivation Pathways

Two independent YAP overactivation routes exist in ARPKD-CHF: (1) FPC loss → FXR impaired → FXR-YAP brake absent → YAP overactivated (Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z); (2) FPC loss → Scribble dysregulation → YAP overactivated (Fabris L, Strazzabosco M et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R). YAP drives CDK1 expression. CDK1 is now confirmed as the top biliary proliferative driver (iScience June 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2026.113369). CDK1 inhibition therefore suppresses biliary expansion regardless of which YAP pathway dominates — making CDK1 more strategically positioned than framed. Research question for Strazzabosco: Is CDK1 upregulated in your PKHD1-/- mouse model downstream of Scribble/YAP?

⭐ YAP Is Now Confirmed From Three Independent Directions in ARPKD-CHF (Sep 22, 2026)

Three independent research groups, using three different upstream triggers and two different cell types, all converge on YAP as a central fibrosis driver in ARPKD-CHF or its closest comparators. No single paper makes this connection — it is a cross-framework synthesis:

  1. Cholangiocytes — FXR→YAP (identity-maintaining when intact): FPC loss impairs ciliary bile acid sensing → FXR signaling impaired → FXR-YAP protective brake absent → YAP overactivated in cholangiocytes → loss of biliary identity, proliferation, fibrotic secretome. Source: Sánchez-Sánchez P et al., Djouder N (corresponding). Nat Metab 8(5):1189-1211, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z. ✅ DOI confirmed.
  2. LSECs — integrin αV→YAP (fibrosis-driving when pressure is elevated): Portal hypertension → elevated sinusoidal hydrostatic pressure → integrin αV mechanotransduction in LSECs → YAP activation → CTGF → perisinusoidal fibrosis → more portal hypertension. Confirmed in mouse hepatic congestion model and human FALD patients (FALD = closest published human comparator to ARPKD-CHF non-cirrhotic portal hypertension). Source: Kato S, Hikita H et al. Gastroenterology 170(5):1000-1016, 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2025.11.014. ✅ DOI confirmed. ⚠️ NOT tested in ARPKD — inference from FALD comparison.
  3. Cholangiocytes — Scribble→YAP (proliferation-driving when polarity is lost): FPC-deficient cholangiocytes lose apicobasal polarity → Scribble/LATS axis dysregulated → YAP overactivated → CDK1 expression → biliary hyperproliferation. Source: Fabris L, Strazzabosco M et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R. ✅ DOI confirmed.

The strategic implication: YAP is not an incidental pathway in ARPKD-CHF. It is the convergence point of the disease's two most important fibrogenic mechanisms — cholangiocyte identity failure and LSEC pressure-driven activation. Therapeutic strategies that modulate YAP (CDK1 inhibitors targeting YAP's output; FXR agonists restoring its brake; integrin αV inhibition reducing its pressure-driven activation in LSECs) each address different parts of the same central hub. No single intervention covers all three inputs. ⚠️ The LSEC pathway (#2) is not confirmed in ARPKD specifically — requires direct testing.

⭐ Cebotaru + Kato/Hikita Together Argue for a Combination Approach (Sep 22, 2026)

Two papers published within days of each other — from completely independent groups — identify two separate fibrogenic mechanisms in ARPKD-CHF that require two separate interventions:

  • Mechanism 1 — Cell-autonomous cholangiocyte failure (Cebotaru/Sharma, Sep 19, 2026): FPC loss → CFTR, PC1, PC2 simultaneously reduced in cholangiocytes → Ca²⁺ and cAMP dysregulated → cyst growth and proliferation. CFTR correctors (VX-809, VX-445) partially restore all three proteins and reduce cysts in primary Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 cholangiocytes. Source: Sharma A, Cebotaru L et al. Cell Calcium 103195, Sep 19, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ceca.2026.103195. ✅ DOI confirmed. ⚠️ Association (not proven causation) between FPC loss and PC1/PC2 reduction — mechanism requires full paper.
  • Mechanism 2 — Pressure-driven LSEC amplification (Kato/Hikita, May 2026): Portal hypertension → hydrostatic pressure → integrin αV → YAP → CTGF in LSECs → perisinusoidal fibrosis → more portal hypertension. CFTR correctors do not address LSECs. Portal pressure reduction, LSEC protection, or integrin αV inhibition is needed for this pathway. Source: Kato S, Hikita H et al. Gastroenterology 170(5):1000-1016, 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2025.11.014. ✅ DOI confirmed. ⚠️ Not tested in ARPKD — inference from FALD comparison.

Mechanism 1 is cholangiocyte-intrinsic and treatable with pharmacological correctors. Mechanism 2 is pressure-driven and operates in a different cell type (LSECs). Neither drug addresses the other's mechanism. The framework's Track 1 strategy — cGMP elevation (riociguat/BI 685509) reducing portal pressure, alongside CFTR correctors restoring biliary cell function — now has a published mechanistic argument for why both are needed simultaneously rather than sequentially.

Spermidine and TDI01 Both Preserve Hepatocyte Zonation via Wnt2/Wnt9b

Chain: Spermidine → LSEC restoration / TDI01 → LSEC fenestrations restored → both maintain LSEC Wnt2 and Wnt9b production (Zhang K et al. Cell 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.02.001) (confirmed by single-cell spatial transcriptomics, PMC9588996 — only Wnt2 and Wnt9b from pericentrally-located ECs control hepatocyte zonation; not WNT2A which is a distinct gene) → hepatocyte zonation maintained. Neither therapeutic is framed this way. Both address hepatocyte zonation loss as an undocumented third mechanism of benefit.

The Complete Bexotegrast → PICP Monitoring Chain

αvβ6 integrin (upregulated on FPC-deficient BECs) → activates latent TGFβ → SMAD3 → LOXL2 → collagen crosslinking → PICP neoepitope released into blood → PICP biosensor measurement (Mederacke I et al. Hepatology 2021. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32098 — αvβ6 in biliary fibrosis). Bexotegrast → reduced αvβ6 → reduced TGFβ → reduced LOXL2 → reduced PICP. The PICP biosensor we recommended to Hartung is the logical monitoring tool for a bexotegrast trial in ARPKD-CHF. Full mechanistic chain from intervention to biomarker now documented.

The ERCP Experiment That Could Answer Six Questions at Once

Three published methodologies never combined for ARPKD: ERCP retrograde delivery (Guimont 2021. DOI: 10.1016/j.omtm.2021.03.004) + bile-derived cholangiocyte organoids (Soroka CJ et al. Hepatology 2019. DOI: 10.1002/hep.30542PSC bile via ERCP) + Sampaziotis cholangiocyte organoid protocol (emailed August 24). The missing experiment: ARPKD patient bile collected during a clinically indicated ERCP → patient-specific PKHD1-mutant cholangiocyte organoids. This platform could simultaneously: (1) measure FXR activity; (2) assess TGR5 localization; (3) test CDK1 inhibitor; (4) test ECM restoration; (5) test linafexor; (6) validate urine RNA for Met3642Ile. No ARPKD patient-derived cholangiocyte organoids from ERCP bile currently exist.

⭐ Bexotegrast Phase 2 INTEGRIS-PSC Published — Cholangitis Reduction (September 1, 2026)

Hirschfield GM et al. (INTEGRIS-PSC). J Hepatol 84:86-98, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.016. PMID: 41016442. Open access. Phase 2 double-blind dose-ranging study, 117 PSC patients with suspected liver fibrosis, up to 40 weeks (doses: 40/80/160/320 mg vs placebo). Results: ELF + PRO-C3 significantly reduced (160 mg). Hepatocyte function + bile flow improved by MRI (320 mg). Cholangitis events LESS FREQUENT on bexotegrast vs placebo — a new anti-cholangitis benefit directly relevant to ARPKD-CHF where cholangitis drives fibrosis acceleration. Key finding from full Phase II (September 2025): At Week 24, limited change from Week 12 except in MRI parameters, which continued to improve — confirming MRI is the most sensitive endpoint for this drug class and validating our argument for MRE as the primary ARPKD liver trial endpoint. PRO-C3 confirmed as validated biliary fibrosis biomarker for αvβ6 inhibitor trials. Bexotegrast 160 mg = reference dose for ARPKD-CHF trial design.

⭐ LZTFL1 — Progressive Secondary Ciliary Dysfunction as ARPKD-CHF Advances

LZTFL1 (BBSome regulator, controls ciliary trafficking of GPCRs + Smoothened) is markedly downregulated in fibrotic liver tissue. Heo J, Chae DH, Park HS, Yu KR (corresponding). "Disease-derived liver organoids as a preclinical screening platform identify Sargassum japonica as an anti-fibrotic candidate." Sci Rep 16:13783, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-026-43852-7 — ⚠️ note: LZTFL1 comes from THIS paper (downregulated genes in CCl₄-fibrotic liver), NOT the PCK rat transcriptomics paper. Re-corrected Sep 16, 2026 after an over-broad bulk fix.. BBSome mediates ciliary localization of TGR5 and SSTR2 — both GPCRs whose ciliary displacement drives ARPKD-CHF progression. Seo S et al. PLoS Genet 7:e1002358, 2011. DOI: 10.1371/journal.pgen.1002358. Proposed self-amplifying loop (hypothesis — ARPKD-specific testing not yet done): FPC absent → primary ciliary dysfunction → biliary fibrosis → LZTFL1 downregulated → BBSome impaired → TGR5 + SSTR2 further displaced from cilia → somatostatin becomes less effective → more cystogenesis → more fibrosis. Any treatment that reduces fibrosis (CDK1 inhibitor, TXA, FXR agonist) may also preserve LZTFL1 as a downstream benefit — maintaining somatostatin responsiveness.

⭐ HNF4G — Fourth Mechanism for Hepatocellular Dysfunction

HNF4G drives cholangiocyte → hepatocyte transdifferentiation via global chromatin reorganization. In ARPKD-CHF, FPC-deficient cholangiocytes may upregulate HNF4G → biphenotypic cells — neither proper cholangiocytes nor proper hepatocytes. Four mechanisms for hepatocellular dysfunction now documented: (1) PHT → Wnt2/Wnt9b loss → zonation; (2) DPM → periportal hepatocyte abnormality; (3) FXR impairment → lost hepatocyte identity signal; (4) HNF4G-driven cholangiocyte transdifferentiation. Galanakis V, Gribben C, Vallier L et al. — J Hepatol 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.08.014. Cambridge Stem Cell Institute / University of Cambridge.

⭐ VITAL + ImPLANT — Two ARPA-H Programs Racing Toward Bioprinted Liver

VITAL (Vascularized Immunocompetent Tissue as an Alternative Liver — UT Southwestern, up to $25M, PI Muhammad Rizwan): Patient-specific cells → iPSC → liver cells. SOLVED THE BILE DUCT PROBLEM — novel method growing blood vessels AND bile ducts simultaneously. No immunosuppression. ~5 years to human testing (~2031). Co-investigator: Hao Zhu MD (CDK1 liver biology). ImPLANT (Immunoshielded Printed Liver Assist NeoOrgan for Transplant — Harvard Wyss Institute + CU Boulder + MIT + Columbia, up to $25M, PI Chris Chen): Off-the-shelf universal compatibility. Suspension printing technique (organoids in hydrogel bath → channels printed → blood vessels form). 5-10 year clinical trial timeline. Both programs converge on early 2030s — meaningful for current ARPKD-CHF pediatric patients. Both part of ARPA-H PRINT program ($176.8M total), announced January 12, 2026. ARPA-H PRINT announcement, January 12, 2026.

SPTAN1 in Cholangiocytes — Connecting Morizane and Waddell/Boulter

SPTAN1 (α-II spectrin) is reduced in FPC-deficient kidney CNT/collecting duct cells → loosened GEF/RAC1 lattice → RAC1 hyperactivation (Kuraoka S, Morizane R et al. Adv Sci 2026. DOI: 10.1002/advs.202524001). α-II spectrin also anchors integrin-basement membrane attachments (Bhatt DL et al. J Cell Biol 200(4):473-489, 2013. DOI: 10.1083/jcb.201207150 — spectrin-integrin-ECM anchoring). If SPTAN1 is reduced in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes, it could simultaneously drive RAC1 activation AND disrupt integrin anchoring to the basement membrane — contributing to Waddell/Boulter's ECM loss (Waddell SH, Brennan E, Boulter L. Dis Model Mech 19(8):dmm052835, 2026. DOI: 10.1242/dmm.052835). One molecule may connect both mechanisms. Research question for Morizane and Waddell. (Hypothesis — not yet tested in cholangiocytes)

STAT3 — 6th independent confirmation (HCC fibrosis context) 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Ma C, Pan K. Front Immunol 17:1930718, 2026. IL-6/STAT3 driving immunosuppression in HSC-activated liver in HCC — sixth independent disease context confirming STAT3 as the central fibrosis mediator. Also confirms CXCL12/CXCR4 spatial T-cell exclusion in fibrotic liver (second confirmation after periportal assembloid paper). Reviewed by Massimiliano Cadamuro — in our network via Strazzabosco.

Gut dysbiosis → mitochondrial proteostasis → organ fibrosis — third independent confirmation 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Fujimoto H, Cann IC, Gabazza E et al. Nat Commun 2026. Corisin (gut bacterial peptide) → mitochondria → proteostasis disruption → pulmonary fibrosis. Three independent disease systems now confirm gut bacteria → mitochondrial disruption → distant organ fibrosis: ImP → p38 (PSC biliary — ARPKD-CHF is extrapolation from PSC data; Molinaro et al. Nature Metabolism Sep 4, 2026), EFHD1 → ER-mitochondria → liver inflammation (⚠️ hepatocyte only — absent in cholangiocytes per transcriptional data; Chaudhuri personal communication Sep 14, 2026, permission granted), corisin → mitochondrial proteostasis → pulmonary fibrosis. ARPKD-CHF gut dysbiosis profile (recurrent cholangitis + antibiotics) may generate multiple bacterial fibrosis drivers simultaneously.

FDA Plausible Mechanism Framework — regulatory pathway now exists for PKHD1 ABE 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

FDA February 23, 2026. Formal approval pathway for individualized genome editing in ultra-rare pediatric genetic disease. No large trial required — plausible mechanism + evidence of pathway modification accepted. Baby KJ (CPS1 deficiency, not ARPKD — urea cycle disorder, MGH/UPenn 2025) is the template case. Primary source: Makary MA, Prasad V. NEJM Nov 12, 2025. Draft Guidance: Feb 23, 2026. Threshold: 42% liver cell editing in mouse models accepted. CHOP ecosystem (Ahrens-Nicklas, Guay-Woodford, ARPA-H THRIVE) is the direct infrastructure. HRFD enrollment is the entry point. Cross-connection: Baby KJ regulatory precedent ↔ PKHD1 His3124Tyr ABE ↔ CHOP ARPA-H THRIVE ↔ HRFD enrollment = the regulatory pathway exists now.

Research priorities — highest-impact experiments

  • Elexacaftor (VX-445, Trikafta) and Lumacaftor (VX-809, Orkambi) CFTR correctors in ARPKD cholangiocytes — ⭐ NOW CONFIRMED: Sharma A, Cebotaru L et al. September 19, 2026. Both VX-809 (first-generation) and VX-445 (next-generation, elexacaftor/Trikafta component) reduced cyst growth and proliferation, increased CFTR expression, and partially restored PC1 and PC2 expression in primary Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 cholangiocytes. VX-445 previously listed as "UNTESTED in ARPKD" — now confirmed effective in the correct ARPKD biliary model. CFTRinh-172 (CFTR inhibitor) failed to reproduce these effects — correction, not inhibition, is the effective strategy. (Sharma A, Afshani M, Sharma T, Cebotaru L. Cell Calcium 103195, Sep 19, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ceca.2026.103195.) Background: Yanda MK et al. (Outeda P). Cell Mol Gastroenterol Hepatol 12:1517, 2021. DOI: 10.1016/j.jcmgh.2021.07.012; Yanda MK, Zeidan A, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol GI 324:G404, 2023. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022.
  • ACT-777991 in PKHD1-deficient biliary organoids — Peltz or Strazzabosco labs. Phase 1 cleared (Boof ML et al. J Pharmacol Exp Ther 390:136-148, 2024. DOI: 10.1124/jpet.124.002195). No ARPKD trial exists. Breaks both CXCL10 and STAT3/SPP1 loops simultaneously.
  • TRNP1hi CD8+ T cells in Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse spleens — Strazzabosco lab. Do ARPKD mice with splenomegaly develop this pro-fibrotic T cell population? (Nat Genet 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41588-026-01754-6 — TRNP1hi CD8+ T cell mechanism in MASH; extension to ARPKD proposed)
  • Bexotegrast in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells — three-way convergence confirmed but no PKHD1-specific testing done yet. (Hirschfield GM et al. (INTEGRIS-PSC). J Hepatol 84:86, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.016)
  • GDF15 as mitochondrial stress and liver fibrosis biomarker in ARPKD-CHF: GDF15 is produced in response to mitochondrial stress and AMPK activation. FPC loss disrupts ICD15 (the mitochondrial fragment of FPC), providing a direct mechanistic basis for GDF15 elevation in ARPKD. ⭐ NEW COMPLETE MECHANISTIC CHAIN Sep 14, 2026 — ICD15 → Mitochondria → AMPK → CDK1: Five previously separate findings now connect into a continuous causal chain: (1) Walker Nat Commun 2023: FPC → ICD15 cleavage → mitochondrial translocation → enhanced respiration. When FPC is lost, ICD15 is not generated → mitochondria unsupported. (2) Liebau 2021 (Nat Metab 2026 Djouder connected): FPC loss → mitochondrial dysfunction confirmed in ARPKD cells. (3) Long KRUK grant: Mitochondrial dysfunction → AMP:ATP ratio rises → AMPK cannot be properly activated because upstream support is lost. (4) BEAT-PKD/SCY-770: Direct AMPK activation suppresses cystogenesis — confirming AMPK as the node. (5) Liu/Ma iScience 2026: CDK1 overactivation in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells → unchecked biliary proliferation → cysts AND fibrosis. AMPK suppresses CDK1 — so lost AMPK activity permits CDK1 overactivation. Therapeutic implication: Bempedoic acid (BEAT-PKD), SCY-770, and CDK1 inhibitors (RO-3306) are not isolated candidates — they all target the same functional axis downstream of FPC/ICD15 loss. Long (UCL GOS ICH) is the critical node: his AMPK work bridges ICD15/mitochondria upstream and CDK1 downstream. ⭐ CONFIRMED Sep 15, 2026 — Long's KRUK grant fully read and corrected (v7.6): £208,000 (corrected from £200K — official KRUK awards page) KRUK Paediatric and Rare Diseases Research Project Grant, October 2025 round (not June 2026 — that was only the news publication date). Official title: "Inhibiting AMP-activated protein kinase to modulate Autosomal Recessive Polycystic Kidney Disease." Mechanism is AMPK/cyst energy metabolism — but note INHIBITING direction (see discrepancy note below). Key structure: Long is PI; Dr Jennifer Chandler is Long's former trainee — now running her own parallel AAV gene therapy strand at the same UCL GOS ICH institution. Dr Daniyal Jafree (independent KRUK £248K AI-lymphatics grant Feb 2026) and Dr Carolina Bebi (independent KRUK £243K PUV lymphatics grant Oct 2025) are also Long lab former trainees now with independent funding. The AMPK project (Long, £208K — corrected Sep 15, 2026) and the AAV FPC fragment project (Chandler, £99K — corrected Sep 15, 2026) are a coordinated UCL GOS ICH ARPKD research program. TAKA clarification: The Oct 19, 2026 TAKA deadline is a separate grant application, not this confirmed project. ⚠️ CRITICAL CORRECTION Sep 15, 2026 — AMPK DIRECTION DISCREPANCY: The official KRUK award title for Long's grant is "Inhibiting AMP-activated protein kinase to modulate Autosomal Recessive Polycystic Kidney Disease." The June 2026 KRUK news article used "how cysts use energy" as public framing. Walker 2023 shows ICD15 drives AMPK activation as the disease-suppressive signal. Long's grant proposes AMPK inhibition. These are mechanistically opposite unless AMPK has a biphasic or cell-type-specific role in ARPKD. This discrepancy is the primary question for Long's September 25 email: "Your KRUK award describes inhibiting AMPK — Walker 2023 shows ICD15 drives AMPK activation as the disease-suppressive signal. How does inhibition fit that framework?" Long's grant was awarded in the October 2025 KRUK Paediatric & Rare Diseases round (not June 2026 — that was the KRUK news publication date). GDF15 mRNA is heavily distributed in hepatic tissue and circulating levels rise in direct proportion to hepatic scarring severity across chronic liver diseases. Updated Sep 10, 2026: GDF15 has already been studied in PCK rat cardiorenal syndrome (left ventricular hypertrophy, myocardial fibrosis, diastolic dysfunction) — confirming upregulation in ARPKD biology. It is already being paired with clinical surrogates (platelet counts, spleen size) in ARPKD-CHF multi-marker clinical assessments. In general hepatology, elevated GDF15 strongly correlates with advanced hepatic remodeling, bacterial translocation, and higher risk of portal hypertension complications including variceal bleeding and splenomegaly. ⚠️ THE DUAL-SOURCE DILEMMA: In ARPKD, elevated GDF15 reflects cumulative stress from BOTH kidney cysts AND liver fibrosis simultaneously. A single GDF15 reading cannot distinguish how much is driven by renal cystogenesis versus hepatic fibrogenesis — requiring paired markers (eGFR, MRE liver stiffness, platelet count) for meaningful interpretation. Mechanistic nuance: GDF15 also reprograms hepatic macrophages into an anti-inflammatory, pro-resolving phenotype — suggesting the body initially produces it as a protective brake on fibrosis. However, chronically sustained elevation in advanced disease turns maladaptive. This creates a ARPKD-CHF paradox: early GDF15 elevation may represent compensation; late elevation signals disease escape from that brake. Gap remaining: GDF15 has NOT been measured systematically in human ARPKD patients as a liver disease severity biomarker with published thresholds separating hepatic from renal contribution. Van Hove JLK, Friederich MW, Miethke AG, Molleston JP, Sokol RJ et al. Hepatol Commun. 2024;8(1) — Dr. Miethke (CCHMC) is the direct contact. Emailed Sep 10, 2026. Also: GDF-15 and uEGF independently associate with CKD progression in pediatric patients (Kidney Int Rep 10(10):3369-3378, 2025). Glucocorticoids (downstream of the GDF15 brain-liver axis) are contraindicated in children with portal hypertension.

    • ICD15 restoration in biliary cholangiocytes / CHF model — confirmed open gap:

    1. Dr. Patricia Outeda Garcia (University of Maryland, co-developer of D67 mouse model — Walker RV, Qian F et al. Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4): ICD15 loss alone produces no biliary phenotype under baseline conditions — only manifests under additional cellular stress. No group currently testing ICD15 in biliary cholangiocytes or CHF model. Personal communication, July 2026. Permission granted September 2, 2026.
    2. Dr. Feng Qian (University of Maryland, senior author — Walker RV, Qian F et al. Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4): "To my knowledge, I am not aware of any study that has specifically investigated the fibrocystin ICD15 domain, particularly its role in mitochondrial function, in biliary cholangiocytes." Model limitation acknowledged: the D67 digenic mouse model used in the 2023 paper limited liver phenotype analysis. Characterized the ICD15-in-biliary-cells hypothesis as "interesting and biologically testable." Plans to extend ICD15 studies to liver/biliary cells. Personal communication, Dr. Feng Qian, University of Maryland, 2026. Permission granted September 4, 2026.

      ⭐ Additional scientific content (Qian, personal communication, 2026 — permission granted Sep 4, 2026): Proximity of c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile) to the ICD15-encoding region raises the possibility that if this variant disrupts exon splicing, it could specifically alter ICD15 production in addition to the Met3642 amino acid change. Experimental validation via RNA analysis or minigene/alternative splicing assay is required to determine whether splicing is affected. Liver is considerably more accessible to exogenous gene expression approaches than kidney — making liver-targeted ICD15 delivery a priority. This is Qian's scientific opinion, not a published finding.
  • PICP measurement in ARPKD-CHF patients and Pkhd1 mice — validate as PKDNet (Hartung, CHOP) trial endpoint. (PICP biosensor FIB-EIS — Chem Eng J 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cej.2026.153547)
  • Mefloquine/verteporfin in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice — autophagy inhibition restores cilia in PLD; PKHD1 confirmation needed. (Masyuk AI, LaRusso NF et al. Hepatology 75(5):1110, 2022. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32298)
  • mTOR inhibitor hepatic endpoints — any ARPKD trial using rapamycin/everolimus must monitor hepatic disease progression. (Masyuk AI, LaRusso NF et al. Hepatology 75(5):1110, 2022. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32298 — three biliary mechanisms of harm)

Open Questions for the Research Community

These questions remain unanswered in the published literature. If your work addresses any of them — preliminary data, an unpublished observation, or a paper we may have missed — a brief reply to nathaniel_johnson@yahoo.com is all we ask.

Molecular Mechanisms & Cell Signaling

  • Are TREM2+ SPP1-expressing macrophages present in ARPKD-CHF periportal fibrotic niches, and does SPP1 silencing in this macrophage subset reduce biliary fibrosis? (Qiu W et al. Mol Biol Rep 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-12723-9) Routing: Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse with splenomegaly). ⚠️ PARTIAL ANSWER (Sep 10, 2026): In PSC cholestasis models, monocyte-derived macrophages (MoMFs) highly expressing TREM2 AND SPP1 are enriched around the portal triad and confirmed in human PSC tissue (Hepatology 2024). In ANKS6-deficient CHF mice (ciliopathy model), macrophage depletion with clodronate liposomes effectively reduced inflammatory gene expression, fibrosis, and biliary function — confirming macrophages are required for CHF fibrosis in ciliopathy models generally. ⭐ ARPKD-CHF SPECIFIC UPDATE (Sep 11, 2026 — full read of Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026): In PKHD1-specific ARPKD-CHF, OPN co-localization with F4/80 macrophage marker was almost absent. Cholangiocytes — NOT macrophages — are the dominant SPP1 source in ARPKD-CHF specifically. This is disease-context-dependent: macrophages are the dominant SPP1 source in MASH and other liver diseases, but not in ARPKD-CHF. Additionally, clodronate macrophage depletion in CHF mice did not fully resolve fibrosis, confirming that a direct cholangiocyte→HSC pathway (via SPP1/α8β1) operates independently. The TREM2+/SPP1+ macrophage question remains valid as a secondary research question — macrophages contribute to CHF fibrosis, but the dominant therapeutic target is cholangiocyte-secreted SPP1. Additional nuance (GDF15 connection, Sep 10, 2026): GDF15 — which is elevated in ARPKD liver disease — reprograms hepatic macrophages toward an anti-inflammatory, pro-resolving phenotype. This means ARPKD-CHF macrophage biology involves competing signals: TREM2+/SPP1+ pro-fibrotic macrophages vs. GDF15-reprogrammed anti-inflammatory macrophages. The balance may determine fibrosis rate and clinical severity.
  • Does the enlarged ARPKD-CHF spleen (>80% of portal hypertension patients) produce pro-fibrotic SPP1+ macrophages that travel to the liver and compound cholangiocyte-driven biliary fibrosis? Routing: Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 + splenomegaly model).
  • Does the SPTAN1/RAC1 pathway operate in ARPKD biliary epithelial cells? Confirmed in kidney CNT cells (Morizane, Adv Sci 2026) — untested in cholangiocytes. Morizane Sep 25 follow-up question.
  • Does PTH1R drive cAMP elevation in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes? ✅ ANSWERED (Sep 20, 2026): No. PTH1R is absent in cholangiocytes. Confirmed by Dr. Navin Gupta (Mayo Clinic, corresponding author), personal communication Sep 20, 2026. The biliary PTH1R question is closed. Additionally corrected: PTH1R is not upregulated in ARPKD kidney either — normal PTH1R signaling drives normal cAMP, which is cystogenic in PKHD1-deficient kidney cells. The receptor expression is normal; the cellular context makes normal signaling pathological.
  • 🆕 NEW (Sep 10, 2026): Is BICC1 dysregulated in ARPKD-CHF cholangiocytes? BICC1 (Bicaudal C1) is the 8th most cholangiocyte-specific gene in the human liver (logFC 3.613, 67.3% of cholangiocytes — Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas (Brancale & Vilarinho, J Hepatol 2021, PMID 34016468)). BICC1 mutations cause a ciliopathy with kidney cysts AND biliary fibrosis resembling ARPKD. BICC1 is a confirmed PKHD1 pathway interactor and RNA-binding protein involved in cAMP regulation. Not in framework. Routing: Harris PC (Mayo), Germino GG (NIDDK).
  • 🆕 NEW (Sep 10, 2026): Is GLIS3 dysregulated in ARPKD-CHF cholangiocytes? GLIS3 is specifically expressed in human cholangiocytes (rank 43, logFC 2.661, 41.9% — Yale atlas). GLIS3 mutations cause neonatal diabetes + congenital hypothyroidism + polycystic kidneys + biliary atresia in humans. GLIS3 regulates PKHD1 expression in kidney — whether this regulatory relationship extends to biliary cells is unknown. Routing: Harris PC (Mayo).
  • 🆕 NEW (Sep 10, 2026): Is EFHD2 dysregulated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes? ⭐ NEW OPEN QUESTION (Sep 14, 2026 — confirmed by Chaudhuri): EFHD2 (sister protein to EFHD1) IS expressed in human cholangiocytes per Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas (Brancale/Vilarinho J Hepatol 2021, 2,357 cholangiocytes from 28 human livers). While EFHD1 is absent in cholangiocytes, EFHD2 may serve analogous ER-mitochondria contact stabilization in biliary cells. If FPC loss disrupts ICD15/mitochondria in cholangiocytes, EFHD2 may be the relevant effector — not EFHD1. ✅ PERMISSION GRANTED Sep 14, 2026 (Chaudhuri personal communication): EFHD1 studied in hepatocytes only — low/absent in cholangiocytes per transcriptional datasets (Fig. S1A). Mitochondrial abundance and antiviral/innate immune sensing differ between hepatocytes and cholangiocytes — hepatocytes are unusual in NOT sensing mitochondrial DNA release; cholangiocytes may lack the relevant innate immune sensors. No known groups studying EFHD1 or EFHD2 in cholangiocytes. EFHD1 → ARPKD-CHF biliary application remains extrapolation. Routing: Chaudhuri D (Utah) — permission confirmed, cite as personal communication Sep 14, 2026.
  • Does AAV-induced NFκB nephrotoxicity (Gupta/Morizane, STTT 2025) also occur in cholangiocytes when AAV is delivered to the biliary system (e.g., via ERCP or portal vein)? Morizane follow-up — October 2026.
  • Does CXCR3 blockade reduce biliary cyst fluid secretion, or only periportal fibrosis?
  • Is AMPK → LATS1/2 → YAP/TAZ the mechanism behind bempedoic acid's liver benefit in PCK rat?
  • Does tolvaptan (V2R antagonist) have any secondary effect on ARPKD biliary disease? → ✅ SUBSTANTIALLY ANSWERED Sep 16, 2026 — the vasopressin axis is confirmed active in biliary epithelium in polycystic liver disease, and suppressing it reduced hepatic cystogenesis in the PCK rat. See Answered Questions.
  • Has FXR been directly measured in PKHD1-deficient or fibrocystin-null biliary epithelial cells?
  • Is CDK1 upregulated downstream of Scribble/YAP dysregulation in PKHD1-/- mouse models?
  • Does somatostatin signaling through ciliary SSTR2 affect TGR5 ciliary localization in FPC-deficient cells?
  • Is SPTAN1 (α-II spectrin) reduced in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes, and does this disrupt integrin-BM anchoring?
  • 🆕 NEW (Sep 22, 2026): Is integrin αV activated in ARPKD-CHF LSECs under portal hypertension, and does this drive a secondary LSEC fibrotic circuit via YAP → CTGF? Kato/Hikita (Osaka) confirmed integrin αV → YAP → CTGF in LSECs under hydrostatic pressure in mouse congestion models and human FALD patient tissue (Kato S, Hikita H et al. Gastroenterology 170(5):1000-1016, 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2025.11.014). Whether the same LSEC pathway is activated in ARPKD-CHF portal hypertension has not been tested. Routing: Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 mice with portal hypertension — could test αV expression and YAP/CTGF in LSECs by scRNA-seq or immunofluorescence); Hikita H (Osaka — potential cold contact, FALD/ARPKD-CHF bridge).
  • Is imidazole propionate (ImP) elevated in ARPKD-CHF patients given gut dysbiosis from recurrent cholangitis and antibiotic cycles? ⚠️ MECHANISTIC ANSWER CONFIRMED (Sep 10, 2026 — full paper retrieved): ImP engages a cell surface receptor on cholangiocytes → phosphorylation cascade → p38 activation → inflammatory cytokines + profibrotic programs. This is confirmed causal mechanism in PSC cholangiocytes (n=1,003 patients). Three therapeutic angles now defined: (a) reduce ImP production via microbiome targeting (dietary histidine, antibiotics, bacteriophages, live biotherapeutics), (b) block ImP receptor on cholangiocytes, (c) inhibit p38 locally in biliary epithelium — paper notes the biliary compartment's relative isolation "might allow more localized approaches." ARPKD-CHF specific measurement: not yet published. ImP in ARPKD-CHF serum is actionable — HPLC or mass spectrometry on stored samples from HRFD, ARegPKD, or any clinical cohort. New network contact: Carpino G (Sapienza Rome, co-author, biliary disease specialist). Routing: Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 model — p38δ question sent Sep 10); Molinaro A / Hov JR (Oslo — potential cold outreach with ARPKD extension hypothesis).
  • Does ⚠️ Per Chaudhuri (Sep 14, 2026): antiviral/innate immune sensing is different in cholangiocytes vs. hepatocytes — hepatocytes are unusual in NOT sensing mitochondrial DNA release (the more common innate immune trigger). Cholangiocytes may lack the innate immune sensors that make EFHD1 functional in hepatocytes. Does EFHD2 (which IS expressed in cholangiocytes) stabilize ER-mitochondria contacts in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes — is it elevated when ICD15 is disrupted? ⚠️ ANSWERED (Sep 10, 2026 — Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas + Human Protein Atlas): EFHD1 is not detected in cholangiocytes — confirmed by two independent publicly available sources: (1) Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas (Brancale & Vilarinho, J Hepatol 2021, PMID 34016468 — 2,357 cholangiocytes from 28 healthy livers) — EFHD1 not detected in any liver cell type at meaningful levels; (2) Human Protein Atlas — EFHD1 highest single-cell expression in distal tubular cells (347.3 nTPM), not liver. Additionally, Fig. S1A of Eberhardt/Chaudhuri et al. (J Clin Invest 2026, DOI: 10.1172/JCI204023) shows low or absent EFHD1 expression in cholangiocytes in the published supplementary data. ⚠️ Permission pending from Dr. Chaudhuri to cite his personal communication in this document. EFHD1 removed from ARPKD-CHF biliary cascade. NEW: EFHD1 IS highly expressed in distal tubular cells — the confirmed ARPKD kidney cyst origin (Kuraoka/Morizane 2026). May be relevant to kidney cystogenesis. NEW: EFHD2 (sister protein) IS expressed in cholangiocytes per Yale atlas — new open question pending Chaudhuri permission reply.

ECM & Biliary Basement Membrane

  • Has tranexamic acid or any plasmin inhibitor been tested in a PKHD1-deficient model for biliary laminin/collagen IV preservation? (Partially answered: TXA reduces Timp1, Col1a1, CK19, type I collagen in chronic bile duct injury mice — PKHD1-specific testing is the next step.)
  • Have PRO-C4 (type IV collagen formation) or C4M (type IV collagen degradation) been measured in any CHF or Caroli disease cohort?
  • What is the current clinical development status of INT-767 (dual FXR/TGR5 agonist)? ⚠️ ANSWERED (Sep 10, 2026): INT-767 completed Phase I (single and multiple ascending dose in healthy volunteers) by Intercept Pharmaceuticals. Intercept was acquired by Alfasigma S.p.A. in 2023 — INT-767 program current status post-acquisition is unclear, likely stalled or deprioritized. No Phase 2 data published. Additionally: the TGR5 agonist component of INT-767 is potentially harmful in ARPKD-CHF where TGR5 is plasma membrane-localized and pro-cystogenic via Gαs (LaRusso/Masyuk data). The correct approach in ARPKD-CHF is FXR agonism + TGR5 antagonism, not dual agonism. INT-767 should not be pursued for ARPKD.
  • What agents can restore laminin or type IV collagen production in FPC-deficient biliary cells?

Drug Discovery & Pharmacology

  • Does AMG-487 (CXCR3 antagonist, Amgen) or any CXCR3 antagonist reduce biliary fibrosis in a PKHD1-specific model? ⚠️ ACT-777991 (Idorsia CXCR3 antagonist for T1D) and dual somatostatin/dopamine agonists are separate drug classes — question revised Sep 14, 2026.
  • Does SCY-770 (direct AMPK activator) benefit ARPKD biliary fibrosis independently of bempedoic acid? ⚠️ PARTIAL ANSWER (Sep 10, 2026): SCY-770 (formerly PXL-770, acquired by SCYNEXIS from Poxel S.A. March 2026) — Phase 1 study initiated June 30, 2026 for ADPKD (NCT pending). Phase 2 ADPKD proof-of-concept planned Q4 2026; early efficacy readout H2 2027. FDA Orphan Drug Designation granted for ADPKD. Previous Phase 2a NAFLD data: 18% reduction in liver fat (MRI-PDFF) at 12 weeks, 500mg QD. Mechanism: AMPK activation → suppresses mTOR AND cAMP — both dysregulated in ARPKD. ARPKD biliary testing: not planned. The ADPKD kidney-focused program does not include liver endpoints. Extension to ARPKD biliary fibrosis requires either a separate study or collaboration with Cadamuro's PCK rat bempedoic acid study (already running). Patent protection until at least 2041.
  • Does elexacaftor simultaneously improve kidney AND biliary disease in the same PKHD1-deficient cells?
