Answered Questions

Questions resolved through researcher replies, new publications, or direct communication — each with full attribution and date. Updated as new answers arrive. Reply to nathaniel_johnson@yahoo.com if your work addresses any open question.

Molecular Mechanisms & Cell Signaling

ANSWERED — Do ARPKD patients develop ocular abnormalities (glaucoma, anterior segment dysgenesis) as seen in Pkhd1del3-67/del3-67 mice? 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

No. No eye abnormalities identified in human ARPKD patients. The eye phenotype in Pkhd1del3-67/del3-67 mice is a mouse-specific artifact. Confirmed from abstract (#8 of abstract-arpkd-set.txt, Sep 22, 2026): The paper uses "genetic, epigenetic, bioinformatic and developmental analyses" to show Pkhd1del3-67/del3-67 mice "lack Tfap2b and AP-2β expression in a subset of periocular mesenchymal cells at E13.5 and its derivatives." The Pkhd1del3-67 deletion "disrupts features of the Pkhd1-Tfap2b genomic architecture essential for Tfap2b cell-specific function." Also confirmed from preprint abstract (#32, same paper, preprint DOI: 10.64898/2026.02.15.706040, PMID: 41757104): "Pkhd1 del3-67/+;Tfap2b ko/+ trans-heterozygotes lack Tfap2b and AP-2β in relevant cell-types and have similar eye abnormalities as neural crest cell-specific Tfap2b ko mutants." GWAS SNPs near PKHD1 associated with POAG "may impact disease risk by altering TFAP2B regulatory landscapes" (abstract #8, verbatim). ⚠️ FULL PAPER NOT READ — Nature Communications page did not return content on Sep 22, 2026. Specific claims about Fig. 5, Hi-C datasets, registry language, BICC1 supplementary data, and reference numbers cannot be confirmed from what was actually retrieved. These details require reading the full paper directly at the link below. Source: Ishimoto Y, Menezes LF, Nakaya N, Barbosa-Sabanero K, Horie Y, Yoshida T, Reece JM, Zhou F, Tomarev S, Kerosuo L, Germino GG. Nat Commun 17(1):8650, Jul 23 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-75698-y. PMID: 42618573. PMC13490598. Open access — full text available at this link.

⚠️ FRAMEWORK CORRECTION — September 10, 2026 (Gregory G. Germino MD, NIDDK Deputy Director, personal communication): Our original characterization of this finding incorrectly implied that TFAP2B regulates PKHD1 expression. Dr. Germino clarifies: the direction is reversed. It is chromatin structure or specific sequences around the PKHD1 gene that regulate TFAP2B expression in a small cell subset — not TFAP2B regulating PKHD1. This distinction is important: the PKHD1 locus acts as a regulatory region for a neighboring gene (TFAP2B) in specific cell types, but this does not mean TFAP2B controls PKHD1 activity. The regulatory genomics of PKHD1 expression itself — particularly in biliary cholangiocytes — remains an open question his group is actively investigating. See the Open Questions in PKHD1 Regulatory Genomics section below.

ANSWERED — Has SPP1 (osteopontin) been measured in serum and correlated with CHF severity in ARPKD patients?

Prof. Jesús M. Banales, FAASLD (Biogipuzkoa Health Research Institute / Donostia University Hospital). Personal communication, September 2026. Permission granted September 8, 2026: "There are currently no robust data on SPP1 levels in serum and their correlation with liver fibrosis in ARPKD." SPP1 has five independent preclinical confirmations as a biliary fibrosis mediator in ARPKD, but no patient serum biomarker data exist. Confirmed gap by a researcher with 20+ years of ARPKD liver research. SPP1 serum measurement in ARPKD-CHF patients remains a priority open question for clinical validation.

ANSWERED — Can ERCP retrograde biliary delivery achieve sufficient transfection of intrahepatic bile ducts? 📌 ANSWERED · Sep 16, 2026

Published efficiency is low, and this framework now prefers hepatic arterial delivery. Retrograde infusion into the bile duct does advance well into the hepatic lobule and demonstrates remarkable permeability of canalicular and cholangiole tight junctions in normal rats — but it is an effective approach for delivering genes to only approximately 1% of hepatocytes (Fabre JW et al., King's College London; PMC1774706). Canalicular tight junction permeability is further altered in damaged livers.

Three findings together supersede ERCP as the preferred biliary route: (1) this low transfection efficiency; (2) the intrahepatic bile ducts are supplied almost exclusively by the hepatic artery while hepatocytes draw mostly from the portal vein — and hepatic artery catheterization is already routine interventional radiology (TACE, HAIC); (3) in ductal plate malformation the peribiliary arterial plexus is hypertrophied, with prolific stout arterioles interdigitating with juvenile ductal elements, while portal vein branches are reduced (de Lédinghen V et al. J Gastroenterol Hepatol 13:720-724, 1998. DOI: 10.1111/j.1440-1746.1998.tb00720.x; Pillai S, Center SA et al. "Ductal Plate Malformation in the Liver of Boxer Dogs," Vet Pathol 53(3):602-613, 2016 — canine study, 30 dogs). The disease itself enlarges the arterial route to the target cells while reducing the portal one.

Additional consideration: ERCP in a child with ARPKD-CHF carries cholangitis risk in an already-abnormal biliary tree, and this framework documents each cholangitis episode as a fibrosis amplifier. The ERCP + KRT19 promoter + ABEmax concept is not invalidated, but hepatic arterial delivery is now the preferred route. See also: Salemdawod A, Walczak P, Janowski M. Mol Ther Nucleic Acids, Dec 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.omtn.2025.102782 — the independent published case for fractionated intra-arterial hepatic delivery.

ANSWERED — Does tolvaptan / the vasopressin axis have any effect on ARPKD biliary disease? 📌 ANSWERED · Sep 16, 2026

Yes — the vasopressin axis is confirmed active in biliary epithelium, and suppressing it was associated with reduced hepatic cystogenesis. Two independent lines of evidence:

(1) Direct biliary evidence: vasopressin regulates the growth of the biliary epithelium in polycystic liver disease (Mancinelli R et al. Lab Invest 96(11):1147-1155, 2016). The axis is not kidney-restricted.

(2) Functional evidence in the correct model: BNP is vasopressin-suppressive, and AAV9-BNP in the PCK rat produced reduced hepatic remodeling and cystic indices alongside renal benefit (Holditch SJ, Harris PC, LaRusso NF, Torres VE, Ikeda Y et al. Kidney Int 92(3):657-668, 2017).

Important distinction: this answers the biology question — the vasopressin axis affects biliary disease — but not the drug question. Tolvaptan carries documented hepatotoxicity risk, a material concern in a patient with existing cholangiopathy and elevated baseline transaminases. The BNP route reaches the same axis endogenously, and 40-fold supraphysiological BNP produced no hypotension and no notable blood chemistry changes in the PCK rat. The question is therefore reframed: not whether the vasopressin axis matters in ARPKD liver (it does), but which agent can suppress it safely in a child with portal hypertension.

NEW FINDING — Soluble guanylate cyclase (sGC) stimulators: a third route to cGMP elevation with anti-fibrotic and portal pressure effects in cholestatic liver disease 📌 NEW · Sep 18, 2026

Context — cAMP/cGMP cross-talk in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes: GC-A (activated by BNP), GC-B (activated by CNP), and sGC (activated by nitric oxide) all generate cGMP. In PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes, cAMP is elevated and drives pathogenic downstream signaling — β-catenin nuclear translocation, Rac-1 activation, and increased cell motility (Spirli C, Locatelli L, Fiorotto R, Fabris L, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58(5):1713-1723, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26554. PMID: 23744610. PMC: PMC3800498). cGMP elevation opposes cAMP through PDE-2 activation — a well-established second-messenger cross-talk mechanism. This provides a mechanistic rationale for why raising cGMP by any route (GC-A, GC-B, or sGC) may benefit ARPKD biliary disease.

Finding 1 — Riociguat (FDA-approved sGC stimulator) reduced liver fibrosis AND portal pressure specifically in cholestatic rats: Schwabl P, Reiberger T, Trebicka J et al. Sci Rep 8:9372, 2018. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-018-27656-y. PMID: 29921982. PMC: PMC6008436. Tested in bile duct ligation (BDL — cholestatic/biliary) and CCl4 (toxic/non-biliary) rat models. In BDL: reduced liver fibrosis, decreased portal pressure without affecting systemic hemodynamics. In early BDL specifically: decreased bile duct proliferation, improved sinusoidal vascular dysfunction, inhibited angiogenesis. In advanced BDL: anti-inflammatory effects. Benefit was substantially less pronounced in CCl4 — the sGC mechanism appears particularly active in biliary/cholestatic disease. Human correlation in the same paper: nitrates (also raise cGMP via sGC) decreased portal pressure more effectively in cholestatic than non-cholestatic cirrhosis patients. Riociguat is FDA-approved for pulmonary arterial hypertension and CTEPH. Oral, twice daily. Paediatric use exists in pulmonary hypertension context.

Finding 2 — Praliciguat (sGC stimulator) suppressed stellate cell fibrotic transformation in NASH: Hall KC, Masferrer JL et al. PNAS 116(22):11057-11062, 2019. DOI: 10.1073/pnas.1821045116. PMID: 31085647. PMC: PMC6561202. sGC is expressed in hepatic stellate cells and stellate-derived myofibroblasts — but not in hepatocytes. Praliciguat inhibited fibrotic and inflammatory signaling through AMPK and SMAD7. ⚠️ Cell-type caveat: effects demonstrated in hepatic stellate cells. ARPKD-CHF fibrosis is driven predominantly by NGFR+ portal fibroblasts. Whether sGC is expressed in NGFR+ portal fibroblasts is not established from available literature — this is the primary gap before sGC stimulators can be confidently proposed for ARPKD-CHF.

Finding 3 — BI 685509 (avenciguat, sGC activator) Phase II trials for cirrhotic portal hypertension: Reiberger T, Berzigotti A, Trebicka J et al. Trials 24:293, 2023. DOI: 10.1186/s13063-023-07291-3. PMID: 37095557. PMC: PMC10123479. Two active Phase II trials (NCT05161481, NCT05282121) of BI 685509 — an sGC activator (works under oxidative stress when NO is impaired, unlike stimulators). Lead investigators: Thomas Reiberger and Jonel Trebicka (Trebicka is co-author of the telmisartan portal pressure paper already in this framework). Target population: compensated cirrhosis. ⚠️ ARPKD-CHF portal hypertension is non-cirrhotic — periportal fibrosis-driven rather than sinusoidal vasoconstriction-driven. Applicability to non-cirrhotic PHT is extrapolated, not established.

Rac-1 triple convergence: Three independent groups found Rac-1 activation in ARPKD biliary pathogenesis without citing each other: (1) Spirli/Strazzabosco 2013 — elevated cAMP activates Rac-1 in Pkhd1-deficient cholangiocytes; (2) Morizane/Kuraoka 2026 — SPTAN1 loss → Rac-1 via calcium; (3) Hall/praliciguat 2019 — sGC stimulation reduced Rac-1 activity in stellate cells. Rac-1 is a confirmed load-bearing therapeutic node in ARPKD biliary disease from three independent directions.

NEW FINDING — CNP/GC-B system in ARPKD biliary disease: liver-intrinsic production, biliary cell receptors, fibroblast anti-fibrotic function, and an FDA-approved pediatric GC-B agonist 📌 NEW · Sep 18, 2026

Finding 1 — The liver produces its own CNP — a local natriuretic peptide system: Vollmar AM et al. Gut 40:145-150, 1997 (already in framework — PMID and DOI pending verification). The liver produces both ANP and CNP mRNA — a local natriuretic peptide system independent of cardiac secretion. CNP is produced primarily by vascular endothelial cells, of which liver sinusoidal endothelial cells (LSECs) are the hepatic population. In ARPKD-CHF, LSECs are structurally abnormal at baseline — part of the ductal plate malformation and confirmed as a safety concern in the Gupta/Morizane AAV paper (PMC12332185, already in framework). ⚠️ If LSECs are the primary hepatic CNP source and they are dysfunctional at baseline in ARPKD-CHF, the endogenous GC-B anti-fibrotic brake may already be impaired before any drug is administered. This is an untested hypothesis requiring specific CNP measurement in ARPKD liver tissue.

Finding 2 — Biliary epithelial cells (cholangiocytes) respond to natriuretic peptides with cGMP: St-Pierre MV, Schlenker T, Dufour JFJ, Jefferson DM, Fitz JG, Arias IM. "Stimulation of cyclic guanosine monophosphate production by natriuretic peptide in human biliary cells." Gastroenterology Issue 4:782-790, 1998. ⚠️ PMID and volume not yet confirmed — verify before citing in correspondence. H69 cells (human biliary epithelial cell line) produced cGMP in response to both ANP and C-type natriuretic peptide. Mz-ChA-1 cells responded only to ANP. Both cell lines expressed mRNA for natriuretic peptide receptors. cGMP activated Cl⁻ channels → regulates bile secretion. This is 25-year-old data that has never been connected to ARPKD biliary fibrosis.

Finding 3 — CNP → GC-B inhibits hepatic stellate cell growth and contraction: Tao Y et al. "Biological effects of C-type natriuretic peptide in human myofibroblastic hepatic stellate cells." J Biol Chem 274(34):23761-23769, 1999 Aug 20. PMID: 10446136. CNP activates NPR-B/GC-B on myofibroblastic hepatic stellate cells → inhibits both proliferation and contraction. This is the foundational paper establishing CNP/GC-B as an anti-fibrotic mechanism in the liver. ⚠️ Cell-type caveat: demonstrated in hepatic stellate cells. ARPKD-CHF fibrosis is driven predominantly by NGFR+ portal fibroblasts — see Finding 4 below.

Finding 4 — GC-B expressed in fibroblasts (not just stellate cells) with confirmed anti-inflammatory, anti-fibrotic function: Weyer R, Völker K, Potapenko T, Krebes L, Abeßer M, Friedrich AL, Lessmann E, Khadim A, Ruppert C, El Agha E, Sheta D, Beilhack A, Santi DV, Schneider EL, Kuhn M, Dabral S. "Pathophysiological and therapeutic implications of C-type natriuretic peptide/cyclic GMP signaling in pulmonary fibrosis." JCI Insight. 2025/2026. DOI: 10.1172/jci.insight.196812. ⚠️ Year and PMID pending confirmation — verify before citing. Key findings: (1) GC-B is expressed and functional in fibroblasts (confirmed by fibroblast-restricted GC-B knockout — enhanced inflammation when GC-B absent); (2) CNP/GC-B provides an anti-inflammatory, anti-fibrotic endogenous brake in fibroblasts; (3) a long-acting CNP analog (MS~[Gln6,14]CNP-38) abrogated both lung inflammation AND fibrosis with a single subcutaneous injection. This substantially narrows the portal fibroblast gap: portal fibroblasts ARE fibroblasts; GC-B is functional in fibroblasts (lung) and in stellate cells (liver). Direct measurement of GC-B in NGFR+ portal fibroblasts has not been published — this is the primary remaining gap. Professor Michaela Kuhn (University of Würzburg, michaela.kuhn@uni-wuerzburg.de) — senior author, creator of fibroblast-specific GC-B KO mouse — contacted Sep 18, 2026.

Finding 5 — CNP is upregulated in liver disease as a compensatory response: Gülberg V, Møller S, Henriksen JH et al. "Increased renal production of C-type natriuretic peptide (CNP) in patients with cirrhosis and functional renal failure." Gut 47(6):852-857, 2000. ⚠️ PMID not yet confirmed — verify before citing in correspondence. CNP is elevated in cirrhosis with functional renal failure — the body compensatorily increases CNP in advanced liver disease. In ARPKD-CHF, the endogenous CNP brake may be activated but overwhelmed by the chronic biliary inflammatory and fibrogenic drive.

Finding 6 — Vosoritide (Voxzogo): FDA-approved GC-B/NPR-B agonist already used in children: Vosoritide (BMN-111, BioMarin Pharmaceutical) is an NPR-B/GC-B agonist analog of CNP, FDA-approved November 2021 for children 5 years and older with achondroplasia (open epiphyses). Daily subcutaneous injection, 15 μg/kg. Phase 3 data: 121 children, 1.57 cm/year improvement in annualized growth velocity. Directly activates the same receptor (GC-B/NPR-B) as endogenous CNP. Has never been evaluated in ARPKD, liver fibrosis, or biliary disease. ⚠️ Safety note for portal hypertension context: transient blood pressure decrease reported in 13% of children (8/60) in Phase 3 trial. In a patient already on NSBBs or other portal pressure-reducing therapy, blood pressure monitoring would be essential. No hepatic safety concerns identified in achondroplasia trials.

Overall CNP/GC-B landscape in ARPKD-CHF: GC-B is present in whole liver (Vollmar 1997), functional in hepatic stellate cells (Tao 1999), functional in fibroblasts as a cell class (Weyer/Kuhn 2025/2026), anti-fibrotic and anti-portal hypertensive (Hobbs/Pérez-Ternero PNAS Nexus 2024 — PMC11734523, already in framework). Biliary cells appear to respond to natriuretic peptides via cGMP (St-Pierre 1998, pending PMID). An endogenous CNP brake is already activated in liver disease (Gülberg 2000). The open questions are: (1) Is GC-B expressed on NGFR+ portal fibroblasts specifically? (2) Is LSEC-derived CNP production impaired at baseline in ARPKD-CHF? (3) Would vosoritide or a long-acting CNP analog reduce biliary fibrosis in a PKHD1 model?

ANSWERED — Does the SPTAN1/RAC1 pathway operate in ARPKD kidney cells?

Yes — confirmed in human PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids, Sptan1-mutant mice, and human ARPKD kidney specimens. SPTAN1 (αII-spectrin) is reduced in FPC-deficient kidney cells → SLC8A1/NCX1 dysregulation → intracellular Ca²⁺ ↑ → RAC1 activation → c-FOS → distal/connecting tubule cyst expansion. Key additional finding: ARPKD kidney cysts originate specifically from SLC8A1+ distal/connecting tubule cells (not collecting duct — definitively resolved by scRNA-seq + human tissue). CRISPRa epigenetic upregulation of SPTAN1 in PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids alleviated cystic phenotypes, normalized intracellular Ca²⁺, and reduced RAC1/c-FOS — first proof-of-concept that epigenetic rescue of a downstream target rescues ARPKD cysts without correcting PKHD1. Authors include Lisa M. Satlin MD (Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai, pediatric nephrology/flow physiology) and Peter C. Harris (Mayo Clinic). (Kuraoka S, Harris PC, Satlin LM, Morizane R et al. Adv Sci 13(25):e2524001, 2026. PMC13137809.) Biliary/cholangiocyte confirmation remains open question — Morizane Sep 25 follow-up.

⭐ ADDED SEPTEMBER 15, 2026 — Walker has a second KRUK project (2024) separate from ICD15 paper: Dr Rebecca Walker, University of Sheffield. £240,000 KRUK Paediatric Award Grant, June 2024. Title: "Studying how communication between cells can lead to cyst development in ARPKD." Walker found that PKHD1 interacts with another PKD-related gene — almost certainly PKD2 — and both encoded proteins co-operate in ciliary signaling to convert signals and tell the cell how to react. Walker is investigating what fails when both proteins are faulty and their signals cannot be interpreted correctly. This is the FPC/PC2 ciliary complex, directly related to Wang et al. 2007 already in the framework. Walker therefore has: (1) ICD15 paper (Walker/Qian, Nat Commun 2023) — characterized the FPC C-terminal mitochondrial fragment; (2) KRUK 2024 grant (£240,000) — investigating FPC/PC2 ciliary signaling co-operation and cystogenesis. Both projects are active. Walker is already in network — emailed August 10, follow-up September 9.

ANSWERED — Has ICD15 been studied in biliary cholangiocytes or CHF models?

Prof. Feng Qian (University of Maryland. Walker, Qian et al. Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4. Permission granted September 4, 2026): "To my knowledge, I am not aware of any study that has specifically investigated the fibrocystin ICD15 domain, particularly its role in mitochondrial function, in biliary cholangiocytes." Prof. Qian characterizes the ICD15-in-biliary-cells hypothesis as "interesting and biologically testable" and plans to extend ICD15 studies to liver/biliary cells. Proximity of c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile) to the ICD15-encoding region raises the possibility that if this variant disrupts exon splicing, it could specifically alter ICD15 production.

Drug Discovery & Pharmacology

ANSWERED — Have CFTR correctors been tested in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells and animal models?

Prof. Liudmila Cebotaru (Johns Hopkins University. Permission granted September 2, 2026): "The CFTR correctors would reduce the proliferation, inflammation, fibrosis and dilatation of bile ducts in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells, and animal models. Truncated form of CFTR, which was introduced in cells and mouse models works as a genetic corrector and has the ability to bind ER-retained CFTR, reduce ER calcium, and carry CFTR, PC2, PC1, and FPC to the correct cellular location."

Published support: Yanda, Zeidan, Cebotaru — Am J Physiol GI 2023 · Ciobanu, Outeda, Guggino, Cebotaru — Am J Physiol GI 2025 · Ciobanu, Cebotaru — Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2025 · ⭐ NEW Sep 19, 2026: Sharma A, Afshani M, Sharma T, Cebotaru L. "CFTR correctors suppress Ca²⁺–cAMP signaling and cyst growth in primary cultures of ARPKD cholangiocytes." Cell Calcium 103195, Sep 19, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ceca.2026.103195. Johns Hopkins. NIH DK125272. First paper to test VX-809 and VX-445 (elexacaftor) in primary Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 cholangiocytes. Both correctors reduced cyst growth, partially restored PC1 and PC2, attenuated Ca²⁺/cAMP signaling. CFTRinh-172 failed. Correction ≠ inhibition.

⭐ NEW FINDING — PC1 and PC2 reduced in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes: The abstract confirms that FPC deficiency is associated with marked reductions in polycystin-1 (PC1) and polycystin-2 (PC2) protein expression in ARPKD cholangiocytes alongside CFTR — all three proteins are simultaneously reduced. CFTR correctors partially restore all three. ⚠️ The abstract demonstrates co-occurrence, not proven causation — it does not establish whether reduced CFTR is causing reduced PC1/PC2, or whether FPC loss reduces all three independently in parallel. The mechanism requires the full paper and is not confirmed from the abstract alone. Source: Abstract of Sharma A, Afshani M, Sharma T, Cebotaru L. Cell Calcium 103195, September 19, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ceca.2026.103195.

⚠️ HYPOTHESIS ONLY — possible mechanistic explanation from Gulati/Guay-Woodford Pediatr Nephrol 2026 (DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x. PMID: 41603896): FPC proteolytic processing produces FPC-PECD which is shed into the urine packaged into exosome-like vesicles (ELVs) together with PC1 and PC2, forming the polycystin protein complex. This packaging is dependent on ligand-triggered C2 cleavage by ADAM metalloproteases within the SEA domain of FPC. If FPC is absent → the ELV complex cannot form properly → PC1 and PC2 are mis-trafficked or degraded → reduced PC1/PC2 at the apical membrane and cilia. This provides a plausible FPC-dependent mechanism for the Cebotaru PC1/PC2 reduction finding. ⚠️ This connection is our inference — neither the Cebotaru paper nor the Guay-Woodford review makes this specific connection. It requires direct experimental testing.

⭐ Ca²⁺ SIGNALING PROFILE OF ARPKD CHOLANGIOCYTES — now complete: Confirmed from abstract: elevated resting cytosolic Ca²⁺, increased thapsigargin-releasable ER Ca²⁺ pool, increased STIM1 expression, reduced IP₃ receptor expression, markedly elevated cAMP, adenylyl cyclase isoform switch (AC3 increased, AC6 reduced), CFTR redistributed toward apical membrane. These are specific, measurable biomarkers for CFTR corrector response. Source: same paper. ⚠️ Specific values not confirmed from abstract.



Supporting precedent from PFIC biology — CFTR correctors rescue non-CFTR mislocalized biliary transporters (partial rescue): Two independent papers show CFTR correctors act on biliary proteins other than CFTR, relevant to the elexacaftor/FPC hypothesis. Important caveat: rescue was partial in both cases. (1) Ben Saad A, Vauthier V, Tóth A, Janaszkiewicz A, Lapalus M, Durand-Schneider AM, Falguières T et al. "Effect of CFTR correctors on the traffic and the function of intracellularly retained ABCB4 variants." Liver Int 41(6):1344-1357, 2021. DOI: 10.1111/liv.14790. PMID: 33650203. ⚠️ DOI discrepancy: HAL preprint of same paper shows DOI 10.1111/liv.14839 — verify correct final-publication DOI before citing. Findings: CFTR correctors (C10, C13, C17) rescued ABCB4 trafficking and canalicular localization for four traffic-defective ABCB4 variants. However, phosphatidylcholine secretion function was NOT rescued, and wild-type ABCB4 activity was inhibited. Conclusion: CFTR correctors can rescue mislocalized biliary transporters but may uncouple trafficking from function — an important limitation for the FPC hypothesis. (2) Mareux E, Lapalus M, Ben Saad A, Zelli R, Lakli M, Riahi Y et al. "In vitro rescue of the bile acid transport function of ABCB11 variants by CFTR potentiators." Int J Mol Sci 23(18):10758, 2022. DOI: 10.3390/ijms231810758. PMID: 36142670. PMC: PMC9502978. CFTR potentiators (ivacaftor/VX-770) rescued bile acid transport function of ER-retained ABCB11 (BSEP/PFIC2) variants — function rescue confirmed in this case. Together, these papers show CFTR correctors/potentiators can act on non-CFTR biliary proteins, but with variable functional outcomes. The implication for FPC rescue remains unconfirmed. Citations sourced from Vather M, Lee J, Vittorio J. Curr Treat Options Peds 12:29, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s40746-026-00386-4.

Gene Therapy & Delivery

ANSWERED — Has biliary AAV gene therapy been tested in a PKHD1-specific model?

Prof. Holger Willenbring (UCSF. Permission granted September 3, 2026): "Recent progress in gene delivery and genome editing technologies support the feasibility of gene therapy of the liver disease in ARPKD-CHF, which has been challenging because of the large size of the PKHD1 gene."

ANSWERED — Base editing PKD1/PKHD1 — what is the current state of the art?

Ibel A, Krappitz M, Kaminski MM et al. confirmed ABEmax base editing corrected a PKD1 missense variant in vivo. Only 5% liver editing efficiency was sufficient to produce significant liver cyst reduction in ADPKD mice. (Mol Ther 33(11):5373-5382, 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2025.08.026. PMID: 40842155)

Immunology & Macrophage Biology

ANSWERED — What organisms predominate in ARPKD cholangitis?

E. coli (22-37%), Pseudomonas spp. (28%), Enterococcus faecalis (15-39%). COBRA trial: 1-day antibiotics after biliary drainage noninferior to 4-7 days (413 patients, 31 Dutch centers). (Cozma MA et al. Antibiotics 2025 · Overdevest AG et al. COBRA trial, Trials 2026)

Clinical & Translational

ANSWERED — What are validated non-biopsy endpoints for ARPKD-CHF clinical trials?

IMAGE-ARPKD trial (NCT07201025, Flask CA, Hartung EA, Dell KM et al., Pediatric Radiology 2026) confirms: MRE liver stiffness = 3.60 kPa in ARPKD patients with portal hypertension. Liver biopsy NOT recommended in ARPKD. Total kidney volume is not useful for ARPKD trials. MRE + SPP1 + ELF score are the recommended endpoints.

ANSWERED — What is the dominant cause of post-transplant mortality in ARPKD?

64-80% of mortality in ARPKD kidney transplant patients is from cholangitis and hepatobiliary sepsis — not kidney failure. Treating CHF is survival-critical, not only quality of life. (Telega G, Cronin D, Avner ED. Pediatr Transplant 17(4):328-335, 2013. DOI: 10.1111/petr.12076. PMID: 23593929)

ANSWERED — Are mTOR inhibitors safe in ARPKD-CHF?

mTOR inhibitors are NOT safe for ARPKD patients with liver involvement. Three independent biliary harm mechanisms confirmed. Every ARPKD trial using mTOR inhibitors must include dedicated hepatic endpoints. (Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 75(5):1110, 2022)

Organoids & Modeling

ANSWERED — Is the Morizane ARPKD organoid-on-chip platform moving toward clinical translation?

Dr. Ryuji Morizane (MGH/Harvard. Permission granted September 3, 2026): Pursuing translational studies with the longer-term goal of moving toward clinical studies if results are sufficiently promising. Letter of support from our network is welcomed.

Genetics & Variant Analysis

ANSWERED — Is Met3642Ile (c.10926G>A) reclassified from VUS toward pathogenic at any institution?

Prof. Peter C. Harris (Mayo Clinic, September 2026. Permission granted September 3, 2026): This variant has been observed in trans with a confirmed pathogenic PKHD1 variant in a Mayo family — relevant PM3 context. Under formal clinical ACMG guidelines it would still be scored as a VUS. This is a research evaluation only, not a clinical reclassification.

ANSWERED — Have CHF variants in genotype zones 1-3 been associated with distinct hepatic outcomes?

Zone 3 (AA 2625-4074) is associated with the poorest hepatic outcomes. Both family variants (His3124Tyr at AA 3124 and Met3642Ile at AA 3642) map to Zone 3. (Lapunzina-Soler P et al. Genes 17(2):229, 2026) This is consistent with a liver-predominant phenotype.

⭐ FPCct sequesters Src at plasma membrane to suppress STAT3-driven cystogenesis — FPCct protein domain as therapeutic lead 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Hassan F, Hahnenstein ST, Dafinger C, Liebau MC, Ziegler WH et al. (Hannover Medical School / Cologne). Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00086.2026. Published August 28, 2026. Full paper read Sep 11, 2026. Tier 1 — Directly confirms FPCct/Src/STAT3 cascade mechanism. ⚠️ KIDNEY COLLECTING DUCT CELLS ONLY — pl-MDCK (canine) model. Zero biliary/liver/cholangiocyte data. All biliary application is our extrapolation, not confirmed by this paper.

Key mechanistic findings:

  • FPCct sequesters Src at the apical plasma membrane — a new location beyond the previously described nuclear interaction. FPCct interacts with Src at two sites: (1) plasma membrane, preventing SRC activation, and (2) nucleus, suppressing SRC-mediated STAT3 transcription. FPC loss unleashes Src at both sites simultaneously.
  • Quantitative ARPKD cell data: Pkhd1 KO cells (pl-MDCK) show 2-fold elevated basal cAMP + 3-fold higher luminal pressure vs wildtype. Forskolin stimulation (cAMP elevation) → 2-3 fold higher cystogenic pressure in KO vs wildtype. STAT3 is self-amplifying — STAT3 transcription drives more STAT3 gene expression.
  • FPCct re-expression rescues phenotype — with precise quantification (full paper read Sep 11, 2026): Wildtype FPCct expressed in Pkhd1 KO kidney collecting duct cells:
    • Reduces pSTAT3-Y705 by 81% (KO#1) and 55% (KO#3) under Fsk stimulation
    • Reduces pSrc-Y416 by 60% (KO#1) and 45% (KO#3) under Fsk stimulation
    • Reduces STAT3-dependent transcription by 56% and 54% (luciferase assay)
    • Reduces proportional lumen (cyst formation) by 52%/17% (unstimulated) and 50%/19% (Fsk)
    • FPCct is ~2.25× more potent than pharmacological Src inhibition (PP2): FPCct reduces luminal pressure by ~90 Pa vs. PP2's ~40 Pa — demonstrating that restoring FPC function outperforms targeting Src pharmacologically
    • Clone variability note: KO#1 responds much better than KO#3, which carry different frameshift mutations. This suggests allele-specific modulation of FPCct therapeutic effectiveness — potentially relevant to how specific PKHD1 missense variants respond
  • Src inhibition reduces liver disease in ARPKD models: PCK rats and bpk mice — Src inhibitors confirmed to reduce cyst formation AND liver cyst disease. First explicit literature confirmation that Src inhibition targets liver disease in ARPKD.
  • No clinical STAT3 trials in PKD — confirmed gap: "So far, there are no clinical trials testing STAT3 inhibition in PKD." Validates our framework's emphasis on STAT3 as untested target.
  • Two human patients with FPC cytoplasmic tail variants → portal hypertension + CKD G4: Direct human evidence that CTT-affecting variants (Zone 3) cause portal hypertension. His3124Tyr (AA 3124) and Met3642Ile (AA 3642) are both in Zone 3 (AA 2625–4074). ⚠️ Note Sep 11, 2026: Neither variant is in the CTT (cytoplasmic C-terminal tail, AA ~3882–4074). UniProt confirms FPC extracellular domain = AA 24–3858 and TM domain = AA ~3859–3881. His3124Tyr is in the extracellular IPT domain of Zone 3. Met3642Ile is extracellular, ~217 AA proximal to the TM domain. Both are Zone 3 ✅ but not CTT.

New therapeutic concept — FPCct protein domain delivery: "The (partial) reconstitution of FPC function may constitute a suitable strategy to dampen Src/STAT3 signaling." A protein domain construct (FPCct) rather than full-length FPC (4074 AA) is sufficient to restore the SRC/STAT3 brake — analogous to PC1-CTT tail suppression of ADPKD. ⚠️ KIDNEY-ONLY — confirmed only in pl-MDCK collecting duct cells; biliary delivery untested. FPCct membrane-bound form is the active suppressor (no cytoplasmic cleavage detected). STAT3 auto-transcription creates self-reinforcing loop — FPCct breaks this at Src level before self-amplification. FPCct ~2.25× more effective than PP2 pharmacological Src inhibition (90 Pa vs 40 Pa luminal pressure reduction). Tesevatinib (Src+EGFR inhibitor, Kadmon/Sanofi) — ADPKD Phase 2 (NCT03203642, no results published). ⭐ ARPKD Phase 1 (September 2017, Kadmon) — first-ever clinical trial specifically in ARPKD patients; first patient dosed Sep 2017. Inhibits EGFR, ErbB2, and c-Src — same Src kinase that FPCct sequesters. Preclinical: reduced renal AND biliary disease in bpk and PCK models. Current clinical status unknown — Kadmon acquired by Sanofi 2021. ⭐ First author Fatima Hassan now at Yale Nephrology — same institution as Strazzabosco (biliary CHF). Natural experiment for biliary FPCct validation.

cAMP → PKA → Src → STAT3 self-amplification loop — confirmed: FPC loss → cAMP elevation → PKA → Src activation → STAT3 Y705 phosphorylation → STAT3 gene upregulation → more STAT3 → amplified fibrogenic transcription. A self-reinforcing cascade — consistent with progressive biliary fibrosis in ARPKD-CHF.

Researcher routing: Ziegler WH (Ziegler.Wolfgang@mh-hannover.de, Hannover Medical School) — corresponding author, PI of BOTH FPCct/SRC/STAT3 (cascade mechanism) AND SPP1 knockdown (cascade endpoint). Emailed August 20, 2026 — three targeted questions, awaiting reply. Follow-up due Sep 19–20. Liebau MC (RWTH Aachen) — co-author, Zoom call Sep 14/15. Hassan F (now Yale Nephrology) — first author, now at same institution as Strazzabosco — potential bridge for biliary FPCct testing; note for Oct 10 Strazzabosco follow-up.

Molecular cascade — root to fibrosis

Each node is annotated with the researchers and publications that confirmed it. All citations link directly to the source paper.

PKHD1 biallelic mutation → fibrocystin absent from biliary cilia

⭐ NEW CASCADE NODE: FPC loss → NDFIP2/NEDD4 ubiquitination failure → TGF-β hyperactivation → fibrosis 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Kaimori JY, Germino GG et al. Nat Commun 8:1520, 2017; confirmed Guay-Woodford LM et al. Pediatr Nephrol 2026. Tier 1 — confirmed new cascade node. FPC-CTT co-localizes with NDFIP2 (adaptor/regulator of HECT E3 ubiquitin ligases SMURF1, SMURF2, NEDD4). FPC loss → SMURF/NEDD4 mislocalized → three downstream consequences: (1) TGF-β receptors and SMAD proteins not properly ubiquitinated/degraded → TGF-β signaling hyperactivated → fibrosis (independent of macrophage cascade); (2) RhoA not ubiquitinated → upregulated → cytoskeletal disruption → cystogenesis; (3) ENaC not ubiquitinated → retained in apical membrane → increased Na+ reabsorption → systemic hypertension. Zone 3 variants (AA 2625-4074, including His3124Tyr at AA 3124) are associated with progressive liver and kidney disease. ⚠️ Note Sep 11, 2026: Zone 3 variants at AA 3124 (extracellular IPT domain) do not necessarily disrupt CTT-specific function — CTT = AA ~3882–4074. His3124Tyr is ~758 AA upstream of CTT. The NDFIP2/CTT interaction applies specifically to CTT-located variants; His3124Tyr likely affects extracellular receptor signaling or protein folding rather than direct CTT-Src binding.

Autosomal recessive · ~1 in 20,000 live births · PKHD1 coding sequence ≈ 12.2 kb (12,222 bp) · 86+ exons
Gulati, Caldovic, Guay-Woodford — Pediatr Nephrol 2026 · Ward et al. — Hum Mol Genet 2003 (PMID 12925574) · Bergmann et al. — Nat Rev Dis Primers 2018 · GeneReviews: ARPKD/CHF (Guay-Woodford et al.)

47% ↓ CD45+ inflammatory infiltrate area · 37% ↓ K19+ cyst area (AMG-487 5 mg/kg orally × 3 months, research compound, Kaffe/Strazzabosco Hepatology 2018 PMID 29140564) ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — TGF-β1 Self-Sustaining Positive Feedback Loop: Four papers from Sep 14, 2026 session confirm a complete self-sustaining TGF-β1 loop in ARPKD-CHF biliary disease: FPC loss → NF-Y transcription factor activation (LaRusso/Gores AJP-GI 2025) → TGF-β1 secretion → SULT2B1 upregulation → Wnt/β-catenin → MMP7 upregulation → MMP7 cleaves latent TGF-β1 → activates more TGF-β1 → NF-Y remains active → cycle repeats indefinitely. This self-sustaining loop explains why ARPKD-CHF fibrosis progresses even without ongoing FPC-specific triggers — once initiated, the cycle is independent of the original cause. Monotherapy cannot break a closed cycle — simultaneous intervention at NF-Y (upstream) and MMP7 (downstream) required. Cholangiokines review (Cai/Tacke Front Immunol 2023, Table 1, reviewed by LaRusso) confirms TGF-β1 and CXCL-10 in Pkhd1-del4/del4 AND human CHF patient liver specimens. ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — CXCR3 Gap = Hidden Opportunity: AMG-487 is a research compound — no clinical development. But ACT-777991 (Idorsia) is a clinical-stage CXCR3 antagonist that completed Phase 1 safety (NCT04798209, healthy subjects 2021-2023) for Type 1 Diabetes. Phase 1 human safety data for CXCR3 antagonism now exists — but no one has connected it to biliary or ARPKD disease. The Kaffe 2018 data shows 47%/37% efficacy in the correct ARPKD biliary model. The translational question: could ACT-777991 or a comparable CXCR3 antagonist be repurposed for ARPKD-CHF given the existing human safety data? Strazzabosco (Yale) has both ends: Kaffe 2018 data and awareness of the clinical CXCR3 landscape. Email him: "Does the publication of Phase 1 safety data for CXCR3 antagonists change the translational potential of the AMG-487 ARPKD-CHF data from 2018?" This is a question only someone who has read both papers could ask. ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — Dual Macrophage Blockade: CX3CL1/CX3CR1 (LaRusso/Gores AJP-GI 2025) and CXCL10/CXCR3 (Kaffe 2018) are TWO PARALLEL macrophage recruitment axes in ARPKD-CHF. Blocking CXCR3 alone left CX3CL1/CX3CR1 intact — explaining the partial 47%/37% effect. Cholangiokines review (Table 1, Cai/Tacke 2023) confirms CXCL-1/2/5/10/12 all in ARPKD-CHF secretome. Dual CXCR3+CX3CR1 combined blockade has never been tested. Question for Strazzabosco. · ⚠️ ACT-777991 is an Idorsia CXCR3 antagonist for T1D — NOT related to ARPKD/biliary disease. No clinical-stage CXCR3 antagonist currently in development for PSC/ARPKD biliary disease.

📌 Added August 26, 2026 — P2X7/NLRP3→IL-1β + Jagged1/Notch2→CXCL10/12
P2X7 CRISPR KO slowed renal cyst growth in PCK rats ✓ARPKD-kidney. P2X7→K⁺ efflux→NLRP3→IL-1β: established inflammasome biology — hypothesis in ARPKD biliary cells, not confirmed in cholangiocytes. NLRP3 in cholangiocytes confirmed in cholestasis ✓. Jagged1/Notch2→CXCL10/12: confirmed in FPC-defective cholangiocytes ✓ARPKD. Jagged1/Notch2 → CXCL10 + CXCL1 + CXCL12 (third chemokine arm not addressed by CXCR3 blockade). Galectin-3 (LGALS3): TREM2 ligand + NLRP3 activator + HSC activator + ECM crosslinker — connects 5 cascade nodes. LMW-HA→CD44/TLR→NLRP3 (Sep 18, 2026 — 📌 NEW): A third NLRP3 activation route that connects the existing FPC→HA accumulation node directly to the inflammasome. Upon tissue injury, high-MW HA is cleaved into low-molecular-weight fragments (LMW-HA) that act as damage-associated molecular patterns (DAMPs). LMW-HA signals through CD44 and Toll-like receptors on Kupffer cells and HSCs → NF-κB activation → NLRP3 inflammasome priming → IL-1β + HSC activation. In ARPKD-CHF, FPC loss reduces hyaluronidase-like activity (TMEM2 homology — already in cascade) → HA accumulates → more LMW-HA fragments generated → amplified DAMP signaling. (Rojano-Alfonso C, López-Vicario C, Romero-Grimaldo B, Contreras BJ, Clària J, Titos E. Int J Mol Sci 26(20):10139, 2025. DOI: 10.3390/ijms262010139. PMC: PMC12562768.) Therapeutic target — 4-methylumbelliferone (4-MU, hymecromone): pharmacological inhibitor of hyaluronan synthase 2 (HAS2) → reduces HA synthesis → less LMW-HA DAMP generation → less NLRP3 activation. 4-MU prevented liver fibrosis in rodent models via reduced HA deposition and FSTL1 modulation. (Andreichenko IN et al. Int J Mol Sci 20(24):6301, 2019. DOI: 10.3390/ijms20246301. PMC: PMC6941058.) Approved as hymecromone in some European/Asian countries for biliary spasm — small clinical history. ⚠️ Not tested in ARPKD-CHF or any fibrocystic biliary model. The ARPKD-specific connection is inferred from: FPC→TMEM2→HA accumulation (existing cascade node) + LMW-HA→NLRP3 (Rojano-Alfonso 2025). Hypothesis only.

Newly identified ★

⭐ SPP1+ macrophages confirmed as conserved pro-fibrotic subset across lung, liver, kidney AND heart by single-cell omics 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Qiu W, Huang Y, Chen J et al. (Hangzhou Medical College / Zhejiang Provincial People's Hospital). Mol Biol Rep 53(1):1549, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s11033-026-12723-9. Tier 1 — Framework validation. Comprehensive single-cell omics review across lung, liver, kidney, and heart fibrosis. Central finding: SPP1-expressing macrophages are the conserved pro-fibrotic end-state across all four organs. Our framework has SPP1 as the central cascade endpoint (FPC loss → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → HSC activation → fibrosis) — this review confirms SPP1-driven fibrosis operates via macrophages independently of cholangiocyte secretion. Two cellular sources of SPP1 in ARPKD-CHF: FPC-deficient cholangiocytes AND recruited macrophages.

Key findings directly relevant to ARPKD-CHF:

  • SPP1+ TREM2+ macrophages — scar-associated, spatially restricted to fibrotic niches adjacent to myofibroblasts. SPP1 silencing specifically within TREM2+ cardiac macrophages suppresses fibrosis. In ARPKD-CHF: TREM2+ macrophage accumulation in periportal niches has NOT been characterized — confirmed open gap.
  • Spleen-liver macrophage axis — splenocyte-derived macrophages travel to the liver and exacerbate scarring independently of bone marrow recruitment. In ARPKD-CHF, splenomegaly is present in >80% of portal hypertension patients. The enlarged ARPKD-CHF spleen may produce pro-fibrotic SPP1+ macrophages that compound cholangiocyte-driven fibrosis — second spleen-liver mechanism (alongside TRNP1hi CD8+ T cells). Both mechanisms testable in Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse with splenomegaly.
  • CCL2/CCR2 recruitment axis — confirmed across all four fibrotic organ systems. Already in our cascade (Strazzabosco/Kaffe 2018 in ARPKD biliary cells) — seventh independent confirmation.
  • PKM2/glycolysis axis — macrophage metabolic reprogramming via PKM2 as transcriptional co-activator of HIF-1α. PFKFB3 inhibition reduces kidney fibrosis. New potential cascade node not previously in framework.

New open questions: (1) Are TREM2+ SPP1-expressing macrophages present in ARPKD-CHF periportal fibrotic niches? (2) Does the enlarged ARPKD-CHF spleen produce pro-fibrotic macrophages that compound cholangiocyte-driven fibrosis? Routing: Strazzabosco lab (Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse with splenomegaly — ideal model to test both simultaneously).

📌 NEW August 26, 2026 — ECM in ARPKD-CHF: Primary Defect is Loss, Not Gain

Source: Waddell SH, Brennan E, Boulter L. Dis. Model. Mech. 19(8):dmm052835, August 24, 2026. DOI: 10.1242/dmm.052835. PMID: 42634972. Cancer Research UK Scotland Centre, University of Edinburgh. First author Scott Waddell — permission granted September 4, 2026. Research focus: "understanding inflammation in fibrocystic liver disease." Senior author Luke Boulter (l.boulter@ed.ac.uk) — new contact.

Critical reframing for ARPKD-CHF therapy: In most fibrotic diseases, the goal is to reduce ECM deposition. In CHF/ARPKD, the primary ECM defect is the opposite — loss of the normal biliary basement membrane.

⭐ CELLULAR EXPLANATION CONFIRMED (Sep 10, 2026 — Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas, Brancale & Vilarinho, J Hepatol 2021, PMID 34016468): In healthy human liver, hepatic stellate cells and portal fibroblasts are the cells responsible for biliary BM maintenance — specifically producing Decorin (DCN, rank 2 most stellate-specific gene, 71.5% of cells) and Biglycan (BGN, rank 5, 91.6% of cells), the core BM proteoglycans around bile ducts. The same cells also express COL1A1 (fibrotic collagen, rank 4) and ACTA2 (myofibroblast activation, 92.7% of cells). In ARPKD-CHF, cholangiocyte-derived CXCL6 and CCL2 activate these stellate cells/portal fibroblasts pathologically → BM production (DCN/BGN) stops → fibrotic collagen production (COL1A1/COL3A1) amplifies. BM loss and fibrosis therefore occur simultaneously as two consequences of the same stellate cell dysfunction — exactly as Waddell and Boulter describe. Critical distinction: NGFR (portal fibroblast marker, rank 43) is present in the stellate cell cluster, confirming ARPKD-CHF fibrosis is driven by portal fibroblasts (NGFR+), not hepatic stellate cells. Anti-fibrotic drugs developed for NAFLD/NASH target hepatic stellate cells and may not be optimally targeted for ARPKD-CHF portal fibrosis.

  • What is lost: Laminin and type IV collagen — normally present in the BEC basement membrane — are absent in CHF and Caroli disease (confirmed in human patients and PCK rat)
  • How: Pkhd1-deficient BECs generate plasmin (via tissue plasminogen activator) → degrades laminin and collagen IV + fibronectin + CTGF
  • The consequence: Loss of normal basement membrane → pro-fibrotic niche → the periportal fibrosis develops as a secondary response to this structural void
  • Therapeutic implication: "Restoring basement membrane integrity, rather than simply suppressing fibrosis, may represent a more appropriate therapeutic objective in selected developmental cholangiopathies."
  • α2β1-integrin inhibition reduces cyst number; SMAD3 inhibition reduces fibronectin and cyst growth (Waddell SH et al. Sci Transl Med 15:eabq2491, 2023. DOI: 10.1126/scitranslmed.abq2491 — α2β1-integrin + SMAD3 inhibition in PLD biliary models)

Self-amplifying feedback circuits

These circuits explain why CHF worsens despite a static PKHD1 mutation. They are proposed mechanisms derived from published evidence — not yet validated in PKHD1-specific models.

Circuit 1 — CXCL10 → STAT3 positive feedback

Cholangiocyte injury → CXCL10 → Macrophages → IL-6 → STAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis → MORE injury ⟳

AMG-487 (CXCR3 research compound, preclinical) at the CXCL10/CXCR3 node breaks both the inflammatory cascade AND the STAT3/SPP1 cascade simultaneously. ⚠️ ACT-777991 (Idorsia) = T1D drug — not applicable to ARPKD — more potent than a single-node intervention. Evidence: Kaffe, Strazzabosco et al. 2018; Comm Biol 2026.

Circuit 2 — Spleen-liver amplification loop

Fibrosis → Portal hypertension → Splenomegaly → TRNP1hi CD8+ T cells → INSR-α → MORE HSC ⟳

TRNP1hi CD8+ T cell mechanism: Nature Genetics 2026 (MASH). Extension to ARPKD proposed by Nathan Johnson, July 2026. TDI01 reduces portal pressure → may break this loop.

MT1/melatonin receptor → Gi → cAMP suppression — ramelteon (FDA-approved) unstudied in ARPKD 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Goyal V, Tosini G. Front Endocrinol 17:1884802, 2026. Tier 3 — mechanistic inference. MT1 is a Gi-coupled receptor — the same signaling class as somatostatin/SSTR2, which is already Tier 2 in our framework. MT1 activation → Gi → cAMP suppression. In ARPKD, elevated cAMP (PKA → SRC → pSTAT3) is the primary cystogenesis driver. Ramelteon (FDA-approved MT1/MT2 agonist, oral, short half-life) suppresses cAMP via Gi — completely unstudied in ARPKD-CHF. Cluster 6 RNA-seq in this paper confirms cAMP signaling among the pathways that fail without MT1. Hypothesis only — no PKHD1-deficient cell data.

FPC / Fibrocystin — Molecular Function

⭐ Guay-Woodford FPC Review 2026 — Authoritative current picture of FPC molecular function 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Gulati A, Caldovic L, Guay-Woodford LM. Pediatr Nephrol 41:2785–2798, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x. Open access. Tier 1 — Authoritative review. Use as primary FPC reference.

Key new framework elements confirmed or added by this review:

  • NDFIP2/NEDD4/TGF-β — NEW cascade node: FPC-CTT co-localizes with NDFIP2 (HECT E3 ubiquitin ligase adaptor). FPC loss → SMURF1/SMURF2/NEDD4 mislocalized → (a) TGF-β signaling increased → fibrosis (direct FPC-to-fibrosis mechanism, parallel to macrophage cascade); (b) RhoA upregulation → cystogenesis; (c) ENaC retention → systemic hypertension. NDFIP2/NEDD4 pathway is a new druggable target.
  • ICD15 + PC1-CTT cooperative CDCA suppression — CONFIRMED: FPC-ICD15 and PC1-CTT act synergistically to suppress the cilia-dependent cyst activation (CDCA) signal. Loss of either FPC OR PC1 alone is sufficient for human cystogenesis — explaining why ARPKD is more severe than mouse models (which require both losses). Walker et al. ICD15 model validated by Guay-Woodford's authoritative review.
  • FPC-CTT/SRC/STAT3 suppression — CONFIRMED: Dafinger C, Liebau MC et al. (J Cell Mol Med 24:14633, 2020) — FPC-CTT directly suppresses SRC activation and SRC-activated STAT3 signaling. This confirms our core cascade: FPC loss → SRC constitutively active → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis. Liebau is a co-author of this key mechanistic paper — directly relevant to the Zoom call scheduled Sep 14/15.
  • Zone 3 (AA 2625-4074) → severe liver disease — CONFIRMED from 304 ARegPKD patients: Burgmaier/Liebau ARegPKD consortium — variants in AA 2625-4074 (including His3124Tyr at AA 3124 and Met3642Ile at AA 3642) associated with worse liver outcomes including portal hypertension, thrombocytopenia, varices, liver transplantation.
  • ARPKD prevalence — 2026 update: ~120 new ARPKD neonatal survivors per year in US. ~1,500 patients aged 0–29 years living with ARPKD in US. 21% perinatal mortality. c.107C>T (p.Thr36Met, exon 3) = 15-20% of all mutant alleles — only significant hotspot. His3124Tyr and Met3642Ile are non-hotspot variants.
  • Mouse models do not phenocopy human ARPKD kidney disease: Mouse ARPKD requires co-deletion of PKD1 or cystin to express kidney phenotype. PCK rat (not mouse) is the preferred model for biliary fibrosis preclinical work. Human ARPKD is true monogenic for both kidney and liver.
  • FPC-NTD hyaluronidase-like activity — OPEN QUESTION REMAINS: TMEM2 homology regions "suggest" hyaluronidase-like activity. Direct confirmation in FPC-deficient biliary tissue has NOT been published. Open question to Guay-Woodford remains valid — remove from Guay-Woodford follow-up only after her explicit answer.

Despite 20+ years of research, FPC molecular function remains incompletely understood. Here is what is now known — and what remains open.

PKHD1 / FIBROCYSTIN (FPC) — MOLECULAR FUNCTION: COMPLETE CURRENT PICTURE

Despite 20+ years of research, FPC molecular function remains incompletely understood. Here is what we now know — and what remains unknown.

Notch-like processing — the unifying framework: (Hogan MC et al. J Mol Med 92:1061-1072, 2014. DOI: 10.1007/s00109-014-1185-7) FPC undergoes regulated 3-step proteolytic cleavage: C1 (proprotein convertase, Golgi) → PECD/PTM heterodimer. C2 (ADAM metalloprotease, triggered by unknown extracellular ligand) → PECD shed into urine as soluble protein or EV-bound polycystin complex (FPC+PC1+PC2) — basis for Chung's uEV therapy. C3 (γ-secretase) → releases FPC-CTT with ciliary targeting (CTS), mitochondrial (MLS→ICD15), and nuclear (NLS) sequences. FPC loss blocks all six downstream functions simultaneously.

1. Ciliary sensing/signaling: FPC+PC1 synergistically suppress the cilia-dependent cyst activation (CDCA) signal. Extracellular ligand unknown after 20+ years.

2. SRC→STAT3 suppression (DIRECT mechanism for cascade node): (Dafinger C, Liebau MC et al. J Clin Invest 130:3756-3771, 2020. DOI: 10.1172/JCI130503) FPC-CTT forms dynamic protein complex with SRC kinase at TWO locations: (1) at the apical plasma membrane — FPCct sequesters Src preventing activation; (2) in the nucleus — FPCct suppresses SRC-mediated STAT3 transcription. FPC-CTT directly suppresses SRC → prevents STAT3 pY705. (Hassan F, Ziegler WH, Liebau MC et al. Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00086.2026 — quantitative confirmation in Pkhd1 KO kidney collecting duct cells (pl-MDCK — NOT biliary): 2-fold elevated cAMP, 3-fold higher luminal pressure; FPCct re-expression suppresses pSTAT3 by 81%/55% and pSrc by 60%/45%; FPCct more effective than PP2. ⚠️ Biliary ARPKD application is our extrapolation — not confirmed in cholangiocytes). FPC loss → SRC constitutively active → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis cascade. Confirmed in kidney epithelial cells; extrapolated to biliary cells based on same FPC biology in cholangiocytes. cAMP/PKA (elevated by FPC loss) further activates SRC → double amplification. JAK2 also in complex → JAK inhibitors (ruxolitinib, baricitinib) are candidate therapeutics.

3. ⭐ Ubiquitination via NDFIP2/SMURF/NEDD4: (Kaimori JY, Germino GG et al. Nat Commun 8:1520, 2017. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-017-01378-x; confirmed Guay-Woodford LM et al. Pediatr Nephrol 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x) FPC-CTT co-localizes with NDFIP2 (HECT E3 ligase adaptor). FPC loss → SMURF1/SMURF2/NEDD4 mislocalized → (a) SMURF dysfunction → TGF-β receptors/Smads not properly degraded → TGF-β pathway hyperactivated — DIRECT FPC-to-fibrosis mechanism, parallel to and independent of macrophage cascade; (b) NEDD4 → RhoA upregulation → cystogenesis; (c) NEDD4 → ENaC retention → sodium reabsorption → direct mechanism for ARPKD hypertension. Zone 3 variants (AA 2625-4074, including our daughter's c.9370C>T at AA 3124 and c.10926G>A at AA 3642) specifically disrupt CTT function → explaining why Zone 3 variants cause more severe liver disease via loss of SMURF-mediated TGF-β regulation in cholangiocytes. (Lapunzina-Soler P et al. Genes 17(2):229, 2026. DOI: 10.3390/genes17020229 — Zone 3 genotype-phenotype data)

4. Mitochondrial function via ICD15: (Walker RV, Qian F et al. Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4) ICD15 → mitochondria → maintains structural integrity and bioenergetics. FPC loss → mitochondrial defects confirmed by EM. GDF15 upregulated as stress response → brain-liver glucocorticoid anti-inflammatory (Wang D, Steinberg GR et al. Cell Metab 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cmet.2026.07.008). Connects to Nrf2/GPX4 anti-ferroptotic protection (spermidine third mechanism).

5. Nuclear signaling via FPC-CTT: Translocates via importin-β1 → activates MYC P1 promoter → MYC upregulated in human (not mouse) ARPKD kidneys. Species-specific (CYS1/PKHD1 expression timing differs). In cholangiocytes: not studied. MYC → cell proliferation → ductular expansion. (Guay-Woodford LM et al. Pediatr Nephrol 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x — FPC-CTT nuclear signaling review)

6. Biliary inflammation/fibrosis: (Locatelli L, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 65:822-838, 2016. DOI: 10.1002/hep.28776 ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026: Locatelli 2016 showed αvβ6 integrin reaches 70% of biliary structures at 12 months in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice with r=0.94 correlation with portal fibrosis — the strongest preclinical rationale for αvβ6 inhibition in ARPKD-CHF. Hirschfield GM et al. J Hepatol 84(1):86-98, Jan 2026 (DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.016) now published Phase 2 bexotegrast (αvβ6/αvβ1 inhibitor) results in PSC. Combined, these make a direct mechanistic argument for bexotegrast testing in ARPKD-CHF — more specific than for PSC. Strazzabosco (Yale) is the connector for both papers.; Tsunoda T et al. J Hepatol 71(1):143-152, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2019.02.024. PMID: 30898581) FPC-deficient Pkhd1del4/del4 cholangiocytes secrete chemokines → macrophage recruitment → pro-fibrogenic cascade. IL-8/CTGF confirmed in iPSC-derived FPC-deficient cholangiocytes (our IL-8/CTGF cascade node).

FXR gap definitively confirmed by Exhaustive literature search by

What remains unknown — and what we've found:

1. Extracellular ligand — RGD domain + αvβ6 hypothesis: FPC has an RGD domain (OMIM #606702 — PKHD1). RGD binds αv integrins. αvβ6 is the key candidate: expressed on cholangiocytes, primary TGF-β activator in biliary fibrosis. (Martínez-Salgado C et al. J Hepatol 78:160-173, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2022.07.025 — αvβ6 in biliary fibrosis) FPC's shed PECD (containing RGD) may normally occupy αvβ6 → blocking TGF-β activation. FPC loss → αvβ6 free → DUAL TGF-β hyperactivation (SMURF dysfunction + αvβ6 activation). PbH1 repeats suggest hyaluronan binding (TMEM2 hyaluronidase homology — Yuan L et al. Nature 650, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09884-1). Bannell/Cockburn (Leeds) working toward FPC structural characterization.

2. cAMP mechanism: FPC in cholangiocyte cilia works with PC2 to sense bile flow → Ca²⁺ signaling. (Masyuk TV, Huang BQ, Ward CJ et al. Gastroenterology 125(5):1303-1310, 2003. DOI: 10.1016/j.gastro.2003.09.001. PMID: 14598246; Masyuk AI, Masyuk TV, Splinter PL, Huang BQ, Stroope AJ, LaRusso NF. Gastroenterology 131(3):911-920, 2006. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2006.07.003. PMC: PMC1866168) FPC loss → malformed cilia → impaired bile flow sensing → reduced Ca²⁺ influx → calcium-inhibited AC5/AC6 relieved → cAMP accumulates → PKA → simultaneously: (a) SRC→STAT3→SPP1 (Liebau 2020) AND (b) β-catenin pSer675 → nuclear β-catenin (Spirli 2013). Elevated cAMP/PKA is the single upstream origin of both parallel fibrogenic pathways.

3. Cholangiocyte functions — partially answered: Confirmed in BECs: FPC in cilia (Masyuk TV 2003); malformed cilia/impaired Ca²⁺ (Masyuk AI 2006); IL-8/CTGF (Tsunoda 2019); chemokine→macrophage (Locatelli/Strazzabosco 2016); CFTR (Cebotaru 2021). NOT confirmed: SRC/STAT3, SMURF, ICD15, MYC, FXR. Freedman iPSC platform = first to test all simultaneously.

4. FXR connection — strongest circumstantial evidence yet still unconfirmed: No direct evidence. But: FPC loss → β-catenin activation (Kaffe E, Fiorotto R, Pellegrino F, Mariotti V, Amenduni M, Cadamuro M, Fabris L, Strazzabosco M, Spirli C. Hepatology 67(5):1903-1919, 2018. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652. PMID: 29140564) MATCHES FXR ablation in BECs → β-catenin activation. FXR reduced in biliary atresia (Front Immunol 13:821968, 2022. DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2022.821968). Mechanistic inference: FPC loss → impaired ciliary Ca²⁺ → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → constitutive YAP → β-catenin. Single most important experiment: measure FXR in Pkhd1del4/del4 BECs (Strazzabosco) or PKHD1-KO iPSC BECs (Freedman).

5. 3D structure — AlphaFold prediction exists, no experimental structure: AlphaFold prediction available at alphafold.ebi.ac.uk (PKHD1, UniProt Q8TCY7). No cryo-EM or X-ray data. Predicted: IPT domains form plexin-like propeller; RGD in PbH1 region. Bannell/Cockburn (Leeds) most likely to attempt experimental structural work.

PCK rat liver RNA-seq (1,298 DEGs, PND15/20/30/90) — ARPKD-specific confirmation of SPP1/STAT3/CTGF/PDGFB cascade nodes + HNF4α loss + CELSR1/PCP + therapeutic window PND20-30. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Cascade nodes confirmed in ARPKD-specific data (Tier upgrade: adjacent-disease → ARPKD-confirmed):

• SPP1 (osteopontin) — upregulated at all time points. Our primary cascade node after FXR-YAP now has ARPKD-specific transcriptomic validation. CD44 co-upregulated. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025; PMID: 42265280 — SPP1 cholangiocyte-specific role confirmed)

• STAT3 — primary upstream regulator, PND30/90. Drives SPP1 transcription in our cascade. STAT1, STAT4, STAT5B also activated.

• CTGF + PDGFB — upregulated at ALL time points. Confirms PDGFR-β has ARPKD-specific transcriptomic support. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025; Guan Y, Peltz G et al. JCI Insight 2024. DOI: 10.1172/jci.insight.185547 — PDGFR-β validation in ARPKD organoids)

• SMAD7 INHIBITED (PND30/90) → enhanced TGF-β pathway confirmed. SMAD7 activation = therapeutic target. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• HNF4α DOWNREGULATED (PND30/90) — first ARPKD-specific confirmation of impaired hepatocyte differentiation. Double failure: HNF4G transdifferentiation blocked (LSEC/laminin — Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021) AND HNF4α hepatocyte identity impaired (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025).

⭐ THERAPEUTIC CONNECTION — MasterSwitch Bio HNF4α mRNA LNP (Sep 22, 2026): University of Pittsburgh + UPenn spinout co-founded by Drew Weissman (2023 Nobel laureate, N1-methylpseudouridine mRNA modification). Received $1.19M NIDDK SBIR Phase II grant (NIH 11408899) to advance HNF4α mRNA LNP toward IND for decompensated cirrhosis and end-stage liver disease.

Primary published paper (updated Sep 22, 2026 — search confirmed): Yang T, Poenisch M, Khanal R et al., Ott M, Sharma AD. "Therapeutic HNF4A mRNA attenuates liver fibrosis in a preclinical model." J Hepatol 75(6):1420-1433, 2021. PMID: 34453962. ⚠️ This is Hannover Medical School group; MasterSwitch (Pitt+Penn/Weissman) builds on this foundation with their modified mRNA (N1-methylpseudouridine) and Pitt/Penn LNP platform.

Confirmed from primary paper: (1) Human patient data: stage-dependent reduction of HNF4α expression confirmed in multiple fibrosis cohorts. (2) Four independent mouse models: CCl4-fibrosis, DDC cholestasis, Mdr2-/-, CCl4-cirrhosis. DDC model is biliary cholestasis producing periportal fibrosis — closest published model to ARPKD-CHF of the four tested. HNF4α mRNA LNP reduced fibrosis in that cholestatic context. (3) Reduced transaminases, bilirubin, hepatic collagen; decreased Col1a2, Col2a1, Acta2. (4) Paracrine mechanism — NOT direct cholangiocyte effect: HNF4α is mainly expressed in hepatocytes (not other hepatic cell types). Paraoxonase 1 (PON1) is a direct HNF4α target that contributes to fibrosis attenuation via macrophage M2 polarization and HSC suppression. Exposure of HSCs to HNF4α reduced desmin, αSMA, LOX. This is the Gouon-Evans paracrine paradigm: hepatocyte LNP → hepatocyte HNF4α → PON1 → M2 macrophage → HSC anti-fibrotic → periportal fibrosis reduction without requiring cholangiocyte targeting.

HNF4α confirmed downregulated in ARPKD PCK rat liver PND30/90 (Khare/Pritchard above) — MasterSwitch's therapeutic target is a documented ARPKD deficit. ⚠️ HYPOTHESIS ONLY — not confirmed by any published work: Biliary analog = HNF1β mRNA LNP to cholangiocytes to restore biliary identity in PKHD1-deficient cells (HNF1β is the validated cholangiocyte-specific promoter per Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026). Routing: MasterSwitch Bio (potential contact — rare liver disease, NIDDK-funded); Pritchard/Apte (KUMC — PCK rat HNF4α data, overdue email); Morizane (PKHD1-deficient organoids — HNF1β hypothesis testable); Gouon-Evans (paracrine LNP platform — direct mechanistic connection to PON1/macrophage finding).

• CELSR1 (Wnt/PCP gene) UPREGULATED — directly confirms non-canonical Wnt/PCP dysregulation in ARPKD-CHF liver (validates DPM Mechanism 2). (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• CXCL16 — upregulated; extends macrophage recruitment cascade via CXCR6 axis. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• Novel target: MTPN (myotrophin) — activated PND15-90; metabolic/insulin connection; miR-375 suppresses it. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Critical therapeutic window: PND20-30 in rats (extended postnatal period in humans — up to ~2 years). (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025 — PCK rat transcriptomics PND15-90) Significant transcriptomic divergence begins at PND30. Maps directly onto neonatal CFTR correction window (Cebotaru) and Wnt5a/PCP intervention window. Network: Pritchard MT (mpritchard@kumc.edu), Apte U — KUMC. Not yet contacted.

⛔ mTOR INHIBITORS CONTRAINDICATED IN ARPKD-CHF — PCK rat sirolimus worsens bile acids + bilirubin. (Renken C, Fischer DC, Kundt G, Gretz N, Haffner D. "Inhibition of mTOR with sirolimus does not attenuate progression of liver and kidney disease in PCK rats." PMID: 20615907) + ⭐ NEW LEAD: Somatostatin/octreotide suppresses cAMP via SSTR-Gi — dual benefit: portal hypertension + STAT3/SPP1 cascade.

⭐ Stattic — p-STAT3 inhibitor validated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes (Sep 11, 2026 — Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026): Stattic is a small molecule that inhibits STAT3 phosphorylation at Tyr705. In si-PKHD1-transfected HIBEpiC human biliary epithelial cells, Stattic at 5 μM significantly reduces p-STAT3 without affecting total STAT3, suppresses SPP1 mRNA and OPN protein expression, and attenuates the SPP1 upregulation caused by PKHD1 knockdown. This is the first direct in vitro validation of p-STAT3 inhibition as a therapeutic approach in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Stattic is a research compound (not FDA-approved) but validates p-STAT3 as a tractable drug target. FDA-approved agents that suppress STAT3 phosphorylation or JAK/STAT signaling upstream: ruxolitinib (JAK1/2 inhibitor, FDA-approved for myelofibrosis/GVHD), baricitinib (JAK1/2, FDA-approved), napabucasin (cancer STAT3 inhibitor). All are candidates for ARPKD-CHF p-STAT3/SPP1 axis suppression.

⛔ mTOR inhibitors — PCK rat evidence: Sirolimus completely failed to improve hepatic fibrosis or cyst area in PCK rats AND significantly elevated bile acids + bilirubin in chronically treated male PCK rats — active worsening of cholestasis. (⚠️ CITATION CORRECTED Sep 16, 2026 — the evidence is Renken C, Fischer DC, Kundt G, Gretz N, Haffner D. "Inhibition of mTOR with sirolimus does not attenuate progression of liver and kidney disease in PCK rats." PMID: 20615907, NOT Masyuk/Hepatology 2022 (an autophagy paper). Verified findings: male PCK rats on sirolimus had significantly increased bile acids and bilirubin at 12 weeks (each P<0.05); neither fibrosis nor cyst area improved; 5 of 43 sirolimus-treated PCK rats died during the study versus none of the controls. ✅ This contraindication is CONFIRMED — and the mortality signal strengthens it beyond what our entry stated.) Mechanism: mTOR regulates bile acid transporter expression; inhibition → reduced BSEP/MRP2 → intracellular bile acid accumulation → cholestatic injury additive to DPM. In ARPKD-CHF with already-compromised ferroptosis risk (bile acid accumulation → GPX4 depletion), mTOR inhibition is doubly dangerous. Clinical: 40% discontinuation rate in polycystic disease trials; exacerbates portal hypertension; accelerates CKD. NOT recommended for ARPKD with liver involvement. Q6 ANSWERED.

YAP-mTOR crosstalk (new node): Constitutive YAP → stimulates mTORC1 → drives ductular proliferation. mTOR inhibition partially blocks this but causes bile acid/liver damage as described. Upstream YAP suppression via linafexor (pulsatile FXR → restores normal YAP cycling) is safer than downstream mTOR blockade.

⭐ NEW THERAPEUTIC LEAD: Somatostatin/Octreotide (cAMP suppression via SSTR-Gi): SSTR2/SSTR5 expressed on cholangiocytes. (Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 57:2328-2337, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290) Octreotide/lanreotide → SSTR → Gi → inhibits AC5/AC6 → cAMP reduced in cholangiocytes → PKA suppressed → less SRC activation → less pSTAT3 → less SPP1 → fibrosis cascade slowed. This directly targets the upstream mechanism (elevated cAMP from impaired ciliary Ca²⁺ sensing). ⚠️ The FXR→SOCS3→STAT3 brake component of this cascade is confirmed in hepatocytes — extrapolation to biliary cholangiocytes. DUAL BENEFIT for ARPKD-CHF: (1) Already standard therapy for portal hypertension (reduces splanchnic blood flow); (2) May suppress cAMP-driven STAT3/SPP1 cascade via cholangiocyte SSTR. Most immediately clinically accessible agent for ARPKD-CHF patients with portal hypertension — addresses both the clinical complication and potentially the root molecular mechanism simultaneously.
