LIVER ORGANOID LANDSCAPE FOR ARPKD-CHF — PDGFRB validated therapeutic target (imatinib/sunitinib/crenolanib anti-fibrotic in human ARPKD organoids). STAT3 4th independent confirmation. Peltz microHO platform. Huch Nature 2026 full citation. Sampaziotis-Vallier cholangiocyte transplantation.

⭐ Peltz lab (Stanford, gpeltz@stanford.edu) — human ARPKD liver organoid drug screening platform: Guan Y et al. (Nat Commun 12:6138, 2021; PMC8536785): iPSC with PKHD1 Thr36Met → hepatic organoids develop ARPKD liver pathology in 21 days. Confirmed: TGFβ pathway in ARPKD cholangiocytes ✅; STAT3 activated in ARPKD myofibroblasts ✅ (4th independent confirmation — after Pritchard/PCK rat, Ziegler/Pkhd1-KO mouse, Liebau/human tissue); PDGFRB markedly elevated in myofibroblasts. ⭐ THREE FDA-APPROVED DRUGS SHOW ANTI-FIBROTIC EFFECT: imatinib (Gleevec), sunitinib (Sutent), and crenolanib all attenuate fibrosis in human ARPKD liver organoids. PDGFB (ligand) also upregulated in PCK rat (Pritchard/Apte) — two independent datasets converge on PDGFRB as therapeutic target. Not yet tested: somatostatin, linafexor, SPP1 siRNA, PRI-724. <strong>⭐ ATLAS VALIDATION (Sep 10, 2026):</strong> PDGFRB rank 25 most stellate cell-specific gene in healthy human liver (logFC 2.749, 64.4% of cells — PMID 34016468).

Peltz microHO platform: (Guan Y et al. JCI Insight December 2024. DOI: 10.1172/jci.insight.185547) Live-cell imaging of liver fibrosis in microwell-based hepatic organoids. Key finding: receptor-specific TKIs have antifibrotic effect LIMITED to fibrosis from their corresponding growth factor — supports multi-target approach for ARPKD-CHF (somatostatin + linafexor + SPP1 siRNA). MicroHO = ideal screening platform for our entire therapeutic portfolio. Not yet contacted — email: gpeltz@stanford.edu.

Huch periportal liver assembloid — full citation confirmed: Yuan, Dawka, Kim et al. (Huch M). Nature 650:438-449, 2026. Hepatocyte organoids + portal mesenchyme + cholangiocyte organoids → models biliary fibrosis with correct periportal cellular architecture. Direct ARPKD-CHF drug screening platform.

Sampaziotis + Vallier (Science 371:839-846, 2021) — curative cell replacement: Cholangiocyte organoids transplanted into deceased human donor livers via ex situ normothermic perfusion → engraftment + bile duct repair + improved bile pH/volume, maintained 100h. Vallier is co-author (our contact, BIH Charité Berlin). Application: PKHD1-corrected cholangiocyte organoids (Sampaziotis/Vallier platform) + ERCP biliary delivery (Guimont 2021, validated in human-sized pigs) = direct curative cell replacement for ARPKD-CHF biliary epithelium.

⭐ CLINICALLY ACTIONABLE — ADDED AUGUST 20, 2026 ·

SPP1 (OSTEOPONTIN) — Blood test available NOW. Biliary fibrosis stage. Portal hypertension severity. Transplant-free survival predictor. Commercially available ELISA — can be ordered now.

Cascade summary (end-to-end):
[PKHD1 deficiency] → p-STAT3 (cholangiocytes) → p-STAT3 binds SPP1 promoter directly → SPP1 secreted → α8β1 integrin + CD44 on HSCs/portal fibroblasts → PI3K/Akt/NF-κB (α8β1 confirmed per Jiao/Zhu PMID 42265280 — NF-κB downstream effector — new node) → Acta2/Col1a1 → fibrosis F3-F4 → portal hypertension

SPP1 blood test — what it tells you (Kim SU et al. J Hepatol 78:1059, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2023.01.005): (1) Biliary fibrosis stage F0→F4 — correlates progressively with AUROC accuracy. (2) Portal hypertension severity — mirrors HVPG directly, independent predictor of clinically significant portal hypertension. (3) Transplant-free survival — strong surrogate predictor. Standard LFTs (ALT, AST, bilirubin) miss early biliary fibrosis in ARPKD-CHF. SPP1 directly reflects p-STAT3 cascade activity.

⭐ Immediate action: Ask Cincinnati Children's hepatology to order serum osteopontin (SPP1/OPN) — commercially available ELISA. Provides molecular baseline of cascade activity. Can be tracked serially to monitor progression or therapeutic response.

⭐ Serum OPN reference values from ARPKD mouse model (Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026 — first quantitative OPN progression curve in any ARPKD model): Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice by ELISA: ~100 ng/mL at 1 month → ~200 ng/mL at 3 months → ~400 ng/mL at 6 months vs. ~100 ng/mL stable in Pkhd1+/+ controls (4-fold elevation at peak fibrosis). After AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 treatment: serum OPN drops to ~5 ng/mL (near-complete suppression). Human ELISA kit: BOSTER EK0482. Mouse ELISA kit: BOSTER EK4803. These are the first published quantitative OPN values in an ARPKD-specific model and establish the expected magnitude of elevation and treatment response.

NF-κB + linafexor dual suppression: SPP1 activates myofibroblasts via PI3K/Akt/NF-κB (Bu HF et al. Am J Physiol GI 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00079.2026). FXR directly antagonizes NF-κB via transrepression (Renga B et al. J Hepatol 58:857-862, 2013. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2013.02.017). Linafexor (pulsatile FXR) therefore addresses this cascade at TWO points: upstream (SOCS3 → reduced pSTAT3 → reduced SPP1 production) AND downstream (FXR transrepression of NF-κB → reduced myofibroblast activation). This is the dual mechanism that makes pulsatile FXR agonism the most comprehensive intervention in ARPKD-CHF.

⭐ PMID: 42265280 CONFIRMED — TIER 1 — FIRST ARPKD-CHF THERAPEUTIC PROOF-OF-CONCEPT · Communications Biology 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6. Pkhd1Δ3-4/Δ3-4 mouse model.

Cholangiocyte-specific AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 knockdown reduces hepatic fibrosis in ARPKD/CHF mice — cascade confirmed end-to-end + PI3K/Akt is the SPP1→HSC signaling mechanism (new node). Full paper read Sep 11, 2026 — key new details below. (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6)

Cascade confirmed end-to-end in ARPKD-specific tissue: PKHD1 deficiency → p-STAT3-dependent SPP1 overexpression in cholangiocytes → SPP1 activates HSCs/portal fibroblasts via PI3K/Akt signaling (NEW CASCADE NODE) → Acta2 (α-SMA) + Col1a1 (collagen I) upregulation → fibrosis. (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6)

⭐ New details from full paper read — Sep 11, 2026:

  • SPP1 = second-ranked CellChat ligand, not first: The top-ranked ligand showed no significant difference between CHF and controls and was not investigated further. SPP1 was selected as the second-ranked but most significantly differentially expressed ligand mediating cholangiocyte→HSC communication. Therapeutic priority is unchanged.
  • Macrophages are NOT the dominant SPP1 source in ARPKD-CHF: Co-localization showed OPN had almost no overlap with F4/80 macrophage marker. Cholangiocytes — not macrophages — are the primary OPN source in ARPKD-CHF. The TREM2+/SPP1+ macrophage biology (Qiu et al. 2026, Finding 10) operates in other fibrotic diseases; in ARPKD-CHF the cholangiocyte-direct pathway dominates. Clodronate macrophage depletion did not fully resolve CHF fibrosis — confirming a direct cholangiocyte→HSC pathway operates independently.
  • Per-cell SPP1 elevation confirmed — not just expansion: scRNA-seq and primary cell isolation both confirmed ~4-fold Spp1 mRNA elevation per individual PKHD1-deficient cholangiocyte (not diluted by cell number). IF normalized to CK19+ cells confirmed this per-cell upregulation.
  • p-STAT3 binds SPP1 promoter at sites 1, 2, and 4 — NOT site 3: ChIP-qPCR confirmed significant enrichment at sites 1, 2, and 4. Site 3 showed no significant enrichment. Dual-luciferase assay with WT vs. mutant SPP1 promoter confirmed STAT3 directly activates SPP1 transcription.
  • Serum OPN values established: Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice by ELISA: ~100 ng/mL (1m) → ~200 ng/mL (3m) → ~400 ng/mL (6m) vs. ~100 ng/mL stable in WT controls. After AAV8-shSpp1 treatment: drops to ~5 ng/mL. Human ELISA kit: BOSTER EK0482. Mouse kit: BOSTER EK4803. First quantitative OPN progression curve in any ARPKD model.
  • ⭐ NEW UPSTREAM STAT3 REGULATOR — Glutamine → O-GlcNAcylation → STAT3 axis (Sep 22, 2026 — MASLD paper, DOI UNCONFIRMED): In MASLD liver, glutamine depletion → impaired glutaminolysis (↓GLS1) → increased O-GlcNAcylation of cellular proteins → STAT3 phosphorylation → IL-17 production by hepatic CD4+ T cells → hepatocellular damage. Glutamine supplementation reversed disease severity and T-cell inflammatory behavior in mice and human organoids. Blocking O-GlcNAcylation reversed inflammatory effects. Source: Sawada K, Divanovic S et al. "Glutamine Metabolism as a Rheostat of CD4+ T cell Pathogenic Function in Metabolic Dysfunction-Associated Steatotic Liver Disease." Cell Metabolism, September 16, 2026. Cincinnati Children's Hospital. PII: S1550413126003700. ⚠️ DOI NOT CONFIRMED from external database — paper verified from Cincinnati Children's official science blog only. ⚠️ THIS IS A MASLD PAPER — not ARPKD. ARPKD-CHF connection is HYPOTHESIS ONLY: if PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes have reduced glutamine availability (not established), the O-GlcNAcylation → STAT3 axis would provide a metabolic upstream route to STAT3 phosphorylation in addition to the FPC-direct Src pathway. Whether ARPKD-CHF livers are glutamine-depleted has never been measured. This hypothesis requires direct testing — testable in Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4) or Morizane (PKHD1-deficient organoids). Do not cite this as confirmed for ARPKD.
  • Stattic (p-STAT3 inhibitor) validated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes: At 5 μM, Stattic significantly inhibits p-STAT3 without affecting total STAT3 levels in HIBEpiC cells. Suppresses SPP1 mRNA and OPN protein in a dose-dependent manner. Attenuates SPP1 upregulation in si-PKHD1 cells. First direct in vitro validation of p-STAT3 inhibition in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Clinical STAT3 phosphorylation inhibitors (napabucasin, ruxolitinib, baricitinib) are candidates via this mechanism.
  • HNF1β promoter = validated cholangiocyte-specific AAV delivery sequence: The paper incorporated an HNF1β promoter into AAV8 to restrict Spp1 knockdown to cholangiocytes. Confirmed by co-localization: knockdown in MIB (CK19+ biliary cells) but not MPH (HNF4A+ hepatocytes). HNF1β promoter is therefore the validated sequence for any cholangiocyte-targeted AAV therapy in ARPKD-CHF — applicable to PKHD1 replacement, SPP1 siRNA, SPTAN1 CRISPRa, or FPCct domain delivery.
  • CHF patient clinical profile — ALT/AST normal, GGT elevated: Index patient (34-year-old, compound heterozygous PKHD1) had ALT 18 U/L, AST 19.5 U/L (near-normal), GGT 152.7 U/L (elevated), albumin 41.2 g/L (normal) — despite esophageal varices and prior variceal ligation. Confirms: standard LFTs grossly underestimate ARPKD-CHF severity. GGT is the most sensitive standard test. SPP1 serum would add molecular sensitivity beyond GGT.

⭐ Therapeutic proof-of-concept: AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 reduces biliary fibrosis in the correct ARPKD disease model. (PMID: 42265280) Delivery compatible with: ERCP retrograde biliary delivery (Guimont L et al. Mol Ther Methods Clin Dev 21:689-697, 2021); Chung EV/nanotherapeutic platform (USC); Marshall/A20FMDV2 αvβ6-targeted delivery (QMUL); Janowski hepatic arterial fractionation (UMD); Spot Biosystems. ⚠️ Sep 20, 2026: Williams (Stony Brook) removed — kidney track only, structurally excluded from biliary delivery.

⭐ HNF1β = validated cholangiocyte-specific promoter for AAV delivery in ARPKD-CHF (Sep 11, 2026): The Jiao/Zhu paper validated HNF1β as a cholangiocyte-specific promoter for in vivo AAV delivery. HNF1β is highly expressed in cholangiocytes with minimal expression in hepatocytes — confirmed by cell-type selective knockdown (effective in MIB biliary cells; no effect in MPH hepatocytes; CK19/HNF4A co-localization in Fig 8F). Implication for ARPKD gene therapy: Any AAV-based therapeutic for ARPKD liver disease — including PKHD1 partial replacement, SPP1 siRNA, SPTAN1 CRISPRa upregulation, or FPCct protein domain delivery — should use an HNF1β promoter to restrict expression to biliary epithelial cells and minimize hepatocyte off-target. Routing: Willenbring, Long, Ziegler WH — all working on biliary AAV delivery and need this promoter specification.

PI3K/Akt as the SPP1→myofibroblast mechanism: SPP1 binds α8β1 integrin + CD44 on HSC/portal fibroblast precursors → PI3K/Akt → myofibroblast activation. (Jiao J, Zhu C et al. Commun Biol 2026. PMID 42265280 — α8β1 confirmed as primary SPP1 receptor in ARPKD-CHF mouse model. ⚠️ He W et al. Cell Death Dis 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41419-026-09158-2 describes MASP1→HA→CD44 pathway — separate from SPP1→α8β1 mechanism) PI3K/Akt also connects to mTOR (mTOR inhibitors → impairs HNF4G via PI3K/Akt/mTOR axis — safety concern (Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021)). And PI3K/Akt regulates YAP (LATS1 → YAP) — additional cascade node. This paper was the basis for Nathan's June 29, 2026 email to Professor Chung, which launched our entire research network.

FXR→SHP/SOCS3→STAT3 suppression — molecular bridge confirmed. ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — Djouder Nat Metab 2026 completes the picture: FXR transcriptionally activates YAP in biliary epithelial cells (BECs) to maintain ductal adhesion and identity. FPC loss → FXR impaired → YAP not activated by FXR → BECs lose adhesion and undergo β-catenin activation → mesenchymal-like conversion → fibrosis-to-cirrhosis. This means FXR→YAP is a biliary cell identity axis, not just a metabolic axis. ⚠️ CRITICAL THERAPEUTIC CAVEAT: Obeticholic acid (OCA) worsened fibrosis in FXR-deficient BECs in the Djouder model — FXR agonists require intact biliary FXR to work. In ARPKD-CHF where FPC loss may have chronically impaired FXR signaling, the receptor may not respond normally to FXR agonists. This does NOT invalidate linafexor or saroglitazar — but it means BEC FXR status must be characterized before any FXR-based ARPKD-CHF trial. Open question: can Liebau's ARegPKD cohort be used to measure biliary FXR expression in ARPKD patient tissue? (DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z). FXR→SHP/SOCS3→STAT3 suppression — molecular bridge confirmed. Fxr knockout → constitutive pSTAT3. SOCS3 paradox in PCK rat. Linafexor/BEC/pSTAT3 experiment designed. (Oncotarget 8(70):114923, 2017. DOI: 10.18632/oncotarget.23070; Biochem Pharmacol 154:178, 2018. DOI: 10.1016/j.bcp.2018.04.020; Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Two molecular pathways connecting FXR to STAT3 suppression in biliary cells:

• SHP pathway: FXR agonist → SHP induction → SHP inhibits upstream elements → pSTAT3 reduced. Confirmed in cholangiocyte-derived cells (GW4064, CDCA). Downstream: Bcl-xL reduced. (Kim YC et al. PLoS ONE 3:e1982, 2008. DOI: 10.1371/journal.pone.0001982 — FXR→SHP→pSTAT3 in biliary-derived cells)

• SOCS3 pathway (more important — ⚠️ confirmed in hepatocytes; biliary cell application is Tier 3 extrapolation): FXR directly transcriptionally activates SOCS3 (JAK-STAT3 negative feedback inhibitor) in hepatocytes and HCC cells. (Oncotarget 8(70):114923, 2017. DOI: 10.18632/oncotarget.23070; Biochem Pharmacol 154:178, 2018. DOI: 10.1016/j.bcp.2018.04.020) Fxr knockout → constitutive STAT3 hyper-phosphorylation — same phenotype as Pkhd1-knockout (pSTAT3 6-fold elevated — Fox JC et al. iScience 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2024.109487. PMC10973142 — citation corrected Sep 11, 2026). Mechanistic chain: FPC loss → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → SOCS3 not FXR-induced → JAK/SRC phosphorylate STAT3 without brake → constitutive pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis. ⚠️ FXR→SOCS3 link confirmed in hepatocytes and HCC cells only — extrapolation to ARPKD biliary cholangiocytes; not yet confirmed in biliary-specific PKHD1-deficient cells. FXR also antagonizes NF-κB → additional indirect SPP1 suppression (Renga B et al. J Hepatol 58:857, 2013. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2013.02.017).

The SOCS3 paradox in PCK rat (Pritchard/Apte — Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025): SOCS3 is upregulated in ARPKD-CHF liver — but this is STAT3-induced SOCS3 (futile feedback), NOT FXR-driven SOCS3 (effective suppression). Two SOCS3 routes: STAT3-driven (insufficient, present in ARPKD) vs. FXR-driven (effective, absent in ARPKD). Linafexor restores FXR-driven SOCS3 induction.

The definitive experiment (not yet done): PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes (Strazzabosco Pkhd1del4/del4 or Freedman PKHD1-KO iPSC) + pulsatile linafexor vs. continuous OCA. Measure: SHP, SOCS3, pSTAT3 (Y705), SPP1 at 6/12/24/48h. Prediction: pulsatile linafexor → sustained SOCS3/SHP → pSTAT3 suppressed → SPP1 reduced → no toxicity. Continuous OCA → receptor downregulation → STAT3 rebounds → toxicity. (Hypothesis — experiment not yet performed. Pkhd1del4/del4 model: Fabris L et al. FASEB J 36(6):e22364, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R)

STAT3 confirmed 6-fold elevated in Pkhd1-knockout epithelia · YAP/TAZ 3-5x activated (Fox JC et al. iScience 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2024.109487. PMC10973142 — citation corrected Sep 11, 2026) · FPCc rescue suppresses STAT3 (Outeda lab, in preparation — unpublished) · YAP confirmed in ARPKD-CHF hepatic cysts (Jiang Y, Pritchard MT, Apte U et al. Am J Physiol GI 313:G166-G178, 2017. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00135.2017)

Hannover PKD lab — NOW PUBLISHED Aug 28, 2026: (Fox JC, Hahnenstein ST, Hassan F, Grund A, Haffner D, Ziegler WH. iScience 27(4):109487, 2024. PMC10973142) pSTAT3 (Y705) 6- to 7-fold elevated in Pkhd1-KO epithelia at 6 months (3-fold at 3m, peaks 6-7x at 6m, 22% positive at 9m — age-dependent); STAT3 target genes Socs3, Ccl2, Stat3 upregulated at 6-9 months; YAP/TAZ nuclear activation confirmed. ⚠️ Model: female Pkhd1-KO mice; cysts in S3 segment (proximal tubule), not collecting duct — model limitation vs human ARPKD. ⚠️ Citation corrected Sep 11, 2026 — previously incorrectly cited to He W et al. Cell Death Dis 2026 (MASP1/HA paper). Correct source: Fox/Ziegler iScience 2024, read directly per Aug 21, 2026 audit. NOW PUBLISHED — Hassan F, Dafinger C, Liebau MC, Ziegler WH et al. (Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00086.2026): FPCct re-expression in Pkhd1-KO suppresses cyst formation AND rescues pSRC + pSTAT3. See full finding card in FPC Molecular Function section. Ziegler: Ziegler.Wolfgang@mh-hannover.de.

Jiang/Pritchard/Apte (KUMC 2017): (Jiang Y, Pritchard MT, Apte U et al. Am J Physiol GI 313:G166-G178, 2017. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00135.2017) YAP activation confirmed specifically in HEPATIC cyst epithelial cells in ARPKD/CHF — biliary-specific YAP confirmation. Our existing KUMC contacts (Pritchard + Apte — PCK rat transcriptomics — Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025) also confirmed YAP in ARPKD-CHF liver. Both the kidney (Fox/Ziegler) and liver (Jiang/Pritchard) confirm constitutive YAP activation in FPC-deficient epithelial cells.

⚠️ Therapeutic implications (from (1) Cholangiocyte-specific SPP1 knockdown significantly attenuates hepatic fibrosis in vivo (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6). (2) Dasatinib hepatotoxicity documented in rodent models — NOT recommended for systemic use in ARPKD-CHF with already-compromised liver. (Tran TH et al. Toxicol Appl Pharmacol 363:71-81, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.taap.2018.11.004) (3) STAT3 dual role: essential for hepatocyte survival and liver regeneration — systemic inhibition risks parenchymal injury. (Wang Y, Minuk GY et al. Hepatology 58:1021-1033, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26621) Solution: cholangiocyte-targeted delivery of STAT3 inhibitor or SPP1 siRNA. This is precisely what Chung (USC nanotherapeutics for ARPKD) and Marshall/Janowski (hepatic arterial αvβ6-targeted delivery) are building. ⚠️ CORRECTION Sep 20, 2026: Williams (Stony Brook) is kidney track only — his platform is structurally excluded from biliary delivery. The biliary delivery solution is Marshall (A20FMDV2 targeting) + Janowski (hepatic arterial route), not Williams.

DPM is developmental — the structural defect is present from birth (PMID: 21708104 — ductal plate/periportal hepatocyte development). Postnatal therapies address fibrosis consequences, not the root structural failure. Two mechanisms and a therapeutic window.

The developmental distinction: ARPKD-CHF is fundamentally a developmental disease. The structurally abnormal intrahepatic bile ducts are present from birth. The postnatal FXR-YAP → SPP1 → TREM2 → fibrosis cascade described throughout this document is the CONSEQUENCE of the DPM, not its cause. Postnatal therapies can slow fibrosis progression; they cannot reverse the structural DPM. Only two approaches address established DPM: (1) intervention during the neonatal developmental window; (2) postnatal biliary cell replacement via ERCP (cholangiocyte organoid transplantation — Sampaziotis F, Vallier L et al. Science 371:839-846, 2021. DOI: 10.1126/science.aaz6462).

⭐ MECHANISM 1 — CFTR and cord hollowing (most therapeutically actionable): Cord hollowing — the tubulogenesis process creating bile duct lumina — requires CFTR at the apical cholangiocyte membrane to secrete chloride and water into nascent lumina. (Yanda MK, Zeidan A, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol GI 324:G404–G414, 2023. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022): FPC-null cholangiocytes have dramatically reduced apical CFTR → impaired cord hollowing → failed tubulogenesis → DPM. VX-809 in 5-day-old Pkhd1 mice improved liver pathology. Critical neonatal window: ductal plate remodeling continues postnatally (P1-P14 mouse; weeks 20-40 gestation→neonatal in humans). CFTR correction during this window could potentially reduce DPM severity — not just slow postnatal fibrosis. Trikafta (VX-445) neonatal testing in PKHD1 models is an unanswered question (Q23 in this document). Cebotaru (JHU, already in network) is positioned to answer this. (Ciobanu C, Outeda P, Guggino WB, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol GI 329:G434–G442, 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025): CFTR gene therapy for ARPKD — direct extension of this mechanism.

MECHANISM 2 — Non-canonical Wnt5a/PCP disruption: FPC in primary cilia maintains planar cell polarity (PCP) during ductal plate morphogenesis. FPC loss → disrupted ciliary PCP signaling → Wnt5a abnormally activated → failed planar arrangement of ductal plate cells → structurally abnormal duct formation. SOX9/Notch amplification: Sox9b↔Notch positive feedback maintains biliary identity; FPC loss disrupts this → reduced SOX9 → impaired branching morphogenesis (SOX9 loss causes DPM-like phenotype — bioRxiv 2024. DOI: 10.1101/2024.09.18.613676). Confirmed in cholangiopathy models: Wnt5a drives biliary PCP disruption → ductular reaction + portal fibroblast accumulation. (Tsunoda T et al. J Hepatol 71(1):143-152, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2019.02.024; CELSR1/PCP upregulation confirmed in PCK rat: Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025) New research question (Q24): Is Wnt5a/PCP disrupted in FPC-deficient ductal plate cells during the developmental window — and can PCP pathway modulators reduce DPM severity neonatally?

FRAMEWORK GAPS IDENTIFIED AUGUST 19, 2026 — Independent systematic research session

Five major gaps and curative approaches not previously in this framework

⚠️ GAP 1 — Portal fibroblasts, not HSCs, are the primary fibrosis-producing cells in biliary disease: Lineage tracing confirmed portal fibroblasts contribute >70% of myofibroblasts at onset in cholestatic/biliary liver injury. (Mederacke I et al. PNAS 111:14516-14521, 2014. DOI: 10.1073/pnas.1414373111) HSCs dominate only in hepatotoxic injury. Our cascade throughout attributes fibrosis to HSC activation — likely incorrect for ARPKD-CHF as a biliary disease. Portal fibroblasts are periductal, self-renewing, apoptosis-resistant, and express PDGFR-β — explaining why Peltz's PDGFR-β inhibitors worked and why CDH11 (Huch assembloid, portal mesenchyme) is an anti-fibrotic target. Framework correction: distinguish portal fibroblasts (primary biliary responders) from HSCs (secondary) throughout cascade descriptions.

⚠️ GAP 2 — Ferroptosis in congenital cholestatic liver disease — YAP/FBXL5 connects to our root cascade (Ohta Y et al. Am J Pathol 196(3):700-715, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ajpath.2025.11.008): Iron + bile acids → GPX4 depletion → ferroptosis in congenital cholestatic pediatric liver disease. Mouse model uses Yap^flox/flox^ × Fbxl5^flox/flox^ — YAP directly promotes cholestatic ferroptosis. In ARPKD-CHF: constitutive YAP (root node) + DCA/LCA accumulation (FiBA-HBOs confirmed) = ferroptosis conditions. Completely absent from framework. Spermidine third mechanism: → Nrf2 → GPX4 upregulation = anti-ferroptotic. (Zhang K et al. Cell 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.02.001) Cascade: bile acid accumulation → GPX4 depletion → ferroptosis → cholangiocyte death → worsened ductular reaction.

⚠️ GAP 3 — Wnt/β-catenin directly implicated in ARPKD-CHF — published mechanism absent from cascade (Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — Pkhd1del4/del4 mice): cAMP/PKA → pSer675-β-catenin → nuclear β-catenin → TCF/LEF activation → E-cadherin loss in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Direct evidence in ARPKD mouse. Wnt and Hippo/YAP mutually cross-activate in biliary fibrosis. (Zhu L, Wu B. Front Immunol 17:1899587, 2026. DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1899587) β-catenin and constitutive YAP are co-activated and mutually reinforcing. Therapeutic: ICG-001/PRI-724 (TCF/β-catenin inhibitor — Lenz HJ et al. Mol Cancer Ther 14(9):2136, 2015. DOI: 10.1158/1535-7163.MCT-14-0223).

⚠️ GAP 4 — LSEC capillarization blocks liver regeneration — vicious cycle connecting portal hypertension to failed regeneration (J Hepatol March 27, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.02.014): Capillarized CD34+ LSECs maintain laminin around DR cells → blocks HNF4G-driven DR-to-hepatocyte transdifferentiation. (Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021) Preventing LSEC capillarization → enables liver regeneration from within. In ARPKD-CHF: biliary fibrosis → portal hypertension → LSEC capillarization → blocked HNF4G regeneration → accelerated disease. Preventing LSEC capillarization (eNOS restoration, SEMA4D blockade — Gracia-Sancho; Gracia-Sancho J et al. J Hepatol 78:888-903, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2022.12.007) = unlock regeneration. Connects Gracia-Sancho (LSEC) + Vallier/Galanakis (HNF4G) contacts contacted August 2026.

⭐ NEW — MR/Aldosterone → Cav1 → LSEC capillarization (Sep 22, 2026 — review paper): MR activation drives LSEC capillarization via Cav1-dependent oxidative stress. MR antagonism (finerenone — FDA-approved nonsteroidal MRA) reverses this in preclinical cholestatic/toxin models. No ARPKD-specific data. No RCT in liver fibrosis. Adds a second independent driver of LSEC capillarization alongside TDI01/ROCK2. Finerenone = potential Track 1 candidate (FDA-approved, oral, well-tolerated) but evidence for liver fibrosis is thin. (Review. J Clin Transl Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.14218/JCTH.2026.00019. ⚠️ Review paper — mechanistic claims from published literature cited within.)

✅ CURATIVE APPROACH — Cholangiocyte organoid transplantation via ERCP: (Sampaziotis F, Vallier L et al. Science 371:839-846, 2021. DOI: 10.1126/science.aaz6462; J Mater Chem B 2025) Cholangiocyte organoids repair human biliary epithelium in ex vivo perfusion (Science 2021). ICOs in decellularized liver hydrogels reduce fibrosis in chronically injured mice (J Mater Chem B 2025). ARPKD-CHF: ABE/CRISPR-corrected PKHD1 cholangiocyte organoids from patient cells → ERCP retrograde delivery (already validated for gene therapy, Guimont 2021) → replace FPC-deficient biliary epithelium with functional cells. Cell replacement rather than in situ editing — potentially more accessible. Completely absent from framework until now.

⭐ NEW — Integrin αV → YAP → CTGF axis in LSECs: pressure-driven fibrosis pathway confirmed in Fontan-associated liver disease (Feb 27 / May 2026) — DIRECTLY RELEVANT TO ARPKD-CHF:

Kato S, Hikita H, Tsukamoto O et al., Tatsumi T, Eguchi H, Takehara T. "Activation of the Integrin αV–YAP–CTGF Axis in Liver Sinusoidal Endothelial Cells Promotes Liver Fibrogenesis, Leading to Portal Hypertension and Liver Carcinogenesis in Congestive Hepatopathy." Gastroenterology 170(5):1000-1016, May 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2025.11.014. Open Access (Creative Commons Attribution 4.0). Published online February 27, 2026. University of Osaka, Department of Gastroenterology and Hepatology.

Confirmed from abstract and press release:

  1. The pathway: Elevated hydrostatic pressure in liver sinusoids → activates integrin αV in LSECs → YAP activation → CTGF upregulation → collagen deposition → fibrosis, portal hypertension, and liver carcinogenesis. Confirmed in mouse model of hepatic congestion and in human patient liver samples.
  2. Inhibition confirmed: Pharmacological inhibition of integrin αV OR genetic knockout of CTGF in LSECs both improved outcomes in the mouse congestion model. Source: University of Osaka press release Feb 27, 2026 + abstract conclusions.
  3. FALD human confirmation: Single-cell and spatial transcriptomic analyses of liver samples from patients with Fontan-associated liver disease (FALD) showed the same pattern: YAP activation in LSECs → increased CTGF. Source: abstract. FALD = non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis in children/young adults following Fontan cardiac surgery — the closest published human disease comparator to ARPKD-CHF (both: non-cirrhotic portal hypertension, pediatric/young, mechanical pressure-driven, no primary hepatocyte disease).
  4. Abstract conclusion (verbatim): "CTGF induction in LSECs may play an upstream role in the fibrogenesis of CH. The integrin αV–YAP–CTGF axis in LSECs could be a potential therapeutic target for CH."

⚠️ ARPKD-CHF relevance is OUR INFERENCE — not stated in this paper: Portal hypertension in ARPKD-CHF elevates sinusoidal hydrostatic pressure. This paper shows elevated sinusoidal pressure activates integrin αV in LSECs → YAP → CTGF → fibrosis. If the same mechanism operates in ARPKD-CHF (not tested), portal hypertension itself creates a secondary LSEC-driven fibrotic circuit — a vicious cycle: biliary fibrosis → portal hypertension → LSEC YAP activation → CTGF → more fibrosis. Whether integrin αV is activated in ARPKD-CHF LSECs specifically requires direct testing in Pkhd1del4/del4 or Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice.

⚠️ CTGF CELL-TYPE DISTINCTION — important for framework accuracy: The existing framework entry (Oe S et al. Am J Pathol 2019, PMID 31155109; Tsunoda T et al. J Hepatol 71(1):143-152, 2019) says "CTGF is YAP-independent" in the cholangiocyte context (IL-8 → MAPK → CTGF). The Kato/Hikita paper shows CTGF is YAP-dependent in the LSEC context (integrin αV → YAP → CTGF). Both are correct — different cell types, different upstream pathways, same effector. CTGF is produced by both FPC-deficient cholangiocytes (YAP-independent, Oe/Tsunoda) and by pressure-stressed LSECs (YAP-dependent, Kato/Hikita).

αV inhibition connection: The paper shows integrin αV inhibition improves outcomes. Our framework already has αvβ6 (a specific αV-containing integrin) as both a delivery address (A20FMDV2/Marshall) and a therapeutic target (bexotegrast, cholangiocyte TGFβ). ⚠️ HYPOTHESIS ONLY: Whether the LSEC pathway in this paper runs through αvβ6 specifically, or through a different αV complex (e.g., αvβ3, αvβ5), is not established from the abstract. The full paper is required to determine which αV integrin is involved in LSECs — bexotegrast (which specifically targets αvβ6) may or may not address the LSEC pathway. Not confirmed without full paper read.

New contact opportunity: Hayato Hikita (senior author, University of Osaka, Gastroenterology & Hepatology) — working on LSEC mechanotransduction in non-cirrhotic portal fibrosis. FALD overlap with ARPKD-CHF makes cold contact plausible.

Flask/Hartung/Dell MRI Biomarker Review — Second quantitative confirmation + full endpoint framework 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

MacAskill CJ, Flask CA, Dell KM, Hartung EA, Serai SD et al. Pediatr Radiol 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5. Open access, July 28, 2026. Tier 1 — second independent confirmation of all quantitative MRE/MRF values. Liver T1-MRF elevated in ARPKD with portal hypertension (1064.5 ms) vs without (918.8 ms) vs healthy (894.9 ms). Liver T2-MRF: 38.1 vs 33.0 vs 32.8 ms. Key additional findings: (1) TKV explicitly stated as NOT useful in ARPKD — confounded by normal growth, no correlation with GFR decline. (2) Liver biopsy explicitly NOT recommended — bile leak risk, thrombocytopenia, anesthesia. (3) MRE entered FDA Biomarker Qualification Program for liver fibrosis — direct pathway to surrogate endpoint status. (4) MRF works in children ≥6 years without sedation, ~3% day-to-day variation. New outreach target: Chris A. Flask PhD (caf@case.edu, Case Western Reserve University / University Hospitals Cleveland) — MRI technical lead for ARPKD, collaborates with Dell and Hartung on IMAGE-ARPKD. Introduce via Dell.

🔬 Molecular MRI contrast agents — gene therapy readout tools for ARPKD animal models (Flask et al. 2026)

The Flask/Hartung/Dell review identifies two specialized MRI contrast agents that directly target fibrogenesis — critical as gene therapy programs (FPC restoration, CFTR, SPP1 silencing) approach animal-model validation and will require liver fibrosis readout tools beyond standard biochemistry:

  • EP-3533 — collagen-targeted gadolinium agent that specifically binds extracellular matrix collagen, providing direct MRI imaging of active hepatic fibrosis. Already validated in NASH and pulmonary fibrosis models. Not yet tested in ARPKD animal models (PCK rat, Pkhd1del4/del4) — a designated gap. Ideal readout for SPP1 silencing or FPC restoration pre-clinical studies where collagen deposition is the endpoint.
  • Gd-Hyd (gadolinium hydrazide conjugates) — bind ECM components including collagen IV specifically. Direct imaging of active biliary fibrosis in ARPKD-CHF models.

Strategic implication: Before any ARPKD gene therapy enters clinical trials, molecular MRI readout in animal models (EP-3533 collagen imaging pre vs. post FPC restoration) would provide non-invasive, spatially resolved evidence of fibrosis regression — stronger than liver enzyme normalization or histology alone. These tools are available now for pre-clinical work. The ARPKD-CHF animal modeling community (Liebau/Ziegler, Outeda, Cebotaru) should be aware of them.

  • Liver biopsy NOT recommended in ARPKD — risk of bile leak, limited sampling, anesthesia risks. (Flask CA, Hartung EA, Dell KM et al. Pediatric Radiology, July 28, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5)
  • Total kidney volume (TKV) is useless for ARPKD clinical trials — can be stable despite ongoing progression. Distinct from ADPKD. (Flask CA, Hartung EA, Dell KM et al. Pediatric Radiology, July 28, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5)
  • Liver T1 correlates with percent bile duct area and percent periportal fibrosis in PCK rat. (Flask CA, Hartung EA, Dell KM et al. Pediatric Radiology, July 28, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5)
  • Liver ASL (non-contrast perfusion): unexplored gap in ARPKD — a compelling new research direction. (Flask CA, Hartung EA, Dell KM et al. Pediatric Radiology, July 28, 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5)
  • Clinical note: MRE liver stiffness benchmark = 3.60 kPa in ARPKD patients WITH portal hypertension — patients at CCHMC should be assessed.

Clinical monitoring — from biopsy to blood

CHF clinical trials require endpoints measurable repeatedly without liver biopsy risks in children with portal hypertension and thrombocytopenia. (Flask CA et al. Pediatric Radiology 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00247-026-06737-5 — liver biopsy contraindicated in ARPKD)

Proposed ARPKD Liver Disease Classification — First Standardized Framework (2026) 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Kurkal K, Gupte GL. JPGN Reports 7:477-478, 2026. DOI: 10.1002/jpr3.70167. Birmingham Children's Hospital, UK. Tier 2 — first proposed standardized liver disease severity classification for ARPKD children.

Two-category framework based on BAVENO VII portal hypertension criteria + ARPKD hepatic complications: (1) Clinically Significant Liver Disease (CSLD) — portal hypertension, varices, cholangitis, hepatosplenomegaly + thrombocytopenia, ascites; (2) Clinically Insignificant Liver Disease (CiSLD) — hepatic involvement on imaging/biopsy without clinical sequelae. Survey of 115 pediatric hepatology/GI colleagues globally: 36 responded, 33 agreed. Existing classifications emphasize renal pathology — this is the first liver-specific ARPKD classification. Intended as a standard reporting tool for future ARPKD publications and trial design.

Clinical relevance: Provides severity staging language for trial eligibility — CSLD = anti-fibrotic trials; CiSLD = monitoring/prevention trials. Complements IMAGE-ARPKD MRE/MRF endpoints. Contact: Girish L. Gupte (girishgupte@nhs.net) — ⭐ Moving to Hospital for Sick Children Toronto as Associate Professor of Paediatric Hepatology (replied Sep 10, signed "Girish"). Intends to continue CSLD/CiSLD research at SickKids. MRE integration question pending reply when settled. Note: SickKids Toronto hepatology also includes Dr. Binita Kamath (ARPKD clinical consensus co-author with Guay-Woodford).  🟢 Replied Sep 10, 2026 — newsletter subscriber · MRE question pending · moving to SickKids Toronto

PKDNet (Hartung, CHOP) — R01FD008225 (FDA-funded, $800K, 2024-2028) — building ARPKD trial infrastructure via PEDSnet (>13 million children, 11 US academic centers). (Guay-Woodford LM et al. Am J Kidney Dis 2025. DOI: 10.1053/j.ajkd.2025.01.022 — PKDNet infrastructure paper) Includes patient/family surveys — direct family contact is appropriate. PICP biosensor directly addresses the stated endpoint gap. (PICP biosensor FIB-EIS — Chem Eng J 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cej.2026.153547)

Breakthrough: CFTR mislocalization in ARPKD

The published finding (2021-2023) — not yet acted on

FPC deficiency → HSP27↓ / HSP70/90↑ → CFTR mislocalized to apical membrane only → excessive Cl⁻ secretion into bile duct lumen → biliary cyst fluid accumulation. [Cebotaru, Outeda et al., Cell Mol Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021, PMID: 34329764]

⭐⭐ Eight new findings from Cebotaru lab papers (September 2, 2026)

  1. HSP70 and HSP90 are therapeutic targets, not just biomarkers. Silencing either HSP70 or HSP90 alone inhibits cyst growth in FPC-null cholangiocytes in 3D culture. HSP90 has a direct independent role in fibrosis etiology. This connects the HSP dysregulation cascade to the PAK4/LOXL2 fibrosis node — HSP90 inhibition is a druggable target in ARPKD-CHF. (Yanda MK, Zeidan A, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 324:G404–G414, 2023. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022)
  2. CFTR and PC2 are both increased in primary cilia of ARPKD cholangiocytes — not simply absent. VX-809 restored both CFTR and PC2 cilia levels toward wild type. This is more nuanced than simple mislocalization — a ciliary overcompensation is occurring, consistent with dysregulated ciliary gating when FPC is absent. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022
  3. ⭐ Standard liver and kidney function tests are NORMAL despite active histological disease. ALB, AST, ALT, BUN, and creatinine were all within normal range at 35 days in Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice even while cholangiocyte proliferation was dramatically elevated and bile ducts were malformed. Biochemical markers lag histological disease. Clinical labs will not detect early ARPKD-CHF — only imaging (MRE) or histology will. Direct implication for trial endpoint design. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022
  4. Liver disease precedes kidney disease — 60% liver phenotype at 3 months vs only 20% severe kidney phenotype. Treatment at PND5 (5 days old) is early intervention during active liver disease progression. Human equivalent: weeks 20-40 gestation through first 1-2 years of life is the critical biliary window. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022
  5. AAV1 transduces both cholangiocytes AND hepatocytes in ARPKD liver. Not just cyst cells — surrounding hepatocytes are also efficiently transduced by IP-injected AAV1 in ARPKD mice. This is an advantage: hepatocyte CFTR regulates ER stress independently, adding a second therapeutic mechanism. Quantitative cyst reduction: cyst index (bile duct area/total area) reduced at P<0.0001 significance in treated vs GFP control mice. Authors state AAV1-Δ27-264-CFTR "rescues the ARPKD liver phenotype" — strongest efficacy language yet for biliary CFTR gene therapy. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025
  6. ⭐ Outeda (UMaryland/Guay-Woodford network) + Cebotaru (JHU) are confirmed co-authors on this paper — direct collaboration between two of our active contacts. Outeda is already in our network (permission granted Sep 2, 2026). This connection means the Guay-Woodford/Outeda/Cebotaru axis is actively working on CFTR gene therapy for ARPKD biliary disease together. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025
  7. α2,3-Sialic acid (MAL) is the dominant AAV1 receptor on ARPKD biliary cyst surfaces — present at high levels apically and basolaterally on cysts, absent from normal bile ducts. α2,6-sialic acid (SNA) is present but lower. This explains why systemically injected AAV1 preferentially targets ARPKD cysts: the sialic acid receptors are basolaterally exposed on cyst membranes, accessible from the bloodstream via IP injection. Hepatocytes surrounding cysts show both MAL and SNA, decreased in ARPKD. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025 · DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00057.2025
  8. Δ27-264-CFTR construct is ~5,060 bp — confirmed to fit within AAV packaging capacity. Missing N-terminal tail and first 4 transmembrane loops (AA 27-264). Does NOT conduct Cl⁻ independently — works purely via transcomplementation: binds ER-retained endogenous CFTR and enhances its processing and trafficking to plasma membrane. Vectors manufactured at Powell Gene Therapy Center (PGTC), University of Florida. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00057.2025
  9. ⭐ Harris-Cebotaru collaboration already exists. The pkd1RC/RC ADPKD mice used in the Ciobanu/Cebotaru 2025 paper were provided by Dr. Peter C. Harris (Mayo Clinic). Harris and Cebotaru are already scientifically connected. The ARPKD newsletter introduction reinforces an existing relationship. Harris is positioned to facilitate Cebotaru's access to additional PKD mouse models. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00057.2025

Cebotaru, Outeda et al., Cell Mol Gastroenterol Hepatol 2021: VX-809 restored CFTR localization in FPC-mutant cholangiocytes and reduced cyst formation. Authors stated: "CFTR correctors, already in clinical use to treat cystic fibrosis, could also be used as a treatment for ARPKD."

Outeda et al., Am J Physiol GI Liver Physiol 2023: VX-809 in Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice returned bile duct abnormalities toward wild-type. "Given that these drugs are already approved for use in humans, they can be fast-tracked for clinical use."

The untested next-generation drug

VX-809 is the old, less potent CFTR corrector. The current standard of care for CF is Trikafta (elexacaftor/tezacaftor/ivacaftor, FDA-approved 2019). Elexacaftor is 5-10× more potent through a distinct Type III allosteric mechanism AND directly inhibits basolateral KCa3.1 potassium channels — a second independent anti-cyst mechanism. (Van Goor F et al. PNAS 108:18843-18848, 2011. DOI: 10.1073/pnas.1105787108)

⭐⭐ TRUNCATED CFTR — DR. CEBOTARU'S CORE INNOVATION (Cebotaru L, personal communication, September 1-2, 2026, permission granted — Ciobanu C et al. Am J Physiol GI 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025): In normal cells, only 20% of CFTR reaches the correct cellular location — 80% stays trapped in the endoplasmic reticulum (ER). Dr. Cebotaru's truncated CFTR form is packaged in AAV and binds directly to that 80% of ER-retained CFTR, relocating it to the correct position. This simultaneously: (1) reduces ER calcium levels, which drives proliferation and cystogenesis; (2) binds PC2, PC1, and FPC — taking all three proteins to the correct cellular location. If the CFTR→FPC direct link is confirmed in ongoing gene therapy mouse experiments, CFTR correction would restore FPC ciliary localization simultaneously — addressing the primary ARPKD defect rather than only the secondary CFTR mislocalization. This would make it the most directly relevant ARPKD-CHF gene therapy approach in the current landscape.

CFTR correctors tested in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells and animal models — they work. Drug Discovery Q1 ANSWERED.

Approved statement — Dr. Liudmila Cebotaru, Johns Hopkins University, September 2, 2026 (permission granted):

"The CFTR correctors would reduce the proliferation, inflammation, fibrosis and dilatation of bile ducts in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells, and animal models. Truncated form of CFTR, which was introduced in cells and mouse models works as a genetic corrector and has the ability to bind ER-retained CFTR, reduce ER calcium, and carry CFTR, PC2, PC1, and FPC to the correct cellular location."

Three supporting manuscripts from Dr. Cebotaru's lab (provided September 2, 2026):

  1. Correctors and ARPKD (liver): Yanda MK, Zeidan A, Cebotaru L. Ameliorating liver disease in an autosomal recessive polycystic kidney disease mouse model. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 324: G404–G414, 2023. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022 — VX-809 (CFTR corrector) reduced bile duct abnormalities and cholangiocyte proliferation by ~70% in Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice. HSP27↓/HSP70/90↑ in FPC-null liver confirmed. CFTR and PC2 co-localize in primary cilia of ARPKD cholangiocytes.
  2. Gene therapy with truncated CFTR in ARPKD (liver): Ciobanu C, Outeda P, Guggino WB, Cebotaru L. A novel gene therapy for ARPKD based on CFTR. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 329: G434–G442, 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025 — AAV1-Δ27-264-CFTR reduced liver cyst area and proliferation in Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice. AAV1 preferentially transduces ARPKD cholangiocytes via α2,3-sialylated glycans on cyst surfaces. Truncated CFTR works via transcomplementation — binds ER-retained endogenous CFTR and relocates it to basolateral membrane.
  3. Gene therapy with truncated CFTR in ADPKD (kidney — demonstrates mechanism works in liver and kidney): Ciobanu C, Cebotaru L. AAV1-CFTR preferentially transduces cysts and reduces cyst size in a mouse model of ADPKD. Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 328: C1783–C1792, 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00057.2025 — ~30% reduction in cyst area, ~50% reduction in cyst size after single dose AAV1-Δ27-264-CFTR in pkd1RC/RC mice. Demonstrates AAV1-CFTR gene therapy platform works across both PKD disease types.

Elexacaftor has never been tested in ARPKD biliary cells. Testing in Peltz's PKHD1 hepatic organoids or Outeda's Pkhd1 mice is immediately feasible. This is the highest-priority untested FDA-approved drug for ARPKD-CHF. (Basis: Yanda MK et al. Cell Mol Gastroenterol Hepatol 12:1517-1529, 2021. DOI: 10.1016/j.jcmgh.2021.07.012 — VX-809 corrector reduced biliary disease; elexacaftor is 5-10× more potent)

⚠ mTOR inhibitor safety warning (UPDATED August 17, 2026 — three independent mechanisms of biliary harm): mTOR inhibitors (rapamycin, everolimus) cause biliary harm by three distinct mechanisms: (1) Activate autophagy → HDAC6 → deplete ciliogenic proteins ARL3/ARL13B from biliary cilia → worsen cystogenesis (Masyuk + LaRusso, Hepatology 2022). (2) Suppress PI3K/AKT/mTOR → reduce HNF4G expression in cholangiocytes → impair the compensatory cholangiocyte-to-hepatocyte regenerative mechanism (Galanakis/Vallier, J Hepatol 2026 — Tier 2; ARPKD application Tier 3 hypothesis). (3) May worsen biliary fibrosis in adjacent cholestatic models. mTOR inhibitors may benefit the ARPKD kidney while simultaneously harming the liver via three independent pathways. Any ARPKD trial using mTOR inhibitors MUST include dedicated hepatic endpoints.

Gene therapy and delivery landscape

📌 Added August 26, 2026 — FDA-approved PPAR agonists for cholestatic bile duct disease (elafibranor, seladelpar)

Elafibranor (PPARα/δ, FDA-approved PBC 2024, brand: IQIRVO, Ipsen) and Seladelpar (PPARδ, FDA-approved PBC 2024, brand: Livdelzi, GSK) — both reduce bile acid accumulation, inflammation, and fibrosis in human cholestatic bile duct disease. PPAR activation overlaps with FXR target genes in bile acid homeostasis. Neither has been tested in ARPKD-CHF specifically. Evidence grade B — FDA-approved in human cholestatic bile duct disease. Elafibranor PSC data: Levy C et al. J Hepatol 84(1):74-85, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.04.025

📌 NEW August 2026 — Haemodynamic Wnt and hepatic zonation loss in ARPKD-CHF

Source: Lee et al. Nature Communications 17:8821, August 22, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-52183-4

  • Portal hypertension → disrupted portal blood flow → disrupted endothelial Wnt → loss of hepatic zonation BEFORE hepatocyte death.
  • β-catenin dysregulated via TWO independent mechanisms: (1) FPC loss → RAC1 → pSer675-β-catenin (biliary, from birth); (2) Portal hypertension → disrupted vascular Wnt → β-catenin zonation loss (hepatocytes, develops over time).
  • LSEC WNT2A is a critical zonation maintainer — LSEC capillarization → reduced WNT2A → second independent zonation loss pathway.
  • TAZ (YAP paralogue) in LSECs prevents capillarization via NO — YAP/TAZ axis bridges our primary biliary cascade to LSEC protection.
  • Ductal plate → periportal hepatocytes (PMID: 21708104): DPM in ARPKD-CHF may cause congenital periportal hepatocyte abnormalities, not just biliary malformation. These cells may be abnormal from birth and less capable of responding to hemodynamic Wnt signals.

📌 NEW August 2026 — CDK1 as a validated therapeutic target in ARPKD liver

Source: iScience S2589-0042(26)01663-9, June 5, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2026.113369. Lineage tracing in PKHD1-deficient rat and mouse models.

  • Single-cell cystogenesis confirmed: Individual Pkhd1-deficient cholangiocytes autonomously generate millimeter-scale biliary cysts within one year — no paracrine input required.
  • CDK1 (cyclin-dependent kinase 1) identified as the top upregulated cell-cycle regulator in transcriptomic profiling of early cystic liver tissue.
  • RO-3306 CDK1 inhibitor significantly reduced liver cyst growth AND fibrosis in vivo. Tier 1 evidence in PKHD1 model.
  • Mathematical model: biliary epithelial proliferation alone accounts for millimeter-scale cysts in weeks to months.
  • CDK1 connects to our cascade: FPC loss → RAC1/cFOS/YAP/STAT3 → CDK1 → biliary expansion → cysts.
  • Gene therapy implication: CDK1 inhibition is the logical second approach alongside gene therapy — suppressing cystogenesis in uncorrected residual cells.
  • OCD disruption alone is INSUFFICIENT for cystogenesis (Pkhd1 mutant mice have OCD defects but minimal cysts). Primary driver = CDK1-driven proliferation.

⭐⭐ ADDED SEPTEMBER 15, 2026 — Li/Harris ABE9: First in vivo proof that base editing works in kidney tissue. Organ-specific promoter principle applies to biliary gene therapy. Cheng AS, Li LX, Zhou JX, Harris PC, Calvet JP, Li X (senior/corresponding). Nature Communications 16:11212, December 11, 2025. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-025-65997-1. Open access. Mayo Clinic, Department of Biochemistry and Molecular Biology. Peter C. Harris is co-author — his pkd1RC/RC mice were the model. Harris is in our network and is the natural gate to Li.

What was proven: ABE7 and ABE8e had NO detectable kidney editing efficiency in prior studies. ABE9 (N108Q + L145T precision variants) is the first adenine base editor to achieve significant kidney editing in vivo. Single IV injection, dual AAV9 system. Two base editor versions: (1) Cbh-ABE9-dual-AAV9 (broadly expressed) — gDNA editing: kidney 11.6%, heart 21.3%, liver 40.1%; cDNA editing: kidney 28.7%, heart 40.9%, liver 57.0%. (2) Ksp-ABE9-dual-AAV9 (kidney-specific cadherin-16 promoter) — kidney gDNA 23.4% (higher than broad!), isolated tubular cells 34.2%, heart essentially zero — organ specificity confirmed. Outcomes: reduced cyst growth, improved BUN/creatinine, decreased cardiac hypertrophy (Cbh only), improved liver function (ALT/AST/ALP normalized), decreased fibrosis in kidney/heart/liver, decreased macrophage recruitment, extended survival in lethal Pkd1RC/null model. Stable editing 3→6 months with possible selective advantage.

Five implications for ARPKD-CHF: (1) ABE9 — not ABE8e-V106W — is the ABE variant to consider for kidney-targeted correction. Krappitz should be aware. (2) The Ksp organ-specific promoter principle is directly transferable to biliary disease — replacing Ksp with a biliary-specific promoter (HNF1β confirmed in framework Sep 11, 2026; CK19; γGT) would concentrate PKHD1 ABE editing in cholangiocytes while sparing hepatocytes. Nobody has done this. (3) Liver is the most efficiently edited organ with systemic AAV9: 40% gDNA, 57% cDNA. For ARPKD-CHF, the liver/biliary system is the primary target — AAV9 will get there. (4) Liver fibrosis was reduced by ABE treatment even in this ADPKD (not ARPKD) model — gene correction upstream reduces the downstream fibrotic cascade. (5) Li explicitly identifies LNP as the next delivery step: "Future efforts are necessary to develop non-viral delivery methods, such as lipid nanoparticles." Li is moving toward LNP-ABE9 for kidney disease — exactly where our framework needs to go for biliary disease.

New contact — Xiaogang Li, PhD (Mayo Clinic, Department of Biochemistry and Molecular Biology). Approach only through Harris — do not cold contact. Harris co-authored this paper with Li and provided the mouse model. Harris → Li is a direct scientific relationship.

⭐ ADDED SEPTEMBER 15, 2026 — Caplan/Onuchic PC1 fragment (2023) is the ADPKD proof-of-concept for the ICD15 minimal-domain approach in ARPKD-CHF. Onuchic L (first author), Caplan MJ (PI/senior), Yale Department of Cellular & Molecular Physiology. Nature Communications, March 30, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-37449-1. Also involves Stefan Somlo (Yale nephrology — responsible for the PC1 re-expression reversal experiment).

A 200 amino acid C-terminal fragment of PC1 (polycystin-1, the ADPKD protein at 4,300 amino acids) is sufficient to suppress cystic disease in a mouse model. This fragment interacts with Nicotinamide Nucleotide Transhydrogenase (NNT) — a mitochondrial enzyme. The 200 aa fragment fits within AAV packaging limits (~600 bp coding sequence).

The explicit ICD15 parallel not previously drawn in this framework:

FeaturePC1 (ADPKD) — Caplan/Onuchic 2023FPC (ARPKD) — Walker/Qian 2023
Full protein4,300 aa — too large for single AAV4,074 aa — too large for single AAV
Functional fragment200 aa C-terminal fragment suppresses ADPKDICD15 (~200 aa C-terminal intracellular domain) retains signaling activity
Mitochondrial interactionInteracts with NNT (mitochondrial enzyme)Translocates to inner mitochondrial membrane
Gene therapy payload~600 bp — fits single AAV or LNPICD15 would fit single AAV or LNP
Disease suppression testedYES — dramatically reduced cyst size in mouseNOT TESTED — open gap

The untested experiment for ARPKD: Has ICD15 been injected as an AAV payload into Pkhd1 mice and tested for disease suppression? No. Walker characterized ICD15's mitochondrial targeting and signaling properties (2023); Long is testing AMPK modulation downstream (2026). The missing step — AAV-ICD15 in Pkhd1del3-4 or cyli mice — is the direct analogue of what Caplan/Onuchic did for PC1. Qian confirmed this is "interesting and biologically testable" (personal communication, September 4, 2026). Chandler (UCL GOS ICH, Long trainee) is doing AAV fragment delivery for ARPKD kidney — biliary AAV-ICD15 remains unassigned.

⭐ CROSS-DISEASE FINDING — PC1 CTT SUPPRESSES PKD2 DISEASE (Sep 22, 2026 — conference presentation, unpublished): At the PKD Foundation Patient Summit September 2026, Caplan (Yale) presented a new unpublished result: "In disease caused by PKD2, so these are mice that have lost PKD2, polycystin 2, we can still partially suppress the disease with this C terminal tail of polycystin one. So this is a new and unpublished result." Source: PKD Foundation Patient Summit, September 2026. Presentation by Michael J Caplan, Professor and Chair, Molecular and Cellular Biology, Yale University. Unpublished data — not peer-reviewed, not in any public database. If the PC1 C-terminal tail (200 aa = ICD15 analogue) suppresses disease caused by loss of a completely different gene (PKD2/polycystin-2), the mechanism must act on a shared downstream node — downstream of both PC1 and PC2. In our framework, that shared downstream node is mitochondrial function via NNT, which is required for the CTT effect (mice lacking NNT show no response to CTT). This finding strengthens the hypothesis that ICD15/NNT acts on a fundamental cystogenic mechanism shared across polycystic kidney diseases — including potentially ARPKD where FPC is lost but polycystins are present. ⚠️ This connection from ADPKD cross-rescue to ARPKD relevance is our inference — the speaker did not make this connection. It requires direct testing in Pkhd1-deficient cells.

Network: Caplan and Onuchic (Yale) are not in our network but Sorin Fedeles (sorin.fedeles@yale.edu) is — warm via Krappitz thread. Fedeles is in Somlo's orbit at Yale. Stefan Somlo (Yale nephrology) is the most senior ADPKD/PKD1 researcher at Yale; Fedeles → Somlo is the natural path if needed.

⭐ ADPKD CLINICAL LANDSCAPE CONTEXT (Sep 22, 2026 — PKD Foundation Patient Summit): For framework awareness — these are ADPKD-only programs not relevant to Gianna's PKHD1 disease, but provide calibration for the gene therapy field timeline. Source: PKD Foundation Patient Summit, September 2026, Chris Chen (VP Research Programs) + Caplan (Yale) presentations. No citations — conference presentations, no published papers cited below.

  • VX-407 (Vertex, Phase 2) — small molecule corrector of misfolded polycystin-1 (PC1). Applying the same corrector strategy as Vertex's trikafta for cystic fibrosis, now to PKD1 mutations that cause misfolding. PC1 protein corrector — ADPKD only. Conference intelligence, no paper DOI.
  • Anti-PAPP antibodies (Calico/AbbVie, GSK, AstraZeneca) — three companies in US trials. PAPP-A (pregnancy-associated plasma protein-A) cleaves IGFBP, releasing IGF → cell growth/proliferation in cysts. Anti-PAPP monoantibodies block this. Preclinical: genetic reduction of PAPP → cyst reduction in ADPKD mice. Phase 2 clinical trials ongoing. ADPKD only. Conference intelligence, no paper DOI.
  • Ferraburen / anti-miR-17 (Novartis, Phase 2) — antisense oligonucleotide against microRNA-17, which downregulates polycystin expression. Blocking miR-17 maintains polycystin levels. Developed from PKD Foundation-funded seed research (Dr. Patel, 2014). ADPKD only. Conference intelligence, no paper DOI.
  • Timeline from Caplan (conference presentation): PC2 gene delivery and PC1 C-terminal tail → clinical trials "within about three years." Base/prime editing → "within about 10 years." Early trials will be small (safety primary endpoint). Retrograde ureteral delivery (immune-privileged space, pioneered by Joshua Lipshutz) mentioned as a kidney delivery route being explored.

⭐ UPDATED SEPTEMBER 15, 2026 (v7.6) — Jennifer Chandler (UCL GOS ICH): Full research profile confirmed. Three research strands. Sci Transl Med paper confirmed. KRUK grant amounts corrected.

Dr Jennifer Chandler (goes by "Jennie"), Research Fellow, UCL Great Ormond Street Institute of Child Health, Kidney Development and Disease group. Publishes as Jennifer C. Chandler. Long's former trainee — now running an independent parallel research program at the same institution.

RESEARCH STRAND 1 — WT1 Glomerular Disease (primary independent arc):
KRUK Intermediate Training Fellowship £206,219 (January 2022): genetic analysis in WT1 animal models to identify treatment targets; developing delivery approaches to glomerular cells.
J Pathology 2024 (DOI: 10.1002/path.6339): disrupted adrenomedullin signaling between podocytes and endothelium is an early feature of WT1 glomerular disease; increasing adrenomedullin levels improves filter cell health. PhD student Emily Moore is KRUK-funded to continue adrenomedullin work.
Science Translational Medicine (August 13, 2026): Malik S, Chandler JC, Long DA. DOI: 10.1126/scitranslmed.adv1289 — "Ultrasound-guided renal artery administration of angiopoietin-1 RNA therapy slows the progression of preclinical WT1 glomerular disease." RNA therapy restoring angiopoietin-1 levels delivered via lipid nanocomplexes + minimally invasive ultrasound-guided renal artery injection. Targeted the glomerulus while limiting systemic exposure. Results: reduced proteinuria, preserved podocytes, reduced scarring in preclinical models. Funded by KRUK + GOSH Charity + LifeArc. This is the August 13, 2026 KRUK article previously logged as only "group photo of Malik, Chandler, Long" — it is a major published paper. Lipid nanocomplex delivery to kidney cells is now confirmed feasible by this group.
