Official title: "Evaluating gene therapy for childhood polycystic kidney disease in a 3D human cellular model." This is Project #2 of 5 from the PKD Partnership £812,000 round (4 of 5 projects not yet publicly named as of Sep 2026 — watch PKD Charity website for announcements). Project: designing a therapy that restores the functional part of the FPC protein in human ARPKD kidney cells, delivered via AAV (confirmed from PKD Charity article Sep 1, 2026), tested in cells carrying PKHD1 mutations. The 3D cystogenesis model (PKDisospheroids) was developed by PhD student Jasmine Kaur in Chandler's lab. This is the most direct active ARPKD gene therapy program in the UK — the same minimal-domain concept as Caplan/Onuchic for PC1 and Walker/Qian for ICD15. Current phase: human ARPKD kidney cells only. Biliary/hepatic extension not started.

RESEARCH STRAND 3 — Gli3/Hedgehog ARPKD (Long lab, published 2025):
Russell LG, Kolatsi-Joannou M, Wilson L, Chandler JC et al. Physiol Rep 13(2):e70191 (2025). DOI: 10.14814/phy2.70191. Chandler co-developed the CRISPR PKHD1-mutant human collecting duct spheroid model used in this study. Result: Gli3 is elevated in both Cpk mice and PKHD1-mutant human spheroids, but reversal does not alter cystogenesis — Hedgehog/Gli3 does not drive ARPKD cysts. ⚠️ Model caveat: Cpk mouse arm = CYS1-deficient (not PKHD1-deficient). Human spheroid data is valid; mouse data should be interpreted with caution. This finding does NOT rule out the SPP1→CD44→Hedgehog→GLI1 FIBROSIS arm (Nature Genetics 2026), which is separate from cystogenesis.

Relationship to Long: Long → Chandler is supervisor to former trainee. The AMPK project (Long, £208K corrected, Oct 2025 round) and the AAV FPC fragment project (Chandler, £98-99K, March 2026 round — PKD Partnership project #2 of 5, AAV confirmed Sep 1 2026) are a coordinated UCL GOS ICH ARPKD program. Additional Long lab members: Dr Daniyal Jafree (independent KRUK £248K AI-lymphatics grant Feb 2026), Dr Carolina Bebi (independent KRUK £243K lymphatics/PUV gene therapy Oct 2025).

Contact strategy: Approach Chandler ONLY through Long's September 25 email. Do not cold-contact. Long is also the primary gate for Dr Saif Malik. The August 13, 2026 KRUK article (Malik/Chandler/Long Sci Transl Med) changes Malik's profile significantly — he is an active delivery/RNA therapy researcher, not just a lab group member.

⚠️ IMPORTANT — cpk mouse ≠ PKHD1 model (August 2026)

Source: Kidney International 2026 (ISN/Elsevier). CYS1 confirmed as third ARPKD-causing gene — 3 patients, 2 families. (DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2026.01.022)

  • Cys1cpk/cpk (cpk) mice are CYS1 (cystin)-deficient, NOT PKHD1/fibrocystin-deficient.
  • PCK rat (Pkhd1 orthologous mutation) IS the correct PKHD1 model for ARPKD-CHF research.
  • Any published study using cpk mice to model ARPKD liver disease is modeling CYS1-deficiency — findings may not directly translate to PKHD1-ARPKD.
  • CYS1 encodes cystin: myristoylated ciliary protein regulated by a myristoyl-electrostatic switch. S17 phosphorylation (PKA-dependent, cAMP-activated) regulates ciliary localization.
  • PPM1A (TGF-β pathway phosphatase) identified as cystin-interacting partner.

📌 NEW August 2026 — RHBD1-fused base editors improve PAM-proximal editing 3.5-fold

Source: Mol Ther S1525-0016(25)00475-7, 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2025.10.021. Validated in adult mouse liver and human disease SNVs.

  • Fusing RHBD1 (RNaseH1 RNA-DNA hybrid binding domain) to ABE8e: up to 3.5-fold improvement in A-to-G editing at PAM-proximal positions A9-A15.
  • BE4max + RHBD1: 3.1-fold improvement at C9-C12.
  • Compatible with ABEmax (used in Krappitz/Kaminski PKD base editing paper — Ibel A, Krappitz M, Kaminski MM et al. Mol Ther 33(11):5373-5382, 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2025.08.026. PMID: 40842155).
  • Validated in adult mouse liver — same target organ as ARPKD-CHF.
  • Critical for base editing of His3124Tyr or Met3642Ile: If the target adenine falls at positions 9-15 from the PAM, RHBD1-ABEmax is required for efficient editing.

PKHD1 coding sequence ≈ 12.2 kb — vastly exceeding the AAV packaging limit (~4.7 kb). (Ward CJ et al. Nat Genet 30:259-269, 2002. DOI: 10.1038/ng833 — original PKHD1 gene characterization) Standard LNPs preferentially target hepatocytes; efficient cholangiocyte delivery requires additional engineering.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · CRISPR Therapeutics Q2 2026 earnings (company communication; FDA approval confirmed)

CTX460 (AATD) Phase 1 + CASGEVY ≥2 years — direct ARPKD precedents (Company-reported — not peer-reviewed)

CTX460: SyNTase™ editing via LNP correcting a SERPINA1 missense variant in hepatocytes for AATD — structural analog to ARPKD liver gene correction (monogenic liver disease → missense mutation → LNP liver delivery → gene correction). Phase 1 initiated August 26, 2026. (CRISPR Therapeutics Q2 2026 earnings — Company reported, not peer-reviewed) Remaining gap for ARPKD: cholangiocyte vs hepatocyte targeting (KRT19 + ERCP strategy).

CASGEVY age ≥2: FDA approved CRISPR therapy for children as young as 2 in 53 days. Regulatory precedent for ARPKD pediatric CRISPR therapy. (FDA approval — CASGEVY (exagamglogene autotemcel) — Agency reported)

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Karikó K et al. Immunity 2005 DOI: 10.1016/j.immuni.2005.06.008 · 2023 Nobel Prize

N1-methylpseudouridine — LNP immune risk is separate from HLA hepatotoxicity

N1-methylpseudouridine modification suppresses endosomal mRNA immune activation in LNP delivery. The Intellia nex-z grade 4 hepatotoxicity was HLA-C*05:01 pharmacogenomic — NOT general LNP immune activation. These are separate risks. For ARPKD LNP-base editor applications, N1-methylpseudouridine is standard and substantially reduces innate immune response.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Hong R et al. EMBO Reports 2024 DOI: 10.1038/s44319-024-00092-y · Fujii R et al. Am J Physiol Renal Physiol 321:F212-F226, 2021. DOI: 10.1152/ajprenal.00045.2021

XIAP → IFT88 → HSC cilia disruption → HSC activation — new cascade node

XIAP degrades IFT88 (intraflagellar transport complex protein) in HSCs → cilia disruption → HSC activation + fibrosis. IFT88 shortening also causes mitochondrial dysfunction. In ARPKD-CHF: FPC traffics to cilia via the IFT complex including IFT88; FPC deficiency impairs cholangiocyte cilia first, then XIAP/IFT88 disrupts HSC cilia as a secondary amplifier. Connects cilia impairment to ICD15/mitochondrial node. Tier 2 — biliary fibrosis adjacent models; not tested in ARPKD specifically.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Yang X et al. Int J Mol Med 58(4) 2026 DOI: 10.3892/ijmm.2026.5949 (review)

p38δ-YAP bile acid axis — second parallel YAP activation route

Taurocholic acid (bile acid) → sphingosine-1-phosphate receptor 2 → p38δ (MAPK13) → YAP dephosphorylation + nuclear translocation → CTGF/CYR61. ARPKD-CHF: bile acid accumulation directly activates YAP via p38δ — parallel to and independent of PAK4/YAP-T428 route. Two convergent upstream inputs to YAP-CTGF fibrosis explains why PAK4 inhibition alone may not fully suppress fibrogenesis. Tier 2 — MAFLD/biliary models.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Yang X et al. Int J Mol Med 58(4), 2026. DOI: 10.3892/ijmm.2026.5949 — BMP-7 counter-regulatory

BMP-7 — endogenous counter-regulatory target for CHF fibrosis

BMP-7 blocks TGF-β1-induced p38 MAPK activation → HSC apoptosis + MET reversal (loss of α-SMA/desmin, reappearance of E-cadherin). Reduced TGF-β1:BMP-7 ratio → decreased p38 MAPK → fibrosis resolution. Not yet tested in ARPKD-CHF. (Yang X et al. Int J Mol Med 58(4), 2026. DOI: 10.3892/ijmm.2026.5949) TGF-β1 → p38 MAPK confirmed as the specific kinase driving integrin-β6 upregulation in cholangiocytes (MMNK-1 biliary cell line). Evidence grade B — multiple hepatic fibrosis models.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Korea Biomedical Review, August 5, 2026 (News — not peer-reviewed) · Son Myung-jin (KRIBB profile)/Daewoong Pharmaceutical · Peer-reviewed platform: Choi SY, Son MJ et al. Toxics 12(5):371, 2024. DOI: 10.3390/toxics12050371 · OECD context: Korea MFDS OECD harmonization press release, 2023

Korean Liver Organoid OECD Regulatory Pathway

South Korea pursuing OECD Test Guideline adoption for liver organoid toxicity testing by April 2028. Multi-cell type 3D organoid models bile excretion pathways (biliary-relevant). Cryopreservable and shippable to international collaborators. If adopted, organoid data from Asai/Morizane/Peltz platforms could be submitted to FDA/EMA for ARPKD drug approvals.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Vunjak-Novakovic G et al. Nature Biomedical Engineering 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01749-w

In Vitro Fibrosis Model Design Criteria + Optogenetic YAP Control

Accepted design criteria: (1) Macrophages required; (2) Fibrotic stiffness; (3) Multi-cell type (cholangiocytes + HSCs + macrophages); (4) ECM markers (collagen, YAP, CTGF). Optogenetic YAP control (Toh PJY et al. EMBO Reports 2022): YAP localization can be precisely controlled with light — since YAP has three independent upstream activators in ARPKD-CHF (PAK4, p38δ, Scribble), optogenetic control enables precise testing of every upstream therapeutic candidate. Directly actionable for Asai's CHF organoid program.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Desplat A et al. J Gen Physiol 153:e202012706, 2021. DOI: 10.1085/jgp.202012706 · Zhang L, Gui X et al. Nat Commun 17:2655, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-70193-w · Fan Q et al. Kidney360 5:715, 2024. DOI: 10.34067/KID.0000000000000453

PIEZO1 → Pannexin1 → ATP → P2X7R — mechanical upstream trigger of NLRP3 cascade

Elevated biliary pressure → PIEZO1 mechanosensitive channel → Pannexin1 → ATP secretion → P2X7R → NLRP3 → IL-1β. Explains HOW biliary obstruction activates NLRP3 at the molecular level. ⚠️ Citation removed — previously attributed to Ishimoto/Germino Nat Commun 2026 (PKHD1/TFAP2B/glaucoma paper), which does not address PIEZO1 or biliary inflammation. Correct primary source needed. ⚠️ KIDNEY/LIVER PARADOX: PIEZO1 activation (Yoda1) is anti-cystogenic in ADPKD kidney cells (Fan Q et al. Kidney360 2024 — 68% cyst reduction) but pro-inflammatory via Pannexin1/P2X7 in biliary cholangiocytes. Systemic PIEZO1 agonism or antagonism has opposite effects in the two affected organs — organ-specific delivery required. Also: Hiratsuka ARPKD organoid-on-chip (Morizane/Lewis, Sci Adv 2022) — RAC1 and FOS identified as mechanosensing therapeutic targets in PKHD1-mutant organoids; R-naproxen (FDA-approved) suppressed ARPKD cysts (NSAIDs contraindicated in ARPKD with renal impairment).

⚠️ UNVERIFIED — Paper not found in search Sep 14, 2026. DOI 10.1038/s41419-026-09158-2 not confirmed. All claims from this source should be treated as unverified pending direct PubMed confirmation.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · He W et al. Cell Death & Disease 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41419-026-09158-2

MASP1→METTL3→HAS2→Hyaluronic acid — ECM fibrosis amplifier connecting to 5 cascade nodes

MASP1 (complement lectin pathway) → METTL3 → m6A methylation of HAS2 → HMW-HA. LMW-HA → CD44-p38-ATF2 → HSC activation. HMW-HA → TLR4-SerpinE1 → prevents HSC senescence → sustained activation. MASP1 knockdown reversed HA metabolism and alleviated fibrosis. ELF test HA component is now mechanistically explained by this axis. Connects to: METTL3 (second mechanism), CD44 (dual-input hub), p38 (4th upstream activator), TLR4 (dual-function hub).

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Malik S, Chandler J, Long DA et al. Science Translational Medicine August 13 2026 DOI: 10.1126/scitranslmed.adv1289

Long lab (UCL GOSH) — kidney-specific LNP delivery via renal artery + ANGPT1 RNA therapy

RNA packaged in lipid nanocomplexes delivered via ultrasound-guided renal artery injection — kidney-specific delivery, bypasses liver. Restores angiopoietin-1 (ANGPT1) in genetic glomerular disease. Mutation-agnostic (targets damaged pathway). ARPKD dual-organ delivery: Liver/biliary = ERCP + KRT19 + ABEmax. Kidney = ultrasound-guided renal artery LNP (Long lab). Both organs now have a delivery strategy. Research question: Is ANGPT1 depleted in Pkhd1-deficient kidney? Gissen (UCL GOSH, liver lentiviral) + Long (UCL GOSH, kidney LNP) = potential intra-UCL dual-organ ARPKD strategy.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Bu HF et al. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 2026 DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00079.2026 · PMID: 42596847

MFG-E8 — basally expressed in cholangiocytes; NLRP3 suppressor; ferroptosis protector

MFG-E8 (Milk Fat Globule-EGF Factor 8) is expressed in cholangiocytes at baseline in normal liver (CK7 spatial confirmation), only shifting to hepatocytes during injury. Five cascade connections: (1) Suppresses NLRP3 inflammasome-induced IL-1β (Deroide JCI 2013); (2) αvβ3/αvβ5 integrin→TGF-β suppression in HSCs; (3) Efferocytosis→prevents HMGB1/TLR4; (4) MFG-E8 deficiency→HDDC3/MESH1 ~8-fold upregulation→ferroptosis susceptibility; (5) MFG-E8 deficiency→TLR4/NF-κB amplification. Research question: Is MFG-E8 reduced in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes?

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Guay-Woodford LM, Gulati A, Caldovic L. Pediatric Nephrology 41:2785-2798, 2026 DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x

FPC functional review — three new cascade connections: hyaluronidase, NEDD4, SRC-STAT3

(1) FPC-NTD TMEM2 homology → hyaluronidase-like activity → FPC deficiency amplifies HA accumulation (connects to MASP1-METTL3-HAS2 cascade); (2) NDFIP2/NEDD4-family E3 ligase dysfunction → TGF-β signaling ↑ (direct molecular FPC→TGF-β connection), RhoA ↑ (cystogenesis), ENaC retention (hypertension); (3) FPC-CTT/SRC/STAT3 suppression — FPC loss derepresses STAT3 (second STAT3 activation route alongside IL-6/SPP1). Source: definitive FPC functional review by world's leading ARPKD researcher.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Germino GG et al. Nature Communications 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-75698-y

PKHD1-TFAP2B genomic architecture — ARPKD patients may have elevated glaucoma risk

Pkhd1del3-67/del3-67 mice develop congenital glaucoma and anterior segment dysgenesis through disruption of TFAP2B regulatory architecture in periocular neural crest-derived cells. PKHD1 and TFAP2B form a regulatory genomic complex. Germino collaborators (Guay-Woodford, Liebau, Chapman, Dahl) searched ARPKD patient cohorts for eye findings. Clinical implication: consider ophthalmologic screening for glaucoma in ARPKD patients.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Brooks A et al. Cell Reports Methods 6, 101545, 2026 DOI: 10.1016/j.crmeth.2026.101545

hMSC liver-on-a-chip — TGR5, CFTR, KRT19 confirmed in human cholangiocytes

Single-source hMSC→cholangiocytes+hepatocytes+endothelial cells. Three-channel microfluidic: sinusoid+hepatocyte spheroid+bile duct. TGR5, CFTR, KRT19, CK7, CK19, SOX9 confirmed in hMSC-cholangiocytes. TGR5 platform for testing odevixibat/SBI-115. CFTR platform for testing elexacaftor. PKHD1-mutant patient hMSCs would create first ARPKD-CHF liver-on-a-chip. Gap: no macrophages (Asai platform complementary).

UPDATED AUGUST 2026 · Galanakis V et al. J Hepatol 84(3):618-630, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021 · Maggiore JC et al. Kidney Int 106(6):1086-1100, 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2024.05.026

Cholangiocyte transdifferentiation — HNF4G is the master regulator (Vallier/Cambridge) · Vascularized kidney organoids (Hukriede/Pittsburgh)

Cholangiocyte→hepatocyte transdifferentiation (Galanakis V, Gribben C, Munteanu A et al., Vallier L corresponding. J Hepatol 84(3):618-630, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021): Intrahepatic cholangiocyte organoids from MASH patients transdifferentiate into biphenotypic cells co-expressing KRT19 and albumin. Full-paper assessment added August 17, 2026. Key mechanistic findings: (1) HNF4G is the master transcription factor driving transdifferentiation — lentiviral HNF4G overexpression significantly increased ALB, APOB, CYP2C9, MRP2 and albumin secretion; HNF4G knockdown abolished hepatocyte marker expression. (2) HNF4G effect is INDEPENDENT of HNF4A — HNF4A expression unchanged by HNF4G overexpression, ruling out HNF4A as an intermediary. This is a new regulatory axis, distinct from HNF4A-driven hepatocyte differentiation. (3) Undifferentiated cholangiocyte organoids (uICOs) have significantly MORE open chromatin than differentiated counterparts across both cholangiocyte and hepatocyte gene loci — hepatocyte genes are already epigenetically primed; transdifferentiation requires a transcription factor switch, not large-scale chromatin remodeling from scratch. (4) Process is NOT dedifferentiation — no foetal markers induced; not stem cell expansion — stem cell markers not predictive of differentiation outcome. (5) HNF4G expression is highest in cholangiocytes at end-stage disease in vivo, validated against MASH patient biopsy snRNAseq data. (6) PI3K/AKT/mTOR is upstream of HNF4G. Tier 2 — confirmed in MASH patient cholangiocytes; not yet tested in PKHD1-deficient cells or ARPKD tissue. ARPKD-CHF hypothesis (Tier 3): FPC-deficient cholangiocytes may express reduced HNF4G → impaired regenerative plasticity → accelerated hepatic failure as the compensatory cholangiocyte-to-hepatocyte regenerative mechanism is lost. Testable in Strazzabosco Pkhd1 mouse model and/or Peltz PKHD1 hepatic organoids. Safety cross-reference: mTOR inhibition suppresses PI3K/AKT/mTOR → would reduce HNF4G in cholangiocytes → third mechanism of biliary harm from mTOR inhibitors (see updated mTOR safety warning). New potential network contact: Ludovic Vallier (ludovic.vallier@bih-charite.de, BIH Berlin) · Vasileios Galanakis (v.galanakis@nhs.net, Cambridge NHS) — not yet in active network.

Vascularized kidney organoids (Maggiore JC, LeGraw R, Przepiorski A et al., Hukriede NA corresponding. Kidney Int 106(6):1086-1100, 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2024.05.026): Genetically inducible ETV2-hiPSC line (1:5 ratio with wild-type hiPSCs, doxycycline days 5-18) generates a robust endothelial network with ~2-fold increase in vessel area and total vessel length, ~3.6-fold increase in junctions. Kidney-specific endothelial subtypes emerge in organoid context (glomerular, arterial, venous). Vascularization drives podocyte maturation (GBM markers, VEGF signaling) and renin-expressing cell emergence without exogenous stimulation. Vessels confirmed to anastomose with mouse vasculature in vivo after renal subcapsular transplantation. Critical gap identified from full-paper assessment (August 17, 2026): The Morizane protocol — used for existing ARPKD organoids — generated only 0.26% endothelial cells vs. 6.9% in vascularized organoids. All current ARPKD kidney organoid models are therefore significantly avascular. Applying iETV2-hiPSCs to PKHD1-mutant iPSC lines (Morizane or Freedman) would yield the first vascularized ARPKD kidney tissue model. Kidney-side complement to SNU vascularized liver organoid. Researcher bridge (now active): Hukriede (Pittsburgh) ↔ Morizane (MGH) — vascularized ARPKD organoid-on-chip is the combination being pursued. Morizane is pursuing translational studies with the longer-term goal of moving toward clinical studies if results are sufficiently promising (Morizane, personal communication, August 2026, permission granted Sep 3, 2026). Specific proposal details not for public sharing at this stage per Morizane's request.

UPDATE AUGUST 19, 2026 — Morizane bioreactor vascularization (Tekguc M et al., Morizane R corresponding. npj Biomed Innov 3:10, February 2, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s44385-025-00063-5): Dr. Morizane's own vascularization approach uses delta-wing stirred bioreactors providing dynamic shear stress → mechanosensory integrin α2β1 → mature human endothelial integration — not a static approach. 50x production efficiency vs conventional culture. scRNA-seq confirmed nephron maturation. Nephron sheet with nephron numbers equivalent to two rat kidneys. This addresses his own critique of ETV2 — noting that without physiological flow, endothelial maturation is limited. His bioreactor provides that flow — making his platform already superior to static ETV2 for mature vascularized ARPKD organoids.

ADDED AUGUST 17, 2026 · Qin Z, Cao L, Liu C et al. (Yang J corresponding). Trends in Biotechnology. April 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.tibtech.2026.00099-5

Scalable multilineage liver organoids via cadherin microspheres — cholangiocytes + hepatocytes + endothelium from MSCs (YAP-dependent)

PLGA microspheres functionalized with epithelial cadherin (E-cadherin) and vascular endothelial cadherin (VE-cadherin) fusion proteins guide MSC self-organization into multilineage liver organoids without genetic engineering. E-cadherin engagement activates YAP, priming MSCs for lineage induction via mechanotransduction. VE-cadherin + VEGF establishes an endothelial niche that drives hepatocyte-cholangiocyte lineage bifurcation. The resulting organoids contain hepatocytes, cholangiocytes, mesenchymal stroma, and endothelial networks, and display clinically relevant drug toxicity responses. The modular design enables spatiotemporal control of cell fate. Tier 2 — healthy MSCs; not tested in PKHD1-deficient cells. ARPKD relevance: (1) Cholangiocyte production from MSCs directly addresses ARPKD-CHF primary pathology. (2) Scalability overcomes the organoid production bottleneck limiting Peltz-style high-throughput ARPKD compound screening — if adapted for patient-specific PKHD1-mutant MSCs, this would be the first scalable ARPKD-CHF liver organoid platform. (3) YAP concern (Tier 3 hypothesis): This platform uses YAP activation as its mechanistic driver of MSC differentiation. FPC-deficient cholangiocytes have chronic YAP hyperactivation. PKHD1-mutant MSCs in this platform may differentiate abnormally or respond differently to cadherin-mediated YAP signaling — this is an untested but important safety consideration for platform adaptation. Researcher bridge: Qin/Yang (China) ↔ Peltz (Stanford) — scalable organoid production + AI-driven PKHD1 compound screening is the combination that does not yet exist.

ADDED AUGUST 17, 2026 · Hogan MC et al. J Mol Med 92(10):1061-1072, 2014. DOI: 10.1007/s00109-014-1185-7 · (Guay-Woodford lab, UAB)

c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile) — predicted splice impact has a published mechanism: PKHD1 missense variants can disrupt exonic splicing enhancers (ESEs) causing exon skipping

Hogan et al. (Guay-Woodford lab) established that PKHD1 missense variants can disrupt ESEs within coding exons, causing pathogenic exon skipping. Demonstrated for R760H (c.2279G>A, PKHD1 exon 22) which abolishes an SRSF5 ESE motif → exon 22 skipping → frameshift → predicted truncation p.V279X. The same G→A transition mechanism that characterizes c.2279G>A (R760H) applies structurally to c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile, Zone 3). Clinical implication: our child's c.10926G>A VUS may function as a truncation (exon skipping → frameshift), not simply a missense — reclassifying it from VUS toward Likely Pathogenic. If confirmed: compound heterozygous truncation (via exon skip at AA 3642) + confirmed pathogenic missense (His3124Tyr, AA 3124), both in Zone 3 = consistent with CHF + portal hypertension phenotype. Therapeutic implication: ABE correction of c.10926G>A would restore BOTH the Met3642 amino acid AND the ESE motif simultaneously — dual correction with a single base edit. ICD15 connection (Tier 3 hypothesis): Exon skipping near AA 3642 is close to the ICD15 C-terminal mitochondrial fragment (studied by Outeda/Walker). Disruption of the exon containing AA 3642 could impair ICD15 processing, adding a mitochondrial dysfunction mechanism to the biliary phenotype — never investigated. What is needed: Minigene assay to confirm exon skipping. The Guay-Woodford lab (HRFD gate) performed this exact experiment for R760H. HRFD enrollment unlocks this laboratory relationship. This finding makes HRFD enrollment the highest-priority action in the project by a wider margin than ever.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Fan Q et al. Kidney360 5:715, 2024. DOI: 10.34067/KID.0000000000000453 · Hiratsuka K et al. Sci Adv 8:eabq3205, 2022. DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.abq3205 · Liao YJ et al. Int J Mol Sci 22:491, 2021. DOI: 10.3390/ijms22020491

PIEZO1 kidney/liver paradox + BHB ketogenic safety warning

PIEZO1 paradox: PIEZO1 activation (Yoda1) ANTI-CYSTOGENIC in kidney (68% cyst reduction — Fan Q et al. Kidney360 5:715-727, 2024. DOI: 10.34067/KID.0000000000000453) but PRO-INFLAMMATORY in biliary cholangiocytes (→Pannexin1→ATP→P2X7R→NLRP3). Systemic PIEZO1 targeting impossible — organ-specific delivery required. Hiratsuka ARPKD organoid: RAC1+FOS as ARPKD mechanosensing targets (Hiratsuka K et al. Sci Adv 8:eabq3205, 2022. DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.abq3205). BHB/Ketogenic safety: High-fat ketogenic diet ENHANCED liver fibrosis in CCl4 models via cholesterol accumulation. BHB supplementation alone is safer than full ketogenic diet for ARPKD-CHF liver. (Liao YJ et al. Int J Mol Sci 22(2):491, 2021. DOI: 10.3390/ijms22020491)

Spot Biosystems demonstrated 11 kb EV delivery — gap to PKHD1 CDS is ~1.2 kb.

Finding 9: ⭐ BEAM-302 — 94% corrected M-AAT as fraction of total AAT, 84% mutant Z-AAT reduction, all patients above protective threshold at up to 12 months — accelerated approval pathway confirmed (company-reported conference data) 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Beam Therapeutics. BEAM-302 Phase 1/2, ERS Congress 2026, September 8, 2026. Tier 1. A→G adenine base editing of E342K missense in SERPINA1 via LNP — identical chemistry to His3124Tyr (c.9370C>T) correction in PKHD1. 29 efficacy-evaluable patients, 38 total dosed: 94% of total AAT comprised corrected M-AAT (M-AAT fraction) with 84% reduction in mutant Z-AAT — all patients above 11 µM protective threshold at up to 12 months follow-up. Note: 94% is the corrected protein fraction of total AAT, not allele editing efficiency. Duration is up to 12 months at March 2026 data cutoff; ERS September 8, 2026 data adds Part B liver disease cohort. (longest human liver ABE durability data); 84% mutant protein reduction; corrected protein rises physiologically during infection — normal gene regulation restored. ⚠️ Source: Beam Therapeutics press release and ERS Congress 2026 presentation — company-reported data, not yet peer-reviewed. Part B specifically enrolls liver disease patients. FDA accelerated approval: biomarker endpoint at 12 months, global pivotal cohort now dosing. ⚠️ Safety flag: Grade 3 ALT/AST in Part B patient with underlying liver disease — third such event across liver-directed gene editing trials. Dedicated monitoring protocol required for ARPKD-CHF patients with elevated baseline liver enzymes. New outreach target: Beam Therapeutics has no ARPKD program and has the exact ABE+LNP platform validated in humans at scale.

⭐⭐ FDA Plausible Mechanism Framework — First formal approval pathway for individualized genome editing in ultra-rare pediatric genetic disease 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

FDA Draft Guidance. February 23, 2026. CBER + CDER joint guidance. Tier 1 — Regulatory. The FDA Plausible Mechanism Framework creates the first formal approval pathway for individualized therapies targeting ultra-rare genetic diseases where randomized controlled trials are not feasible. Applies explicitly to genome editing technologies (CRISPR, base editing) and RNA-based therapies. Inspired by Baby KJ (CPS1 deficiency, CHOP 2024) — confirmed by Drs. Ahrens-Nicklas and Musunuru (CHOP).

Three requirements for approval under the Framework: (1) The therapy directly targets the genetic or molecular cause of disease. (2) The biological mechanism is well-understood and scientifically validated. (3) The therapy successfully modifies the disease-causing pathway. All three are met for PKHD1 His3124Tyr + ABE: single missense with confirmed pathogenic mechanism, FPC loss → cAMP → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis cascade fully mapped, ABE A→G correction confirmed as the correct editing strategy.

ARPKD-CHF implications: (1) His3124Tyr qualifies explicitly — single missense, known mechanism, confirmed ABE substrate, ultra-rare (fewer than 800 patients worldwide with this specific genotype). (2) No large trial required — substantial evidence via plausible mechanism accepted. (3) Baby KJ precedent is the template — N-of-1 genome editing in pediatric ultra-rare disease now has a formal regulatory path. (4) This is the most important regulatory development for ARPKD-CHF gene therapy — more directly applicable than GSD1a (surrogate endpoint), BEAM-302 (biomarker endpoint), or YOLT-101 (LNP delivery) because it applies specifically to individualized ultra-rare genetic disease genome editing. (5) CHOP ecosystem (Ahrens-Nicklas, Musunuru, Guay-Woodford, ARPA-H THRIVE) is now the direct regulatory infrastructure. HRFD enrollment is the entry point.

Researcher routing: Ahrens-Nicklas RC (CHOP) — co-creator of this framework, already in our network via HRFD/Guay-Woodford. Musunuru K (UPenn) — co-author Baby KJ, direct line to CHOP regulatory team. Guay-Woodford L (CHOP) — HRFD PI, gateway to ARPA-H THRIVE ecosystem that funded Baby KJ. HRFD enrollment is now the direct entry point to this regulatory pathway.

Finding 10: ⭐ Gene therapy delivery framework review — validates dual-AAV split-intein for PKHD1 packaging, ABE for transition mutations in nondividing cells, and early intervention as the decisive variable 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Polat M, Akad Dincer S, Terzi YK. "Gene Therapy Approaches for Rare Genetic Diseases: Current Advances, Challenges and Future Perspectives." Gene Therapy — From Molecular Tools to Clinical Applications and Future Horizons. IntechOpen, Chapter online first. DOI: 10.5772/intechopen.1017730. Published September 5, 2026. Open access. Tier 2 — Framework synthesis and validation. Başkent University, Ankara, Turkey.

This peer-reviewed review chapter independently validates the gene therapy delivery landscape as it applies to PKHD1. Does not mention ARPKD directly — but every core technical lesson maps onto our situation with precision.

(1) The PKHD1 packaging problem is formally confirmed. AAV packaging limit ~4.7 kb. PKHD1 coding sequence ~12–16 kb (3× limit). The paper confirms dual-AAV split-intein as the leading workaround: two AAV vectors each carry half of a large therapeutic protein, reconstituting it inside the target cell via protein splicing. Preclinical proof-of-concept established for DMD (~14 kb, same size class as PKHD1). This is the technical pathway any AAV-based PKHD1 gene therapy must use.

(2) ABE is the optimal modality for transition mutations in nondividing cells — confirmed for both of Gianna's variants. Adenine base editors (ABE) convert A→G without a double-strand DNA break, operating efficiently in postmitotic tissues. Both PKHD1 variants are transition mutations: c.9370C>T (His3124Tyr) requires A→G on the antisense strand; c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile) requires A→G on the coding strand. Both are confirmed ABE substrates. The paper explicitly validates ABE in nondividing cells — directly applicable to both renal tubular cells and cholangiocytes.

(3) Threshold effects are real and well-established. The Hemophilia B model proves that correcting only a proportion of hepatocytes producing a secreted protein provides durable clinical benefit — you do not need 100% correction. For PKHD1-CHF, partial fibrocystin restoration in biliary epithelial cells may be sufficient to interrupt the SRC/STAT3 cystogenic cascade. This principle underlies the Krappitz/Kaminski 5% liver correction → significant cyst reduction finding (Finding 6) and the BEAM-302 data (Finding 9).

(4) Early intervention is the single most important variable — supersedes vector potency. The SMA/Zolgensma case is the explicit model: treating before irreversible motor neuron loss determines outcome. Incremental dose increases after cell loss provide marginal benefit. For ARPKD, where renal cystogenesis and biliary fibrosis begin prenatally, the same logic applies: presymptomatic neonatal intervention should be the therapeutic goal. This is the scientific foundation for every urgency around HRFD enrollment and the CHOP ARPA-H THRIVE ecosystem.

(5) N-of-1 regulatory pathway is viable — this paper and the FDA Plausible Mechanism Framework (Finding FDA above) converge. The paper documents that individualized genomic medicine and N-of-1 clinical investigations for ultra-rare conditions now have validated safety frameworks (variant-aware computational algorithms, long-read sequencing, patient-specific primary cell testing). Combined with the FDA Plausible Mechanism Framework, this confirms no large randomized trial is required for PKHD1 His3124Tyr ABE.

⚠️ Safety note carried forward: Grade 3 ALT/AST transaminase elevations documented in liver disease patients receiving ABE+LNP (Finding 9, BEAM-302). ARPKD-CHF patients have elevated baseline liver enzymes. A dedicated hepatic monitoring protocol is required in any future PKHD1 ABE trial.

Network routing — Sep 25 follow-ups: Krappitz (CU Anschutz — ABEmax for PKHD1; this paper validates his approach and confirms both variants are ABE substrates); Li (Mayo — kidney-specific ABE); Kaminski (MDC Berlin — ciliopathy base editing); Long (UCL — liver LNP; SORT technology section directly supports his STM 2026 work and the upcoming TAKA grant LOI); Williams (Stony Brook — polymer nanocarriers; Section 3.2.2 validates his platform; Oct 9 follow-up).

Finding 11: ⭐ HITI gene correction — 100× more efficient than HDR in adult liver, mutation-agnostic, durable through native locus integration 📌 NEW · Sep 14, 2026

Ginn SL, Doroudian F, Christina S, Chan OPY, Lucas CW, Zhu E, Yang SF, Devanapalli B, Klein AH, Scott S, Vitale J, Cunningham SC, Liao SHY, Cabanes-Creus M, Lisowski L, Alexander IE. Molecular Therapy 34(9):5426–5443, September 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2026.06.044. Open access (CC BY). Full paper read September 14, 2026. Children's Medical Research Institute, The University of Sydney, Westmead, NSW, Australia. Tier 2 — Framework validation and extension. Not ARPKD-specific but directly translatable. Contact: sginn@cmri.org.au

Homology-Independent Targeted Integration (HITI) using dual-AAV CRISPR-Cas9 repairs a faulty gene directly at its natural chromosomal location, capturing the gene's own regulatory machinery. Tested in OTC deficiency (urea cycle disorder, 500+ causative mutations — identical mutation-number challenge to PKHD1).

Five findings directly relevant to ARPKD gene therapy:

(1) 100× more efficient than HDR in adult liver. HDR achieves 0.2–0.3% correction in adult hepatocytes (cycling-cell dependent). HITI via NHEJ achieved 24–40% correction in adult mouse liver and 48% in patient-derived human hepatocytes — a 100-fold improvement. Cholangiocytes and renal tubular cells rarely divide. HITI is specifically designed for non-dividing adult tissues.

(2) Mutation-agnostic. One universal construct treats all mutations in the target gene. PKHD1 has 500+ pathogenic variants. HITI uses a promoterless donor cassette targeted to the first intron — the same construct corrects any downstream mutation regardless of which exon is affected.

(3) Durable through native locus integration. Conventional gene therapy adds an episomal copy that is diluted as children grow. HITI corrects at the chromosomal locus — corrected cells pass the repair to daughter cells. This directly solves the pediatric durability problem.

(4) Native promoter capture ensures physiological expression. The promoterless cassette integrates and borrows the gene's own regulatory machinery — tissue-specific expression in the correct cells at physiological levels. For PKHD1: fibrocystin expressed specifically in cholangiocytes and collecting duct cells under its own promoter.

(5) Human patient-derived cells confirmed. 48% correction in OTC-deficient patient-derived primary human hepatocytes — authors state this would provide therapeutic benefit if recapitulated clinically. The paper explicitly frames broader implications for all liver diseases with high therapeutic thresholds.

⚠️ Critical caveat for ARPKD-CHF patients with cholangiopathy: Delivery vehicle is dual AAV (AAV8/SYD12). Liver-directed AAV has caused deaths in children with pre-existing cholangiopathy (Krappitz personal communication, Sep 11, 2026). HITI is a technique — not a delivery system. Combining HITI + LNP delivery (Long/Williams/Gissen) would address both the PKHD1 therapeutic challenge and the AAV safety contraindication in cholangiopathy patients. ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — Neither team knows the other exists in this context: Ginn/Alexander (CMRI Sydney) have the optimal editing technology for adult non-dividing biliary cells but use AAV which is contraindicated in cholangiopathy. Long/Gissen (UCL) have LNP liver delivery platforms but have not applied them to gene correction in biliary disease. The Sep 25 email to Long should explicitly name this: "There is now a published HITI gene correction approach achieving 100× the efficiency of HDR in adult human liver cells using a dual-vector system — but it uses AAV, which is contraindicated for cholangiopathy patients like Gianna. Your LNP delivery platform combined with the HITI technique from Dr. Samantha Ginn at CMRI Sydney would together constitute the first complete non-AAV, durable, mutation-agnostic gene correction system for pediatric biliary disease. Would you be open to an introduction?" Contact: sginn@cmri.org.au.

⚠️ Exon 1 limitation: Donor cassette starts at exon 2 — exon 1 and upstream regulatory mutations are not corrected. Neither of Gianna's variants (c.9370C>T His3124Tyr; c.10926G>A Met3642Ile) is in exon 1 — this limitation does not apply to her.

⚠️ Imperfect integration noted: A significant proportion of integration events were configured in complex arrangements — honest limitation flagged by the authors for further optimization.

New network contacts: Samantha Ginn (sginn@cmri.org.au — lead researcher, CMRI Sydney, cold contact appropriate); Ian Alexander (ialexander@cmri.org.au — PI/senior author). Routing: Krappitz (complementary — ABE for specific transitions, HITI for any mutation); Long (LNP bridge for cholangiopathy-safe delivery of HITI approach). ⭐ NEW CONTACT Sep 14, 2026 — Nabil Djouder (CNIO Madrid): Djouder published the FXR→YAP biliary identity paper (Nat Metab 2026, DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z). His lab is the world's only group studying FXR-YAP specifically in biliary epithelial cells. He almost certainly does not know that ARPKD/PKHD1 loss impairs this exact pathway. Email after Krappitz permission resolved. Approach: "Your 2026 Nature Metabolism paper demonstrates that FXR→YAP protects biliary epithelial cell identity. There is a rare pediatric disease — ARPKD-CHF — in which fibrocystin loss appears to impair biliary FXR signaling. Does your model predict whether FXR-deficient ARPKD biliary cells would respond to FXR agonists or whether the downstream pathway has rewired permanently?" Cold contact, no gate.

Finding 13: ⭐⭐ David Liu lab — In vivo ABE rescues rare pediatric liver disease with cholestasis and fibrosis via both AAV and LNP · most important base editing precedent for ARPKD-CHF (April 2026) 📌 NEW · Sep 14, 2026

Gao XD, Presa M, Duby JE, Hsu A, Banskota S, Jiang AY, Newby GA, Levy JM et al. Liu DR (senior/corresponding). Howard Hughes Medical Institute + Harvard University. Nature Biomedical Engineering, April 14, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01651-5. Open access. Tier 1 — Not ARPKD-specific but mutation-class and disease-phenotype matched. Most prestigious gene therapy precedent in framework. David R. Liu = inventor of adenine base editing.

Disease and mutation: Zellweger Spectrum Disorder (ZSD), caused by PEX1-p.G843D (c.2528G>A) — a G→A transition present in ~30% of ZSD alleles. Causes peroxisome biogenesis failure → fatty liver, chronic cholestasis, progressive hepatic fibrosis, portal hypertension, potential cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma. Clinical phenotype substantially overlaps ARPKD-CHF: both are rare pediatric genetic liver diseases with biliary involvement, cholestasis, progressive fibrosis, and hepatomegaly.

Key results — numbers that directly translate to ARPKD-CHF planning:

(1) >80% correction in patient-derived human fibroblasts (ex vivo, ABE8e-V106W mRNA + gRNA, electroporation). Peroxisome homeostasis fully restored confirmed by catalase/ABCD3 co-localization and GFP-PTS1 punctate structure formation.

(2) Up to 60% bulk liver correction via AAV9 (neonatal, in vivo). Since hepatocytes = ~60% of liver cells, this implies robust correction in the majority of hepatocytes. Complete liver transcriptome normalization — zero differentially expressed genes vs. wildtype at 16 weeks for optimal doses. Eliminated toxic VLCFAs, BCFAs, and C27-bile acid intermediates. Rescued liver steatosis and glycogen metabolism.

(3) 27% correction via LNP delivery (OF-02 LNP formulation, 4-week-old mice, non-viral). 7.6-fold increase in peroxisomal membrane protein (ABCD3). Partial rescue of liver steatosis and full glycogen restoration. For Gianna specifically: LNP is the required delivery route (AAV contraindicated in cholangiopathy). This paper demonstrates LNP-ABE achieves clinically relevant functional rescue in pediatric liver disease even at 27% efficiency — supporting the entire LNP strategy.

(4) Selective advantage of corrected cells: 29% → 53% correction over 10 weeks at the lowest dose. Corrected cells outcompeted uncorrected cells. If this applies to PKHD1-corrected biliary cells — and there's no reason it wouldn't (functional fibrocystin gives cells survival advantage over dysfunctional ones) — then modest initial LNP correction efficiency will grow to therapeutic levels without repeat dosing. This is the most important single data point for ARPKD-CHF LNP planning.

(5) Minimal off-target editing with ABE8e-V106W (the high-fidelity version with V106W substitution). Two intergenic sites at ≤0.35% above background in mouse liver. Suitable pediatric liver delivery safety profile.

Why G→A mutation class matters for Gianna: PEX1-p.G843D is c.2528G→A. Gianna's Met3642Ile is c.10926G→A. Identical mutation class — both ABE substrates. The editor used here (ABE8e-V106W) is the same class of editor Krappitz is proposing for Gianna's variants. Liu's paper confirms this editor class achieves high correction efficiency for G→A transitions in human liver disease.

The FDA precedent stack is now three deep: Baby KJ (CPS1 deficiency, UPENN/CHOP 2025) → ZSD (Liu lab, Nature Biomed Eng April 2026) → ARPKD-CHF (next). Three independent rare pediatric liver diseases with G→A mutations, each corrected by ABE. The FDA Plausible Mechanism Pathway (Draft Guidance Feb 23, 2026) was designed for exactly this sequence.

New open question — peroxisomes and FPC/ICD15: ZSD disrupts peroxisome biogenesis. Our framework's Walker 2023 paper found FPC's ICD15 fragment targets mitochondria. Peroxisomes and mitochondria are functionally coupled — both participate in fatty acid oxidation and ROS metabolism. Does FPC deficiency also affect peroxisomal function? If PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes have peroxisomal abnormalities alongside mitochondrial dysfunction, C27-bile acid intermediate accumulation seen in ZSD may have a partial equivalent in ARPKD-CHF biliary disease. Completely unexplored.

Routing: This paper goes directly to Krappitz (CU Anschutz) — ask whether he is aware of Liu's ZSD paper and whether he has connections to Liu's group. Krappitz's proposed ABE testing of Gianna's variants is now backed by the most prestigious base editing lab in the world demonstrating the same approach in pediatric liver disease. David R. Liu (HHMI/Harvard) — approach through Krappitz, not cold contact directly.

Finding 12: ⭐ FL0445-LNP + Cap-cirRNA — 10-fold mRNA efficiency, negligible inflammation, branched architecture carries large payloads — most advanced LNP platform for ARPKD gene delivery (August 2026) 📌 NEW · Sep 14, 2026

Kimura S, Abe H et al. Nagoya University + FUJIFILM Corporation Bioscience & Engineering Laboratories. Cell Biomaterials DOI: 10.1016/j.celbio.2026.100555. Published August 19, 2026. Contacts: abe.hiroshi.p4@f.mail.nagoya-u.ac.jp · kimura.seigo.v9@f.mail.nagoya-u.ac.jp Tier 2 — Not ARPKD-specific but directly addresses the core LNP delivery problem. FUJIFILM industrial partnership signals real-world manufacturing pathway.

Two innovations simultaneously:

(1) FL0445-LNP — Branched biodegradable lipid architecture. Conventional LNPs use linear lipid chains. FL0445-LNP uses branched biodegradable chains, adding internal flexibility that allows it to carry a wider variety of payloads — light, heavy, and structurally different nucleic acids. PKHD1 mRNA is enormous (12+ kilobases, one of the largest therapeutic genes anyone has attempted to deliver). The architectural flexibility of branched chains is specifically what would be needed to carry PKHD1-encoding payload. Result: 10-fold increase in mRNA activity with negligible inflammatory response vs. conventional LNPs. The negligible inflammation is critical for cholangiopathy patients like Gianna, who cannot tolerate the immune activation that can accompany current LNP platforms.

(2) Cap-cirRNA — Circular RNA with a translation cap. Standard mRNA is a linear strand. Degradation enzymes attack both ends, limiting expression to hours or days — hence the need for repeat dosing in LNP gene therapy. Circular RNA (cirRNA) has no ends — it is a loop — so degradation enzymes have no entry point. Expression is dramatically more durable. The historical problem: circular RNA translates proteins less efficiently because it lacks the cap-end that guides ribosomes. Cap-cirRNA adds an engineered cap to the loop, combining the durability of cirRNA with the translation efficiency of linear mRNA. For ARPKD-CHF: fibrocystin-encoding Cap-cirRNA delivered via FL0445-LNP could produce functional FPC protein in biliary cells over an extended period from a single dose — a protein supplementation approach that does not require correcting the underlying PKHD1 mutation.

Four direct connections to ARPKD delivery problem:

(1) 10-fold efficiency amplifies limited biliary uptake. Even if only 5% of FL0445-LNPs reach cholangiocytes instead of hepatocytes, a 10-fold activity increase means that 5% now achieves what 50% conventional uptake used to require. The targeting problem and the efficiency problem are partially separable — better efficiency reduces how precise targeting needs to be. (2) Large payload capacity addresses PKHD1 size. PKHD1 mRNA is 12+ kb. Conventional LNP packaging limits have excluded large genes. Branched lipid flexibility may extend this range. (3) Negligible inflammatory response is essential for cholangiopathy. Standard LNPs activate innate immune responses. In children with pre-existing biliary inflammation, this is a serious safety concern. FL0445-LNP's negligible inflammatory profile may make it suitable where conventional LNPs are not. (4) FUJIFILM partnership signals manufacturing viability. Academic-only LNP platforms rarely reach clinical translation. FUJIFILM has commercial lipid manufacturing infrastructure — this platform is on a real development pathway.

⚠️ What this paper does NOT solve: Cholangiocyte-specific targeting. The paper demonstrated delivery in mice for GLP-1 — liver hepatocytes, not specifically biliary cells. FL0445-LNP is a better delivery truck but still drives to the same neighborhood. Biliary-specific targeting requires surface ligands that bind cholangiocyte-specific receptors. This is exactly what Marshall (QMUL/Barts — A20FMDV2/αvβ6) and Janowski (UMD — hepatic arterial fractionation) are working on. ⚠️ CORRECTION Sep 20, 2026: Williams (Stony Brook) is kidney track only — his 350 nm PLGA-PEG MNPs are structurally excluded from liver by sinusoidal fenestrations. He is NOT part of the biliary delivery solution. FL0445-LNP + Marshall αvβ6 targeting + Janowski arterial route = the complete biliary delivery system.

⭐ Three-way convergence now confirmed (Sep 14, 2026): Three independent 2026 advances converging on the polymer nanocarrier delivery problem for pediatric kidney gene therapy: Long/Gissen (UCL STM 2026) — LNP targeting to collecting duct cells; Williams (Stony Brook) — lipid-free PLGA-PEG polymer nanocarriers for ARPKD kidney proximal tubule (⚠️ kidney track only — 350 nm structurally excludes liver sinusoidal fenestrations); Abe/Kimura/FUJIFILM (Cell Biomaterials 2026) — branched LNP + Cap-cirRNA 10-fold efficiency. ⭐ WILLIAMS PLATFORM CORRECTION (Sep 20, 2026): Williams' MNPs target renal proximal tubule epithelium specifically — NOT biliary cells. His platform is kidney track only. For the biliary delivery problem (ARPKD-CHF liver), the relevant convergence is Janowski (hepatic arterial route) + Marshall (αvβ6/A20FMDV2 targeting) — a separate two-part system. Williams' lipid-free polymer platform connects to Janowski's argument that polymer carriers are the alternative to ionizable LNPs for LSEC-compromised patients — but in the kidney compartment, not the biliary one.

Open question for Abe (priority first contact): Has FL0445-LNP been tested in biliary or cholangiocyte cell lines? The paper tested GLP-1 delivery but does not specify which liver cell types took it up. If they have unpublished cholangiocyte uptake data, that changes the entire picture.

New network contacts: Hiroshi Abe (abe.hiroshi.p4@f.mail.nagoya-u.ac.jp — PI, Nagoya University, cold contact appropriate); Seigo Kimura (kimura.seigo.v9@f.mail.nagoya-u.ac.jp — lead author/assistant professor). Routing: Long (UCL — his LNP work + this platform = direct connection); Williams (Stony Brook — polymer nanocarrier work + cirRNA payload = direct connection); Krappitz (CU Anschutz — ABE delivery via this platform instead of conventional LNP).

Finding 12b: ⭐ MIMAC — Microfluidic restructuring of preformed LNPs boosts cellular ATP 4.2-fold before therapeutic mRNA translation — compatible with all RNA types and lipid formulations (September 22, 2026) 📌 NEW · Sep 22, 2026

Zheng Z, Jiang Y, Gong Y et al., Zhang L and Xu J (corresponding). "Microfluidics-mediated spatial control of mRNA lipid nanoparticles primes translation and enhances vaccine potency." Nature Biomedical Engineering. Published September 22, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01796-3. Institutions: CAS National Center for Nanoscience (Beijing), Jiaxing University, Shanghai Virogin Biotech, US FDA (Silver Spring), University of Waterloo, Harvard Medical School (Blavatnik Institute). Tier 2 — Not ARPKD-specific. All findings below are from vaccine studies (HPV cancer, SARS-CoV-2). Relevance to ARPKD-CHF gene therapy is hypothesis, not confirmed.

What MIMAC does (confirmed from abstract): A microfluidics-based method that restructures preformed LNPs by inserting a metabolic-enhancing RNA — specifically NADH dehydrogenase (ND) mRNA, which catalyzes NADH→NAD+ and drives ATP generation — into an internal peripheral compartment, while relocating the therapeutic RNA towards the core. This creates a defined peripheral-to-core arrangement enabling sequential cytosolic release: the ND mRNA releases first, elevates cellular ATP levels up to 4.2-fold, and then the therapeutic RNA releases into a cell that now has sufficient energy to translate it efficiently. Compatible with linear, circular, and self-amplifying RNA and plasmid DNA, and with varied lipid formulations. A benchtop device produces 200 doses per hour.

Results confirmed from abstract (vaccine contexts only — not gene therapy):
· HPV cancer vaccine in syngeneic mouse model: tumour growth suppressed by 89.2%, increased survival
· SARS-CoV-2 vaccines: antibody titres boosted 62.3- to 174.8-fold, equivalent potency at one-tenth the normal dose

⚠️ HYPOTHESIS — NOT CONFIRMED: Possible relevance to ARPKD-CHF
The following is our inference from the framework. It has NOT been tested in ARPKD, in cholangiocytes, or in any gene therapy context.

Our framework documents a mitochondrial dysfunction axis in ARPKD: FPC loss → ICD15 fragment not generated → mitochondria unsupported → AMP:ATP ratio altered → AMPK dysregulation (Walker et al. Nat Commun 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4). Whether this translates to reduced ATP availability in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes specifically has NOT been directly measured or published. If ARPKD-CHF cholangiocytes do have reduced cellular ATP as a consequence of mitochondrial dysfunction, the MIMAC metabolic priming approach could address this bottleneck for therapeutic mRNA translation in exactly those cells. This is a hypothesis requiring direct measurement in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells — it is not supported by the Zheng et al. paper itself, which was conducted in immune and tumour models.

Open question for the framework: Are cellular ATP levels reduced in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes compared to wild-type? If yes, MIMAC metabolic priming would be especially relevant for biliary mRNA delivery. If no, MIMAC still improves translation efficiency via the same mechanism but without the disease-specific argument. This question could be addressed by Strazzabosco (Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse) or Morizane (human PKHD1-deficient organoids) without requiring any additional research infrastructure.

What this paper does not address: Cholangiocyte-specific targeting (solved separately by Marshall/A20FMDV2), arterial delivery safety (Janowski), or gene correction vs. protein supplementation. MIMAC improves what happens inside the cell after delivery — it does not solve getting the particle to the right cell in the first place.

Routing: Mitchell (UPenn — BA-LNP targeting + MIMAC efficiency = potentially combinable); Abe/Kimura (Nagoya/FUJIFILM — FL0445-LNP efficiency + MIMAC efficiency = two orthogonal approaches to the same problem — which performs better in biliary cells?); Morizane and Strazzabosco (ATP measurement in PKHD1-deficient organoids = the falsification test for the ARPKD-CHF hypothesis).

Finding 3: Novel compound heterozygous PKHD1 mutations — Chinese ARPKD pedigree + 605-variant hepatobiliary sensitivity analysis 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Zhao J, Yu K, Huang Y. Front Med 13:1741795, 2026. DOI: 10.3389/fmed.2026.1741795. Shanghai Children's Hospital. Full re-evaluation Sep 8, 2026 — contains three items of genuine framework value.

Case — G8 domain variants → liver-predominant ARPKD at age 5: Proband: 5-year-old boy, compound heterozygous for c.5850_5851insTTCAT (p.Gly1951Phefs*25, frameshift IN the first G8 domain, Zone 2/3) + c.8710G>A (p.Glu2904Lys, missense ADJACENT TO second G8 domain, Zone 3). Presentation: significant hepatosplenomegaly, hepatic dysfunction, MRI showing liver cirrhosis features at age 5, while renal function remained compensated. Both alleles map to G8 domains — authors hypothesize G8 domain integrity is specifically important for hepatobiliary system development.

⭐ 605-variant genotype-phenotype analysis — hepatobiliary system is more sensitive to PKHD1 deficiency than kidney:

  • Caroli disease (isolated bile duct dilation, no kidney cysts): 3 patients — all compound heterozygous missense mutations. No truncating mutation required for liver-only disease.
  • Polycystic liver disease (PCLD, isolated, no kidney): 4 patients — all heterozygous loss-of-function (frameshift/nonsense/splice). A single truncating allele is sufficient for hepatobiliary disease while kidneys remain unaffected.
  • Von Meyenburg complexes (VMC): 2 patients — heterozygous.
  • Conclusion: "The hepatobiliary system may be more sensitive to PKHD1 mutations compared to renal phenotypes." Liver disease does NOT require biallelic loss-of-function — a single allele can cause hepatobiliary disease while kidneys are spared.

Framework implication: This supports why even partial FPC restoration — via FPCct protein domain delivery, ASO therapy, CFTR correction, or any approach that partially restores FPC function — could have disproportionate benefit in liver vs kidney. The liver is more sensitive; the therapeutic threshold is lower. This also suggests that our daughter's compound heterozygous genotype (one Zone 3 missense + one Zone 3 missense) may produce liver disease even with residual partial FPC function from both alleles.

PC1/PC2 compensation hypothesis: Authors propose kidney is relatively spared because polycystin-1/polycystin-2 can partially compensate for FPC dysfunction in collecting duct development — but the biliary system lacks this compensatory mechanism. Consistent with our framework's FPC/PC2 interaction node (Wang et al. 2007).

Note on Glu2904Lys (AA 2904) vs our daughter's variants: Glu2904 is in the extracellular domain (Zone 3 boundary), NOT in the C-terminal intracellular domain where FPCct sequesters SRC. This mutation impairs extracellular adhesion/receptor function rather than the SRC-suppression mechanism. Our daughter's His3124Tyr (AA 3124, IPT domain) and Met3642Ile (AA 3642, extracellular Zone 3 — proximal to TM domain; corrected Sep 11, 2026: UniProt confirms AA 3642 is extracellular, not in TM domain) are deeper in Zone 3 and more proximal to the CTT SRC-sequestration site.

Section 14: Patient-Specific Variant Analysis

Confirmed PKHD1 variants — Nathan and Flora Johnson's daughter

c.10926G>A (p.Met3642Ile) — VUS per PreventionGenetics; predicted aberrant splicing acceptor site (Alamut v2.7); see also PMID: 41751613 — ACMG PM3 in ARPKD; found with truncating PKHD1 variant in another patient. ⭐ Research evaluation note (September 2, 2026): Dr. Peter C. This is a research evaluation only, not a clinical reclassification. Personal communication — Dr. Peter C. Harris, Mayo Clinic, September 2026.

Why liver disease is primary — the genotype explanation

Burgmaier K et al. Kidney Int 99(3):670-682, 2021. DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2020.09.018 — 304 ARegPKD patients:

  • AA 709–1837: milder renal disease
  • AA 1838–2624: better hepatic outcome
  • AA 2625–4074: POORER hepatic outcome

His3124Tyr = AA 3124 → Zone 3. Met3642Ile = AA 3642 → Zone 3. Both variants in the worst hepatic outcome zone. (Lapunzina-Soler P et al. Genes 17(2):229, 2026. DOI: 10.3390/genes17020229) Both confirm why CHF and portal hypertension dominate over renal disease. His3124Tyr is a hypomorphic allele (missense, not truncation) — survival to childhood is indirect evidence of residual fibrocystin function.

Citations: IPT domain structure & function, TM domain (AA 3859-3881), PC2 binding, CTS, MLS/ICD15: Gulati, Caldovic, Guay-Woodford — Pediatr Nephrol 2026 · Bannell & Weimbs — Ann Hum Genet 2024 · His3124Tyr IPT disruption: Furu et al. — JASN 2003 · ICD15 mitochondrial targeting: Walker, Qian et al. — Nat Commun 2023 · PC2/Ca²⁺ signaling complex: Ward et al. — Hum Mol Genet 2003 (PMID 12925574) · Zone 3 (AA 2625-4074) poorer hepatic outcome: Burgmaier K et al. ARegPKD consortium — Kidney Int 100:650-659, 2021 (PMID 33940108). "Refining genotype-phenotype correlations in 304 patients with ARPKD and PKHD1 gene variants." This is the source paper establishing that AA 2625-4074 variants → more severe liver disease. Gianna's His3124Tyr (AA 3124) and Met3642Ile (AA 3642) are both in this zone. Confirmed from Gulati/Guay-Woodford Pediatr Nephrol 2026, ref [41].

📌 Published case: juvenile-onset liver-predominant ARPKD with compound heterozygous variants including VUS (Seattle Children's, Front. Pediatr. 2025)

Source: Marquez J et al. Front Pediatr 13:1677417, 2025. DOI: 10.3389/fped.2025.1677417. University of Washington / Seattle Children's. Open access.

  • 14-year-old male: Leu2665Pro (pathogenic, maternal) + Gln905Lys (novel VUS, paternal) confirmed IN TRANS. Juvenile-onset, liver-predominant ARPKD — hepatic cysts at 8 months, kidney cysts at age 14. (Marquez J et al. Front Pediatr 13:1677417, 2025. DOI: 10.3389/fped.2025.1677417)
  • Seattle Children's proceeded with ARPKD diagnosis and management despite the VUS — published precedent for this approach.
  • Truncating variant at same amino acid (Gln905TER) causes severe neonatal ARPKD. Missense (Gln905LYS) causes juvenile-onset. Maps to Met3642Ile: pure missense → liver-predominant; exon skipping → truncating-equivalent → more severe. Urine RNA analysis determines this.
  • Independently cites Molinari E et al. JASN 31:2422, 2020. DOI: 10.1681/ASN.2020010010. PMID: 33059616 (urine renal epithelial cells for PKHD1 VUS confirmation) and Guay-Woodford LM et al. Am J Kidney Dis 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.ajkd.2025.01.022 as management standards.

⭐ HIGHEST PRIORITY CLINICAL ACTION — PM3 reclassification for Met3642Ile

Source: PMID: 41751613, 2026 (68 ARPKD patients, phase-aware ACMG). When a VUS is found IN TRANS with a confirmed pathogenic variant in clinical ARPKD, ACMG PM3 criterion applies → VUS upgrades toward Likely Pathogenic.

  1. Confirm variants are in trans via parental testing (standard blood draw) — if confirmed, PM3 reclassification can be requested at the treating institution.
  2. Urine-derived tubular epithelial cell RNA analysis to confirm whether c.10926G>A causes aberrant splicing (non-invasive, no biopsy needed). Validated by multiple independent sources including Molinari E et al. JASN 31(10):2422-2438, 2020. DOI: 10.1681/ASN.2020010010. PMID: 33059616.
  3. Minigene assay — gate: HRFD enrollment (contact guaywoodfl@chop.edu, REDCap YY3AJN93T). (Guay-Woodford LM et al. Am J Kidney Dis 2026. DOI: 10.1053/j.ajkd.2025.01.022 — PKDNet/HRFD management standards)

Convergent therapeutic targets

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · LiverTox 2026; ZYIL1 NCT05186051; Youm YH Nat Med 2015; Liao YJ IJMS 2021

NLRP3 clinical pipeline safety update — MCC950 contraindicated; ZYIL1 safe; BHB paradox

MCC950 — caution in ARPKD-CHF.
Confirmed: MCC950 (also CRID3, CP-456773) was discontinued in a Phase II rheumatoid arthritis trial due to elevated transaminases indicating hepatotoxicity. The toxicity is dose-dependent — reported at high doses (1200 mg total daily dose, >100 μM plasma Cmax) — and is attributed to the furan ring in the molecule. Next-generation inhibitors engineered to remove that liability are in development: Inzomelid (IZD174/MCC7840), Somalix (IZD334), ZYIL1 (NCT05186051), the NT series, emlenoflast and selnoflast. (LiverTox 2026)
⚠️ What we had missed: MCC950 is repeatedly reported to be hepatoPROTECTIVE in preclinical liver disease — alleviating chronic cholestatic liver injury (Qu J, Yuan Z, Wang G et al. Int Immunopharmacol 70:147-155, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.intimp.2019.02.016. PMID: 30802677 — BDL cholestatic mice: ↓ hepatic inflammation, injury, cholestasis, fibrosis; confirmed in Reiberger et al. J Hepatol 2024 review as Table 2 reference [154]), fulminant hepatitis and liver fibrosis, and reducing ALT/AST with M1→M2 macrophage repolarisation in CCl₄-induced acute liver injury. The accurate statement is not "MCC950 damages livers" but "MCC950 is protective at research doses in animal liver disease, and produced transaminase elevation in humans at high clinical doses."
Position for ARPKD-CHF: caution remains justified in a child with pre-existing cholangiopathy and elevated baseline transaminases — a drug whose dose-limiting toxicity is hepatic is a poor fit when the liver is the organ of concern. But the NLRP3 target itself is not discredited. The next-generation inhibitors above exist precisely to separate NLRP3 inhibition from furan-ring hepatotoxicity, and they are the appropriate candidates to evaluate rather than abandoning the target. ZYIL1 (Zydus Lifesciences) — CAPS Phase 2 positive with NO liver or kidney toxicity — safest clinical NLRP3 inhibitor for ARPKD evaluation. (NCT05186051). BHB/Ketogenic diet safety paradox: BHB (ketone body) alone → NLRP3 inhibition (Youm YH et al. Nat Med 21:263-269, 2015. DOI: 10.1038/nm.3804). Full high-fat ketogenic diet → enhanced liver fibrosis via cholesterol accumulation in CCl4 models (Liao YJ et al. Int J Mol Sci 22(2):491, 2021. DOI: 10.3390/ijms22020491). For ARPKD-CHF: BHB supplementation alone is safer than full ketogenic diet.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Nature Communications May 2025 DOI: 10.1038/s41467-025-59724-z · Sci Rep 11:9291, 2021. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-021-88840-5

VDR/Vitamin D → TXNIP in ductular cells — directly biliary, immediately actionable

Vitamin D supplementation attenuated ductular reaction and liver fibrosis via VDR/TXNIP axis specifically in ductular cells (DDC cholestatic mice, Nature Comms 2025). In biliary atresia children — same patient age range as ARPKD-CHF — calcitriol inhibited primary HSC proliferation. VDR also disrupts NET-HSC activation via FAPα suppression (connects to Strazzabosco neutrophil paper). ⚠️ CLINICAL NOTE — IMMEDIATELY ACTIONABLE: Cholestasis causes fat-soluble vitamin malabsorption. ARPKD-CHF patients are at high risk of vitamin D deficiency. Vitamin D monitoring (25-OH-D) and supplementation can begin at the next clinical appointment.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 — Cholangitis as fibrosis accelerator; Elafibranor ELMWOOD/ELASCOPE; Nebokitug CCL24; NorUDCA Phase 3; FXR→NLRP3

New PSC biliary therapeutics + cascade connections

Cholangitis = fibrosis accelerator: Each bacterial cholangitis episode → LPS → TLR4 → NF-κB → NLRP3 → IL-1β + STAT3 → HSC activation. Aggressive cholangitis management is directly anti-fibrotic. Seki E et al. Hepatology 47(3):1089-1091, 2008. DOI: 10.1002/hep.22232 (LPS→TLR4→NF-κB→HSC — foundational paper) · Dong Z et al. Ann Transl Med 8(5):168, 2020. DOI: 10.21037/atm.2020.02.21. PMID: 32309315 (TLR4→NF-κB→NLRP3 in HSCs).

TAK-242 (resatorvid, TLR4 inhibitor) — cholestatic model data: TAK-242 specifically suppressed liver injury induced by biliary obstruction (BDL) + intraportal LPS in rats: ↓ hepatic inflammation, injury and mortality. This directly applies to ARPKD-CHF where cholangitis episodes deliver LPS through a compromised biliary system to the portal circulation. Oya S, Yokoyama Y, Kokuryo T et al. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 306(3):G244-252, 2014. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00366.2013. PMID: 24371015 (confirmed in Reiberger et al. J Hepatol 2024 review, Table 2 reference [168]). Clinical translation: An RCT of TAK-242 in alcohol-related cirrhosis with ACLF is ongoing (NCT04620148 — status confirmed as ongoing in Reiberger et al. J Hepatol 2024 review; verify current status). ⚠️ Not yet tested in ARPKD or any non-cirrhotic/biliary fibrocystic model.

⚠️ Atypical cholangitis presentation — diagnostic trap (Borden/Dell, Adv Kidney Dis Health 2026): In ARPKD patients with dilated bile ducts, ascending cholangitis can present as unexplained fever and bacteremia without Charcot's triad (right upper quadrant pain + fever + jaundice). The bile duct ectasia in ARPKD-CHF disrupts the normal pressure dynamics that would otherwise produce localized pain, making this presentation easy to miss. Clinicians should maintain a low threshold for cholangitis workup in any ARPKD-CHF patient with unexplained fever or blood culture-positive bacteremia. Cascade implication: Each unrecognized cholangitis episode drives a full LPS→TLR4→NF-κB→NLRP3→IL-1β→STAT3→HSC cycle — fibrosis acceleration without the diagnosis ever being made. This is one mechanism by which liver disease progresses silently in ARPKD.

⭐ ARPKD bile duct bacteriology — confirmed data (2024-2025): Multicenter bile culture studies confirm predominant organisms in acute cholangitis: E. coli (22-37%), Pseudomonas spp. (28%), Enterococcus faecalis (15-39%). Cozma MA et al. Antibiotics 14(7):679, 2025 (Bucharest/Bordeaux multicenter: E. coli 30.7%, Pseudomonas 27.8%, Enterococcus faecalis 15.5%) · Stathopoulos et al. J Gastroenterol Hepatol 39:935–941, 2024 (ERCP bile culture: Enterococcus faecalis 37.6%, E. coli 32.8%). COBRA trial: 1-day antibiotics after adequate biliary drainage noninferior to 4-7 days in acute cholangitis — 413 patients, 31 Dutch centers. Overdevest AG et al. Trials 27:222, 2026. Presented at DDW 2026. Direct implication for ARPKD-CHF: most cholangitis episodes can be managed with 1-day antibiotics post-drainage, reducing antibiotic burden in patients requiring frequent interventions.

Elafibranor (IQIRVO, Ipsen): FDA-approved for PBC (2024). ELMWOOD Phase 2 PSC: ALP reduction -35.3% (80mg) and -54.7% (120mg) vs placebo at 12 weeks; ELF score stabilization. Levy C et al. J Hepatol 84(1):74-85, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.04.025. PMID: 40350321. ELASCOPE Phase 3 PSC launched April 2026 (~350 patients, NCT06622018). Shortest regulatory path to ARPKD-CHF investigator study.

Nebokitug (CM-101, Chemomab): Anti-CCL24 (eotaxin-2) monoclonal antibody. Phase 3 PSC protocol submitted FDA Q2 2025. CCL24 = new macrophage recruitment node parallel to CXCL10/CXCR3. (Chemomab press release, Q2 2025 — Company reported, not peer-reviewed). CCL24 peer-reviewed mechanism in biliary fibrosis: Ilan Y et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1062-1074, 2022. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2022.01.003 (CCL24/eotaxin-2 drives macrophage recruitment and fibrosis in PSC — Phase 2 data).

NorUDCA Phase 3 (NUC-5) positive: OR 4.16 vs placebo for ALP normalization; ~25% liver scarring reversal. First drug to show Phase 3 disease-modifying activity in any biliary cholestatic disease. Mechanism: cholehepatic shunting, bicarbonate-rich choleresis, T-cell modulation. Fickert P et al. J Hepatol 81(3):433-443, 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2024.04.004.

FXR → NLRP3 suppression: FXR agonists (saroglitazar, linafexor) directly suppress NLRP3. ⚠️ CRITICAL CAVEAT (Djouder Nat Metab 2026): obeticholic acid (OCA, FXR agonist) WORSENED fibrosis in FXR-deficient mice — FXR agonist therapy requires intact biliary FXR. In ARPKD-CHF where biliary FXR may be dysregulated by fibrocystin loss, this precision medicine consideration is relevant. via NF-κB inhibition, in addition to reducing bile acid accumulation that activates NLRP3. Wang H et al. Front Pharmacol 12:762826, 2021. DOI: 10.3389/fphar.2021.762826 (FXR suppresses NLRP3 inflammasome via NF-κB inhibition).
