ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Hindy G et al. Nature 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41586-026-10864-2 · Baba M et al. PLoS ONE 13:e0197505, 2018. DOI: 10.1371/journal.pone.0197505

FNIP1/AMPK paradox — 1-million-person human validation + PKD kidney safety warning

Human FNIP1 loss-of-function (1,032,116 people) → lower liver fat, lower ALT, ~60% lower cardiometabolic disease odds. (Hindy G et al. Nature 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41586-026-10864-2) Liver-targeted FNIP1 inhibition benefits liver. ⚠️ SAFETY: Kidney-specific Fnip1 loss in mice → decreased AMPK → mTOR activation → renal cysts (fatal PKD with Fnip1/2 double KO). Kidney-targeted FNIP1 inhibition is contraindicated in ARPKD. Metformin/bempedoic acid/HL156A activate AMPK upstream of FNIP1 and remain safe. Also: HL156A (Seo et al. Biomolecules 2024) — more potent AMPK activator than metformin, reduced cysts in Pkd1 KO mice.

ADDED AUGUST 2026 · Liguori A et al. Liver International 46(8):e70774, 2026 DOI: 10.1111/liv.70774 · PSC: Vesterhus M et al. J Hepatol 62:1387, 2015. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2015.01.029 · PBC: Fujinaga Y et al. Hepatol Res 51:891, 2021. DOI: 10.1111/hepr.13571

ELF test (Enhanced Liver Fibrosis) — blood-based non-invasive fibrosis staging

ELF measures TIMP-1 + PIIINP + hyaluronic acid. Cut-offs validated in 289-patient biopsy-controlled cohort: ELF <9.8 = 1% events; ELF 9.8–11.3 = 27.8%; ELF ≥11.3 = 72.7% liver-related events. AUC 0.939. (Vesterhus M et al. J Hepatol 62(6):1387, 2015. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2015.01.029) Also validated in PBC (Fujinaga Y et al. Hepatol Res 51:891, 2021. DOI: 10.1111/hepr.13571). Requires one blood draw. No ARPKD-specific ELF data — Tier 2 (MASLD + cholestatic biliary disease validation). Could serve as ARPKD-CHF clinical trial monitoring endpoint alongside FibroScan.

★ Newly identified node 2021-2026 · Spermidine addresses 4 cascade nodes simultaneously — most broadly active single compound in the framework

miRNA network approach reverses established hepatic fibrosis — wwtr1/TAZ (YAP paralog) suppressed 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Lai YH, Her GM et al. (National Yang Ming Chiao Tung University, Taiwan). J Mol Med 104(1):70, 2026. Tier 2. miR-29b + anti-miR-21 + network approach reverses established fibrosis histologically in zebrafish. wwtr1 (= TAZ, YAP paralog) suppressed — directly relevant to YAP cascade node. Col1a1, α-SMA, fibronectin, CTGFA, itga6a all suppressed simultaneously. miR-29b (anti-collagen) + anti-miR-21 (restores SMAD7 brake on TGF-β) are both LNP-deliverable. Key distinction: fibrosis regression, not just stabilization. Her GM's Tet-ON zebrafish platform adaptable to PKHD1-deficient biliary fibrosis modeling. New potential contact: Her GM (Taiwan).

FPCct protein domain delivery — new therapeutic modality for ARPKD 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Hassan F, Ziegler WH et al. Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2026. Tier 2 — novel therapeutic concept, no in vivo validation yet. FPCct (the C-terminal domain of FPC, ~192 AA) re-expressed in Pkhd1 KO kidney epithelial cells restores SRC/STAT3 suppression and reduces cystogenic phenotype. Proof-of-concept that a protein domain — rather than full-length 4074 AA FPC — is sufficient to restore the core functional brake. Analogous to PC1-CTT tail suppression of ADPKD. Biliary application: FPCct delivery to cholangiocytes via LNP or AAV (biliary-targeted) is theoretically feasible. No biliary-specific data published. Routing: Ziegler WH (Hannover) + Sampaziotis (ERCP biliary delivery).

⭐ TheRaCil EU Consortium — €7.4M Active ARPKD Therapeutic Program Including ASO Targeting PKHD1 mRNA 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

TheRaCil — Therapies for Renal Ciliopathies. EU Horizon grant 101080717. July 2023 – June 2027. Total: €7,425,447. 14 partners. Tier 1 — Active funded consortium explicitly targeting ARPKD with ASO therapy, drug repurposing, and clinical trial readiness. Liebau MC (Cologne/RWTH Aachen) is a funded partner (€709K).

Therapeutic approaches under development:

  • Antisense oligonucleotides (ASO) targeting PKHD1 mRNA — directly relevant to His3124Tyr (c.9370C>T) and Met3642Ile (c.10926G>A). ASOs can be designed to correct splicing defects, skip exons, or upregulate the healthy allele. New therapeutic modality not previously named in our framework.
  • Drug repurposing — targeting shared pathological pathways in ARPKD and nephronophthisis (NPH)
  • Modifier gene identification — federated European patient databases to explain phenotypic variability
  • Biomarker development and patient stratification — ⭐ PUBLISHED OUTPUT: Burgmaier K et al. "Urinary peptide signature distinguishes autosomal recessive polycystic kidney disease from other causes of chronic kidney disease." Clin Kidney J 18(5):sfaf093, May 2025. DOI: 10.1093/ckj/sfaf093. 77-peptide urinary signature (CE/MS), sensitivity 84.2%, specificity 100%, AUC 0.994 in ARPKD vs paediatric CKD validation cohort. 83% collagen fragments. First ARPKD-specific urinary diagnostic biomarker — TheRaCil consortium product.
  • Clinical trial readiness — patient-centered outcome measures, endpoint development

Key consortium members: Imagine Institute (Necker Enfants Malades, Paris — coordinator, €1.88M) · Klinikum Universität Köln / RWTH Aachen (Liebau, €709K) · Radboud UMC Nijmegen (€809K) · University of Copenhagen (€789K) · INSERM (€592K) · Heidelberg (€537K) · Carsten Bergmann/Bioscientia (€92K) · Ospedale San Raffaele Milan (€339K). Partners: Ciliopathy Alliance (UK), University of Newcastle (Sayer JA).

Critical implication: PKHD1 ASO therapy is being actively developed within a funded EU consortium with Liebau as a partner. The Zoom call (Sep 14/15) must address: which PKHD1 variants are eligible for ASO targeting? Could c.9370C>T (His3124Tyr) be corrected by exon-skipping or splice-correcting ASO? What is the timeline to clinical trial?

Source: Discovered via Liebau MC funding disclosure in GCND congress proceeding (DOI: 10.33393/gcnd.2025.3745, AIRP congress September 2025).

Gut-Liver Axis & Biliary Microbiome

⭐⭐ Imidazole propionate (ImP) → p38γ/δ → mTORC1 drives biliary fibrosis in PSC cholangiocytes

Source: Molinaro A, Braadland PR, Hov JR et al. (Oslo/Mayo/Karolinska). Nature Metabolism 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01600-1. September 4, 2026. Open access.

ImP — a gut bacterial metabolite produced from histidine via the urdA gene — is elevated in PSC across 4 independent cohorts (n=1,003 patients). High ImP predicts reduced transplant-free survival. PSC-patient cholangiocyte organoids treated with ImP show elevated TNF, IL-6, fibronectin. p38γ/δ double knockout mice completely protected from ImP-induced biliary fibrosis — genetic proof that p38γ/δ is the obligate mediator. mTORC1 activated in PSC cholangiocytes downstream of p38. AASLD abstract confirms ImP also aggravates CCl4 hepatic fibrosis via M1 macrophage polarization — general hepatic fibrosis driver beyond PSC.

ARPKD-CHF connections: (1) p38δ already confirmed cascade node — bile acid → p38δ → YAP. This paper provides genetic knockout proof-of-concept for p38 as the obligate cholangiocyte fibrosis mediator in biliary disease. (2) Direct cholangiocyte organoid confirmation — same cell type. (3) mTORC1 in cholangiocytes confirmed as fibrogenic effector — explains why mTOR inhibitors harm ARPKD biliary cells. (4) ARPKD gut dysbiosis (recurrent cholangitis + antibiotic cycles) may produce elevated ImP — untested. New open question: is ImP elevated in ARPKD-CHF patients? (5) Pirfenidone (p38 inhibitor) now has biological rationale in ARPKD-CHF. (6) Lazaridis (Mayo) co-author — in our network. Cross-connection: ImP → p38γ/δ (PSC) ↔ bile acid → p38δ (ARPKD-CHF) — two diseases, one cholangiocyte kinase.

Cholangitis as fibrosis accelerator — biliary bacteriology

Each bacterial cholangitis episode → LPS → TLR4 → NF-κB → NLRP3 → IL-1β + STAT3 → HSC activation. Aggressive cholangitis management is directly anti-fibrotic. Confirmed predominant organisms in acute cholangitis: E. coli (22-37%), Pseudomonas spp. (28%), Enterococcus faecalis (15-39%). (Cozma MA et al. Antibiotics 14(7):679, 2025) COBRA trial: 1-day antibiotics after adequate biliary drainage noninferior to 4-7 days — 413 patients, 31 Dutch centers. (Overdevest AG et al. Trials 27:222, 2026) Direct implication: most ARPKD-CHF cholangitis episodes can be managed with 1-day antibiotics post-drainage, reducing antibiotic burden.

FXR in cholangiocytes → FGF15/19 → p38 (not JNK)

FXR in cholangiocytes signals primarily via FGF15/19 → p38, not JNK. This directly connects the FXR gap (FXR never measured in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells) to the p38δ cascade node. If FPC loss reduces FXR activation in cholangiocytes, it would reduce FGF15/19-mediated p38 regulation — potentially amplifying ImP-driven p38γ/δ activation. FXR → FGF15/19 → p38 is a new mechanistic bridge between two of our most important open questions. Researcher routing: Gonzalez (NIH, FXR in biliary epithelial cells), Djouder (CNIO, FXR-YAP axis).

Finding 5: ⭐⭐ Gut microbiota-derived ImP drives biliary fibrosis via p38γ/δ → mTORC1 in PSC cholangiocytes 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Molinaro A, Braadland PR, Carpino G, Carreras A, Nikolaidis M, Juran BD, Overi D, … Hov JR et al. Nat Metab 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01600-1 — Oslo/Mayo/Karolinska/Sapienza Rome, September 4, 2026. Open access. Tier 1 (upgraded Sep 10, 2026 — full paper retrieved). Complete molecular sequence confirmed: ImP engages a cell surface receptor on cholangiocytes → phosphorylation cascade → p38 activation → inflammatory cytokine amplification + profibrotic transcriptional programs. Blocking pathway components substantially blunted all effects — causal mechanism, not correlation. Animal experiments confirmed: mouse models with ImP exposure exacerbated bile duct inflammation and fibrosis; interventions reducing ImP production or blocking signaling mitigated disease. p38γ/δ double knockout mice completely protected — genetic proof. Three therapeutic angles: (a) reduce ImP production — dietary histidine restriction, targeted antibiotics, bacteriophages against producing strains, live biotherapeutics; (b) block ImP receptor on cholangiocyte surface; (c) inhibit p38 in biliary epithelium locally — paper notes biliary compartment's relative isolation "might allow more localized approaches," making biliary-targeted p38 inhibition plausible. New network contacts: Gianluca Carpino G (biliary disease specialist, Sapienza University Rome — has published on CHF and ductal plate malformation) · Juran BD (Mayo Clinic PSC genetics, connects to Harris PC). Directly confirmed: ImP acts on cholangiocytes (the same cell type that fails in ARPKD-CHF). New open question: is ImP elevated in ARPKD-CHF patients given recurrent cholangitis and antibiotic cycles?

Organoids & Disease Models

FDA 2026 — Organoids and organ-on-chip qualify as new approach methodologies

The FDA's 2025 roadmap and 2026 draft guidance explicitly identify organoid and organ-on-chip systems as qualifying new approach methodologies (NAMs), reducing animal testing requirements and supporting regulatory data packages. The Dekkers CF organoid CFTR-modulator precedent (2016) — patient rectal organoids predicting individual clinical drug response — is the benchmark model for ARPKD drug screening. FDA regulatory acceptance of organoid data is accelerating: directly relevant to Peltz/Stanford AI + hepatic organoid screening, Cebotaru CFTR correctors in PKHD1-deficient biliary organoids, and Morizane organ-on-chip. (Makkar SK et al. Front Pharmacol 17:1927806, 2026.)

Biomarkers & Clinical Endpoints

Non-invasive monitoring tools validated for biliary cholestatic disease and applicable to ARPKD-CHF clinical trials — avoiding liver biopsy risks in children with portal hypertension and thrombocytopenia.

📌 NEW August 26, 2026 — Complete biliary-specific PKHD1 base editing strategy (Krappitz Mol Ther Aug 2025 + KRT19 + ERCP)

Ibel A, Bhardwaj R, Yilmaz DE, Kong S, Wendlinger S, Cordero C, Papaioannou D, Papazian M, Schönauer R, Meng Q, Eckardt KU, Hassan F, Volpe I, Klämbt V, Halbritter J, Fedeles S, Krappitz M, Kaminski MM. — Molecular Therapy 33(11):5373-5382, November 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.ymthe.2025.08.026. PMID: 40842155. ABEmax base editing corrected a C→T PKD1 missense variant (c.6646C>T, R2216W) in vivo. Only 5% liver editing efficiency was sufficient to produce a significant reduction in liver cysts in the ADPKD mouse model — first evidence of ADPKD reversibility through genome editing. Liver cyst reduction occurred alongside renal benefit. Authors: Krappitz (CU Anschutz) · Kaminski (MDC-BIMSB Berlin) · Fedeles (Yale). Kaminski email: michael.kaminski@mdc-berlin.de · Krappitz email: matteus.krappitz@cuanschutz.edu

KRT19 promoter for cholangiocyte restriction: KRT19 (cytokeratin 19) is expressed by ALL cholangiocytes and NOT by hepatocytes. Incorporating KRT19-driven promoter into AAV restricts base editor activation to cholangiocytes only — editor cannot activate in hepatocytes even if AAV enters them. KRT7, SOX9, CFTR promoters are alternatives. The PKHD1 promoter itself (4.7 kb HNF-1β binding fragment) is the most precise option.

ERCP-mediated hydrodynamic biliary injection: Validated gene delivery directly into bile duct lumen in human-sized pig model (Guimont et al. Mol Ther Methods Clin Dev 2021, PMC8081268). Only mild transient AST elevation; no bile duct rupture at flow rates up to 10 mL/sec. For ARPKD-CHF: dilated intrahepatic bile ducts are directly accessible. Delivers AAV right to cystic cholangiocyte luminal surface. Pediatric ERCP established in children ≥3 years.

Complete strategy: ERCP retrograde delivery + KRT19/PKHD1 promoter + ABEmax = biliary-specific correction of c.9370C>T (His3124Tyr). No systemic delivery required. No hepatocyte off-target risk.

Evidence grade A — in vivo mouse + human cells confirmed

📌 Added August 26, 2026 — Xiaogang Li, Mayo Clinic (Nature Communications January 2026)

Kidney-specific base editor + broad multi-organ editor. Single AAV dose reduced kidney cysts, improved liver health, extended survival. First in vivo evidence base editing works efficiently in kidney tissue. ⚠️ Citation removed — previously attributed to Ishimoto/Germino Nat Commun 2026 (PKHD1/TFAP2B/glaucoma paper), which contains no base editing data. Correct primary source needed. Nanoparticle delivery under development. Part of Mayo Clinic Genesis regenerative medicine initiative.

ADDED AUGUST 17, 2026 · Gao XD et al. (Liu DR, Lutz C co-senior). Nature Biomed Eng 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01651-5 · Commentary: Ahrens-Nicklas RC. N Engl J Med 395(7):711-714, 2026. DOI: 10.1056/NEJMcibr2606991

ZSD base editing: closest published proof-of-concept to ARPKD-CHF base editing — ~60% liver correction rescues biallelic recessive pediatric liver disease via AAV9. (Gao XD et al. Nat Biomed Eng 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01651-5)

Primary paper (Gao/Liu): Adenine base editor ABE8e-V106W delivered via AAV9 corrected PEX1-p.G843D (c.2528G>A) — a single-letter A→G correction — achieving ~60% pathogenic allele correction in bulk liver of both neonatal and 4-week-old mice. (Gao XD, Liu DR et al. Nat Biomed Eng 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41551-026-01651-5) Zellweger spectrum disorder (ZSD) is a biallelic recessive PEX gene disease causing liver pathology, toxic C27-bile acid intermediates, and very long-chain fatty acid accumulation. Base editing eliminated toxic metabolites, restored peroxisome function, rescued liver pathology and body weight. Demonstrated in mouse and human patient-derived cells. The structural parallel to ARPKD-CHF is direct:

FeatureZSD (Gao/Liu 2026)ARPKD-CHF (our patient)
InheritanceBiallelic recessiveBiallelic recessive
Variant typePEX1 c.2528G>A (G→A, ABE target)PKHD1 c.9370C>T (C→T, ABE substrate ✓)
Primary organLiver + brainLiver + kidney
DeliveryAAV9 IV or neonatalERCP biliary (Krappitz strategy)
Correction threshold~60% sufficient≥5% likely sufficient (Krappitz PKD1)
Clinical statusMouse + human cells; pre-INDTHRIVE IND Q3 2026 (CPS1, not PKHD1)

Critical cargo note: ABE for specific point mutations does NOT require delivering the full PKHD1 gene — only the ABE + sgRNA. This fits AAV9 (~4.7 kb). Cargo size constraint applies to gene replacement (requiring LNP/lentiviral), not to base editing of specific variants. Our child's c.9370C>T and c.10926G>A mutations are both ABE substrates deliverable via AAV9 + ERCP. Tier 2 — confirmed in ZSD mouse model and human patient cells; not yet demonstrated for PKHD1 specifically. The ZSD paper is now the closest published proof-of-concept for what ARPKD base editing would look like. Commentary author: Rebecca C. Ahrens-Nicklas, MD PhD (ahrens-nicklasr@chop.edu · CHOP/Penn · ARPA-H THRIVE lead) wrote the NEJM commentary on this paper — published August 12, 2026. She is a gated contact in our network (approach via Guay-Woodford); HRFD enrollment unlocks this gate. This paper makes the Guay-Woodford → Ahrens-Nicklas introduction more urgent than any prior point in this project.

Evidence grade A — in vivo mouse + human patient cells; biallelic recessive pediatric liver disease

ADDED AUGUST 19, 2026 · Human: Yuan L, Dawka S, Kim Y et al. (Huch M corresponding). Nature 650, 2026 (online Dec 17, 2025). DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09884-1 Mouse: Dowbaj AM et al. (Huch M corresponding). Nature 644(8076):473-482, May 2025. DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09183-9 MPI-CBG Dresden / TU Dresden.

Huch periportal liver assembloids — most relevant published platform for ARPKD-CHF human modeling. CXCL12 independently confirmed in fibrotic mesenchyme. CDH11 + ITGβ1 new anti-fibrotic targets.

Human assembloid (Nature Dec 2025): h-HepOrgs (human hepatocyte organoids) + human cholangiocyte organoids + portal mesenchyme from 28 donors = patient-specific periportal assembloid retaining bile canaliculi-to-duct functional connection. 20× mesenchyme excess → biliary fibrosis: cholangiocyte expansion, hepatocyte death, collagen deposition — mirrors ARPKD-CHF pathology directly. h-HepOrgs transplanted into mouse liver disease model rescued phenotype in vivo. Tier 2 — healthy donors; not PKHD1-mutant. ARPKD-CHF application: Incorporating PKHD1-mutant patient-derived cells would generate the first comprehensive human ARPKD-CHF periportal model — with functional bile flow as a quantifiable readout. Could test FXR activation, AMG-487 (CXCR3, research compound), PDGFRB inhibitors, CDH11 inhibitors, 4-OI, and spermidine in human periportal biliary fibrosis context simultaneously. New network contact (no gate): Meritxell Huch (mhuch@mpi-cbg.de, MPI-CBG Dresden) — world's leading liver organoid scientist; has not connected this platform to ARPKD.

Mouse assembloid (Nature May 2025): Mouse periportal assembloids with functional bile canaliculi-bile duct connection. Fibrotic assembloids (10× mesenchyme) upregulate CXCL12, CXCL1, CCL11, MMP2, MMP3 — independently confirming the CXCL12/CXCR4 secondary cascade node from a completely orthogonal experimental system. New therapeutic targets (Tier 2): CDH11 (cadherin-11) knockdown in portal mesenchyme prevented fibrotic compaction and collagen deposition. ITGβ1 knockout reduced collagen deposition. Neither CDH11 nor ITGβ1 was previously in our therapeutic framework — both are now anti-fibrotic candidates for ARPKD-CHF.

ADDED AUGUST 19, 2026 · Xu J, Sang X et al. (Shi X, Wu F corresponding). JHEP Reports 2026, vol. 8:101884. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhepr.2026.101884 Sun Yat-sen University / Shenzhen Third People's Hospital.

BA-HBO: hiPSC-derived hepatobiliary organoids with 33-species bile acid metabolomics — cholangiocytes with primary cilia + fibrosis model with quantifiable bile acid dysregulation

Saikosaponin A (PPARα/LXR dual activator) generates hiPSC-derived BA-HBOs containing both hepatocytes (HNF4α, ALB, CYP3A4+) and cholangiocytes with acetylated α-tubulin primary cilia (CK7, CK19, CFTR, AQP1, ASBT, ZO-1, Notch2+). LC-MS/MS quantified 33 bile acid species — glycine-conjugated predominant, matching human physiology. TGF-β treatment → FiBA-HBOs (fibrosis model): α-SMA, COL1A1, COL3A1, MMP2 upregulation; TNF-α, IL-2, IL-6, IL-8 upregulation; GGT, ALT, AST elevation; AND significantly increased DCA, LCA, CDCA, UDCA, 7-KDCA — toxic bile acid accumulation accompanies fibrosis. Tier 2 — healthy hiPSC; not PKHD1-mutant. ARPKD-CHF applications: (1) Cholangiocytes with primary cilia = correct ARPKD cell type; FPC is expressed in these cilia. (2) 33-species bile acid metabolomics platform quantifies the specific bile acid dysregulation in PKHD1-mutant organoids and measures whether FXR activation or anti-fibrotics restore normal profiles. (3) FiBA-HBO bile acid pattern (DCA, LCA, CDCA ↑) validates our bile acid accumulation → fibrosis cascade axis. (4) ASBT (SLC10A2) — apical sodium-dependent bile acid transporter in cholangiocytes — new specific bile acid transport readout not previously in our framework. (5) PPARα/LXR activation (Saikosaponin A): Natural compound with dual PPARα/LXR activity. Safety note: LXR can antagonize FXR — test against pulsatile FXR approach for pathway interaction before recommending.

ADDED AUGUST 19, 2026 · Lapunzina-Soler P et al. (Nevado J corresponding). Genes 2026;17(2):229. DOI: 10.3390/genes17020229 Hospital La Paz, Madrid. 40 molecularly confirmed ARPKD patients. ⚠️ CLINICAL VALIDATION OF HEPATIC CASCADE FRAMEWORK

PKHD1 LoF allele burden determines renal severity — hepatic disease is INDEPENDENT of genotype class. Clinical validation of distinct kidney vs liver mechanisms in 40 confirmed ARPKD patients.

Phase-aware NGS (68 referred; 40 molecularly confirmed ARPKD by segregation-informed ACMG) — Lapunzina-Soler P et al. Genes 17(2):229, 2026. DOI: 10.3390/genes17020229. Strong allele-dose effect on kidney: LoF/LoF = 88% neonatal onset, 65% renal replacement therapy (median age 1.2 years); LoF/non-LoF = 56% neonatal onset, 31% RRT (median age 14.8 years); non-LoF/non-LoF = 29% neonatal onset, 0% RRT. Hepatic disease showed NO significant association with LoF burden — 74% hepatic involvement, 34% portal hypertension, 8% liver transplantation across ALL genotype classes. Paper conclusion: "Distinct pathogenic mechanisms may underlie renal and biliary disease." This is the most important clinical validation of our hepatic cascade framework. Our FXR-YAP-SPP1-TREM2 cascade explains WHY hepatic disease is mutation-independent: FPC loss in cholangiocyte primary cilia disrupts FXR signaling and activates YAP regardless of mutation type — because it is the loss of FPC's ciliary signaling function, not fibrocystin dosage, that drives the biliary cascade. Additional finding: "liver manifestations were sometimes the earliest detectable abnormality, even in individuals with relatively preserved renal function" — directly describes liver-predominant ARPKD phenotypes. For our daughter's genotype class: If both variants are non-LoF → non-LoF/non-LoF class: best renal prognosis, 0% RRT in this cohort, liver-predominant presentation expected. If c.10926G>A causes exon skipping (LoF) → LoF/non-LoF class: 31% RRT risk, median age 14.8 years. The minigene assay for c.10926G>A is therefore simultaneously mechanistic AND prognostic.

ADDED AUGUST 17, 2026 · Hogan MC et al. J Mol Med 92:1061-1072, 2014. DOI: 10.1007/s00109-014-1185-7 (Guay-Woodford lab) · Li H et al. J Clin Med 12:1428, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/jcm12041428 ⚠️ HIGHEST PRIORITY PATIENT-SPECIFIC QUESTION

c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile) — ESE disruption mechanism: may be a truncation in disguise. ASO splice correction is the most immediately accessible therapeutic strategy.

The ESE mechanism (Hogan/Guay-Woodford 2014): PKHD1 missense G→A variants can abolish exonic splicing enhancer (ESE) motifs within coding exons, causing downstream exon skipping and frameshifts. Demonstrated for R760H (c.2279G>A, same mutation class as c.10926G>A): abolished SRSF5 ESE → exon 22 skipping → truncation p.V279X. If c.10926G>A (Met3642Ile, Zone 3) disrupts an ESE similarly, it functions as a truncation-equivalent — reclassifying it from VUS toward Likely Pathogenic. Both Zone 3 variants would then be truncation-type, consistent with the CHF + portal hypertension phenotype. ABE correction would restore BOTH Met3642 amino acid AND the ESE simultaneously. The minigene assay is required to confirm. The Guay-Woodford lab published this exact methodology. HRFD enrollment (contact Guay-Woodford lab, guaywoodfl@chop.edu) is the gate to that relationship. The ASO therapeutic angle (Li et al. 2023): Antisense oligonucleotides rescued two PKHD1 exon-skipping splice variants (c.2141-3T>C, c.11174+5G>A) in minigene systems by restoring normal splicing. If c.10926G>A causes exon skipping, a splice-switching ASO targeting that exon could restore functional PKHD1 mRNA — without genomic modification, and faster to clinical translation than base editing. ASO platforms: Ionis Pharmaceuticals (GalNAc-ASO liver targeting, established pediatric safety); ProQR (RNA editing ASOs). This is the most immediately accessible precision medicine approach for this specific patient. ICD15 connection (Tier 3): Exon skipping at AA 3642 may disrupt ICD15 C-terminal mitochondrial fragment processing — adding mitochondrial dysfunction to the biliary phenotype. Steps: (1) Minigene assay → Guay-Woodford lab; (2) ASO design → Ionis or ProQR; (3) Test in Pkhd1 mouse or PKHD1 biliary organoids → Strazzabosco or Asai.

ADDED AUGUST 17, 2026 · Zhu L, Wu B. Front Immunol 17:1899587, 2026. DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1899587 · NEW CASCADE NODE

IRG1-Itaconate-β-arrestin 2 axis — counter-regulatory brake on macrophage anti-inflammation; cenicriviroc failure lesson; FXR/TGR5 macrophage mechanism

IRG1-Itaconate counter-regulatory brake (new cascade node): In MASH macrophages, compensatory upregulation of IRG1 → itaconate production provides anti-inflammatory braking via SDH inhibition, Nrf2 activation (Keap1 alkylation), and IκBζ modification → IL-6 suppression. However, β-arrestin 2 (upregulated by sustained NF-κB signaling) scaffolds MDM2 → K48-linked ubiquitination of IRG1 → proteasomal degradation → IRG1 half-life reduced from ~8h to <2h → itaconate production insufficient → SDH unrestricted → succinate accumulates → HIF-1α stabilized → glycolytic macrophage lock → NLRP3 → IL-1β → CXCL10. This connects directly to the existing CXCL10/CXCR3 cascade node. ARPKD-CHF hypothesis (Tier 3): FPC-deficient biliary disease chronically activates NF-κB in hepatic macrophages → β-arrestin 2 upregulation → IRG1 degradation → insufficient itaconate braking → perpetuation of hepatic inflammation. Therapeutic targets (Tier 3): 4-OI (4-octyl itaconate, 50mg/kg systemic, tested in murine MASH); DI (dimethyl itaconate); β-arrestin 2 inhibition in myeloid cells. Cenicriviroc Phase 3 failure (AURORA trial) lesson: CCR2/CCR5 inhibition alone failed (22.3% vs 25.5% placebo, p=0.21). (Anstee QM et al. Hepatology 76:1842-1854, 2022. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32198) Blocking monocyte recruitment without metabolic reprogramming of existing macrophages is insufficient. AMG-487/CXCR3 antagonist monotherapy faces the same risk — must combine with metabolic reprogramming (itaconate, PPAR agonists, spermidine). FXR/TGR5 → macrophage mechanism: TGR5 activation on Kupffer cells promotes M2-like polarization and suppresses NLRP3 inflammasome. FXR signaling in macrophages directly suppresses NF-κB. Pulsatile FXR activation therefore has DUAL benefit: (1) cholangiocyte benefit — controlled cilia/FXR signaling; (2) macrophage benefit — TGR5-mediated M2 polarization + FXR-mediated NF-κB suppression. Second mechanistic rationale for pulsatile FXR previously undocumented.

ADDED AUGUST 19, 2026 · Jain A, Monga SP. "Sexual dimorphism in the liver." Nature Reviews Gastroenterology & Hepatology (2026). DOI: 10.1038/s41575-026-01244-2 Review. TIER 3 HYPOTHESIS for ARPKD-CHF.

Sex-based liver differences: ER-α/FXR cross-talk may partially compensate for FXR impairment in female ARPKD-CHF patients — SERM therapeutic hypothesis. (Liguori A et al. Liver International 46(8):e70774, 2026. DOI: 10.1111/liv.70774)

Comprehensive review establishing that estrogen receptor (ER-α) regulates bile acid metabolism, bile acid transport, and anti-inflammatory pathways that overlap with FXR target genes in the liver. Pre-menopausal women have lower rates of cholestatic progression and fibrosis. ARPKD-CHF hypothesis (Tier 3): ER-α activation could partially compensate for FXR impairment in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes — estrogen may restore some FXR target gene expression even when FXR signaling itself is impaired. If true: (1) SERM (selective estrogen receptor modulator) agonism could restore FXR-like biliary protection in ARPKD-CHF; (2) Post-menopausal ARPKD-CHF females may experience accelerated hepatic disease as ER-α compensation erodes; (3) LSEC eNOS has known sex-based differences (connects to Gracia-Sancho work on sinusoidal portal hypertension). The Nevado 2026 ARPKD cohort found no sex-based differences in outcomes — but cohort was small and may lack power to detect adult/post-menopausal effects. Untested in PKHD1-deficient cells.

⭐ ADDED SEPTEMBER 15, 2026 · Yang C, Harafuji N, Caldovic L, Yu W, Boddu R, Bhattacharya S, Barseghyan H, Gordish-Dressman H, Foreman O, Bebok Z, Eicher EM, Guay-Woodford LM (senior/corresponding). "Pkhd1cyli/cyli mice have altered renal Pkhd1 mRNA processing and hormonally sensitive liver disease." J Mol Med (Berl) 101(9):1141-1151, September 2023. DOI: 10.1007/s00109-023-02351-2. PMID: 37584738. PMC10482757. Open access. Children's National Research Institute, Washington DC. Guay-Woodford is in our network — emailed.

What this paper found: A spontaneous insertion/deletion mutation in Pkhd1 exon 48 creates a frameshift and premature termination codon (UGA), producing the cyli (cystic liver) mouse — severe progressive hepato-biliary disease with NO renal cystic disease up to 6 months. The liver phenotype is exacerbated by age, sex, and parity. Histology shows: P0 (nulliparous) < P1 (1 litter) < P2 (2 litters) < P3 (3 litters) in disease severity — each pregnancy dramatically worsens biliary cyst formation independently of age. Female mice are also more severely affected than age-matched male mice without any pregnancies — implicating estrogen itself (not only the parity-specific hormonal surge).

⚠️ TWO THINGS NOT CAPTURED BY THE EXISTING ER-α/FXR ENTRY ABOVE:

1. Parity as a catastrophic disease accelerator in a PKHD1-specific model. The existing ER-α/FXR note discusses post-menopausal estrogen LOSS potentially accelerating ARPKD-CHF. The cyli paper shows the opposite and more urgent direction — active pregnancy hormones (estrogen/progesterone surge) ACCELERATE biliary cyst formation during reproductive years in PKHD1-deficient mice. Three pregnancies essentially destroy the liver parenchyma. This is a direct empirical finding in a PKHD1 mouse model, not a theoretical extrapolation. For Gianna's family: each future pregnancy carries substantial risk of accelerating her biliary liver disease. This must eventually be communicated when she is older.

2. The cyli mouse as the best available CHF-predominant test platform. The cyli model is liver-predominant ARPKD, matching Gianna's hepatic-predominant CHF presentation. Unlike the Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 model (used by Cebotaru) which also has renal involvement, the cyli model specifically recapitulates isolated hepato-biliary disease. It is the most direct animal analog to Gianna's clinical situation. As Guay-Woodford's own model, it could be proposed as the test platform for: Cebotaru's AAV1-CFTR approach; biliary-targeted LNP delivery; loop-breaking therapies (losartan, doxycycline). Guay-Woodford and Cebotaru are both in the DC-area / Children's National network — a collaboration may already exist or be natural to suggest.

mRNA processing finding: Several alternatively spliced Pkhd1 transcripts containing exon 48 are expressed in cyli kidneys at lower abundance — these escaped nonsense-mediated decay (NMD) via an AAAAAT ribosomal frameshifting motif upstream of the cyli indel. The kidney makes marginally more residual FPC via frameshifted transcripts than the liver — this explains why the renal phenotype is milder despite the same mutation. The liver is more sensitive to FPC deficiency even at the same genetic background. Directly relevant to Gianna: if Met3642Ile (her VUS) causes exon skipping → premature termination → NMD, the resulting transcript pool and degree of NMD escape will determine residual FPC in her biliary cells. Krappitz's splicing test is the experimental answer.

Action items: (1) Ask Guay-Woodford whether she has characterized the specific sex hormone mechanism — ER-α vs. progesterone receptor involvement.. (2) Ask whether anyone has tested liver-directed gene therapy in the cyli model.

ADDED AUGUST 19, 2026 · Wang D, Jabile MJT et al. (Steinberg GR corresponding). "GDF15 suppresses liver inflammation independently of weight loss through neuroendocrine glucocorticoid signaling." Cell Metabolism, August 26, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cmet.2026.07.008 McMaster University. TIER 3 HYPOTHESIS for ARPKD-CHF.

GDF15 brain-liver glucocorticoid pathway — FPC-deficient mitochondrial dysfunction may upregulate GDF15 as compensatory anti-inflammatory signal

GDF15 (stress-induced hormone, TGF-β superfamily) signals brain → nervous system → glucocorticoid release → liver macrophage M2 shift → reduced NF-κB, NLRP3, fibrosis. Operates INDEPENDENTLY of weight loss or metabolic status. (Steinberg/Wang et al. Cell Metabolism August 10, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cmet.2026.07.004) — directly relevant to ARPKD-CHF where liver disease is genetic, not metabolic. ARPKD-CHF hypothesis (Tier 3): FPC-deficient cholangiocytes have mitochondrial dysfunction (ICD15, Walker/Qian 2023). Mitochondrial stress is a primary GDF15 inducer. ARPKD-CHF biliary tissue may constitutively upregulate GDF15 as a compensatory anti-inflammatory response — elevated but insufficient to suppress the FXR-YAP cascade. GDF15 agonism could augment this protection. Metformin connection: metformin is a known GDF15 inducer — partially explains hepatoprotective effects of AMPK activators in ARPKD-CHF (new mechanism, complements Hallows BEAT-PKD context). Sex connection: GDF15 is higher in females — parallel mechanism to ER-α/FXR cross-talk in providing female liver protection. Industry: Steinberg co-founded Espervita Therapeutics — watch for GDF15 agonist pipeline.

⭐ AUGUST 21, 2026 · Personal communication — Dr. Nabil Djouder (ndjouder@cnio.es, CNIO Madrid). FXR/YAP KO mice do NOT develop liver cysts. Two-hit framework confirmed.

Djouder lab: FXR/YAP knockout → fibrosis but NO cysts. Two-hit framework: active driver (cAMP/STAT3/SPP1) + absent brake (FXR-YAP) both required. Neither alone produces full ARPKD-CHF.

Djouder finding: Despite strong biliary effects in FXR/YAP KO mice, no cystic phenotype. Djouder lab had extensively discussed cilia dysregulation hypothesis. "Worth investigating experimentally in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells. We may start exploring in near future as mouse models already generated."

Two-hit framework: ARPKD-CHF requires BOTH: (1) Active driver — FPC loss → cAMP → PKA → SRC → pSTAT3 → SPP1 (confirmed — PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6 · PCK rat transcriptomics, Liebau/Ziegler group, Commun Biol 2026); (2) Absent brake — FPC loss → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated (hypothesis — unmeasured in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells) → SOCS3 not induced → pSTAT3 unchecked. Therapeutic: pulsatile linafexor (brake) + SPP1 siRNA (driver) + somatostatin (root cAMP).

⭐ ADDED AUGUST 24, 2026 · Tang Y, Feng Y, Cheng L et al. Signal Transduction and Targeted Therapy 11:343, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41392-026-02781-7 Open access. Published today.

Xenogeneic bioreactor review: Pig kidney xenotransplantation in 5 living humans (one >3 months). Sampaziotis confirmed. ⭐ CRITICAL: PKHD1 collecting duct niche has NEVER been targeted — all blastocyst complementation targets nephron progenitors. Fundamental gap in PKHD1-specific cell replacement.

Pig kidney xenotransplantation: 5 living human cases reported as of 2026. One functioned >3 months. Gene-edited minipigs (10-gene knockout). Nearest-term curative option for ARPKD renal failure. Remaining challenge: antibody-mediated rejection, thrombocytopenia from porcine vWF. (Porrett PM et al. Nat Med 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41591-026-03468-4. Review; individual cases reported 2023-2026 by NYU Langone, Mass General, and University of Alabama)

⭐ CRITICAL DEVELOPMENTAL BIOLOGY GAP: PKHD1 is expressed in the COLLECTING DUCT (ureteric bud lineage). Published blastocyst complementation/organogenic niche approaches (SIX2-iDTR, SALL1-KO) target the NEPHRON progenitor niche. Chimeric kidneys have donor nephrons but HOST-derived collecting ducts — exactly where PKHD1 pathology resides. Blastocyst complementation for ARPKD specifically requires targeting the ureteric bud progenitor niche — NEVER DONE. Same for liver: biliary ductal plate progenitor niche (not hepatocyte niche) must be targeted for ARPKD-CHF cell replacement. This is a fundamental gap in current PKHD1-specific curative strategies.

Sampaziotis cholangiocyte organoid: Explicitly cited as landmark biliary organoid achievement. Confirms Sampaziotis/Vallier platform as the leading ARPKD-CHF curative cell replacement approach (bile duct-targeted, not hepatocyte-targeted).

Chimeric hepatocytes → attenuate liver fibrosis: mESC-derived hepatocytes (~15% contribution) from mouse-rat chimeras attenuated chronic liver fibrosis when transplanted. Adjacent proof-of-concept for chimeric hepatocyte approaches in biliary fibrosis. mTOR inhibition used to facilitate hiPSC chimerism — connects to our mTOR biliary harm triple-mechanism warning. (Huang Y et al. Stem Cell Reports 19:527-541, 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.stemcr.2024.02.008)

SLC8A1 + TRPV4 = Unified Calcium Dysregulation Mechanism: Two independent research lines converge on intracellular calcium dysregulation as the driver of ARPKD cystogenesis: (1) Kuraoka/Morizane Advanced Science 2026: SLC8A1 (sodium/calcium exchanger, identified by single-cell RNA-seq as cyst-origin marker in distal/connecting tubules) — SPTAN1 loss → RAC1 activation → altered calcium signaling → increased intracellular calcium → cyst formation. CRISPRa restoring SPTAN1 normalizes calcium. (2) Masyuk/LaRusso Hepatology 2017 + ACS 2025: TRPV4 (a calcium channel) is upregulated in PCK rat cholangiocytes; TGR5 → cAMP → cholangiocyte proliferation — with calcium dysregulation as a shared downstream effector. SLC8A1 exports calcium; TRPV4 imports calcium. Both are dysregulated in ARPKD biliary/tubular cells from opposite directions. This convergence has not been stated explicitly in either paper or anywhere else in the literature. Morizane's group (Kuraoka) and LaRusso's group (TGR5/TRPV4) are approaching the same calcium node from different directions and may not know their work is converging. Routing: Morizane + LaRusso — this connection is the basis for a specific question to both. ⭐ NEW NODE Sep 14, 2026 — NF-Y Transcription Factor → TGFβ1 in Cholangiocytes (LaRusso/Gores, AJP-GI 2025): Olson KE, Krishnan A, Splinter P, Wixom AQ, Guicciardi ME, Jalan-Sakrikar N, Azad A, LaRusso NF, Gores GJ. Am J Physiol Gastrointest Liver Physiol 329(5):G597-G613, Nov 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00243.2025. PMID: 41021740. In cholestatic cholangiocytes: (1) CX3CL1 expression recruits CX3CR1+ macrophages to periportal region — a SECOND macrophage recruitment axis parallel to CXCL10/CXCR3 (Kaffe 2018). (2) Cholangiocyte transcriptomics show activation of NF-Y transcription factor complex (NF-YA/NF-YB/NF-YC). (3) In human cholangiocytes, NF-Y drives TGFβ1 expression specifically — first time NF-Y identified as the cholangiocyte-specific TGFβ1 transcriptional regulator. siRNA knockdown of NF-YA reduces TGFβ1. ⚠️ Model is reversible cholestasis (DDC diet) — ARPKD-CHF is irreversible because fibrocystin loss is permanent. Critical open question: Is NF-Y constitutively activated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes? If fibrocystin loss creates a permanently cholestatic biliary microenvironment, NF-Y would chronically drive TGFβ1 — which appears in the ARPKD-CHF cholangiokine secretome (Cai/Tacke Front Immunol 2023, confirmed by LaRusso review). New contacts: Gregory Gores (gores.gregory@mayo.edu — corresponding author, approach through LaRusso); Adrien Guillot (Charité Berlin — first author Cai/Tacke cholangiokines review, macrophage-cholangiocyte crosstalk expert). Literature note: One interpretation held Scribble loss secondary to cascade — but the primary paper shows the opposite: "membrane expression of Scribble was absent in biliary cysts and microhamartomas since the earliest maturation age (1 month)." Fibrosis doesn't start until 3-6 months. Scribble loss PRECEDES fibrosis. The therapeutic hope of "preventing fibrosis prevents Scribble loss" is NOT supported.

FPC → Scribble: explicitly unknown. Paper states: "The relationships between Scribble and FPC remain to be elucidated — direct physical interaction OR shared signaling pathways." Birth timepoints not tested. Whether Scribble loss is congenital or develops in first weeks of life is unknown.

✅ Therapeutic validation (primary data): β-catenin deletion in Pkhd1del4/del4 → cysts −40%, inflammation −40%, fibrosis −35%, nYAP UNCHANGED. YAP → β-catenin (one-directional, confirmed). PRI-724 (blocks CBP+p300) has proof-of-concept support. ICG-001 used in paper — doesn't affect nYAP, confirms YAP is upstream of β-catenin. (Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R)

✅ TRPV4 ARPKD-specific confirmed: Gradilone SA, Masyuk TV et al. Gastroenterology 139(1):304-14, 2010. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2010.03.052. PMC2902576. PCK rat — "Activation of Trpv4 reduces the hyperproliferative phenotype of cystic cholangiocytes from an animal model of ARPKD." Primary ARPKD data. Network: Cadamuro is co-corresponding author of this paper. Barbara Seller (co-author) = Hannover Medical School = same institution as Ziegler.

Scribble mislocalization is an EARLY feature in ARPKD-CHF, present before fibrosis. Scribble is already absent at 1 month — the earliest timepoint tested — while fibrosis begins at 3–6 months, so Scribble loss precedes fibrosis rather than following it. The paper states explicitly that the FPC–Scribble relationship is unknown, describing it only as "direct physical interaction OR shared signaling pathways"; birth timepoints were not tested. (Fabris L, Milani C, Fiorotto R, Mariotti V, Kaffe E, Seller B, Sonzogni A, Strazzabosco M, Cadamuro M. FASEB J 36(6):e22364, 2022. PMC9150862)

Direct therapeutic evidence from the same study: conditional β-catenin deletion in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice produced −40% cysts, −40% inflammation and −35% fibrosis, with nuclear YAP unchanged — establishing a one-directional YAP → β-catenin relationship and providing proof-of-concept for CBP/β-catenin antagonism.

Open question for Cadamuro and Strazzabosco (co-corresponding authors): were birth or pre-1-month timepoints examined, and is the FPC–Scribble relationship direct?

For Djouder: His FXR/YAP KO mice may develop Scribble mislocalization gradually if aged long enough — the FXR-driven fibroinflammatory cascade may eventually drive E-cadherin loss + Scribble shedding. Longitudinal aging + Scribble IHC over time is a direct actionable experiment. Do NOT frame FPC as directly binding or anchoring Scribble — there is no evidence for this. (Fabris L et al.
