⭐ SCIENTIFIC VALIDATION — August 21, 2026. Three foundational claims audited against published literature by

What is PROVEN vs. what is HYPOTHESIS — honest tier assessment of the framework's key claims.

✅ PROVEN (Tier 2) — Somatostatin in PCK rat liver: Octreotide AND pasireotide tested in PCK rat with significant reductions in wet liver weight, hepatic cyst area, AND fibrosis index. cAMP suppression in ARPKD cholangiocytes confirmed as mechanism. Real preclinical ARPKD-specific evidence. Somatostatin is the most directly validated pharmacological intervention for ARPKD-CHF liver. ESTABLISHED SCIENCE. Primary citations: (1) Masyuk TV et al. Hepatology 56(6):2208-18, 2012. DOI: 10.1002/hep.25921 (octreotide in PCK rat liver); (2) Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 57(6):2328-37, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290 (pasireotide vs octreotide — primary source for all percentage data); Phase 2 trial: NCT01670110.

⚠️ UNPROVEN (Tier 3) — Scribble "dominance" over FXR-YAP: No published direct comparative experiment showing Scribble mislocalization overrides FXR-YAP in cholangiocytes. The Scribble paper (Fabris/Strazzabosco/Cadamuro — PMC9150862) and the FXR-YAP paper (Djouder lab, Nat Metab 2026 (corrected)) both describe real published science — their hierarchical relationship has never been directly tested. MUST BE FRAMED AS HYPOTHESIS when communicating with Djouder or any researcher. Component citations: Fabris L et al. Hepatology 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32298 · Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z

⚠️ UNPROVEN (Tier 3 extrapolation) — FXR→SOCS3→STAT3 in biliary cells: FXR-SOCS3-STAT3 suppression confirmed in hepatocytes, HepG2, and HCC models only — not in biliary epithelial cells/cholangiocytes. Supporting citations for hepatocyte data: (1) Oncotarget 8(70):114923-114934, 2017. DOI: 10.18632/oncotarget.23070; (2) Biochem Pharmacol 154:178-189, 2018. DOI: 10.1016/j.bcp.2018.04.020. SOCS3 expressed in biliary cells confirmed: PMC4612779 (Hepatology 2016. DOI: 10.1002/hep.28510). FXR→SOCS3 induction specifically in biliary cells: NOT CONFIRMED. Biologically plausible extrapolation. MUST BE FRAMED AS TESTABLE HYPOTHESIS, not established biliary mechanism.

Scribble and FXR are PARALLEL — no physical interaction, no transcriptional link. Both fail simultaneously in ARPKD-CHF. "Functional short-circuiting" explains why linafexor alone is insufficient. Three-layer model confirmed. (Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R — Scribble; Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z — FXR/YAP; Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — β-catenin/cAMP driver)

Three-layer model:
LAYER 1 (STRUCTURAL): FPC → Scribble membrane anchoring → physical restraint of YAP/β-catenin. FPC loss → Scribble drifts → YAP/β-catenin burst → cystogenesis. (Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R)
LAYER 2 (HOMEOSTATIC): FXR → transcriptional YAP maintenance → biliary barrier. FPC loss → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → SOCS3 absent → STAT3/SPP1 unchecked. (Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z)
LAYER 3 (cAMP DRIVER): FPC loss → Ca²⁺ impaired → AC5/AC6 relieved → cAMP → PKA → SRC/STAT3 + pSer675-β-catenin. (Masyuk AI, Masyuk TV, Splinter PL, Huang BQ, Stroope AJ, LaRusso NF. Gastroenterology 131(3):911-920, 2006. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2006.07.003. PMC: PMC1866168 — cAMP; Dafinger C, Liebau MC et al. J Clin Invest 130:3756, 2020. DOI: 10.1172/JCI130503 — SRC/STAT3; Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — pSer675)

Functional short-circuiting: "Even if FXR expression is normal, Scribble mislocalization breaks the physical brake on YAP — rendering the FXR-YAP protective axis entirely ineffective." Linafexor restores Layer 2 (FXR transcriptional) but cannot restore Layer 1 (Scribble structural). Only FPC restoration fixes all three layers simultaneously → why curative approaches are essential. (Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R + Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z — framework synthesis)

Djouder model explained: His FXR/YAP KO disrupts Layer 2 only (Scribble intact → no cysts — Djouder N et al. Nat Metab 2026 (⚠️ corrected Sep 14: was 2021 Nat Cell Biol — paper is actually April 2026 Nature Metabolism Vol 8:1189-1211). DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z). Strazzabosco's Pkhd1del4/del4 mouse disrupts Layer 1 (Scribble paper confirmed — Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R). Neither alone = full ARPKD-CHF. A compound Pkhd1 x FXR-KO model or direct Scribble localization measurement in his FXR/YAP KO mice would confirm the parallel pathway model and define the synergistic severity of losing both layers.

Two β-catenin pathways in ARPKD-CHF are therapeutically distinct. ICG-001 worsens the dominant cAMP/pSer675 pathway. PRI-724 covers both. Rac-1 inhibitors block nuclear entry. Somatostatin prevents pSer675 formation upstream. (Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — pSer675 pathway; Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R — Scribble/canonical pathway; Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 57:2328-2337, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290 — somatostatin validation)

The distinction: cAMP/PKA → pSer675-β-catenin (destruction complex INTACT — PKA bypasses it; nuclear entry via Rac-1 activation) vs. Wnt-Hippo/Scribble → canonical β-catenin (destruction complex COMPROMISED). Same nuclear destination, completely different upstream mechanisms requiring completely different treatments. (Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — pSer675; Fabris L et al. J Hepatol 76(5):1386-1403, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R — Wnt-Hippo/Scribble canonical pathway)

CBP/β-catenin antagonists (ICG-001, PRI-724) — plausible anti-fibrotic candidates requiring evaluation in a PKHD1 model. β-catenin/CBP-driven transcription maintains an undifferentiated proliferative state; the switch to β-catenin/p300 initiates differentiation. ICG-001 reversed bleomycin-induced pulmonary fibrosis with downregulation of S100A4/FSP-1, cyclin D1 and survivin (Henderson WR Jr, Chi EY, Ye X, … Kahn M. PNAS 107(32):14309-14314, 2010), and ameliorated renal interstitial fibrosis by abolishing TGF-β1-induced Snail1, PAI-1, collagen I, fibronectin and α-SMA through a Smad-independent mechanism (Hao S, He W, Li Y, … Kahn M, Liu Y. JASN 22(9):1642-1653, 2011). PRI-724 is a second-generation CBP/β-catenin-specific antagonist — the same mechanistic class as ICG-001, its active metabolite C-82 derived from it — and has been assessed for safety, tolerability and antifibrotic effect in patients with hepatitis C cirrhosis, making it the clinical-stage option. The open question specific to ARPKD-CHF: does CBP/β-catenin antagonism behave the same way against PKA-driven pSer675-β-catenin as against canonical Wnt-driven β-catenin? That distinction is untested. Direct support for targeting this axis: conditional β-catenin deletion in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice produced −40% cysts, −40% inflammation and −35% fibrosis with nuclear YAP unchanged (Fabris L, … Strazzabosco M, Cadamuro M. FASEB J 36(6):e22364, 2022).

✅ Rac-1 inhibitors — new angle: pSer675 nuclear translocation requires Rac-1 GTPase activation. Low-dose Rac-1 inhibition blocks nuclear entry even after pSer675 forms — downstream block that works if cAMP suppression is incomplete. Novel angle not previously in framework. (Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — Rac-1/pSer675 nuclear entry mechanism in ARPKD-CHF)

✅ Somatostatin — third independent validation: "Somatostatin receptor agonists are necessary to completely avoid creation of the pSer675 species." Preventing pSer675 formation (upstream) is better than blocking its nuclear activity (downstream). SSTR-Gi → AC5/AC6 inhibition → no cAMP → no PKA → no pSer675. Now validated 3 times independently: (1) cAMP suppression in PCK rat cholangiocytes: Masyuk TV et al. Hepatology 56(6):2208, 2012. DOI: 10.1002/hep.25921; (2) Pasireotide liver cyst reduction: Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 57(6):2328, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290; (3) pSer675 prevention: Kaffe E, Fiorotto R, Pellegrino F, Mariotti V, Amenduni M, Cadamuro M, Fabris L, Strazzabosco M, Spirli C. Hepatology 67(5):1903-1919, 2018. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652. PMID: 29140564

MMP14 (molecular scissors) — THIRD SELF-AMPLIFYING CIRCUIT in ARPKD-CHF. Proteolytic circuit: macrophage differentiation → MMP14 → endotrophin from periportal collagen VI → TLR4/NFκB → CXCL10 → more macrophages → more MMP14. Also amplifies SPP1 cascade. TREM2-ARC platform therapeutic angle. (Wang X et al. Nat Commun 15:8765, 2024. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-024-52449-1 — MMP14→endotrophin→TLR4/NFκB→CXCL10 circuit in fibrosis)

The three self-amplifying circuits in ARPKD-CHF:
1. MECHANICAL: fibrosis → stiffness → PIEZO1 → TGF-β/calpain2 → more fibrosis (Zhang L, Gui X et al. Nat Commun 17:2655, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-70193-w)
2. INFLAMMATORY: CXCL10 → macrophages → STAT3/SPP1 → more CXCL10 (PMID: 42265280 — Liebau/Ziegler, Commun Biol 2026)
3. PROTEOLYTIC (NEW): CXCL10 → monocytes → macrophage differentiation → MMP14 → periportal collagen VI cleaved → endotrophin → TLR4/NFκB → more CXCL10. (Wang X et al. Nat Commun 15:8765, 2024. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-024-52449-1) The structural CHF fibrosis itself becomes a substrate for inflammatory amplification independent of ongoing cholangiocyte injury.

MMP14 also amplifies SPP1: MMP14 is a published SPP1-processing enzyme. (Barker HE et al. J Biol Chem 286:10606-10615, 2011. DOI: 10.1074/jbc.M109.030254 — MMP14 cleaves SPP1/osteopontin) Macrophage MMP14 in ARPKD-CHF portal areas cleaves cholangiocyte-secreted SPP1 → SPP1 fragments with potentially enhanced α8β1/CD44 binding → amplified HSC activation even without additional SPP1 transcription.

Therapeutic target: NSC405020 (selective MMP14 inhibitor — Remacle AG et al. J Med Chem 60:2) 359-372, 2017. DOI: 10.1021/acs.jmedchem.6b00505). TREM2+ macrophage-targeting ARC platform (anti-TREM2 antibody-siRNA conjugate — already in framework for SPP1 siRNA) → could deliver MMP14 siRNA specifically to portal macrophages. Most cell-type-specific intervention available for the macrophage arm of the cascade. Tier 3 — not tested in biliary fibrosis.

⛔ ADDED AUGUST 21, 2026 ·

PIEZO1 CONTRAINDICATED in ARPKD-CHF — pro-fibrotic via TGF-β/calpain2/FAK. ADPKD kidney Yoda1 data did not measure liver fibrosis. New mechanofibrotic amplification loop identified. (Konstantinou C et al. Nat Commun 2024. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-024-49985-9 — PIEZO1 promotes fibrosis via TGF-β/FAK in hepatic tissue) Somatostatin + tolvaptan are safer cAMP suppressors. TRPV4 as alternative calcium pathway worth investigating.

PIEZO1 — status unresolved in ARPKD-CHF. PIEZO1 agonism (Yoda1) produced a reported 68% cyst reduction in ADPKD kidney (Fan Q et al. Kidney360 5:715-727, 2024), but that study did not measure liver fibrosis endpoints. PIEZO1 activation is pro-fibrotic in pulmonary tissue via CAPN2-mediated STAT3 phosphorylation and IL-33 secretion (Zhang L, Gui X et al. Nat Commun 17:2655, 2026) — endothelial and pulmonary, not biliary. Two open questions determine whether PIEZO1 is viable here: does PIEZO1 activation drive fibrosis in hepatic and biliary tissue specifically, and does Yoda1 raise global cytoplasmic Ca²⁺ rather than restoring the ciliary microdomain signal? Neither has been answered.

⭐ New mechanofibrotic amplification loop: Periportal fibrosis → matrix stiffness → mechanical PIEZO1 activation → TGF-β + calpain2 + FAK → more fibrosis → more stiffness → more PIEZO1. Self-amplifying loop partly explaining CHF progression despite static genetic cause. PIEZO1 inhibition could interrupt it — but blocks anti-cystogenic Ca²⁺ effect (Catch-22). No approved PIEZO1 inhibitor. (Konstantinou C et al. Nat Commun 2024. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-024-49985-9 — PIEZO1→TGF-β/calpain2/FAK→fibrosis in hepatic tissue)

Safer cAMP approaches + new lead: Somatostatin/octreotide (SSTR-Gi → AC inhibition) + tolvaptan (V2R antagonism) — both approved, neither pro-fibrotic. TRPV4 (TRP vanilloid 4 — bile flow mechanosensor in cholangiocytes) suggested as alternative calcium-raising pathway potentially without PIEZO1 pro-fibrotic consequence. TRPV4 → Ca²⁺ → AC5/AC6 suppression → cAMP reduction without TGF-β/FAK fibrosis cascade? Needs investigation. GSK1016790A (TRPV4 agonist) exists but no clinical-stage agent. TRPV4 in ARPKD cholangiocytes: Gradilone SA, Masyuk TV et al. Gastroenterology 139(1):304-14, 2010. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2010.03.052. Somatostatin: Masyuk TV et al. Hepatology 56:2208, 2012. DOI: 10.1002/hep.25921.

⭐ ADDED AUGUST 21, 2026 · Personal communication — Dr. Nabil Djouder (ndjouder@cnio.es, CNIO Madrid). Reply to our August 19 email. ⭐ Update: TWO-HIT MODEL

Djouder lab: FXR/YAP knockout mice do NOT develop hepatic cysts — FXR-YAP loss alone is insufficient. Two-hit framework: active driver (FPC→cAMP→STAT3→SPP1) + absent brake (FXR-YAP) both required for full ARPKD-CHF phenotype.

Critical finding: Despite strong biliary dedifferentiation and fibrosis in FXR/YAP knockout models, no hepatic cystic phenotype was observed (Djouder, N. personal communication, August 19, 2026. Permission granted September 2, 2026. CNIO Madrid). FXR-YAP loss alone = biliary fibrosis and dedifferentiation but NOT cysts. Djouder lab had extensively discussed the cilia dysregulation hypothesis in connection with FXR-YAP.

Two-hit framework: ARPKD-CHF requires BOTH: (1) ACTIVE DRIVER — FPC loss → cAMP → PKA → SRC → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis (confirmed — PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6 · PCK rat transcriptomics, Liebau/Ziegler group, Commun Biol 2026); (2) ABSENT BRAKE — FPC loss → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → SOCS3 not FXR-induced → pSTAT3 brake absent → biliary identity lost. FXR/YAP KO alone misses the active driver → no cysts. FPC loss provides both simultaneously → full ARPKD-CHF phenotype.

Djouder validation + next steps: "Your suggestion that FXR loss could potentially be affected downstream of PKHD1/FPC mutations is worth investigating experimentally in PKHD1-deficient biliary cells. This is something we may start exploring in the near future as mouse models are already generated in our lab." His FXR/YAP KO mice ready — potential Pkhd1 cross with Strazzabosco's Pkhd1del4/del4 model. Therapeutic implication: must address BOTH hits — pulsatile linafexor (brake) + cholangiocyte-targeted SPP1 siRNA (PMID: 42265280) (driver) + somatostatin/octreotide (Masyuk AI et al. 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290) (root cAMP). (Djouder, N. personal communication, August 19, 2026. Permission granted September 2, 2026.)

Finding 4: ⭐ EFHD1 — ER-mitochondria contact protein drives liver inflammation via PKR antiviral response 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Eberhardt DR, Chaudhuri D et al. J Clin Invest 2026. DOI: 10.1172/JCI204023 — University of Utah, September 2026. Tier 2-3. EFHD1 stabilizes ER-mitochondria contacts. Excessive EFHD1 → mitochondrial fragmentation → dsRNA escape → PKR fires → inflammation and fibrosis. ⚠️ NOT EXPRESSED IN CHOLANGIOCYTES (Sep 10, 2026): Yale Human Liver Single Cell Atlas (Brancale & Vilarinho, J Hepatol 2021, PMID 34016468) — EFHD1 not detected in any liver cell type. HPA: highest expression in distal tubular cells (347.3 nTPM), not liver. Fig. S1A of this paper also shows low/absent cholangiocyte expression. ⚠️ Personal communication from Dr. Chaudhuri with mechanistic insight — permission pending before citing. EFHD1 and PKR→STAT3 removed from ARPKD-CHF biliary cascade. NEW: EFHD1 highest expression = distal tubular cells (ARPKD kidney cyst origin, Kuraoka/Morizane 2026) — kidney relevance possible. NEW: EFHD2 (sister protein) IS in cholangiocytes per Yale atlas — new open question. Contact: Dipayan Chaudhuri MD PhD (dipayan.chaudhuri@hsc.utah.edu) — replied Sep 10 · permission pending.

PKHD1 / FIBROCYSTIN (FPC) — MOLECULAR FUNCTION: COMPLETE CURRENT PICTURE

Despite 20+ years of research, FPC molecular function remains incompletely understood. Here is what we now know — and what remains unknown.

Notch-like processing — the unifying framework: (Hogan MC et al. J Mol Med 92:1061-1072, 2014. DOI: 10.1007/s00109-014-1185-7) FPC undergoes regulated 3-step proteolytic cleavage: C1 (proprotein convertase, Golgi) → PECD/PTM heterodimer. C2 (ADAM metalloprotease, triggered by unknown extracellular ligand) → PECD shed into urine as soluble protein or EV-bound polycystin complex (FPC+PC1+PC2) — basis for Chung's uEV therapy. C3 (γ-secretase) → releases FPC-CTT with ciliary targeting (CTS), mitochondrial (MLS→ICD15), and nuclear (NLS) sequences. FPC loss blocks all six downstream functions simultaneously.

1. Ciliary sensing/signaling: FPC+PC1 synergistically suppress the cilia-dependent cyst activation (CDCA) signal. Extracellular ligand unknown after 20+ years.

2. SRC→STAT3 suppression (DIRECT mechanism for cascade node): (Dafinger C, Liebau MC et al. J Clin Invest 130:3756-3771, 2020. DOI: 10.1172/JCI130503) FPC-CTT forms dynamic protein complex with SRC kinase. FPC-CTT directly suppresses SRC → prevents STAT3 pY705. FPC loss → SRC constitutively active → pSTAT3 → SPP1 → macrophage cascade. Confirmed in human ARPKD cyst-lining cells. cAMP/PKA (elevated by FPC loss) further activates SRC → double amplification. JAK2 also in complex → JAK inhibitors (ruxolitinib, baricitinib) are candidate therapeutics.

3. ⭐ Ubiquitination via NDFIP2/SMURF/NEDD4: (Kaimori JY, Germino GG et al. Nat Commun 8:1520, 2017. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-017-01378-x; confirmed Guay-Woodford LM et al. Pediatr Nephrol 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x) FPC-CTT co-localizes with NDFIP2 (HECT E3 ligase adaptor). FPC loss → SMURF1/SMURF2/NEDD4 mislocalized → (a) SMURF dysfunction → TGF-β receptors/Smads not properly degraded → TGF-β pathway hyperactivated — DIRECT FPC-to-fibrosis mechanism, parallel to and independent of macrophage cascade; (b) NEDD4 → RhoA upregulation → cystogenesis; (c) NEDD4 → ENaC retention → sodium reabsorption → direct mechanism for ARPKD hypertension. Zone 3 variants (AA 2625-4074, including our daughter's c.9370C>T at AA 3124 and c.10926G>A at AA 3642) specifically disrupt CTT function → explaining why Zone 3 variants cause more severe liver disease via loss of SMURF-mediated TGF-β regulation in cholangiocytes. (Lapunzina-Soler P et al. Genes 17(2):229, 2026. DOI: 10.3390/genes17020229 — Zone 3 genotype-phenotype data)

4. Mitochondrial function via ICD15: (Walker RV, Qian F et al. Nat Commun 14:6513, 2023. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-023-42196-4) ICD15 → mitochondria → maintains structural integrity and bioenergetics. FPC loss → mitochondrial defects confirmed by EM. GDF15 upregulated as stress response → brain-liver glucocorticoid anti-inflammatory (Wang D, Steinberg GR et al. Cell Metab 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cmet.2026.07.008). Connects to Nrf2/GPX4 anti-ferroptotic protection (spermidine third mechanism).

5. Nuclear signaling via FPC-CTT: Translocates via importin-β1 → activates MYC P1 promoter → MYC upregulated in human (not mouse) ARPKD kidneys. Species-specific (CYS1/PKHD1 expression timing differs). In cholangiocytes: not studied. MYC → cell proliferation → ductular expansion. (Guay-Woodford LM et al. Pediatr Nephrol 2026. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-025-07129-x — FPC-CTT nuclear signaling review)

6. Biliary inflammation/fibrosis: (Locatelli L, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 65:822-838, 2016. DOI: 10.1002/hep.28776 ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026: Locatelli 2016 showed αvβ6 integrin reaches 70% of biliary structures at 12 months in Pkhd1del4/del4 mice with r=0.94 correlation with portal fibrosis — the strongest preclinical rationale for αvβ6 inhibition in ARPKD-CHF. Hirschfield GM et al. J Hepatol 84(1):86-98, Jan 2026 (DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.016) now published Phase 2 bexotegrast (αvβ6/αvβ1 inhibitor) results in PSC. Combined, these make a direct mechanistic argument for bexotegrast testing in ARPKD-CHF — more specific than for PSC. Strazzabosco (Yale) is the connector for both papers.; Tsunoda T et al. J Hepatol 71(1):143-152, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2019.02.024. PMID: 30898581) FPC-deficient Pkhd1del4/del4 cholangiocytes secrete chemokines → macrophage recruitment → pro-fibrogenic cascade. IL-8/CTGF confirmed in iPSC-derived FPC-deficient cholangiocytes (our IL-8/CTGF cascade node).

FXR gap definitively confirmed by Exhaustive literature search by

What remains unknown — and what we've found:

1. Extracellular ligand — RGD domain + αvβ6 hypothesis: FPC has an RGD domain (OMIM #606702 — PKHD1). RGD binds αv integrins. αvβ6 is the key candidate: expressed on cholangiocytes, primary TGF-β activator in biliary fibrosis. (Martínez-Salgado C et al. J Hepatol 78:160-173, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2022.07.025 — αvβ6 in biliary fibrosis) FPC's shed PECD (containing RGD) may normally occupy αvβ6 → blocking TGF-β activation. FPC loss → αvβ6 free → DUAL TGF-β hyperactivation (SMURF dysfunction + αvβ6 activation). PbH1 repeats suggest hyaluronan binding (TMEM2 hyaluronidase homology — Yuan L et al. Nature 650, 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41586-025-09884-1). Bannell/Cockburn (Leeds) working toward FPC structural characterization.

2. cAMP mechanism: FPC in cholangiocyte cilia works with PC2 to sense bile flow → Ca²⁺ signaling. (Masyuk TV, Huang BQ, Ward CJ et al. Gastroenterology 125(5):1303-1310, 2003. DOI: 10.1016/j.gastro.2003.09.001. PMID: 14598246; Masyuk AI, Masyuk TV, Splinter PL, Huang BQ, Stroope AJ, LaRusso NF. Gastroenterology 131(3):911-920, 2006. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2006.07.003. PMC: PMC1866168) FPC loss → malformed cilia → impaired bile flow sensing → reduced Ca²⁺ influx → calcium-inhibited AC5/AC6 relieved → cAMP accumulates → PKA → simultaneously: (a) SRC→STAT3→SPP1 (Liebau 2020) AND (b) β-catenin pSer675 → nuclear β-catenin (Spirli 2013). Elevated cAMP/PKA is the single upstream origin of both parallel fibrogenic pathways.

3. Cholangiocyte functions — partially answered: Confirmed in BECs: FPC in cilia (Masyuk TV 2003); malformed cilia/impaired Ca²⁺ (Masyuk AI 2006); IL-8/CTGF (Tsunoda 2019); chemokine→macrophage (Locatelli/Strazzabosco 2016); CFTR (Cebotaru 2021). NOT confirmed: SRC/STAT3, SMURF, ICD15, MYC, FXR. Freedman iPSC platform = first to test all simultaneously.

4. FXR connection — strongest circumstantial evidence yet still unconfirmed: No direct evidence. But: FPC loss → β-catenin activation (Kaffe E, Fiorotto R, Pellegrino F, Mariotti V, Amenduni M, Cadamuro M, Fabris L, Strazzabosco M, Spirli C. Hepatology 67(5):1903-1919, 2018. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652. PMID: 29140564) MATCHES FXR ablation in BECs → β-catenin activation. FXR reduced in biliary atresia (Front Immunol 13:821968, 2022. DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2022.821968). Mechanistic inference: FPC loss → impaired ciliary Ca²⁺ → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → constitutive YAP → β-catenin. Single most important experiment: measure FXR in Pkhd1del4/del4 BECs (Strazzabosco) or PKHD1-KO iPSC BECs (Freedman).

5. 3D structure — AlphaFold prediction exists, no experimental structure: AlphaFold prediction available at alphafold.ebi.ac.uk (PKHD1, UniProt Q8TCY7). No cryo-EM or X-ray data. Predicted: IPT domains form plexin-like propeller; RGD in PbH1 region. Bannell/Cockburn (Leeds) most likely to attempt experimental structural work.

Guay-Woodford confirmed email (from paper authorship): guaywoodfl@chop.edu. HRFD coordinator: Raven Reid (contact via guaywoodfl@chop.edu). REDCap enrollment code: YY3AJN93T · Phone: 267-425-3933

PCK rat liver RNA-seq (1,298 DEGs, PND15/20/30/90) — ARPKD-specific confirmation of SPP1/STAT3/CTGF/PDGFB cascade nodes + HNF4α loss + CELSR1/PCP + therapeutic window PND20-30. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Cascade nodes confirmed in ARPKD-specific data (Tier upgrade: adjacent-disease → ARPKD-confirmed):

• SPP1 (osteopontin) — upregulated at all time points. Our primary cascade node after FXR-YAP now has ARPKD-specific transcriptomic validation. CD44 co-upregulated. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025; PMID: 42265280 — SPP1 cholangiocyte-specific role confirmed)

• STAT3 — primary upstream regulator, PND30/90. Drives SPP1 transcription in our cascade. STAT1, STAT4, STAT5B also activated.

• CTGF + PDGFB — upregulated at ALL time points. Confirms PDGFR-β has ARPKD-specific transcriptomic support. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025; Guan Y, Peltz G et al. JCI Insight 2024. DOI: 10.1172/jci.insight.185547 — PDGFR-β validation in ARPKD organoids)

• SMAD7 INHIBITED (PND30/90) → enhanced TGF-β pathway confirmed. SMAD7 activation = therapeutic target. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• HNF4α DOWNREGULATED (PND30/90) — first ARPKD-specific confirmation of impaired hepatocyte differentiation. Double failure: HNF4G transdifferentiation blocked (LSEC/laminin — Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021) AND HNF4α hepatocyte identity impaired (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025).

• CELSR1 (Wnt/PCP gene) UPREGULATED — directly confirms non-canonical Wnt/PCP dysregulation in ARPKD-CHF liver (validates DPM Mechanism 2). (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• CXCL16 — upregulated; extends macrophage recruitment cascade via CXCR6 axis. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

• Novel target: MTPN (myotrophin) — activated PND15-90; metabolic/insulin connection; miR-375 suppresses it. (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Critical therapeutic window: PND20-30 in rats (extended postnatal period in humans — up to ~2 years). (Khare S, Jiang L, Paine-Cabrera D, Apte U, Pritchard MT. Livers 3(3):331-346, 2023. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025 — PCK rat transcriptomics PND15-90) Significant transcriptomic divergence begins at PND30. Maps directly onto neonatal CFTR correction window (Cebotaru) and Wnt5a/PCP intervention window. Network: Pritchard MT (mpritchard@kumc.edu), Apte U — KUMC. Not yet contacted.

⛔ mTOR INHIBITORS CONTRAINDICATED IN ARPKD-CHF — PCK rat sirolimus worsens bile acids + bilirubin. (Renken C, Fischer DC, Kundt G, Gretz N, Haffner D. "Inhibition of mTOR with sirolimus does not attenuate progression of liver and kidney disease in PCK rats." PMID: 20615907) + ⭐ NEW LEAD: Somatostatin/octreotide suppresses cAMP via SSTR-Gi — dual benefit: portal hypertension + STAT3/SPP1 cascade.

⭐ Stattic — p-STAT3 inhibitor validated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes (Sep 11, 2026 — Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026): Stattic is a small molecule that inhibits STAT3 phosphorylation at Tyr705. In si-PKHD1-transfected HIBEpiC human biliary epithelial cells, Stattic at 5 μM significantly reduces p-STAT3 without affecting total STAT3, suppresses SPP1 mRNA and OPN protein expression, and attenuates the SPP1 upregulation caused by PKHD1 knockdown. This is the first direct in vitro validation of p-STAT3 inhibition as a therapeutic approach in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Stattic is a research compound (not FDA-approved) but validates p-STAT3 as a tractable drug target. FDA-approved agents that suppress STAT3 phosphorylation or JAK/STAT signaling upstream: ruxolitinib (JAK1/2 inhibitor, FDA-approved for myelofibrosis/GVHD), baricitinib (JAK1/2, FDA-approved), napabucasin (cancer STAT3 inhibitor). All are candidates for ARPKD-CHF p-STAT3/SPP1 axis suppression.

⛔ mTOR inhibitors — PCK rat evidence: Sirolimus completely failed to improve hepatic fibrosis or cyst area in PCK rats AND significantly elevated bile acids + bilirubin in chronically treated male PCK rats — active worsening of cholestasis. (⚠️ CITATION CORRECTED Sep 16, 2026 — the evidence is Renken C, Fischer DC, Kundt G, Gretz N, Haffner D. "Inhibition of mTOR with sirolimus does not attenuate progression of liver and kidney disease in PCK rats." PMID: 20615907, NOT Masyuk/Hepatology 2022 (an autophagy paper). Verified findings: male PCK rats on sirolimus had significantly increased bile acids and bilirubin at 12 weeks (each P<0.05); neither fibrosis nor cyst area improved; 5 of 43 sirolimus-treated PCK rats died during the study versus none of the controls. ✅ This contraindication is CONFIRMED — and the mortality signal strengthens it beyond what our entry stated.) Mechanism: mTOR regulates bile acid transporter expression; inhibition → reduced BSEP/MRP2 → intracellular bile acid accumulation → cholestatic injury additive to DPM. In ARPKD-CHF with already-compromised ferroptosis risk (bile acid accumulation → GPX4 depletion), mTOR inhibition is doubly dangerous. Clinical: 40% discontinuation rate in polycystic disease trials; exacerbates portal hypertension; accelerates CKD. NOT recommended for ARPKD with liver involvement. Q6 ANSWERED.

YAP-mTOR crosstalk (new node): Constitutive YAP → stimulates mTORC1 → drives ductular proliferation. mTOR inhibition partially blocks this but causes bile acid/liver damage as described. Upstream YAP suppression via linafexor (pulsatile FXR → restores normal YAP cycling) is safer than downstream mTOR blockade.

⭐ NEW THERAPEUTIC LEAD: Somatostatin/Octreotide (cAMP suppression via SSTR-Gi): SSTR2/SSTR5 expressed on cholangiocytes. (Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 57:2328-2337, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26290) Octreotide/lanreotide → SSTR → Gi → inhibits AC5/AC6 → cAMP reduced in cholangiocytes → PKA suppressed → less SRC activation → less pSTAT3 → less SPP1 → fibrosis cascade slowed. This directly targets the upstream mechanism (elevated cAMP from impaired ciliary Ca²⁺ sensing). ⚠️ The FXR→SOCS3→STAT3 brake component of this cascade is confirmed in hepatocytes — extrapolation to biliary cholangiocytes. DUAL BENEFIT for ARPKD-CHF: (1) Already standard therapy for portal hypertension (reduces splanchnic blood flow); (2) May suppress cAMP-driven STAT3/SPP1 cascade via cholangiocyte SSTR. Most immediately clinically accessible agent for ARPKD-CHF patients with portal hypertension — addresses both the clinical complication and potentially the root molecular mechanism simultaneously.

⭐ ADDED AUGUST 21, 2026 · Liver organoid landscape research. Peltz: Nat Commun 12:6138, 2021 (PMC8536785) + JCI Insight Dec 2024. Huch: Nature 650:438-449, 2026. Sampaziotis+Vallier: Science 371:839-846, 2021.

LIVER ORGANOID LANDSCAPE FOR ARPKD-CHF — PDGFRB validated therapeutic target (imatinib/sunitinib/crenolanib anti-fibrotic in human ARPKD organoids). STAT3 4th independent confirmation. Peltz microHO platform. Huch Nature 2026 full citation. Sampaziotis-Vallier cholangiocyte transplantation.

⭐ Peltz lab (Stanford, gpeltz@stanford.edu) — human ARPKD liver organoid drug screening platform: Guan Y et al. (Nat Commun 12:6138, 2021; PMC8536785): iPSC with PKHD1 Thr36Met → hepatic organoids develop ARPKD liver pathology in 21 days. Confirmed: TGFβ pathway in ARPKD cholangiocytes ✅; STAT3 activated in ARPKD myofibroblasts ✅ (4th independent confirmation — after Pritchard/PCK rat, Ziegler/Pkhd1-KO mouse, Liebau/human tissue); PDGFRB markedly elevated in myofibroblasts. ⭐ THREE FDA-APPROVED DRUGS SHOW ANTI-FIBROTIC EFFECT: imatinib (Gleevec), sunitinib (Sutent), and crenolanib all attenuate fibrosis in human ARPKD liver organoids. PDGFB (ligand) also upregulated in PCK rat (Pritchard/Apte) — two independent datasets converge on PDGFRB as therapeutic target. Not yet tested: somatostatin, linafexor, SPP1 siRNA, PRI-724.

Peltz microHO platform: (Guan Y et al. JCI Insight December 2024. DOI: 10.1172/jci.insight.185547) Live-cell imaging of liver fibrosis in microwell-based hepatic organoids. Key finding: receptor-specific TKIs have antifibrotic effect LIMITED to fibrosis from their corresponding growth factor — supports multi-target approach for ARPKD-CHF (somatostatin + linafexor + SPP1 siRNA). MicroHO = ideal screening platform for our entire therapeutic portfolio. Not yet contacted — email: gpeltz@stanford.edu.

Huch periportal liver assembloid — full citation confirmed: Yuan, Dawka, Kim et al. (Huch M). Nature 650:438-449, 2026. Hepatocyte organoids + portal mesenchyme + cholangiocyte organoids → models biliary fibrosis with correct periportal cellular architecture. Direct ARPKD-CHF drug screening platform.

Sampaziotis + Vallier (Science 371:839-846, 2021) — curative cell replacement: Cholangiocyte organoids transplanted into deceased human donor livers via ex situ normothermic perfusion → engraftment + bile duct repair + improved bile pH/volume, maintained 100h. Vallier is co-author (our contact, BIH Charité Berlin). Application: PKHD1-corrected cholangiocyte organoids (Sampaziotis/Vallier platform) + ERCP biliary delivery (Guimont 2021, validated in human-sized pigs) = direct curative cell replacement for ARPKD-CHF biliary epithelium.

⭐ CLINICALLY ACTIONABLE — ADDED AUGUST 20, 2026 ·

SPP1 (OSTEOPONTIN) — Blood test available NOW. Biliary fibrosis stage. Portal hypertension severity. Transplant-free survival predictor. Commercially available ELISA — can be ordered now.

Cascade summary (end-to-end):
[PKHD1 deficiency] → p-STAT3 (cholangiocytes) → p-STAT3 binds SPP1 promoter directly → SPP1 secreted → α8β1 integrin + CD44 on HSCs/portal fibroblasts → PI3K/Akt/NF-κB (α8β1 confirmed per Jiao/Zhu PMID 42265280 — NF-κB downstream effector — new node) → Acta2/Col1a1 → fibrosis F3-F4 → portal hypertension

SPP1 blood test — what it tells you (Kim SU et al. J Hepatol 78:1059, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2023.01.005): (1) Biliary fibrosis stage F0→F4 — correlates progressively with AUROC accuracy. (2) Portal hypertension severity — mirrors HVPG directly, independent predictor of clinically significant portal hypertension. (3) Transplant-free survival — strong surrogate predictor. Standard LFTs (ALT, AST, bilirubin) miss early biliary fibrosis in ARPKD-CHF. SPP1 directly reflects p-STAT3 cascade activity.

⭐ Immediate action: Ask Cincinnati Children's hepatology to order serum osteopontin (SPP1/OPN) — commercially available ELISA. Provides molecular baseline of cascade activity. Can be tracked serially to monitor progression or therapeutic response.

⭐ Serum OPN reference values from ARPKD mouse model (Jiao/Zhu Commun Biol 2026 — first quantitative OPN progression curve in any ARPKD model): Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice by ELISA: ~100 ng/mL at 1 month → ~200 ng/mL at 3 months → ~400 ng/mL at 6 months vs. ~100 ng/mL stable in Pkhd1+/+ controls (4-fold elevation at peak fibrosis). After AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 treatment: serum OPN drops to ~5 ng/mL (near-complete suppression). Human ELISA kit: BOSTER EK0482. Mouse ELISA kit: BOSTER EK4803. These are the first published quantitative OPN values in an ARPKD-specific model and establish the expected magnitude of elevation and treatment response.

NF-κB + linafexor dual suppression: SPP1 activates myofibroblasts via PI3K/Akt/NF-κB (Bu HF et al. Am J Physiol GI 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00079.2026). FXR directly antagonizes NF-κB via transrepression (Renga B et al. J Hepatol 58:857-862, 2013. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2013.02.017). Linafexor (pulsatile FXR) therefore addresses this cascade at TWO points: upstream (SOCS3 → reduced pSTAT3 → reduced SPP1 production) AND downstream (FXR transrepression of NF-κB → reduced myofibroblast activation). This is the dual mechanism that makes pulsatile FXR agonism the most comprehensive intervention in ARPKD-CHF.

⭐ PMID: 42265280 CONFIRMED — TIER 1 — FIRST ARPKD-CHF THERAPEUTIC PROOF-OF-CONCEPT · Communications Biology 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6. Pkhd1Δ3-4/Δ3-4 mouse model.

Cholangiocyte-specific AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 knockdown reduces hepatic fibrosis in ARPKD/CHF mice — cascade confirmed end-to-end + PI3K/Akt is the SPP1→HSC signaling mechanism (new node). Full paper read Sep 11, 2026 — key new details below. (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6)

Cascade confirmed end-to-end in ARPKD-specific tissue: PKHD1 deficiency → p-STAT3-dependent SPP1 overexpression in cholangiocytes → SPP1 activates HSCs/portal fibroblasts via PI3K/Akt signaling (NEW CASCADE NODE) → Acta2 (α-SMA) + Col1a1 (collagen I) upregulation → fibrosis. (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6)

⭐ New details from full paper read — Sep 11, 2026:

  • SPP1 = second-ranked CellChat ligand, not first: The top-ranked ligand showed no significant difference between CHF and controls and was not investigated further. SPP1 was selected as the second-ranked but most significantly differentially expressed ligand mediating cholangiocyte→HSC communication. Therapeutic priority is unchanged.
  • Macrophages are NOT the dominant SPP1 source in ARPKD-CHF: Co-localization showed OPN had almost no overlap with F4/80 macrophage marker. Cholangiocytes — not macrophages — are the primary OPN source in ARPKD-CHF. The TREM2+/SPP1+ macrophage biology (Qiu et al. 2026, Finding 10) operates in other fibrotic diseases; in ARPKD-CHF the cholangiocyte-direct pathway dominates. Clodronate macrophage depletion did not fully resolve CHF fibrosis — confirming a direct cholangiocyte→HSC pathway operates independently.
  • Per-cell SPP1 elevation confirmed — not just expansion: scRNA-seq and primary cell isolation both confirmed ~4-fold Spp1 mRNA elevation per individual PKHD1-deficient cholangiocyte (not diluted by cell number). IF normalized to CK19+ cells confirmed this per-cell upregulation.
  • p-STAT3 binds SPP1 promoter at sites 1, 2, and 4 — NOT site 3: ChIP-qPCR confirmed significant enrichment at sites 1, 2, and 4. Site 3 showed no significant enrichment. Dual-luciferase assay with WT vs. mutant SPP1 promoter confirmed STAT3 directly activates SPP1 transcription.
  • Serum OPN values established: Pkhd1del3-4/del3-4 mice by ELISA: ~100 ng/mL (1m) → ~200 ng/mL (3m) → ~400 ng/mL (6m) vs. ~100 ng/mL stable in WT controls. After AAV8-shSpp1 treatment: drops to ~5 ng/mL. Human ELISA kit: BOSTER EK0482. Mouse kit: BOSTER EK4803. First quantitative OPN progression curve in any ARPKD model.
  • Stattic (p-STAT3 inhibitor) validated in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes: At 5 μM, Stattic significantly inhibits p-STAT3 without affecting total STAT3 levels in HIBEpiC cells. Suppresses SPP1 mRNA and OPN protein in a dose-dependent manner. Attenuates SPP1 upregulation in si-PKHD1 cells. First direct in vitro validation of p-STAT3 inhibition in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Clinical STAT3 phosphorylation inhibitors (napabucasin, ruxolitinib, baricitinib) are candidates via this mechanism.
  • HNF1β promoter = validated cholangiocyte-specific AAV delivery sequence: The paper incorporated an HNF1β promoter into AAV8 to restrict Spp1 knockdown to cholangiocytes. Confirmed by co-localization: knockdown in MIB (CK19+ biliary cells) but not MPH (HNF4A+ hepatocytes). HNF1β promoter is therefore the validated sequence for any cholangiocyte-targeted AAV therapy in ARPKD-CHF — applicable to PKHD1 replacement, SPP1 siRNA, SPTAN1 CRISPRa, or FPCct domain delivery.
  • CHF patient clinical profile — ALT/AST normal, GGT elevated: Index patient (34-year-old, compound heterozygous PKHD1) had ALT 18 U/L, AST 19.5 U/L (near-normal), GGT 152.7 U/L (elevated), albumin 41.2 g/L (normal) — despite esophageal varices and prior variceal ligation. Confirms: standard LFTs grossly underestimate ARPKD-CHF severity. GGT is the most sensitive standard test. SPP1 serum would add molecular sensitivity beyond GGT.

⭐ Therapeutic proof-of-concept: AAV8-HNF1β-shSpp1 reduces biliary fibrosis in the correct ARPKD disease model. (PMID: 42265280) Delivery compatible with: ERCP retrograde biliary delivery (Guimont L et al. Mol Ther Methods Clin Dev 21:689-697, 2021); Chung EV/nanotherapeutic platform (USC); Marshall/A20FMDV2 αvβ6-targeted delivery (QMUL); Janowski hepatic arterial fractionation (UMD); Spot Biosystems. ⚠️ Sep 20, 2026: Williams (Stony Brook) removed — kidney track only, structurally excluded from biliary delivery.

⭐ HNF1β = validated cholangiocyte-specific promoter for AAV delivery in ARPKD-CHF (Sep 11, 2026): The Jiao/Zhu paper validated HNF1β as a cholangiocyte-specific promoter for in vivo AAV delivery. HNF1β is highly expressed in cholangiocytes with minimal expression in hepatocytes — confirmed by cell-type selective knockdown (effective in MIB biliary cells; no effect in MPH hepatocytes; CK19/HNF4A co-localization in Fig 8F). Implication for ARPKD gene therapy: Any AAV-based therapeutic for ARPKD liver disease — including PKHD1 partial replacement, SPP1 siRNA, SPTAN1 CRISPRa upregulation, or FPCct protein domain delivery — should use an HNF1β promoter to restrict expression to biliary epithelial cells and minimize hepatocyte off-target. Routing: Willenbring, Long, Ziegler WH — all working on biliary AAV delivery and need this promoter specification.

PI3K/Akt as the SPP1→myofibroblast mechanism: SPP1 binds α8β1 integrin + CD44 on HSC/portal fibroblast precursors → PI3K/Akt → myofibroblast activation. (Jiao J, Zhu C et al. Commun Biol 2026. PMID 42265280 — α8β1 confirmed as primary SPP1 receptor in ARPKD-CHF mouse model. ⚠️ He W et al. Cell Death Dis 2026 DOI: 10.1038/s41419-026-09158-2 describes MASP1→HA→CD44 pathway — separate from SPP1→α8β1 mechanism) PI3K/Akt also connects to mTOR (mTOR inhibitors → impairs HNF4G via PI3K/Akt/mTOR axis — safety concern (Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021)). And PI3K/Akt regulates YAP (LATS1 → YAP) — additional cascade node. This paper was the basis for Nathan's June 29, 2026 email to Professor Chung, which launched our entire research network.

FXR→SHP/SOCS3→STAT3 suppression — molecular bridge confirmed. ⭐ NEW CONNECTION Sep 14, 2026 — Djouder Nat Metab 2026 completes the picture: FXR transcriptionally activates YAP in biliary epithelial cells (BECs) to maintain ductal adhesion and identity. FPC loss → FXR impaired → YAP not activated by FXR → BECs lose adhesion and undergo β-catenin activation → mesenchymal-like conversion → fibrosis-to-cirrhosis. This means FXR→YAP is a biliary cell identity axis, not just a metabolic axis. ⚠️ CRITICAL THERAPEUTIC CAVEAT: Obeticholic acid (OCA) worsened fibrosis in FXR-deficient BECs in the Djouder model — FXR agonists require intact biliary FXR to work. In ARPKD-CHF where FPC loss may have chronically impaired FXR signaling, the receptor may not respond normally to FXR agonists. This does NOT invalidate linafexor or saroglitazar — but it means BEC FXR status must be characterized before any FXR-based ARPKD-CHF trial. Open question: can Liebau's ARegPKD cohort be used to measure biliary FXR expression in ARPKD patient tissue? (DOI: 10.1038/s42255-026-01521-z). FXR→SHP/SOCS3→STAT3 suppression — molecular bridge confirmed. Fxr knockout → constitutive pSTAT3. SOCS3 paradox in PCK rat. Linafexor/BEC/pSTAT3 experiment designed. (Oncotarget 8(70):114923, 2017. DOI: 10.18632/oncotarget.23070; Biochem Pharmacol 154:178, 2018. DOI: 10.1016/j.bcp.2018.04.020; Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025)

Two molecular pathways connecting FXR to STAT3 suppression in biliary cells:

• SHP pathway: FXR agonist → SHP induction → SHP inhibits upstream elements → pSTAT3 reduced. Confirmed in cholangiocyte-derived cells (GW4064, CDCA). Downstream: Bcl-xL reduced. (Kim YC et al. PLoS ONE 3:e1982, 2008. DOI: 10.1371/journal.pone.0001982 — FXR→SHP→pSTAT3 in biliary-derived cells)

• SOCS3 pathway (more important — ⚠️ confirmed in hepatocytes; biliary cell application is Tier 3 extrapolation): FXR directly transcriptionally activates SOCS3 (JAK-STAT3 negative feedback inhibitor) in hepatocytes and HCC cells. (Oncotarget 8(70):114923, 2017. DOI: 10.18632/oncotarget.23070; Biochem Pharmacol 154:178, 2018. DOI: 10.1016/j.bcp.2018.04.020) Fxr knockout → constitutive STAT3 hyper-phosphorylation — same phenotype as Pkhd1-knockout (pSTAT3 6-fold elevated — Fox JC et al. iScience 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2024.109487. PMC10973142 — citation corrected Sep 11, 2026). Mechanistic chain: FPC loss → impaired bile acid sensing → FXR not activated → SOCS3 not FXR-induced → JAK/SRC phosphorylate STAT3 without brake → constitutive pSTAT3 → SPP1 → fibrosis. ⚠️ FXR→SOCS3 link confirmed in hepatocytes and HCC cells only — extrapolation to ARPKD biliary cholangiocytes; not yet confirmed in biliary-specific PKHD1-deficient cells. FXR also antagonizes NF-κB → additional indirect SPP1 suppression (Renga B et al. J Hepatol 58:857, 2013. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2013.02.017).

The SOCS3 paradox in PCK rat (Pritchard/Apte — Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025): SOCS3 is upregulated in ARPKD-CHF liver — but this is STAT3-induced SOCS3 (futile feedback), NOT FXR-driven SOCS3 (effective suppression). Two SOCS3 routes: STAT3-driven (insufficient, present in ARPKD) vs. FXR-driven (effective, absent in ARPKD). Linafexor restores FXR-driven SOCS3 induction.

The definitive experiment (not yet done): PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes (Strazzabosco Pkhd1del4/del4 or Freedman PKHD1-KO iPSC) + pulsatile linafexor vs. continuous OCA. Measure: SHP, SOCS3, pSTAT3 (Y705), SPP1 at 6/12/24/48h. Prediction: pulsatile linafexor → sustained SOCS3/SHP → pSTAT3 suppressed → SPP1 reduced → no toxicity. Continuous OCA → receptor downregulation → STAT3 rebounds → toxicity. (Hypothesis — experiment not yet performed. Pkhd1del4/del4 model: Fabris L et al. FASEB J 36(6):e22364, 2022. DOI: 10.1096/fj.202101924R)

STAT3 confirmed 6-fold elevated in Pkhd1-knockout epithelia · YAP/TAZ 3-5x activated (Fox JC et al. iScience 2024. DOI: 10.1016/j.isci.2024.109487. PMC10973142 — citation corrected Sep 11, 2026) · FPCc rescue suppresses STAT3 (Outeda lab, in preparation — unpublished) · YAP confirmed in ARPKD-CHF hepatic cysts (Jiang Y, Pritchard MT, Apte U et al. Am J Physiol GI 313:G166-G178, 2017. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00135.2017)

Hannover PKD lab — NOW PUBLISHED Aug 28, 2026: (Fox JC, Hahnenstein ST, Hassan F, Grund A, Haffner D, Ziegler WH. iScience 27(4):109487, 2024. PMC10973142) pSTAT3 (Y705) 6- to 7-fold elevated in Pkhd1-KO epithelia at 6 months (3-fold at 3m, peaks 6-7x at 6m, 22% positive at 9m — age-dependent); STAT3 target genes Socs3, Ccl2, Stat3 upregulated at 6-9 months; YAP/TAZ nuclear activation confirmed. ⚠️ Model: female Pkhd1-KO mice; cysts in S3 segment (proximal tubule), not collecting duct — model limitation vs human ARPKD. ⚠️ Citation corrected Sep 11, 2026 — previously incorrectly cited to He W et al. Cell Death Dis 2026 (MASP1/HA paper). Correct source: Fox/Ziegler iScience 2024, read directly per Aug 21, 2026 audit. NOW PUBLISHED — Hassan F, Dafinger C, Liebau MC, Ziegler WH et al. (Am J Physiol Cell Physiol 2026. DOI: 10.1152/ajpcell.00086.2026): FPCct re-expression in Pkhd1-KO suppresses cyst formation AND rescues pSRC + pSTAT3. See full finding card in FPC Molecular Function section. Ziegler: Ziegler.Wolfgang@mh-hannover.de.

Jiang/Pritchard/Apte (KUMC 2017): (Jiang Y, Pritchard MT, Apte U et al. Am J Physiol GI 313:G166-G178, 2017. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00135.2017) YAP activation confirmed specifically in HEPATIC cyst epithelial cells in ARPKD/CHF — biliary-specific YAP confirmation. Our existing KUMC contacts (Pritchard + Apte — PCK rat transcriptomics — Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025) also confirmed YAP in ARPKD-CHF liver. Both the kidney (Fox/Ziegler) and liver (Jiang/Pritchard) confirm constitutive YAP activation in FPC-deficient epithelial cells.

⚠️ Therapeutic implications (from (1) Cholangiocyte-specific SPP1 knockdown significantly attenuates hepatic fibrosis in vivo (PMID: 42265280 · DOI: 10.1038/s42003-026-10476-6). (2) Dasatinib hepatotoxicity documented in rodent models — NOT recommended for systemic use in ARPKD-CHF with already-compromised liver. (Tran TH et al. Toxicol Appl Pharmacol 363:71-81, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.taap.2018.11.004) (3) STAT3 dual role: essential for hepatocyte survival and liver regeneration — systemic inhibition risks parenchymal injury. (Wang Y, Minuk GY et al. Hepatology 58:1021-1033, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.26621) Solution: cholangiocyte-targeted delivery of STAT3 inhibitor or SPP1 siRNA. This is precisely what Chung (USC nanotherapeutics for ARPKD) and Marshall/Janowski (hepatic arterial αvβ6-targeted delivery) are building. ⚠️ CORRECTION Sep 20, 2026: Williams (Stony Brook) is kidney track only — his platform is structurally excluded from biliary delivery. The biliary delivery solution is Marshall (A20FMDV2 targeting) + Janowski (hepatic arterial route), not Williams.

DPM is developmental — the structural defect is present from birth (PMID: 21708104 — ductal plate/periportal hepatocyte development). Postnatal therapies address fibrosis consequences, not the root structural failure. Two mechanisms and a therapeutic window.

The developmental distinction: ARPKD-CHF is fundamentally a developmental disease. The structurally abnormal intrahepatic bile ducts are present from birth. The postnatal FXR-YAP → SPP1 → TREM2 → fibrosis cascade described throughout this document is the CONSEQUENCE of the DPM, not its cause. Postnatal therapies can slow fibrosis progression; they cannot reverse the structural DPM. Only two approaches address established DPM: (1) intervention during the neonatal developmental window; (2) postnatal biliary cell replacement via ERCP (cholangiocyte organoid transplantation — Sampaziotis F, Vallier L et al. Science 371:839-846, 2021. DOI: 10.1126/science.aaz6462).

⭐ MECHANISM 1 — CFTR and cord hollowing (most therapeutically actionable): Cord hollowing — the tubulogenesis process creating bile duct lumina — requires CFTR at the apical cholangiocyte membrane to secrete chloride and water into nascent lumina. (Yanda MK, Zeidan A, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol GI 324:G404–G414, 2023. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00255.2022): FPC-null cholangiocytes have dramatically reduced apical CFTR → impaired cord hollowing → failed tubulogenesis → DPM. VX-809 in 5-day-old Pkhd1 mice improved liver pathology. Critical neonatal window: ductal plate remodeling continues postnatally (P1-P14 mouse; weeks 20-40 gestation→neonatal in humans). CFTR correction during this window could potentially reduce DPM severity — not just slow postnatal fibrosis. Trikafta (VX-445) neonatal testing in PKHD1 models is an unanswered question (Q23 in this document). Cebotaru (JHU, already in network) is positioned to answer this. (Ciobanu C, Outeda P, Guggino WB, Cebotaru L. Am J Physiol GI 329:G434–G442, 2025. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00109.2025): CFTR gene therapy for ARPKD — direct extension of this mechanism.

MECHANISM 2 — Non-canonical Wnt5a/PCP disruption: FPC in primary cilia maintains planar cell polarity (PCP) during ductal plate morphogenesis. FPC loss → disrupted ciliary PCP signaling → Wnt5a abnormally activated → failed planar arrangement of ductal plate cells → structurally abnormal duct formation. SOX9/Notch amplification: Sox9b↔Notch positive feedback maintains biliary identity; FPC loss disrupts this → reduced SOX9 → impaired branching morphogenesis (SOX9 loss causes DPM-like phenotype — bioRxiv 2024. DOI: 10.1101/2024.09.18.613676). Confirmed in cholangiopathy models: Wnt5a drives biliary PCP disruption → ductular reaction + portal fibroblast accumulation. (Tsunoda T et al. J Hepatol 71(1):143-152, 2019. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2019.02.024; CELSR1/PCP upregulation confirmed in PCK rat: Sci Rep 2026. DOI: 10.3390/livers3030025) New research question (Q24): Is Wnt5a/PCP disrupted in FPC-deficient ductal plate cells during the developmental window — and can PCP pathway modulators reduce DPM severity neonatally?

FRAMEWORK GAPS IDENTIFIED AUGUST 19, 2026 — Independent systematic research session

Five major gaps and curative approaches not previously in this framework

⚠️ GAP 1 — Portal fibroblasts, not HSCs, are the primary fibrosis-producing cells in biliary disease: Lineage tracing confirmed portal fibroblasts contribute >70% of myofibroblasts at onset in cholestatic/biliary liver injury. (Mederacke I et al. PNAS 111:14516-14521, 2014. DOI: 10.1073/pnas.1414373111) HSCs dominate only in hepatotoxic injury. Our cascade throughout attributes fibrosis to HSC activation — likely incorrect for ARPKD-CHF as a biliary disease. Portal fibroblasts are periductal, self-renewing, apoptosis-resistant, and express PDGFR-β — explaining why Peltz's PDGFR-β inhibitors worked and why CDH11 (Huch assembloid, portal mesenchyme) is an anti-fibrotic target. Framework correction: distinguish portal fibroblasts (primary biliary responders) from HSCs (secondary) throughout cascade descriptions.

⚠️ GAP 2 — Ferroptosis in congenital cholestatic liver disease — YAP/FBXL5 connects to our root cascade (Ohta Y et al. Am J Pathol 196(3):700-715, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ajpath.2025.11.008): Iron + bile acids → GPX4 depletion → ferroptosis in congenital cholestatic pediatric liver disease. Mouse model uses Yap^flox/flox^ × Fbxl5^flox/flox^ — YAP directly promotes cholestatic ferroptosis. In ARPKD-CHF: constitutive YAP (root node) + DCA/LCA accumulation (FiBA-HBOs confirmed) = ferroptosis conditions. Completely absent from framework. Spermidine third mechanism: → Nrf2 → GPX4 upregulation = anti-ferroptotic. (Zhang K et al. Cell 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.cell.2026.02.001) Cascade: bile acid accumulation → GPX4 depletion → ferroptosis → cholangiocyte death → worsened ductular reaction.

⚠️ GAP 3 — Wnt/β-catenin directly implicated in ARPKD-CHF — published mechanism absent from cascade (Spirli C, Strazzabosco M et al. Hepatology 58:1728-1738, 2013. DOI: 10.1002/hep.29652 — Pkhd1del4/del4 mice): cAMP/PKA → pSer675-β-catenin → nuclear β-catenin → TCF/LEF activation → E-cadherin loss in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes. Direct evidence in ARPKD mouse. Wnt and Hippo/YAP mutually cross-activate in biliary fibrosis. (Zhu L, Wu B. Front Immunol 17:1899587, 2026. DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1899587) β-catenin and constitutive YAP are co-activated and mutually reinforcing. Therapeutic: ICG-001/PRI-724 (TCF/β-catenin inhibitor — Lenz HJ et al. Mol Cancer Ther 14(9):2136, 2015. DOI: 10.1158/1535-7163.MCT-14-0223).

⚠️ GAP 4 — LSEC capillarization blocks liver regeneration — vicious cycle connecting portal hypertension to failed regeneration (J Hepatol March 27, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2026.02.014): Capillarized CD34+ LSECs maintain laminin around DR cells → blocks HNF4G-driven DR-to-hepatocyte transdifferentiation. (Galanakis V, Vallier L et al. J Hepatol 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021) Preventing LSEC capillarization → enables liver regeneration from within. In ARPKD-CHF: biliary fibrosis → portal hypertension → LSEC capillarization → blocked HNF4G regeneration → accelerated disease. Preventing LSEC capillarization (eNOS restoration, SEMA4D blockade — Gracia-Sancho; Gracia-Sancho J et al. J Hepatol 78:888-903, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2022.12.007) = unlock regeneration. Connects Gracia-Sancho (LSEC) + Vallier/Galanakis (HNF4G) contacts contacted August 2026.

✅ CURATIVE APPROACH — Cholangiocyte organoid transplantation via ERCP: (Sampaziotis F, Vallier L et al. Science 371:839-846, 2021. DOI: 10.1126/science.aaz6462; J Mater Chem B 2025) Cholangiocyte organoids repair human biliary epithelium in ex vivo perfusion (Science 2021). ICOs in decellularized liver hydrogels reduce fibrosis in chronically injured mice (J Mater Chem B 2025). ARPKD-CHF: ABE/CRISPR-corrected PKHD1 cholangiocyte organoids from patient cells → ERCP retrograde delivery (already validated for gene therapy, Guimont 2021) → replace FPC-deficient biliary epithelium with functional cells. Cell replacement rather than in situ editing — potentially more accessible. Completely absent from framework until now.

The hepatorenal molecular linkage — why one gene causes two diseases

The most fundamental question in ARPKD biology. The answer reveals shared therapeutic targets, explains divergent drug effects, and defines the sequential organ failure pattern that determines prognosis.

Fibrocystin — one protein, two organs

FPC is expressed on primary cilia of both renal collecting duct cells AND biliary epithelial cells (Masyuk TV, Huang BQ, Ward CJ et al. Gastroenterology 125(5):1303-1310, 2003. DOI: 10.1016/j.gastro.2003.09.001. PMID: 14598246). These are the only two cell types requiring high levels of FPC for normal function — precisely why ARPKD affects only kidneys and liver.

The mechanism is identical in both: FPC deficiency → impaired cilia Ca²⁺ entry → elevated cAMP (Masyuk AI, Masyuk TV, Splinter PL, Huang BQ, Stroope AJ, LaRusso NF. Gastroenterology 131(3):911-920, 2006. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2006.07.003. PMC: PMC1866168) → proliferative and pro-secretory cascades. But disease manifestation diverges because V2R (the primary cAMP driver in collecting ducts) is absent in biliary cells — which is why tolvaptan works in the kidney but has no direct effect on CHF.

Shared therapeutic targets — drugs that could help both organs

TargetDrug candidateKidney evidenceBiliary evidence
