Elexacaftor is the most compelling dual-organ candidate. (Yanda MK et al. (Outeda P). Cell Mol Gastroenterol Hepatol 12:1517-1529, 2021. DOI: 10.1016/j.jcmgh.2021.07.012) demonstrated VX-809 improved both kidney AND biliary disease in Pkhd1 mice simultaneously. Elexacaftor is 5-10× more potent. No one has tested it yet.

⚠ Divergent drug effects — where treating one organ harms the other

mTOR inhibitors: Beneficial in kidney (reduces cyst proliferation). Harmful in liver via three mechanisms: (1) autophagy → HDAC6 → ARL3/ARL13B depletion from biliary cilia (Masyuk AI et al. Hepatology 75:1110, 2022. DOI: 10.1002/hep.32298); (2) suppresses HNF4G → impairs cholangiocyte regenerative plasticity (Gribben C, Galanakis V, Calderwood A et al., Vallier L. Nature 630(8015):166-173, 2024. DOI: 10.1038/s41586-024-07465-2. PMID: 38778114 — PI3K/AKT/mTOR confirmed in cholangiocyte organoids; Galanakis V, Gribben C, Munteanu A et al., Mohorianu I, Vallier L. J Hepatol 84(3):618-630, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2025.09.021 — HNF4G identified as master TF; independent of HNF4A; chromatin remodeling; PI3K/AKT/mTOR upstream — full paper confirmed September 2026); (3) possible direct biliary fibrosis worsening. Every ARPKD trial using mTOR inhibitors MUST include hepatic endpoints — three reasons, not one.

Statins: Beneficial in cirrhotic portal hypertension. Potentially harmful in non-cirrhotic portal hypertension (Uschner FE, Ranabhat G, Choi SS, Granzow M, Klein S, Schierwagen R, Trebicka J et al. Sci Rep 5:14573, 2015. DOI: 10.1038/srep14573 — activate canonical Hedgehog → aggravate portal hypertension). ARPKD-CHF portal hypertension is partially non-cirrhotic. Caution warranted without ARPKD-specific data.

Sequential organ failure pattern

Life stageDominant diseaseKey events
0-5 yearsKidneyNephromegaly · hypertension · concentrating defect · potential neonatal respiratory failure · haeTKV predicts kidney survival
5-15 yearsLiver emergesPortal hypertension · splenomegaly → thrombocytopenia · cholangitis begins · NSBBs introduced
15-25 yearsBoth>50% CKD stages 1-3 · 31% kidney transplant · 13% liver/combined transplant (ARegPKD — Burgmaier K et al. Sci Rep 9:7919, 2019. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-019-43488-w — 49 patients, mean age 21.4 years) · portal hypertension 54% (ARegPKD — Burgmaier K et al. Sci Rep 11:21677, 2021. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-021-00523-z — 456 patients · ⚠️ confirm 54% is in this paper vs 2019 paper) · Guay-Woodford LM et al. Am J Kidney Dis 2025. DOI: 10.1053/j.ajkd.2025.01.022
Post-transplantLiver dominates mortality64-80% of mortality is from cholangitis and biliary sepsis — not kidney failure. CHF treatment is a survival issue, not quality of life.

CHF clinical emergence — the 40% → 65% trajectory

Only ~40% of ARPKD neonates show clinical evidence of CHF at birth, even though all patients have some degree of microscopic liver involvement from the start. Standard liver function tests (ALT, AST, bilirubin) are frequently normal despite active histological disease. By age 15, 60–65% of neonatal survivors have developed clinically evident liver disease — portal hypertension, splenomegaly, or cholangitis — confirming that liver disease is progressive and becomes the dominant challenge throughout childhood and adolescence. (Borden C, Dell KM. Adv Kidney Dis Health. June 23, 2026. DOI: S2949-8139(26)00032-7, citing Burgmaier K, Liebau MC et al. Sci Rep 9:7919, 2019.) Clinical implication: Routine abdominal ultrasound + liver function studies are insufficient to detect early CHF — normal labs do not rule out active biliary fibrosis. MRE liver stiffness and ELF score are the correct monitoring tools (IMAGE-ARPKD protocol).

Why gene therapy is the logical endpoint

FPC restoration via lentiviral delivery — proof-of-concept established in ARC syndrome (VPS33B) by (Cozmescu AC, Gissen P et al. Nat Commun June 2026. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-026-73631-x; Gissen P, personal communication July 2026) — or correcting FPC mislocalization via elexacaftor (⚠️ hypothesis only — untested in FPC-deficient cholangiocytes; introduced by Outeda August 5, 2026 as open question) could in principle address both organs simultaneously — because the molecular cause of disease (FPC loss) is shared across cholangiocytes and collecting duct cells. This is the fundamental biological rationale for gene therapy as the long-term therapeutic goal for ARPKD: one genetic cause, two organ manifestations. ⚠️ Three caveats that qualify this framing: (1) Downstream pathways are not identical. Principle 1 documents opposite signaling directions in kidney vs liver for AMPK and STAT3. The correct framing is identical genetic cause with potentially divergent downstream consequences — not identical protein dysfunction. (2) The Gissen 2026 proof-of-concept is for ARC syndrome (VPS33B), not PKHD1. Direct lentiviral evidence in PKHD1-deficient cells does not yet exist. (3) Critical safety caveat from Gissen 2026: non-liver-targeted (systemic) lentiviral delivery caused hepatic tumors in ~30% of mice; only the liver-specific version was tumor-free. For ARPKD — which requires both liver and kidney delivery — the oncogenic risk of systemic lentiviral vectors is unresolved. The liver-targeted approach leaves the kidney untreated; a systemic approach showed tumor risk. This is a delivery strategy conflict that must be resolved before lentiviral PKHD1 therapy can be planned.

⚠️ AAV Gene Therapy Safety Flag — NFκB Nephrotoxicity (Gupta, Morizane et al., STTT 2025)

AAV vectors drive nephrotoxic NFκB inflammation in human kidney organoids regardless of cargo — including empty-vector AAV. This safety signal was missed in animal models and detected only using human kidney organoids. Context: real patient deaths occurred in AAV-based DMD gene therapy trials, attributed to inflammatory responses that preclinical animal studies failed to predict. (Gupta N, Zhang K, Sabbisetti V, Shu J, Morizane R. Sig Transduct Target Ther 10(1):252, 2025. PMID: 40774951. Open Access.)

AAV serotype kidney organoid tropism data:

  • AAV2: Best kidney transduction — 46.2% proximal tubules, 45.8% distal tubules — but confirmed NFκB nephrotoxicity
  • AAV8: Negligible kidney tropism → safer renal profile for liver/biliary-targeted delivery (validates Liebau/Ziegler AAV8-SPP1 kidney safety)
  • AAV9: Negligible kidney tropism — similar liver-safety advantage as AAV8

Strategic implications: (1) For biliary-targeted ARPKD gene therapy (SPP1 knockdown, CFTR, FPCct delivery): AAV8 is both the correct serotype AND the kidney-safer option. (2) For kidney-targeted ARPKD gene therapy (collecting duct FPC restoration): AAV2 achieves highest transduction but carries real NFκB nephrotoxicity risk — kidney organoid safety screening is mandatory before any clinical application. (3) LNPs do not share the innate immune capsid recognition that drives AAV-NFκB — reinforcing LNP preference for renal delivery. (4) Morizane's kidney organoid platform is the validated safety screening infrastructure.

NEW FINDING — VERVE-102: GalNAc-LNP base editing platform — single IV infusion achieves durable hepatocyte gene editing in humans, confirming the platform; cholangiocyte targeting gap identified 📌 NEW · Sep 19, 2026

Vafai SB, Täubel J, Ashdown T, Patel RS, Diamondali S, Cegla J et al., Kathiresan S. "In Vivo Base Editing of PCSK9 with VERVE-102 for Hypercholesterolemia." N Engl J Med 395:648-659, 2026. DOI: 10.1056/NEJMoa2601283. Published May 25, 2026. Phase 1, 35 participants. Key findings: A single IV infusion of GalNAc-lipid nanoparticles encapsulating adenine base-editor mRNA + guide RNA reduced plasma PCSK9 by 88% and LDL cholesterol by 62% at the highest dose. Reductions appeared durable through 18 months of follow-up. No dose-limiting toxicity. Transient ALT elevation in 3/35 participants (all resolved by day 8). LNP half-life <20 hours — cleared rapidly from circulation. GalNAc moiety enables hepatocyte uptake through asialoglycoprotein receptor (ASGPR) and LDL receptor. Editing was specific to the liver; biodistribution in 26 tissues of nonhuman primates confirmed high liver editing with low editing in adrenals and spleen.

⚠️ The cholangiocyte gap — why this platform does not reach ARPKD-CHF target cells as formulated: ASGPR is expressed almost exclusively on hepatocytes — not on cholangiocytes (biliary epithelial cells). The VERVE-102 platform reaches the correct organ (liver) but the wrong cell type for ARPKD-CHF. The disease-driving cells in ARPKD-CHF — PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes and NGFR+ portal fibroblasts — are not transfected by GalNAc-LNP. This is the delivery gap that defines the ARPKD-CHF gene therapy problem: systems that target hepatocytes efficiently (GalNAc-LNP, GalNAc-ASO) are readily available, but no equivalent cell-type-specific address exists yet for cholangiocytes. Candidate biliary targeting addresses: αvβ6 integrin (A20FMDV2 peptide, Kd ~50 nM), EpCAM, KDR/VEGFR2. Contact: Sekar Kathiresan (skathiresan@lilly.com) — contacted Sep 19, 2026 regarding biliary targeting ligand substitution.

NEW FINDING — Gouon-Evans / VEGFA mRNA-LNP: paracrine delivery strategy bypasses the need for direct cholangiocyte transfection — paradigm shift for ARPKD-CHF delivery 📌 NEW · Sep 19, 2026

Rizvi F, Lee YR, Diaz-Aragon R, Bawa PS, So J, Florentino RM, Wu S, Sarjoo A, Truong E, Smith AR, Wang F, Everton E, Ostrowska A, Jung K, Tam Y, Muramatsu H, Pardi N, Weissman D, Soto-Gutierrez A, Shin D, Gouon-Evans V. "VEGFA mRNA-LNP promotes biliary epithelial cell-to-hepatocyte conversion in acute and chronic liver diseases and reverses steatosis and fibrosis." Cell Stem Cell 30(12):1640-1657, December 7, 2023. DOI: 10.1016/j.stem.2023.10.008. PMID: 38029740. PMC: PMC10843608. Predecessor platform paper: Rizvi F, Everton E, Smith AR et al., Gouon-Evans V. Nat Commun 12:613, 2021. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-021-20903-3.

Key findings: Standard IV mRNA-LNP injection → transfects virtually all hepatocytes + endothelial cells + Kupffer cells — NOT cholangiocytes directly. When VEGFA mRNA is the cargo: hepatocytes produce and secrete VEGFA → VEGFA acts paracrinally on adjacent KDR (VEGFR2)-expressing biliary epithelial cells → robust BEC-to-hepatocyte conversion. In both acute (acetaminophen) and chronic (CDE diet) injured mouse livers: fibrosis reversal, steatosis elimination, liver function restoration. KDR-expressing BECs identified as facultative progenitors. Blocking VEGF receptors in zebrafish abrogated BEC-driven repair; VEGFA overexpression promoted it — causality confirmed. Safety: same mRNA-LNP platform as COVID-19 vaccines, safety widely validated.

Paradigm shift for ARPKD-CHF delivery: This paper establishes that you do NOT need to transfect cholangiocytes directly to achieve a biliary therapeutic effect. By delivering a secreted therapeutic protein to hepatocytes via standard IV mRNA-LNP, that protein can act paracrinally on biliary cells through their surface receptors. The strategic implication: CNP (C-type natriuretic peptide) is a secreted peptide hormone that acts through GC-B/NPR-B on portal fibroblasts and biliary cells. Delivering CNP mRNA to hepatocytes via standard mRNA-LNP would cause hepatocytes to produce and secrete CNP locally, which could then act on GC-B-expressing portal fibroblasts and biliary cells without requiring cholangiocyte-specific targeting. This is technically feasible with the Gouon-Evans platform today. ⚠️ This is an inference from two independent lines of published evidence (Gouon-Evans 2023 delivery paradigm + CNP/GC-B biology). It has not been tested. Contact: Valerie Gouon-Evans (valerige@bu.edu) — contacted Sep 19, 2026.

⚠️ ARPKD-CHF-specific caveat: In ARPKD-CHF the BECs themselves carry the PKHD1 mutation. Whether BEC-to-hepatocyte conversion in a fibrocystic biliary context is beneficial (reducing fibrogenic BEC burden), neutral, or harmful (losing remaining biliary function) is unknown and has not been studied. This is the primary open question for the Gouon-Evans contact.

PKHD1-TFAP2B genomic architecture — TAD disruption model for glaucoma GWAS associations 📌 NEW · Sep 2026

Ishimoto Y, Menezes LF, Nakaya N, Barbosa-Sabanero K, Horie Y, Yoshida T, Reece JM, Zhou F, Tomarev S, Kerosuo L, Germino GG. Nat Commun 17(1):8650, Jul 23 2026. PMID: 42618573. PMC13490598. DOI confirmed from abstract set and PubMed. Tier 2. PKHD1 and TFAP2B are part of the same Topologically Associated Domain (TAD) — cis-acting chromatin interactions within the PKHD1 locus control TFAP2B expression in specific neural crest cell derivatives. GWAS SNPs near PKHD1 associated with POAG likely alter TFAP2B regulatory landscapes, not PKHD1 function. Specific causal SNP confirmed: rs17752199 (proximal to PKHD1) forms long-range chromatin loop contacts with the TFAP2B promoter — independently confirmed by companion chromatin dynamics paper (bioRxiv, March 2026. DOI: 10.64898/2026.03.12.711121). TFAP2B is a known regulator of PKHD1 and CYS1 expression in the kidney — if also active in biliary cells, FPC loss could disrupt TFAP2B-mediated transcriptional programs in cholangiocytes. Untested hypothesis. ⚠️ DIRECTION CORRECTED Sep 10, 2026 (Germino personal communication): the causal direction is the reverse — it is sequences/chromatin architecture around the PKHD1 locus that regulate TFAP2B expression in a restricted periocular neural crest cell subset, not TFAP2B regulating PKHD1. Whether the PKHD1 locus exerts similar cis-regulatory effects on TFAP2B in biliary cholangiocytes is unknown and untested. The biliary hypothesis above requires reformulation in light of this correction. ⚠️ Companion bioRxiv DOI: 10.64898/2026.03.12.711121 — unverified preprint, verify before citing. New contact: Gregory G. Germino MD, NIH/NIDDK (germinogg@mail.nih.gov) — co-discoverer of PKHD1, PI of Walker et al. ICD15/mitochondria paper (Nat Commun 2023), Outeda/Qian collaborator.

Kidney disease — complete framework

ARPKD is a hepatorenal disease. Both sides require understanding. This section was added July 24, 2026 following recognition that the kidney side had been under-represented in the framework.

Root kidney mechanism

Kidney cysts arise from collecting ducts and distal tubules — not glomeruli (Guay-Woodford LM et al. J Pediatr 165(3):611-617, 2014. DOI: 10.1016/j.jpeds.2014.06.015. PMID: 25015577). FPC-deficient primary cilia → calcium entry failure → elevated cAMP (Masyuk TV, Huang BQ, Ward CJ et al. Gastroenterology 125(5):1303-1310, 2003. DOI: 10.1016/j.gastro.2003.09.001. PMID: 14598246; Masyuk AI, Masyuk TV, Splinter PL, Huang BQ, Stroope AJ, LaRusso NF. Gastroenterology 131(3):911-920, 2006. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2006.07.003. PMC: PMC1866168) → PKA → Src/STAT3 (Dafinger C, Liebau MC et al. J Clin Invest 130:3756-3771, 2020. DOI: 10.1172/JCI130503) → epithelial proliferation and fluid secretion → cyst expansion.

The vasopressin/V2R loop: ARPKD kidneys lose concentrating ability early → the body secretes more vasopressin (AVP) to compensate → AVP binds V2R on collecting duct cells → more cAMP → faster cyst growth. A self-amplifying cycle. This is why tolvaptan (V2R antagonist) is now in ARPKD-specific trials. (Torres VE et al. (TEMPO 3:4 Investigators). N Engl J Med 367:2407-2418, 2012. DOI: 10.1056/NEJMoa1205037. PMID: 23121377 — tolvaptan ADPKD mechanism; NCT04786574 — ARPKD tolvaptan study withdrawn before enrollment)

SCY-770 — First direct AMPK activator in clinical development for PKD (June 2026)

SCYNEXIS acquired SCY-770 (formerly PXL-770, Poxel) in March 2026 for up to $196M. FDA Orphan Drug Designation. Phase 1 initiated June 30, 2026 with Phase 2 ADPKD proof-of-concept trial targeted for Q4 2026. (SCYNEXIS press release March 2026 — Company reported, not peer-reviewed) More potent and direct than metformin or bempedoic acid. ADPKD-focused — ARPKD kidney and biliary data pending. Key mechanism: AMPK activation suppresses both mTOR/cAMP (kidney cysts) AND LATS1/2→YAP/TAZ (liver HSC fibrosis), providing potential dual-organ benefit.

SPTAN1/RAC1/c-FOS (Kuraoka, Harris, Satlin, Morizane et al., Adv Sci 2026): SPTAN1 (αII-spectrin) is reduced in PKHD1⁻/⁻ cells → SLC8A1/NCX1 dysregulation → intracellular Ca²⁺ ↑ → RAC1 activation → c-FOS transcription (AP-1) → distal/connecting tubule cyst expansion. scRNA-seq + human ARPKD tissue confirms SLC8A1+ distal/connecting tubule as the definitive origin of ARPKD kidney cysts (not collecting duct as previously assumed). CRISPRa epigenetic upregulation of SPTAN1 in PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids alleviated cystic phenotypes, normalized intracellular Ca²⁺, and reduced RAC1/c-FOS — proof-of-concept that epigenetic rescue downstream of PKHD1 is feasible without correcting the mutation. Validated in organoid-on-chip + Sptan1-mutant transgenic mice + human ARPKD specimens. Drug targets tested in ARPKD models (Hiratsuka K, Miyoshi T, Gupta NR, Yamashita M, Morizane R et al. Sci Adv 8(38):eabq0866, 2022. DOI: 10.1126/sciadv.abq0866. PMID: 36129975 — the 2022 predecessor organoid-on-chip paper from the same group): R-Naproxen + R-Ketorolac (R-enantiomers that specifically inhibit Rac1-GEF interaction; FDA-approved; therapeutic efficacy confirmed in ARPKD organoid-on-chip) · T-5224 (c-FOS/AP-1 inhibitor; investigational; therapeutic efficacy confirmed in ARPKD organoid-on-chip 2022). ⚠️ NSC23766 and EHT1864 are established Rac1 research-tool inhibitors not specifically tested in ARPKD. SLC8A1 modulation — untested. Specific drugs tested in the 2026 Kuraoka/SPTAN1 paper not confirmed from abstract. Authors: Kuraoka S, Higashi Y, Saito S, Pourgonabadi S, Honjoh H, Ishigaki S, Harris PC (Mayo), Satlin LM (Mount Sinai), Yamashita M (Cedars-Sinai), Morizane R (MGH). Open question: Is SPTAN1 reduced in PKHD1-deficient cholangiocytes? If so, RAC1 activation in biliary cells may parallel the kidney pathway and contribute to ARPKD-CHF. Untested. Morizane Sep 25 follow-up question.

PTH1R/cAMP/pPKA/pCREB — second parallel cystogenic axis (Afrin, Harris, Gupta et al., Am J Physiol Renal Physiol 2025): PTH1R (parathyroid hormone 1 receptor) drives a second cAMP-elevating pathway in ARPKD kidney, parallel to V2R/vasopressin. PTH1R → cAMP↑ → PKA phosphorylation → CREB phosphorylation → cystogenic gene transcription. Demonstrated in human iPSC-derived PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids + isogenic WT controls + scRNA-seq + decedent human ARPKD tissue. Tolvaptan suppresses V2R-driven cAMP but leaves PTH1R-driven cAMP unaddressed — this partially explains tolvaptan's incomplete efficacy. PTH1R is a druggable GPCR. No PTH1R antagonist has been tested in ARPKD. Dual V2R + PTH1R suppression theoretically more effective than tolvaptan monotherapy.

⭐ NEW FROM FULL PAPER READ (Sep 22, 2026) — Three specific findings not previously documented:

(1) PTH2R agonism is NOT cystogenic. TIP39 (selective PTH2R agonist, 0.1–1.0 μM) failed to induce organoid growth in PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids (Supplemental Fig. S4 of Afrin et al., same paper). This distinguishes PTH1R as the therapeutic target — PTH2R is not the relevant receptor. Only PTH1R antagonism is warranted.

(2) PKHD1⁻/⁻ organoids have REDUCED PTH1R at baseline compared to wild-type — a significant reduction confirmed by confocal quantification of PTH1R:DAPI area ratio (Fig. 5F, 5G of Afrin et al.). Forskolin treatment restored PTH1R to wild-type levels. This means ARPKD cells don't overexpress PTH1R — rather, the downstream cAMP pathway is so sensitized that normal PTH1R signaling becomes pathological. This confirms and sharpens Gupta's personal communication (Sep 20, 2026).

(3) Quantitative data (confirmed from published figures): PTH 1.0 μM → 1.384 ± 0.246-fold organoid growth (P=0.0259); PTHrP 1.0 μM → 2.104 ± 0.700-fold growth (P=0.008511); tubular diameter 83.90 ± 5.75 μm (vehicle) → 118.02 ± 11.34 μm (PTH) → 151.27 ± 11.81 μm (PTHrP). AC inhibitor (2',5'-dideoxyadenosine, 10 μM) suppressed PTH-mediated growth: 1.553 → 0.934 (P=0.0218). PKA inhibitor (KT5720, 10 μM): 1.553 → 0.934 (P=0.03087). Tubular diameter rescue: 118.02 → 89.61 μm with ACi (P=0.0428), 88.85 μm with PKAi (P=0.0425). All from Afrin H, Robichaud JH, Qamar U, Harris PC, Gupta N. Am J Physiol Renal Physiol 329(2):F257-F268, 2025. PMID: 40602764. DOI: 10.1152/ajprenal.00056.2025.

(4) Morizane protocol connection: Gupta's group explicitly uses the Morizane differentiation protocol (ref 15 in Afrin et al.: Morizane R, Lam AQ, Freedman BS et al. Nat Biotechnol 33:1193-1200, 2015. DOI: 10.1038/nbt.3392) for all organoid generation. This is a direct published scientific link between the Mayo Clinic group (Gupta/Harris) and Morizane (Brigham/Harvard) — they use the same protocol. Context for Morizane introduction email (Sep 25).

⭐ MECHANISM CORRECTION (Gupta N, personal communication, Sep 20, 2026): PTH1R is NOT upregulated in ARPKD kidney. The cystogenic effect arises from normal PTH1R signaling — normal receptor activity drives normal cAMP elevation, which is cystogenic in PKHD1-deficient cells. The pathway is real but it is the PKHD1-deficient cellular context that makes normal cAMP signaling pathological, not abnormal PTH1R expression. ⭐ BILIARY QUESTION CLOSED (Gupta N, personal communication, Sep 20, 2026): PTH1R is absent in cholangiocytes. No biliary PTH1R work is warranted. Authors: Afrin H, Robichaud JH, Qamar U, Harris PC (Mayo), Gupta N (Mayo). Am J Physiol Renal Physiol 329(2):F257-F268, 2025. PMID: 40602764. DOI: 10.1152/ajprenal.00056.2025.

⭐ Three parallel kidney cystogenic pathways — summary (September 2026)

PathwayTriggerSignalTherapeutic targetStatus
V2R/vasopressin → cAMPVasopressin → V2R (Gs)cAMP ↑Tolvaptan (V2R antagonist)✅ Approved adults; not pediatric
PTH1R → cAMPPTH → PTH1R (Gs)cAMP ↑ (parallel)PTH1R antagonist (untested)🔴 No therapy; new target
SPTAN1 → RAC1 → c-FOSFPC loss → SPTAN1↓ → Ca²⁺↑RAC1 GTPase activatedSPTAN1 CRISPRa; RAC1 inhibitor🔴 Proof-of-concept only (organoids)

Note: Tolvaptan addresses only one of three pathways. The existence of two additional parallel cystogenic axes partially explains incomplete tolvaptan efficacy and the absence of approved pediatric ARPKD therapy.

⚡ First-ever interventional trials in ARPKD — active now

Tolvaptan — Study 204 (NCT04786574, withdrawn before enrollment) + Study 307 (NCT04782258, Otsuka)
Study 204 (NCT04786574) was withdrawn before enrolling any patients — no data were generated. Study 307 remains active and recruiting (28 days to 18 years, open-label, non-randomized, 10–20 participants, primary completion 2028). Study 307 is the first interventional drug trial ever conducted in ARPKD.

Critical hepatorenal mortality finding

64-80% of mortality in ARPKD kidney transplant patients is from cholangitis and hepatobiliary sepsis — not kidney failure. Patients who successfully receive a kidney transplant are then dying from liver disease complications. Treating CHF is survival-critical for the post-transplant population, not just quality of life. Telega G, Cronin D, Avner ED. Pediatr Transplant 17(4):328-335, 2013. DOI: 10.1111/petr.12076. PMID: 23593929

Kidney progression biomarkers

  • haeTKV (height-adjusted total kidney volume): Best early predictor of kidney survival. Maximal haeTKV in first 18 months of life stratifies risk — highest quartile: mean eGFR of 21 ml/min/1.73m² at year 1 vs 101 in lowest quartile. (ARegPKD — Burgmaier K et al. Sci Rep 11:21677, 2021. DOI: 10.1038/s41598-021-00523-z)
  • eGFR: Late marker. ARPKD-specific data: Mean yearly decline = 1.3 ml/min/1.73m² (Burgmaier K et al. Kidney Int 107(5):903-915, 2025. DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2025.01.023 — 658 patients). Kidney failure probability by age 20 = 50.1%. Note: the >3 ml/min/year "rapid progression" threshold is derived from ADPKD/ERA tolvaptan guidelines — not validated specifically for ARPKD.
  • Urine concentrating ability: Early defect confirmed in ARPKD. Drives AVP elevation → cAMP → accelerated cyst growth. Torres VE et al. JASN 15(12):3184-3192, 2004. PMID: 15579505 (PCK rat ARPKD model: AVP knockout nearly abolished cystogenesis).
  • Shrinking kidney phenotype: A subgroup develops shrinking (not enlarging) kidneys with progressive function loss. Size alone cannot monitor disease. (Arriola-Montenegro J, Mohan A, Gregory AV, Kline TL, Harris PC, Dahl NK. Kidney Int Rep 11(3):103755, 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.ekir.2025.103755)

Second ARPKD gene — DZIP1L

Rare ARPKD-like disease is also caused by biallelic DZIP1L mutations (cilia transition zone protein), causing an ARPKD-like phenotype including renal cystic disease and hepatobiliary involvement. ⚠ Whether CHF is consistently documented in DZIP1L patients requires full-text confirmation of Lu et al. 2017 — the abstract does not specifically mention CHF. Genetic testing should include DZIP1L when PKHD1 sequencing is negative. (Lu H et al. Nat Genet 49:1025-1034, 2017. DOI: 10.1038/ng.3871)

⚠ Transplant decision framework: Isolated kidney transplant is appropriate when liver disease is minimal. Combined liver-kidney transplant should be considered when recurrent cholangitis or severe portal hypertension complications are present. (Guay-Woodford LM et al. J Pediatr 165(3):611-617, 2014. DOI: 10.1016/j.jpeds.2014.06.015) Given that 64-80% of post-transplant mortality is from biliary sepsis, pre-transplant liver disease severity assessment is mandatory. (Telega G, Cronin D, Avner ED. Pediatr Transplant 17(4):328-335, 2013. DOI: 10.1111/petr.12076. PMID: 23593929)

Neonatal ARPKD — the earliest presentation

Neonatal ARPKD carries the highest early mortality of any phenotype. Historically up to 30% of affected neonates died in the perinatal period from respiratory failure; with modern neonatal intensive care, survival in the US improved to approximately 79% for the period 2010–2014. (Guay-Woodford LM et al. J Pediatr 165(3):611-617, 2014. DOI: 10.1016/j.jpeds.2014.06.015. PMID: 25015577; Liebau MC. Pediatr Nephrol 36:3561-3570, 2021. DOI: 10.1007/s00467-021-04970-8)

⚠ Pulmonary hypoplasia is the leading cause of neonatal death — not kidney failure

FPC-deficient kidneys lose concentrating ability in utero → reduced fetal urine output → oligohydramnios → Potter sequence: flat nasal bridge, low-set ears, limb positional deformities from uterine constraint, and pulmonary hypoplasia. Many neonates die of respiratory failure before kidney replacement therapy becomes relevant.

Bilateral nephrectomy decision

When massively enlarged kidneys compress the diaphragm and cause respiratory failure, bilateral nephrectomy may be performed as a life-saving intervention followed immediately by dialysis. This eliminates residual kidney function but respiratory survival takes priority.

Anemia — two mechanisms

CKD reduces erythropoietin (EPO) → normocytic normochromic anemia. Erythropoiesis-stimulating agents (ESAs) are standard once eGFR falls below 30-40 ml/min/1.73m². (KDIGO Anemia Work Group. KDIGO Clinical Practice Guideline for Anemia in Chronic Kidney Disease. Kidney Int Suppl 2(4):279-335, 2012. DOI: 10.1038/kisup.2012.37 — standard of care reference) Splenomegaly from portal hypertension causes hypersplenism → thrombocytopenia and hemolytic anemia — a separate mechanism that compounds EPO-deficiency anemia. Accurate diagnosis of the dominant mechanism is essential before treating.

Nutritional management in ARPKD

No ARPKD-specific dietary trials exist. The following is extrapolated from cholestatic biliary disease literature (primarily PBC, which shares impaired bile flow — Hirschfield GM et al. PBC guidelines, J Hepatol 77(5):1469-1484, 2022. DOI: 10.1016/j.jhep.2021.11.016) and renal management literature — both apply simultaneously in ARPKD.

Issue Mechanism in ARPKD Management
Fat-soluble vitamins
A, D, E, K		 Bile required for absorption — impaired bile flow reduces uptake of all four. (PBC/cholestasis guidelines — J Hepatol 2022; Vitamin D specifically: Nat Commun May 2025. DOI: 10.1038/s41467-025-59724-z) Routine screening; supplement based on levels. Vitamin D also impaired by CKD (reduced renal activation). Vitamin K deficiency increases coagulopathy risk alongside portal hypertension.
Low-sodium diet DUAL impairment — kidney disease reduces renal sodium excretion AND portal hypertension impairs hepatorenal sodium regulation Pediatric target <2g sodium/day. More important in ARPKD than either disease alone. Adjust as renal function declines. (Cardenas A et al. Gastroenterology 139(6):1912-1922, 2010. DOI: 10.1053/j.gastro.2010.05.056 — sodium in portal hypertension and cirrhosis)
Dietary fat choices Saturated fats require more bile for digestion — adds demand to impaired bile ducts Emphasize omega-3 fats (fish, nuts, seeds, avocado) over saturated fats. Anti-inflammatory omega-3s also modestly counteract the CHF inflammatory cascade. (Argo CK et al. Liver Int 40(9):2161-2169, 2020. DOI: 10.1111/liv.13965 — omega-3 in cholestatic/biliary disease)
Adequate fluid intake Low fluid intake elevates vasopressin (AVP) → V2R activation → cAMP → accelerates kidney cyst growth (Torres VE et al. JASN 15:3184, 2004. PMID: 15579505) High fluid intake suppresses AVP. Targets guided by nephrology team based on renal function.
Sugar restriction Excess sugar drives hepatic fat accumulation — can superimpose MASLD on top of ARPKD-CHF Limit added sugars and ultra-processed foods, particularly in adolescence and adulthood.
☕ Note on caffeine: In adults with cholestatic liver disease, regular coffee consumption is associated with slower hepatic fibrosis progression. (Kennedy OJ et al. Hepatology 56(6):2139-2150, 2012. DOI: 10.1002/hep.25907) Proposed mechanism: adenosine A2A receptor activation on hepatic stellate cells reduces their fibrosis output. (Peng Z et al. Am J Physiol GI 297(3):G575-583, 2009. DOI: 10.1152/ajpgi.00116.2009) Not applicable to children; not studied in ARPKD specifically.

🔬 Diagnostics — What Standard Testing Misses

  • Deep-intronic PKHD1 (AJKD 2025. DOI: 10.1053/j.ajkd.2025.03.016): c.8643-2945 G>A — 2,945 bp into intron, ALL prediction tools gave benign score. Only long-read WGS + urine-derived tubular cell RNA detected it. Mechanism: donor-gain pseudo-exon inclusion (121 bp, premature stop). Non-invasive urine RNA analysis validated as diagnostic method. (Molinari E et al. JASN 31:2422, 2020. DOI: 10.1681/ASN.2020010010)
  • Genomic autopsy (PMID: 40816622, 2025): Trio exome/WGS achieves 100% diagnostic yield in atypical prenatal PKD. Biallelic PKD1 mimics ARPKD prenatally — absent oligohydramnios discriminates from ARPKD.
  • CYS1 as third ARPKD gene (Kidney Int 2026. DOI: 10.1016/j.kint.2026.01.022): 3 patients, 2 families. ~24kb regulatory deletion (missed by exon sequencing) + c.4G>A myristoylation motif variant. Long-read WGS essential.
  • Biallelic PKD1 hypomorphic alleles (PMID: 41317153, 2025): 7 prenatal cases, severe ultrasound but mild outcomes — no oligohydramnios, no rapid ESKD. Long-read + haplotype resolution needed.
  • Phase-aware ACMG (PMID: 41751613, 2026): 68 ARPKD patients — PM3 criterion (VUS in trans with confirmed pathogenic variant + clinical ARPKD) upgrades VUS toward Likely Pathogenic.
  • Standard recommendation: Long-read WGS + urine-derived RNA analysis (Molinari E et al. JASN 31:2422, 2020. DOI: 10.1681/ASN.2020010010) + phase confirmation = complete PKHD1 diagnostic standard.

🧬 Open Questions in PKHD1 Regulatory Genomics

This section documents what is currently unknown about how the PKHD1 gene is transcriptionally controlled — a question distinct from the functional cascade of FPC protein loss. The Ishimoto/Germino Nat Commun 2026 paper is the first published work on PKHD1 locus chromatin architecture, establishing that sequences around the PKHD1 gene regulate TFAP2B expression in a small subset of periocular neural crest cells. The regulatory controls governing PKHD1 expression itself — particularly in biliary cholangiocytes — remain largely uncharacterized.

📬 Reviewer Invitation: If your work touches any of the questions below — or if you can confirm, correct, or extend any of the framing — we would be grateful for your input. This section will be updated with attributed responses. Researchers who have reviewed this section: Gregory G. Germino MD (NIH/NIDDK) — framework correction provided September 10, 2026. Please write to nathaniel_johnson@yahoo.com.

What is known (published)

  • PKHD1 locus chromatin or specific sequences regulate TFAP2B expression in periocular neural crest cells — not the reverse. (Ishimoto/Germino Nat Commun 2026; Germino personal communication Sep 10, 2026)
  • Large genomic deletions disrupting the TAD between PKHD1 and TFAP2B produce a periocular phenotype in mice — this disruption is mouse-specific and not reported in human ARPKD.
  • PKHD1 is expressed in renal collecting duct cells and biliary cholangiocytes — the two cell types that fail in ARPKD. Expression levels differ substantially between tissues. (Multiple sources)
  • FPC undergoes Notch-like proteolytic cleavage and the C-terminal ICD localizes to mitochondria — suggesting tissue-specific post-translational processing that may be regulated in part by transcriptional output. (Walker/Germino Nat Commun 2023)

Open Questions — Awaiting Investigation

  1. What transcription factors control PKHD1 expression specifically in biliary cholangiocytes? The regulatory elements driving PKHD1 expression in biliary epithelial cells have not been identified. Does a biliary-specific enhancer or transcription factor complex exist at the PKHD1 locus analogous to the periocular neural crest regulatory element? Primary routing: Germino GG (active effort underway, personal communication Sep 2026).
  2. Does chromatin architecture at the PKHD1 locus differ between renal tubular and biliary epithelial cells? The cell-type-specific chromatin state at the PKHD1 locus in kidney vs. liver has not been mapped. Such differences could explain why the kidney and liver disease trajectories in ARPKD do not perfectly track with genotype — if biliary-specific regulatory elements are independently vulnerable. Primary routing: Germino GG; secondary: Harris PC (Mayo), Outeda P (Maryland).
  3. Could coding variants within Zone 3 of PKHD1 (e.g., c.9370C>T / His3124Tyr; c.10926G>A / Met3642Ile) have secondary effects on local regulatory elements at the locus, beyond their primary effects on FPC protein? If coding region sequence changes alter local chromatin accessibility or affect regulatory elements embedded within the gene body, this could contribute to genotype-independent liver disease severity. Primary routing: Germino GG; secondary: Harris PC.
  4. Are analogous PKHD1 locus regulatory relationships likely in other cell types beyond periocular neural crest? The periocular neural crest relationship was discovered serendipitously via the glaucoma GWAS signal. Are there other cell types where PKHD1 locus chromatin controls expression of neighboring or distant genes — and could these explain any ARPKD-associated phenotypes beyond kidney and liver? Primary routing: Germino GG.
  5. What is the full tissue-specific regulatory map of PKHD1 expression across development? PKHD1 expression is highest in fetal kidney and liver, declines postnatally, and persists in adult biliary epithelium. The transcription factors driving this developmental trajectory have not been systematically characterized. Primary routing: Germino GG; secondary: McCracken KW (CCHMC, collecting duct organoid platform).

Section created September 10, 2026. Framework correction acknowledged from Gregory G. Germino MD (NIH/NIDDK, personal communication September 10, 2026). Permission pending for unpublished observations. All questions reflect published literature gaps and publicly available information.
